Draft 0.1 wrote both as bare `nA`, so `1A`, `3A`, `4A`, `6A` and `7A` each named two different decisions in one document; every occurrence is now prefixed. `C…`, `T…` and `R…` were already unique.

**One further input, and it is an input, not a plan.** The K branch carries `docs/design/08f-e7-implementation-plan.md`, a *requisition and production vertical slice*: work orders, facilities, reservations on the economy journal, typed-clock progress with an explicit generation-change policy, K5 offers, milestones E7a–E7e + E7k. This plan calls it **the production-slice proposal**, mines it for cuts 2–4, and adopts its §9 review checklist wholesale (§4). **Where it conflicts with a binding decision, the decision wins and the cut says so**; every override is marked **Ov** in §6.

**One tree now.** Draft 0.2 cited two trees — *(main)* at `3967e12` and *(K)* at `1412f96`. The K branch (`adc02cb`) merged into main at `1d5aa3d`, the one failing test was fixed in `2b62190`, and the whole-tree `rustfmt` landed as **`91208ab`** (08i headnote). Every *(main)*- and *(K)*-marked citation below therefore resolves in **one** tree, and every `file:line` in this document **predates the reformat and is historical**: the symbol beside it is the citation, the line is not. Draft 0.3 adds no line numbers; it cites by path and symbol, as 08i does, and §4 step (1) re-confirms each cut's slice by symbol on the tree it is built on.

**The sequencing question is closed, not merely resolved.** Draft 0.2 made the K merge a precondition of the whole plan (§11.3). It has happened: E7 develops on `main`, E keeps working on `main` with small commits in the shared files (`crates/mush-server/src/modules.rs`, `mush-config`, `ci.yml`), and K7 develops on fresh `codex/` branches from `origin/main`, one reviewable branch per K7.x slice with a `git merge-tree` check before each merge-back (08i answer 14). **That is the agreed standard, not what happened** (08j S12): K7.0–K8.6 crossed in **one** branch, `codex/k7-convergence`, 24 commits, a single merge-back at `4c80014`, with no pre-merge `git merge-tree` recorded. The branch origin, the K6-memo-first start order and E's main-line discipline all held. Whether K9 re-affirms per-slice branching or ratifies the single-convergence-branch practice is an open owner question (08j §9 Q5); until it is answered, answer 14 stands as written. What Draft 0.2 waited on has landed and is named by symbol in §2 and 08i §2 rows 1–17: `WorldTimeline`/`Ctx::world_ic_now`, `ClockProviderRegistry`, `ReservationRecord`/`ReservationBook`, `ChannelGrant`/`World::channel_grant_is_current`, `OfferRouteTable`/`Engine::resolve_legal_action`, `ControlLeaseState`/`crates/mush-sim/src/handoff.rs` (a private module; `HandoffProvider`/`HandoffScheduleAdapter` are not re-exported), `ContactState`/`mush-tactics`' contact actions, `ActionWindowRecord`, `ResolutionRecord`, `scheduled_action_ingress`/`DurableScheduleDriver`, `Ctx::action_state`. **No E7 cut waits on a K item that is not in the tree**, with three narrow exceptions stated where they bite: E7k's kernel-migration gates 1 and 3 (K8.0, §5), cut 10's LLM-driven rows (K7.0–K7.3, §3.10), and the hook-read context for movement/carry/render/perception hooks (G-B; §5), which no cut 1–11 needs.

## 1. Scope — what E7 is, in the owner's terms

Doc 10 §1 is a **solar-system industrial warfare sandbox** where every battle has an economic cause. Doc 10 §11 names the first playable corridor — **Mars–Phobos–Ceres** — and its proving story: *extract water and metals near Ceres → hire players to haul to Phobos → fight or evade an interception → manufacture or refit a ground unit → load it aboard a carrier → deploy to Mars → complete a ground operation that changes control or access → salvage, repair, pay, repeat.* **Q22A** bounds E7 to **one coherent narrow vertical slice**: that loop end to end, with **one ground engagement and one local-space interception**, both fought with **authored** units from the rules pack. E7 builds:

- **A world made of packs, not code** (Q6A/Q18A) — immutable digest-referenced rules packs and once-applied campaign-seed packs; only what a table cannot express is Rust.
- **Quantity truth with a location** (Q2D/Q13A) — a neutral `mush-inventory` owning location-bound pools *and* lineage-bearing lots, every balance on an **E3 custody endpoint**, never a locationless account balance.
- **Industry that plans legibly** (Q1A/Q14A/Q3A) — deposits with deterministic curves and revealed *bands*; `ClaimRecord` tenure with licences as entitlements; facilities, recipes and work orders reserving matter, money and capacity through a **C8 shape-conformant** record.
- **One character, one active station** (C2A/C4A/C5A/Q20A) — billets stay many; the live control surface is the one focused station's K5a `ChannelGrant`, never a union.
- **Voyages as inhabited play** (C16A/C20A/Q15A) — the carrier root travels with its interiors, occupants, cargo and carried craft coherent; an interception **promotes** it into a live domain and demotes it at a quiet boundary with a durable summary.
- **One propagation layer** (Q4A/Q16A/R4) — detection and delivery over one physical context; contacts belong to a platform, never a side; orders are envelopes that must actually arrive.
- **A deterministic pulse** (S2A/Q12A/T2) — `DomainClock::Pulse`, tactical ingress on the prepared K lane, **one commit per domain per tick**, and no economic transaction inside a pulse.
- **Declared operations that settle** (Q5D/T6/Q9A/Q17A) — frozen terms plus mutable runtime under one `OperationId`, formations as constraint sets bound to org scopes, a bond as an economy hold, one journaled resolution root fact with idempotent consumers.
- **Crew and seats** (C11A/C15A/T10–T16/Q10D) — named decision NPCs plus an aggregate complement, readiness on the operation cadence, **consent-grant** proxy seats with dual attribution and deterministic fallback.
- **A floor under loss** (S3A/Q7A/T7) — a funded recovery organization leasing loaners against an eligibility test over *accessible* assets, never a faucet.

**Exit criterion.** Doc 10 §11's proving loop runs end to end on the seed pack, with the ground engagement and the local-space interception both resolving through the pulse driver and settling through `campaign.operation.resolved`.

### 1.1 Explicitly out of E7

- **The belief engine.** Doc 10 §14 splits the knowledge dependency; E7 ships only (a), the deterministic contact/observer-projection layer. Rich NPC beliefs, inference, misinformation, forged orders and relationship-sensitive pricing are later (Q16A; doc 10 §14 (c)).
- **Full construction and manufacturing balance; the full weapon-family matrix; boarding actions; aerospace transitions between ground and orbit; fleet-scale tuning** (Q22A, verbatim). E7 authors *enough* recipes to run the loop — "a proving loop that must first be balanced is not one."
- **Kernel migration of reservations and settlement** — gated on the two remaining kernel-migration gates, which are now a *named* K item, **K8.0** (a Ledger-bearing `ReplayWrite` and the seal inside the authoritative bundle; 08i answer 13), plus the K8.1–K8.3 harness (§2.4, §5). Gate 2 (typed reads) is closed by `Ctx::action_state` for commands, ticks, boot and observers, and open for the movement/carry/render/perception hook contexts (G-B). No cut 1–11 blocks on it (10a §4 dep 2).
- **The market — and its whole vocabulary.** `mush-market` — listings, order books, quotes, vendors, the safe public projection — is **E6c's** (10a §5; 08e answer 20; doc 10 §14). doc 10 §11's "one open market" is **explicitly incomplete until E6c**, and E7 builds no substitute. **Correction to Draft 0.1 and to 10a §4 dep 1:** both said the corridor ships "direct vendor sale (E6b's vendor-from-stock path)". There is no such E6b path — `Supply::Stock { vault }`, quotes and vendors are all in **E6c** (08f §2's cut table; §5.2 *"Listings, quotes, vendors"*). What E7 actually needs is what 08f §5.2 says accepting a quote *drafts*: **an ordinary E4b2 contract**, which is landed. So the corridor's sale is a **plain two-party E4b2 contract with a funded NPC corporation principal** — `InstrumentKind::{Currency, Commodity}` consideration, a `Before` deadline, and the physical transfer as an ordinary cut-2 inventory act linked back by `ExternalRef` (T2, D-E7-31). No listing, no quote, no vendor record, no order book. §9 q12 asks whether the owner would rather cuts 3 and 11 wait for E6c's vendor path instead.
- **Line/panel projection parity and any TacSim panel.** The proposal's §7 item 10 and E7e require a structured panel beside the line render. E7 keeps the *rule* — one internal view feeds both the line render and any DTO, and DTO bytes stay bounded (§4) — and **ships no TacSim panel**; `PanelFeedHandle` (`crates/mush-core/src/module.rs:605-635`, main) is there when a later cut wants one.
- **`scene.outcome`** — **E6b's** producer, registered by the server under adjudicator/facilitator authority (08f D-E6-7). E7 consumes it and registers no scene-outcome kind of its own.
- **The party subsystem** — **E6a's** `mush-party` (Q21A). There is never a TacSim-specific party record.
- **The proposal's E7e "bind every mutation to K5 legal offers"** — *as a blanket rule over cuts 1–7 and 9–11.* K5b **has landed**: `OfferRouteTable` and `ProjectedLegalAction` (`crates/mush-core/src/offer.rs`), `Engine::resolve_legal_action`, and the unified callers `ActionCoordinator::{gateway_ingress, scheduled_ingress, prepare_and_stage_resolved, prepare_and_stage_scheduled}` (`crates/mush-server/src/action_coordinator.rs`; 08i §2 row 5). Cut 8's tactical actions and cut 4's `@wo/quote` offer bind to it (§3.4, §3.8); the legacy-lane workflow verbs of the other cuts do not, because they are exempt records under §4.1 rather than mechanical offers, and their binding is E7k's migration review (§5), not a cut.

## 2. What E7 consumes as landed

### 2.1 K4a — the versioned world timeline *(K)*

- `WorldTimelineEpoch { real_anchor, ic_anchor, rate, generation }`, `rate` in IC seconds per real second, zero meaning a paused world clock — `crates/mush-core/src/clock.rs:167-176`. A new epoch anchors to the exact IC value the preceding generation produced at `real_anchor` — the rule at `:161-164`, the construction `next_rate_epoch` at `:290-295` (decisively `ic_anchor: current.ic_at(real_anchor)`, `:292`), the validator `:333-341` — so **a rate change never reinterprets an earlier fact**. (`:180-190` is `WorldTimelineEpoch::ic_at`, the evaluator, not the anchoring rule.)
- `ClockRef::{ Utc, WorldIc{generation}, SceneTurn{scene,generation}, DomainTick{owner,id,generation}, Registered{owner,id,generation} }` — `clock.rs:64-103` (doc `:64-66`, body `:69-103`). **`DomainTick { owner, id, generation }` is cut 8's clock.** Its provider side is also landed and is composition-frozen: `ClockProviderRegistry` and `ClockProviderKind::{DomainTick, Registered}` in `crates/mush-core/src/clock_provider.rs`, registered only through `Registrar::clock_provider` at compose time and read through `Engine::clock_now` (there is no `Ctx` verb). The only `DomainTick` registrant on the tree is the `arena` fixture (`crates/mush-contract-tests/src/tactical_gate.rs`); the production provider is cut 8's — **one owner-scoped `DomainClockProvider` per owner, receiving the runtime `domain_id` and generation**, which K adds as G-A (08i answers 4 and G-A), so a domain promoted at runtime never registers anything and never mutates the frozen registry (§3.6, §3.8).
- `ClockStamp` with `checked_cmp` / `checked_duration_since`, which refuse across clock identities — struct `:105-114`, impl `:116-136`, `checked_cmp` at `:119`; **two** error types, `ClockMismatch` `:141-146` and `ClockArithmeticError` `:148-157`.
- Append-only history with contiguity, monotonic-anchor and IC-continuity validation — `WorldTimeline::{from_epochs, current, stamp_at, next_rate_epoch, append}`, `clock.rs:193-341`; bounded at `MAX_WORLD_TIMELINE_EPOCHS = 4_096` (`:53`).
- Immutable per-generation documents plus an atomically updated head — `world_timeline_epoch_key` / `world_timeline_persistence_ops`, `clock.rs:429-465`; `WorldTimelineHead::validated_generation`, `:397-427`.
- Persistence and **boot hydration before modules** — `crates/mush-server/src/timeline.rs`, `load_or_seed_world_timeline` `:21-67` and `hydrate_world_timeline` `:71-79`: configuration seeds generation one only (`if docs.is_empty()`, `:29-30`), persisted truth wins thereafter, a changed `[game]` clock warns rather than re-anchoring (`:57-65`).
- **Synchronous module-context read** — `Ctx::world_ic_now()`, `:482-484` — not `async`, no `.await` — inside `impl Ctx` at `:478`. Boot order is independently visible at `crates/mush-server/src/lib.rs:247` (hydrate) before `:249` (`boot_modules`). Doc 09's record of the cut: `09:2140-2145` *(K)*.

### 2.2 K5a — station channel grants *(K)*, and the hazard it closes *(main)*

- `ChannelGrant { actor, subject, station, relation, owner, priority, channels, epoch }` — `crates/mush-core/src/world.rs:50-69`; `CommandSubjectLink` is now an alias (`:71-73`).
- **One grant per occupied station, no union and no max-priority promotion** — `crates/mush-sim/src/links.rs:18-59` *(K)* builds exactly one grant per seat on a placed, live unit, taking `channels` and `priority` from **that station's own** `StationSpec` and the unit's control generation as `epoch`; `refresh_actor_links` republishes from scratch (`:63-74`).
- **Draft 0.2's "real difference from main" is closed.** Pre-merge main unioned channels across an actor's seats and took the maximum priority — the promotion hazard doc 09 C8 names. On the converged main, `crates/mush-sim/src/links.rs` `desired_links` / `refresh_actor_links` build **one grant per seat, no union** (08i §2 row 4, verified in code), so cut 5 builds on `main` as it stands; "the merge, not a rebase, supplies K5a" was true and is done (`1d5aa3d`).
- **Exact station/channel/epoch revalidation for delayed work** — `World::channel_grant(actor, subject, owner, station)` and `channel_grant_is_current(.., epoch, required_channel)`, `world.rs:670-699` *(K)*: absence, revocation, an epoch bump or a narrowed channel set all fail closed. Doc 09's record: `09:2146-2151` *(K)*. Its stated boundary — per-channel active leases and safe-boundary transfer as K6 work — **is now delivered too**: `ControlLeaseState::{checked_request_handoff, checked_cancel_handoff, checked_apply_handoff}` and `ControlHandoffDraft { request, requested_by, next_holder, requested_at, boundary, reason }` in `crates/mush-core/src/control.rs`; `crates/mush-sim/src/handoff.rs` (a private module; `HandoffProvider`/`HandoffScheduleAdapter` are not re-exported): its `HandoffProvider` (an `ActionProvider`) and `HandoffScheduleAdapter` (a `ScheduleActionAdapter`), with apply gated by `require_scheduler`; and the `ControlLeaseGuardValidator` registered under `AuthorityGuardKind::ControlLease` by `mush-sim`'s own `register` (`crates/mush-sim/src/lib.rs`) — 08i §2 row 6. `World::channel_grant_is_current` has **no production caller** yet; the kernel's `AuthorityGuard::ChannelGrant` revalidates in `Engine::take_staged_action`. What is *not* in the tree is policy: controller selection and boundary policy (K6-open-2), which cuts 5 and 10 supply as ingress **over** these types, never beside them (§3.5, §3.10).

### 2.3 K3 audit closures E7 may assume *(K)*

- **K3.1a — checkpoint proof (closed):** memory and Redis atomically verify every exact replay post-image **before** deleting the full intent; a mismatch retains the intent and fails closed (`09:2057-2058`).
- **K3.1b — store-owned admission (closed):** the store refuses a second unresolved authoritative reservation, so two pending bundles cannot claim the same fresh object id (`09:2059-2061`).
- Bundle hard caps — `crates/mush-core/src/authoritative.rs:27-38` *(main)*: replay writes 1_024 (`:27-28`), definitions 256 (`:29-30`), witnesses 1_024 (`:31-32`), durable-outbox intents 256 (`:33-34`), payload 256 KiB (`:35-36`), bundle 16 MiB (`:37-38`). Q12A and 10a §6 sharpening 4 call these "the real cap on a domain's active unit count"; **that is true of the tick commit, but only three of the six can bind it** — `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES` (1_024) bounds *how many* per-unit post-images a tick may write, `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_BUNDLE_BYTES` (16 MiB) and `MAX_DURABLE_OUTBOX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` (256 KiB) bound *how large* they may be. The other three are irrelevant to a tick. Cut 8 states which one binds, and §9 q6 measures it. **`MAX_DURABLE_OUTBOX_INTENTS` (256) is not the accepted-intent cap** — outbox intents are what a bundle emits, not what a tick consumes; Draft 0.1 derived `MAX_INTENTS_PER_TICK` from it, which was a category error (§7).
- **K3.1e — Redis-gated coverage (closed):** Redis tests are explicit ignored tests locally **and** an explicit Linux CI job, so skipped Lua coverage cannot count as passing (`09:2071-2078`).

### 2.4 The three kernel-migration gates, and the K6 control gate

Before **any** production reservation or settlement moves off the economy journal onto authoritative K3 state families, all three must land (proposal §2.3; 08c §12's K3 review, `08c:1793-1813` *(main)*, the three gates named at `:1804-1805`): (1) an authoritative bundle can carry the **mixed owner write set**, including the Ledger append; (2) ordinary module code can **synchronously read its typed state families outside an action**; (3) the **Ledger seal is folded into the same exact bundle** rather than acting as an external predecessor barrier. **Status on `91208ab`** (08i §2 rows 13 and 26; answers 13 and 15, G-B): **gate 2 is closed** — `Ctx::action_state` / `Ctx::scan_action_state` (`crates/mush-core/src/module.rs`), owner-scoped, work in commands, module ticks, boot callbacks, observers and contextual hooks — **except** inside `move_attempt`, `carry_attempt`, rendering and perception hooks, for which K adds a narrow immutable hook-read context (G-B); no cut 1–11 reads typed state from those hooks. **Gates 1 and 3 are open and now owned**: K names them **K8.0** — idempotently replayable Ledger materialization, the seal inside the authoritative bundle, memory/Redis conformance, failure/restart probes — sequenced before K8.1 (answer 13); 08i's E fallback draft for them is withdrawn. `crates/mush-core/src/authoritative.rs` `ReplayWrite` has no Ledger variant and `flush_prepared_action` still rejects on a non-empty `RetainedSeals`.

Then a **migration review** opens. Q3A adopts 08c §12 verbatim: it "is then still an explicit **owner decision**, not a mechanical port" (`08c:1806-1808`; `:1810-1813` is the separate E0-hazard note) — **the trigger opens a review, never an automatic migration**. §5 states what would move.

**Separately, the K6 control gate — now a memo predicate, not a kernel wait.** K5a grants "establish eligibility and stale-intent witnesses; they are not active exclusive leases" (proposal §2.4; `09:2151` *(K)*); the active exclusive lease is K6's `ControlLeaseState`, which is in the tree (§2.2). What is not in the tree is the **K6 look-back memo**, which K will file with this honest status (08i answer 2): **control leases/handoffs — production kernel and `mush-sim` authority, policy ingress incomplete; contacts — production family and lifecycle actions, sensor ingress/compaction incomplete; atomic tactical damage, unit DTOs, bridges and projection-leak proofs — fixture-complete only; production unit authority, placement authority and viewer feeds — incomplete, handed to E** (cuts 6 and 8). Cut 10's activation predicate therefore reads **the memo's production rows plus E's own placement (cut 6) and unit (cut 8) migrations** (§3.10), and the standing rule is unchanged: E7 may assign title, maintenance and custody, but never silently evicts a live operator or transfers an active movement/weapons lease outside a `ControlHandoffDraft` at its `boundary`.

The other seven are still absent and are E7's to create. **One correction:** a `Deposit`-named type *does* exist — `contract.rs:567` `DepositRecord { object, vault, deposited_by, at }`, E4b2 escrow bookkeeping — so cut 3's mining `Deposit` must not be introduced into `mush-economy`'s namespace and must not be confused with it in prose. (`ruleset-tacsim` also already appears as a forward reference in doc comments at `mush-sim/src/links.rs:4` and `mush-tactics/src/mobility.rs:7`.)

**Four gaps the survey surfaced, all E7 work.** (G1) **There is no route *definition* type at all — confirmed against the whole tree.** The only route-shaped types are `Leg` (`route.rs:47-52`), `JourneyStatus` (`:56`), `Journey` (`:69-90`) and `RouteState` (`:112`); a route exists only as whitespace-separated `<room>[:secs]` tokens parsed per invocation inside `route_create` (`crates/mush-sim/src/commands.rs:1584-1648`). (`InteractionRoute`, `RouteConfig` and the SSH `Route` are unrelated.) Cut 1's pack route tables therefore need a real route definition; cut 6 consumes it. (G2) **No `sim.*`/`tactics.*`/`inventory.*`/`industry.*`/`campaign.*` fact provider exists**, so every contract `Fact` condition on a boundary fact answers `None` today — cuts 2, 3, 6, 8 and 9 register them (§10). (G3, **narrowed on `91208ab`**) **No `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator exists on the tree** — only the enum arm in `crates/mush-core/src/action.rs` — so cut 2 is the first *Reservation* registration. Draft 0.2's wider claim ("nothing in the tree registers an `AuthorityGuardKind` validator; no in-tree pattern to copy") is stale: `mush-sim`'s `register` registers `ControlLeaseGuardValidator` under `AuthorityGuardKind::ControlLease` and `mush-tactics` registers `ContactGuardValidator`, and those are the patterns cuts 2 and 4 copy. (G4, **closed**) Draft 0.2's "the control-epoch comparison does not exist in main" was true of pre-merge main; on the converged main it is `World::channel_grant_is_current(.., epoch, required_channel)` (`crates/mush-core/src/world.rs`), which fails closed on absence, revocation, an epoch bump or a narrowed channel set, and the kernel's `AuthorityGuard::ChannelGrant` runs it in `Engine::take_staged_action`. Cut 5 writes no comparison; it makes sure every focus change bumps the witnesses the comparison reads (§3.5).

**Two constraints the E1–E5 look-back adds (08g §4, §10).** (C1) **One custody effect per plan** — `MAX_DEPOSITS` 1, the module-layer cap of one custody op per plan, and no two-phase custody provider (E4b2-D27, E3-D17/D60): a contract moves one object, so a multi-item consignment is one container object or one contract per object (DL-15); cuts 2–4 shape their lots and deliverables to that. (C2) **Economy schedules on `ClockRef::DomainTick` are forbidden.** Draft 0.2 hung this on a *trigger* — "until the declared-cadence registration lands at convergence" (DL-14; 08h D-LBK-7). **That trigger has fired** (`0afe161`: `[economy.cadences]` → `crates/mush-economy/src/declared.rs` `register`), so the prohibition is restated on the **shape of `EconomicClock`**, where it is structural rather than conditional: `EconomicClock` is `{RealUtc, WorldIc, Manual, Cadence}` with **no `DomainTick` arm and no `From<ClockRef>`**, so an economy schedule on a domain tick is *unwritable*, not merely forbidden. The two clock families never meet: E registers one composition-frozen `ClockProviderKind::Registered` provider per declared cadence, K supplies the separate owner-keyed `DomainClockProvider` (`crates/mush-core/src/clock_provider.rs`), and the only `DomainTick` registrant today is the `arena` fixture (08j §6 seam 1). Cut 8's domain clock drives sorties and propagation, never an obligation or a work-order schedule; adding a `DomainTick` arm to `EconomicClock` would be the change this rule forbids.

---

## 3. Cut structure

The eleven cuts of 10a §4, in order. Each is independently CI-green and adversarially verified before the next builds on it (§4). Cut 1 is playable content, not scaffolding, so feedback comes early. **The shortest proving path** is 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 6 → 11 (partial); if the owner wants a playable corridor sooner than a correct campaign layer, cuts 9/10 move behind a stub operation record (10a §4 dep 3).

| # | Crate(s) | One line | Verify axis | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | `ruleset-tacsim` (skeleton) | the rules-pack loader and a tiny corridor fixture — three locations, two commodities, one recipe — so every later cut has a world to run in | pack digest identity, load determinism, fixture reproducibility | nothing |
| **2** | `mush-inventory` (new) | pools and lots, locality bound to E3 custody endpoints, split/merge/dissolve/crystallize, custody reservations | lineage loss, quantity conservation, locality escape, reservation double-spend | E3 custody |
| **3** | `mush-industry` (deposits) | deposits, `ClaimRecord` + licence entitlements, surveying with bands, extraction and depletion | band leakage, depletion arithmetic, claim contest and seizure | cut 2 |
| **4** | `mush-industry` (production) | facilities, recipes, capacity reservations shape-conformant to C8, work orders as scheduled journaled production | restart mid-work-order, atomic consume, capacity double-book | cut 3 |
| **5** | `mush-sim` | actor-side **active-station focus** as world-owned ephemeral state, the campaign **handoff ingress** (holder, reason, boundary) over `mush-sim`'s `handoff.rs`, reach/traversal, republished links — **no lease or release code of its own** | one-focus enforcement, stale-epoch intents, handoff at a boundary | cut 4 (a titled unit with **no** station); K5a's `ChannelGrant`/`channel_grant_is_current` and K6's `ControlLeaseState` (both landed; 08i §2 rows 4, 6). Cut 5 **ships** the Q11 seam rather than depending on it |
| **6** | `mush-sim` | **carrier journeys** — the root travels with interiors, occupants and cargo coherent — plus tactical **promotion/demotion** | carrier topology across a leg, state preserved across promotion, active-domain limits | cuts **1** (`RouteDef`), 2, 5 |
| **7** | `mush-propagation` (new) | detection and delivery over one propagation context; **observation intents into `mush-tactics`' `ContactState` family** (never a second contact record), source/age/confidence as a side projection; order envelopes and acknowledgement | fog-of-war leaks, order supersession under ECM, ack round trip, false contacts as propagation-owned phantoms | cut 6; K6's contact family (landed; 08i §2 row 7) |
| **8** | `mush-sim` + `mush-tactics` | the **minimal deterministic pulse driver**: `DomainClock::Pulse` as the owner-scoped `DomainClockProvider`, the domain tick as a schedule-source action over an `ActionWindowRecord`, one commit per domain per tick inside the **measured** K3 bundle limits; the production **unit typed family** (`TacticalUnit` only) and `DomainRecord` authority | determinism and replay, tick commit bounds, restart mid-tick | cut 7; the K3 lane and K5b offers (landed); cut 5 (focus → grant witnesses); K's G-A `DomainClockProvider` contract |
| **9** | `mush-campaign` (new) | declared operations as terms + runtime under one `OperationId`, formations as constraint sets bound to org scopes, bond as an economy hold, resolution fan-out | freeze-at-activation, one-operation-per-unit, audience leaks, resolution idempotency | cut 8, `mush-faction`, E4b2 |
| **10** | `mush-campaign` + `mush-party` | crew complements and readiness obligations, **proxy seat offers/grants** with dual attribution and deterministic fallback, musters and open crew calls, participation facts | guardrail escapes, revocation at a safe boundary, absent-player settlement, audit irreversibility | cut 9; E6a's `mush-party`; the Q11 oracle; the K6 memo's production rows + cuts 6/8 (§3.10); LLM-driven rows K7-gated |
| **11** | `ruleset-tacsim` + `mushd` config | the **recovery provider** and the full Mars–Phobos–Ceres corridor seed pack; the timeline's rate policy | doc 10 §11's proving loop end to end; pack versioning and digest stability under live contracts | cuts 1–10. **Not** E6c: the sale path is a plain E4b2 contract (§1.1) |

**Dependencies, corrected.** (1) **E6c gates only the "one open market" criterion**, not the physical loop: the corridor's sale is a landed E4b2 contract, and Draft 0.1's "E6b's vendor-from-stock path" does not exist (§1.1). Cut 11 therefore does **not** depend on E6c. (2) **No E7 cut 1–11 blocks on a K item that is not in the tree** — Q3A's C8 interim is K-independent; K5a, K5b and the K6 kernel are landed (§2.2, headnote); what remains K-side is the migration *review*'s K8.0 gates (§5), the LLM rows of cut 10 (K7.0–K7.3), the G-A `DomainClockProvider` contract cut 8 implements (a doc-only K item, before SP2), and the K6 memo cut 10's predicate reads. Draft 0.2's "cut 5 needs K5a … neither of which exists in main" and its merge-precondition were correct on `3967e12` and are discharged on `91208ab`. (3) The proposal's milestones **E7a–E7e** are **superseded** by cuts 2–4 and this table (D-E7-25); its **E7k** becomes §5. (4) ~~Cut 10 needs `mush-party`, which E6a has not built (§9 q7)~~ — **struck by cut 1's §4.0 pass:** E6a shipped `crates/mush-party` and the server composes `PartyModule` in `server_modules_with`, so §9 q7 is moot and closes and **the eleven cuts are self-contained**. There is no cross-programme dependency left in the table.

### 3.1 Cut 1 — `ruleset-tacsim` skeleton and the corridor fixture

**Records** (Q6A: everything expressible as a table is a versioned TOML content pack, in two kinds).

```rust
enum PackKind { RulesDefinition, CampaignSeed }
struct PackManifest { id: CanonicalId, version: u32, kind: PackKind, digest: DefinitionDigest,
                      requires: Vec<(CanonicalId, u32)> }
struct RulesPack { manifest: PackManifest, entries: BTreeMap<CanonicalId, PackEntry> }
struct RouteDef { id: CanonicalId, endpoints: (LocationId, LocationId), legs: Vec<LegDef>,   // ← G1
                  alignment_band: CadenceId, drive_class: CanonicalId, propellant: u64 }
struct SeedApplication { pack: CanonicalId, version: u32, digest: DefinitionDigest,
                         applied_at: ClockStamp, objects: u32 }          // `tacsim:seed:{pack}`
```

**Two corrections to the block above, from cut 1's §4.0 pass and the owner's OPEN rulings (2026-09-05).** (a) **`RouteDef` and `LegDef` are `mush-sim`'s types, not `ruleset-tacsim`'s** (OPEN 2). `mush-sim → {core, economy, proto}` and every ruleset depends on `mush-sim`, so `mush-sim → ruleset-tacsim` would be a cycle and the cut as drafted is unbuildable. The **type** goes in `crates/mush-sim/src/route.rs` beside `Leg`, behind a `TryFrom` wire, adding **no crate edge**; the **data** is authored in the tacsim rules pack and loaded by `ruleset-tacsim` — `crates/mush-tactics/src/mobility.rs`' stated pattern, *the crate owns the state machine, the ruleset provides the data*. `endpoints` is `(ObjId, ObjId)`, since `LocationId` does not exist and a place in this tree is an object; the pack authors them symbolically and the seed resolves them. (b) **the seed document key carries the version** — `tacsim:seed:{pack}@{version}`, §4 rule 3's shape, because invariant (ii) is "at most once per `(pack, version)`" (OPEN 5). The comment above saying `tacsim:seed:{pack}` is struck.

A **rules-definition pack** is immutable and referenced by digest: propulsion and route tables, the ten commodity families (S4A), component definitions, recipes, formation kinds (Q9A), combat and insurance policy. A **campaign-seed pack** instantiates mutable world state **once**: corridor locations, organizations, deposits, facilities, inventories, NPCs. The distinction is load-bearing — *a rules pack is re-pinned and diffed, while re-running a seed pack would duplicate a mine* (Q6A). `[tacsim.*]` host config carries deployment switches and pack paths only, mirroring `[shadowrun.economy].catalog_paths` (08f §9).

**`RouteDef` closes G1.** There is no route type in the tree: a journey's route is only `<room>[:secs]` tokens parsed in `route_create` (`crates/mush-sim/src/commands.rs` — the symbol; the `:1584-1648` cite is off by one at the start and is historical) over `Leg { to, travel_s }` (`route.rs`). T1 needs a *definition* — route cost and duration must be deterministic functions of the route definition, drive and payload, declared departure, propellant plan, and the body-pair alignment band on a campaign cadence — so the **type** lands in `mush-sim` (correction (a) above), the **data** is pack data from cut 1, and cut 6 is what makes journeys consume it. Cut 1 changes no existing type: `Leg`, `Journey`, `RouteState` and `route_create` are untouched.

**Invariants.** (i) A published digest is never mutated; a change publishes a **new version that coexists** (Q18A), and live units, blueprints, recipes and open contracts stay **pinned** until an explicit migration or in-world refit — T1's guarantee that a published hauling contract's route assumptions cannot shift underneath it. (ii) A seed pack applies at most once per `(pack, version)`; the `SeedApplication` document is the idempotency latch, written in the same batch as the objects. (iii) Loading is deterministic — same bytes, same digest, same iteration order. (iv) An unknown, oversized or digest-mismatched pack **fails closed at boot** and names the file. The engine already enforces the shape: `DomainRecord.driver` is an exact digest-bearing `DefinitionRef` (`crates/mush-sim/src/data.rs:227-232`, main) and K3 validates exact descriptors at registration (`crates/mush-core/src/module.rs:622-634`, main).

**The tiny corridor fixture.** Three locations — a Ceres extraction site, a transfer node, a Phobos yard; two commodities — `metals` and `structural_alloys`, both from S4A's ten; one recipe — `alloy_smelt@1`; one `RouteDef` joining Ceres to Phobos. Enough for cuts 2–4 to run and for the owner to see output first.

**Commands.** `@tacsim/pack list|show <id>|verify` · `@tacsim/seed <pack> [dry|commit]` (staff) · `@tacsim/pin <subject>=<pack>@<version>` (staff, for a migration or refit).

**`ruleset-tacsim` is not a `[rulesets]` id** (owner, 2026-09-05, overriding OPEN 3's default and closing contradiction (vi)). `ruleset_modules` knows `"wod"` and `"shadowrun"` only and **refuses more than one** — that guard is unchanged, and `[rulesets].enabled` stays exactly one character ruleset. Doc 10 §20 is why: *TacSim supplies physical and civil context, not ruleset metaphysics … a ruleset decides what those facts mean to its characters.* So `TacsimRuleset` is a **`GameModule` with no `SheetSchema`** (`mush_ruleset::Ruleset` requires one, and cut 1 has none), enabled by **`[tacsim].enabled`** independently of `[rulesets].enabled`, and composed **after** the character ruleset so it can read that ruleset's facts and the ruleset can read TacSim's environmental facts through the generic seam. Its order position is pinned by a boot-order test. The crate keeps doc 10's name because its packs carry rules. `npc_agent_adapter` still answers `None` for it, for a new reason: it is not a ruleset id at all.

**Tests (~22).** *Digest:* identical bytes → identical digest; a whitespace-only edit changes it; a reordered table does not (canonical encoding); `MAX_PACK_ENTRIES` refuses. *Load:* two loads are byte-identical in memory; an unknown key, a missing `requires` dependency and a `requires` version mismatch each fail closed naming the file. *Seed:* `dry` writes nothing and lists every planned object; `commit` applies once; a second `commit` is a HIT writing nothing; a *different version* of the same pack is refused with the applied version named; a crash between object writes and the latch converges on reboot. *Pinning:* a subject pinned to `v1` is unaffected by publishing `v2`; both versions load concurrently; an unpinned consumer of a retired version fails closed rather than silently upgrading. *Routes:* a `RouteDef`'s duration is a pure function of definition, drive and payload; two evaluations agree; an unknown drive class refuses. *Fixture:* the three locations, two commodities, recipe and route exist after `commit` and nowhere else; the fixture is reproducible from an empty store; boot with a corrupt pack refuses to start. *Added by the review, and rewritten by cut 1's §4.0 pass (OPEN 5, closing contradiction (viii)):* ~~two `commit`s racing (two processes, one store)~~ — **the world is a single writer, so "two processes, one store" is not a reachable race.** The two reachable ones are asserted instead: **two `@tacsim/seed … commit` invocations in one world**, serialized by that single writer, leaving exactly one applied set and one latch with the loser a **HIT** rather than a partial write; and **a reboot after commit**, which converges because the objects and the latch ride **one `WriteBatch`** (`World::create` marks dirty, `Ctx::persist` queues a `ModuleOp`, and the world task's flush folds `take_dirty()` and `take_module_ops()` into a single batch), so a restart sees **both or neither**. A partial write is unreachable by construction and a test pins that it stays so. Also: a pack whose bytes parse but whose declared digest was computed over **non-canonical** bytes is refused at load, not at first use; the digest covers the canonical encoding and **not** the manifest's own `digest` field (a self-referential hash is unverifiable); every `PackManifest`, `RulesPack` and `SeedApplication` **re-enters through its constructor on load** (`TryFrom` wire, §4) and a stored pack over `MAX_PACK_ENTRIES` is refused rather than truncated; `@tacsim/pack show` on an unknown id and on a **known but unreadable** id answer identically (no pack-existence oracle for a non-staff viewer); `@tacsim/seed` on a store already carrying a *different* pack's objects applies only its own and names nothing about theirs.

**Verify lenses.** **P (pack identity)** — can two byte streams produce one digest, or one stream two? **S (seed idempotency)** — can any interleaving or crash duplicate a seeded object or lose the latch? **B (boot fail-closed)** — does any malformed pack degrade silently instead of refusing?

**Hands to cut 2:** the commodity family list, `alloy_smelt@1`, the `RouteDef` type, and the digest discipline every later definition reference uses.

#### 3.1 As built (2026-09-05, on `a1934a7`)

**Crates and edges.** New crate **`ruleset-tacsim`** (`crates/ruleset-tacsim/`): `lib.rs`, `pack.rs`, `record.rs`, `store.rs`, `seed.rs`, `commands.rs`, `config.rs`, `fixture.rs`, plus the two source-controlled pack files and `tests/e7c1.rs`. Its whole dependency set is **`{mush-core, mush-sim}`** plus `serde`, `serde_json`, `smol_str`, `thiserror`, `tracing` (dev: `mush-testkit`, `mush-proto`). **The tree gained exactly two crate edges**: `ruleset-tacsim → mush-core` and `ruleset-tacsim → mush-sim`, and `mush-server → ruleset-tacsim` at the composition root. `mush-sim` gained **no** edge (`CadenceId` already reached it through `mush-economy`). There is no economy, org, party, faction, market or tactics edge, and `cargo tree -p ruleset-tacsim` is the test. Cut 1 registers **no** `FactProvider`, canon outcome kind, authority-guard validator, clock provider, schedule source or typed state family, touches `mush-core`'s resources not at all, and adds no `WorldCommand::ModuleAsyncDone` arm.

**Signatures.**

```rust
// crates/ruleset-tacsim/src/pack.rs — B1, the P lens
pub const PACK_DIGEST_DOMAIN: &str = "tacsim.pack.v1";
pub const MAX_PACK_ENTRIES: usize = 512;   pub const MAX_SEED_OBJECTS: usize = 256;  // ← P5
pub const MAX_PACK_BYTES:   usize = 512 * 1_024;   pub const MAX_REQUIRES: usize = 16;
pub const MAX_LABEL_BYTES:  usize = 120;   pub const MAX_RECIPE_LINES: usize = 16;
pub const MAX_RECIPE_UNITS: u64 = 1_000_000_000;   pub const MAX_ROUTE_PROPELLANT: u64 = 1_000_000_000;
pub const MAX_LEG_SECS: u32 = 31_536_000;          // ← P3, the magnitude bounds
pub enum PackKind { RulesDefinition, CampaignSeed }
pub struct PackRequirement { pub id: CanonicalId, pub version: u32,
                             pub digest: Option<DefinitionDigest> }   // ← P8
pub struct PackManifest { /* private */ }        // new(id, version, kind, digest, requires)
pub enum PackEntry { Commodity(CommodityDef), Recipe(RecipeDef), Route(RouteEntry), Location(LocationDef) }
pub struct RulesPack { /* private */ }           // new(manifest, entries: BTreeMap<CanonicalId, PackEntry>)
pub fn pack_digest(canonical: &serde_json::Value) -> Result<DefinitionDigest, PackError>;
pub fn load_pack(bytes: &str) -> Result<RulesPack, PackError>;            // NO filesystem
pub fn check_requires(pack: &RulesPack, loaded: &BTreeMap<(CanonicalId, u32), RulesPack>) -> Result<(), PackError>;
// crates/ruleset-tacsim/src/record.rs — the three persisted records
pub struct PackRecord      { /* id, version, kind, digest, entries: u32, first_seen: ClockStamp */ }
pub struct SeedApplication { /* pack, version, digest, applied_at: ClockStamp, objects: u32,
                                placed: BTreeMap<CanonicalId, ObjId> */ }
pub struct PinRecord       { /* subject, pack, version, pinned_at: ClockStamp */ }
// crates/ruleset-tacsim/src/store.rs, seed.rs, lib.rs
pub fn load(kv: &[(SmolStr, serde_json::Value)], cfg: TacsimConfig) -> TacsimStore;   // total
pub const PACK_PAGE: usize = 32;   pub const MAX_PINS: usize = 128;               // ← B1/B3/B4
impl TacsimStore { pub fn is_applied(&self, pack: &CanonicalId, version: u32) -> bool;
                   pub fn applied_version(&self, pack: &CanonicalId) -> Option<u32>;
                   pub fn published_digest(&self, id: &CanonicalId, v: u32) -> Option<DefinitionDigest>; // ← P1
                   pub fn resolve(&self, subject: &CanonicalId) -> Result<(CanonicalId, u32), ResolveError>;
                   pub fn route(&self, id: &CanonicalId) -> Option<&RouteDef>;          // resolves the subject
                   pub fn route_in(&self, pack: &CanonicalId, v: u32, id: &CanonicalId) -> Option<&RouteDef>; // ← S4
                   pub fn missing_placements(&self) -> impl Iterator<Item = (&CanonicalId, u32, usize)>; } // ← S3
pub fn plan(store: &TacsimStore, pack: &CanonicalId) -> Result<SeedPlan, SeedError>;  // writes nothing
pub fn apply(ctx: &mut Ctx<'_>, plan: &SeedPlan, owner: ObjId) -> Result<SeedApplication, SeedError>;
pub fn verify_sources(cfg: &TacsimConfig) -> Result<Vec<LoadedPack>, String>;         // names the file
pub struct TacsimRuleset;  impl GameModule for TacsimRuleset { /* no SheetSchema */ }
// crates/mush-sim/src/route.rs — B5, beside `Leg`
pub const MAX_LEGS: usize = 32;   pub use mush_economy::CadenceId;
pub struct LegDef       { /* to: ObjId, base_s: u32 */ }
pub struct RouteDef     { /* id, endpoints: (ObjId, ObjId), legs, alignment_band: Option<CadenceId>,
                             drive_class, propellant */ }
pub struct DriveProfile { /* class, thrust_permille, payload_capacity */ }
pub fn route_duration(route: &RouteDef, drive: &DriveProfile, payload: u64) -> Result<i64, RouteDefError>;
// crates/mush-server — the host half
pub fn config_ext::tacsim_config(cfg: &TacsimCfg) -> Result<ruleset_tacsim::TacsimConfig, String>;

```

**The digest, and exactly what it covers.** `pack_digest` is a thin wrapper over `DefinitionDigest::for_canonical_value_with_limit(PACK_DIGEST_DOMAIN, value, MAX_PACK_BYTES)` — the function `mush-core` already had. **Cut 1 defines no second digest and no second canonical encoder.** The hashed value is built by one function, `RulesPack::canonical_value`, and is exactly `{ "manifest": { id, version, kind, requires }, "entries": { id → entry } }`. *Hashed:* the pack id, the version, the kind, the ordered `requires`, and every entry. *Not hashed:* the manifest's own `digest` field (a self-referential hash is unverifiable), and the encoding version — which rides the **domain tag**, length-prefixed by `hash_parts`, per doc 09 §2.6.

*How one stream cannot make two digests:* `pack_digest` is pure over a `serde_json::Value`, the entries are a `BTreeMap` so iteration is id-ordered, and `canonical_json_bytes_with_limit` is the shared canonical encoder — no map ordering, no float, no locale and no clock enters it. Two loads of one file are asserted byte-identical in memory *and* byte-identical re-encoded. *How two streams cannot make one:* the comparison at load is against the **content's** digest, so a declaration computed over any other bytes — including the file's own raw bytes, which is the mistake an author actually makes — is refused **at load** naming both digests, and the domain tag makes a pack digest differ from a same-bytes digest under any other domain. The one thing that deliberately *does* collide is **formatting**: reordering the entries, reordering the keys inside an entry, and reflowing whitespace all produce one digest, because what is hashed is content. A whitespace edit **inside a value** changes it. That is the only self-consistent reading of §3.1's digest list — hashing raw file bytes would make a reordered table change the digest, which the same list forbids — and both halves are asserted.

**The store's document table** (the module's own doc-comment carries it in full):

| doc | class | corrupt → | direction of the loss |
|---|---|---|---|
| `tacsim:pack:{id}@{version}` | ledger | **skipped loudly**, world boots | the **key** still counts, so the version stays *published* and is never treated as new. **What stops a silent re-publication is the row's `digest`, compared at boot** (P1, and the owner decision of 2026-09-05): a loaded pack whose content disagrees with its row **refuses the boot** naming the file and both digests. A corrupt row costs exactly that comparison for that one version — the first-published digest is gone with the body — so boot repairs the row from the loaded pack and **pages level 2 naming the pack**, rather than repairing in silence |
| `tacsim:seed:{pack}@{version}` | latch | **skipped loudly**, world boots | the **key** still bars a re-seed. This is the one place where losing the body must not lose the fact: reading "cannot parse" as "not applied" would duplicate a mine |
| `tacsim:pin:{subject}` | pin | **skipped loudly**, world boots | a lost pin does **not** float its subject onto a newer version — resolution refuses instead (invariant (i)) |
| `tacsim:meta` | allocators | tolerated field by field | floors are re-derived from every parsed key, so losing it costs nothing (E6b's R8) |

Every one of the three is behind a `TryFrom` wire re-running its constructor with documented caps, and the **key-derived** floors (`published_keys`, `seeded_keys`) are E6b's R8 finding applied where it bites hardest: the latch is the key, not the body.

**The seed latch's one-batch argument.** `apply` creates its objects through `World::create` (which marks them dirty) and writes the latch through `Ctx::persist` (which queues a `ModuleOp`); `mush-server`'s `flush_dirty` folds `take_dirty()` and `take_module_ops()` into **one** `WriteBatch` before it reaches the store. A reboot therefore sees the objects **and** the latch or neither, and a test asserts exactly that round trip. The only remaining race is two `@tacsim/seed … commit` invocations, and the world is a single writer: they serialize, and the loser is a **HIT** that writes nothing (asserted from two different staff actors, with `take_module_ops()` empty afterwards). *What could break it:* `apply` performs the only fallible work — the owner pre-flight and the digest lookup — **before** the first `World::create`, so no `?` survives into the irreversible half; the one structurally-unreachable create failure is handled rather than propagated, latching **exactly what exists** and paging level 2 with the command filled in, because a partial seed recorded honestly and never re-run is the fail-closed direction and a re-run would duplicate a mine.

**Config.** `[tacsim]` and `[tacsim.time]` are `TacsimCfg`/`TacsimTimeCfg` in `crates/mush-config/src/config.rs` beside `ShadowrunCfg`, `#[serde(default)]`, `enabled = false` off-config, in `Config` and its doc table. **Live:** `enabled`, `pack_paths`, `seed_paths`, `corridor`. **Parsed and inert:** `active_domain_limit` (cut 6), `ground_pulse_ms`/`space_pulse_ms` (cut 8), all of `[tacsim.time]` (cuts 4/8/11) — and deliberately **not validated**, on E6a's `catalog_paths`/`quote_ttl_*` precedent: a knob that configures nothing must not be able to refuse a boot, and the cut that reads a field is the cut that validates it. `[tacsim.recovery]` is not added. **The proof that `[tacsim.time]` never seeds the world timeline** is `timeline::tests::e7c1_config_tacsim_time_never_seeds_the_world_timeline`: two configurations differing *only* in `[tacsim.time]` (rate 4000 against absent) seed a byte-identical generation 1 whose rate is `[game]`'s; and a persisted second generation still wins. Structurally, nothing in `ruleset-tacsim` constructs a `GameClock` or a `WorldTimelineEpoch` at all.

**Where `RouteDef` landed, and why.** In **`crates/mush-sim/src/route.rs`**, beside `Leg` — OPEN 2's default, taken. §3.1/§4.1 put it in `ruleset-tacsim` and §3.6 makes `mush-sim` its consumer; since every ruleset depends on `mush-sim`, `mush-sim → ruleset-tacsim` is a cycle and the cut as drafted is unbuildable. The split is `mush-tactics/src/mobility.rs`'s stated pattern: the crate owns the state machine, the ruleset provides the data. It **adds no crate edge** and changes no existing type — `Leg`, `Journey`, `RouteState` and `route_create` are untouched, asserted by a test. `route_duration` is a pure function of definition, drive and payload: checked integer arithmetic in a fixed order, no clock, no world, no RNG, so two evaluations always agree — which is the only thing T1's frozen route assumptions can rest on.

**The five OPEN decisions, as taken.**

1. **Who reads the pack files — taken as written.** The **host** reads bytes (`config_ext::tacsim_config`, bounded by `MAX_TACSIM_PACK_BYTES`, directories searched in sorted order) and the ruleset owns parse + digest + validation; `load_pack` is a pure `bytes → Result<RulesPack, PackError>` with no filesystem, so every P-lens test is a unit test. **E6c chose differently and we say so:** `ruleset-shadowrun`'s `SrCatalogSource::pack` reads a file itself at `@market/catalog load` time. That is right *there* — an optional staff-triggered catalogue must fail the *pack*, never the server — and wrong here: invariant (iv) requires a malformed pack to fail the **boot** naming the file, and only the host can fail a boot. Both answers have their own test, in different crates.
2. **Where `RouteDef` lives — taken as written** (above).
3. **How `tacsim` composes — OVERRIDDEN by the owner** (2026-09-05, doc 10 §20). Not a `[rulesets].enabled` id: a **layer** that coexists with the one character ruleset, a `GameModule` with no `SheetSchema`, switched by `[tacsim].enabled`, composed **after** the ruleset in `mushd`. `ruleset_modules` and its single-ruleset guard are untouched; the unknown-id fixture moved from `"tacsim"` to `"nosuch"`; `npc_agent_adapter` still answers `None`, now because tacsim is not a ruleset id at all. The tripwire's `RULESETS` did **not** gain `"tacsim"` (it would fail — `ruleset_modules` rejects it); the layer is pushed into `production_commands`'s composition instead, which enumerates its verbs under both single-ruleset compositions.
4. **Which knobs cut 1 ships — taken as written** (above).
5. **The seed key and the "two processes" test — taken as written.** The document key carries the version, the store keeps a re-derived `pack → applied version` index, and a different version is refused **naming the applied one**. The unreachable-race test is replaced by the two reachable ones plus the reboot-convergence test.

**Deviations, with reasons.** (a) **Packs are JSON, not TOML.** Q6A says "TOML content pack"; the brief fixes this crate's dependency set with no `toml` edge, E6c's content packs are JSON, and the digest's canonical encoder *is* JSON — a TOML front end would add a second parse whose canonicalization is not the one the digest hashes. The shape Q6A cares about (everything expressible as a table) is unchanged. (b) **`SeedApplication` gains `placed: BTreeMap<CanonicalId, ObjId>`** beside §3.1's `objects: u32`. A count proves the seed ran but names nothing it made, so after a reboot the corridor would be unreconstructible and no route could be materialized; `objects` is kept as the declared count and the wire refuses a record whose count and map disagree, turning the redundancy into an invariant. (c) **`RouteDef.alignment_band` is `Option<CadenceId>`**, not `CadenceId`. `CadenceId` is a runtime-allocated `u64` a pack cannot author, and no campaign cadence is declared in cut 1; a sentinel `CadenceId(0)` would be exactly the fail-open shape this programme avoids. Cut 6 binds it, and `route_duration` never reads it — an alignment band is a departure window, not a duration term. (d) **`endpoints` is `(ObjId, ObjId)`**, since `LocationId` does not exist and a place in this tree is an object; the pack authors endpoints **symbolically** and the seed's `placed` map resolves them, which is also what keeps a route's meaning independent of load order (a route may only name places its own pack declares). (e) **Two extra document families beyond §3.1's list** — `tacsim:pack:{id}@{version}` (the published-version ledger) and `tacsim:pin:{subject}`. The ledger is what makes *retirement* detectable across a reboot; without it nothing can distinguish "never published" from "published and then withdrawn", and invariant (i)'s "an unpinned consumer of a retired version fails closed" is unimplementable. **Scope, corrected by the independent verify (P2):** invariant (i)'s rule is **per subject**, and so is this ledger's use of it. A retired version fails closed for *the subjects the retired pack governs* — the ones a loaded version of that pack still declares, plus the ones that pack's own seed latch actually placed — and for nothing else. As first built, the retired-seed check looped the whole `applied` index and admitted `published_keys` as an independent trigger, which is a fact about the **pack** and not about the **subject**: dropping one already-applied campaign-seed file made `resolve` refuse for *every* subject in *every* still-loaded rules pack and misattribute each to the unrelated seed pack. `published_keys` may widen the **evidence** that a candidate pack was retired; it never makes an unrelated pack a candidate. (f) **A second `commit` is a success, not a refusal** — the brief says HIT, so the same `(pack, version)` answers "already applied; nothing was written" and returns `Ok`; a *different* version is the refusal.

**Every contradiction, and what was built instead.** (i) `mush-party` exists and is composed — §9 q7 closed, §3's dependency note (4) struck, and cut 1 built nothing party-shaped. (ii) the canon-outcome table is not empty — E7 still registers no kind. (iii) `org_capability_vocabulary()` chains housing too — cut 1 adds no capability; `force.*` is still cut 9's. (iv) `advance_cadence_manual` has E5b's caller and still no **sim** caller — cut 1 adds neither. (v) `RouteDef` moved to `mush-sim` (OPEN 2). (vi) `ruleset-tacsim` is a layer, not a `[rulesets]` id (OPEN 3, overridden). (vii) the digest already existed and was **not** re-derived (§9 q3). (viii) "two processes, one store" replaced by the two reachable races (OPEN 5). (ix) `[tacsim]`'s live/inert split written down (OPEN 4). (x) the drifted citations are struck in §4.0. Beyond the ten: **§9 q11 is discharged** — E6b shipped `SceneRecord.ic_floor` and `raise_ic_floor`, so §10's first upstream ask is met and cut 6 owns only the arrival raise.

**Rule 1 and the allowlist.** `@tacsim` is in `crates/mush-contract-tests/legacy-handlers.toml` in the same commit, sorted between `@stats` and `@teleport`, `category = "legacy-post-image"`, `family = "tacsim.pack"`, with a note naming the four record families, their strict wires, their deterministic keys and the fact that none of them takes part in authority or prepared mechanics. **`PINNED_BASELINE` is untouched at 158** — a new handler is never `baseline`. K8.5: `TacsimRuleset::register` calls `reg.classify_commands(CommandCompatibility::LegacyPostImage)` before its first `reg.command`, and `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` now asserts it by name.

**Tests.** 32 new: 23 in `crates/ruleset-tacsim/tests/e7c1.rs` (digest ×5, load ×5, seed ×6, pinning ×2, fixture/config/disclosure/verify/ledger ×5), 4 in `crates/mush-sim/src/route.rs`, 2 in `mush-server`'s `config_ext` (the boot-refusal half of the B lens), 1 in `mush-server`'s `timeline` (`[tacsim.time]`), 2 boot-order/composition pins in `mush-server`'s `modules`. **The independent verify fold below adds 20 more** and rewrites one of the 23.

**Cut-2 handoff.** Cut 2 takes **the commodity family list** (ten ids, authored in `tacsim.core`, `metals` and `structural_alloys` in use), **`alloy_smelt@1`**, **the `RouteDef` type** (`mush-sim`, with `route_duration` as its only evaluator), and **the digest discipline** — `DefinitionDigest::for_canonical_value_with_limit` under a domain tag, the manifest's own digest excluded, versions coexisting behind pins. **Nothing else.** Cut 2 writes **K's** `ReservationRecord`/`ReservationBook` from `crates/mush-core/src/resource.rs` and defines no reservation shape of its own (D-E7-2 as re-grounded in §4.0), and it is the first `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` registration, copying `mush-sim`'s `ControlLeaseGuardValidator` and `mush-tactics`' `ContactGuardValidator`. Cut 1 leaves it **no quantities of anything** to migrate out of (§9 q4).

##### Independent verify fold (2026-09-05, on `4f8ff6c`)

Step (3)'s **independent, probe-confirming adversarial verify** of cut 1, folded per step (4). 19 confirmed items across the three lenses, each with a regression test in the `// ---- E7 cut 1 independent verify fold ----` section of the file it names, named `vf_<lens><n>_…`. Four cross-lens duplicates are merged and land once: **P1** covers S1, **P2** covers S2, **B4** covers P4, **B5** covers S6 — the gaps in each lens's numbering are those merges and nothing else. **Four claims this cut's own text made are false and are corrected in place** in the same edit (marked ✗ below). **B7 is refuted and nothing changed.**

**Owner decision folded (P1, 2026-09-05).** A pack whose `(id, version)` is already published to this world but whose content digest differs at boot is a **boot refusal** — the host fails the boot naming the file and both digests, the same fail-closed shape as a missing or unreadable pack file, because a seeded world depends on the exact bytes. Never page-and-drop, never adopt; a **new** `(id, version)` with a new digest is the normal path. `@tacsim/pack verify` recomputes and compares against the **ledger's stored digest** so it can print a mismatch, and is staff-only, paged and bounded like every other list.

| # | Finding | What changed | Test |
|---|---|---|---|
| **P1** (HIGH, +S1) | The ledger's `digest` was **written and read by nothing**, so `boot_reconcile`'s bare `contains_key` skip adopted an in-place re-signed pack silently, and `pack_verify` compared the loaded pack with its own manifest — the equality `load_pack` already proved, so it could structurally only print `ok` | `lib.rs::boot_reconcile` compares digests and **refuses the boot** naming `loaded.source` and both digests; the "already published" test reads `published_keys`, so a corrupt row cannot re-publish under new bytes, and its repair from the loaded pack now **pages level 2**. `store::TacsimStore::published_digest` is the one read; `commands.rs::pack_verify` compares against it. ✗ **the two document tables in `store.rs` and `record.rs` corrected in place** (they claimed the guarantee this fix supplies) | `vf_p1_a_re_published_version_refuses_the_boot_and_verify_reports_the_mismatch` (`tests/e7c1.rs`) |
| **P2** (HIGH, +S2) | `store.rs::resolve`'s retired-seed loop walked the whole `applied` index and took `published_keys` as an independent trigger, so retiring **one** applied seed pack refused **every** subject of **every** loaded pack, misattributed | The loop is scoped to packs that could govern **this** subject: a loaded pack that declares it, or an applied pack whose own latch `placed` it. `published_keys` is no longer a trigger. ✗ **deviation (e)'s scope sentence corrected in place** | `vf_p2_a_retired_seed_refuses_only_the_subjects_it_declares` |
| **B1** (HIGH) | `@tacsim/pack verify` was `require_enabled` alone and **unpaged**, re-hashing up to `MAX_CONFIGURED_PACKS × MAX_PACK_BYTES` = 32 MiB synchronously on the world's writer, from any player, at command rate | Staff-only; the no-argument form pages on records **visited** at `PACK_PAGE`; a named `<id>[@<version>]` is a direct lookup, so exactly one re-hash. `commands.rs`'s **Bounds** block now names every switch | `vf_b1_verify_is_staff_only_and_paged` |
| **P3** (MED) | `PackEntry::check` bounded counts and lengths and **no magnitude**: a zero-input recipe, a `u64::MAX` output, zero propellant and a 136-year leg all loaded | `MAX_RECIPE_UNITS`, `MAX_ROUTE_PROPELLANT`, `MAX_LEG_SECS` in `pack.rs` beside `MAX_RECIPE_LINES`, with `ZeroRecipeUnits` / `RecipeUnitsTooLarge` / `LegTooLong` / `PropellantTooLarge` refusing **on load**. `propellant == 0` is **explicitly legal** (a ballistic transfer or a towed leg) | `vf_p3_magnitudes_are_bounded_on_the_wire_and_the_bounds_still_evaluate` |
| **S3** (MED) | `reindex_routes` had no `World`, so a placed object destroyed since the seed was neither paged nor re-created — the route was re-materialized over a tombstone | `lib.rs::boot_check_placements` walks each latch's `placed` map against `ctx.w.get`, pages **level 2 naming the pack** with the command filled in, and records the symbols in `TacsimStore::missing`; `materialize_route` and `seeded_objects` skip them | `vf_s3_a_destroyed_placed_object_skips_its_route_and_pages_the_pack` |
| **S4** (MED) | `TacsimStore.routes` was keyed by the route's **pack-local** id alone, so a second seeded pack whose id merely sorts later silently replaced the corridor's `ceres_phobos` | Keyed `(pack, version, id)`; `route_in(pack, version, id)` is the exact query and `route(id)` resolves the subject through `resolve` first. **Signature change** to the as-built block above | `vf_s4_two_packs_declaring_one_route_id_are_both_addressable` |
| **B2** (MED) | `@tacsim/pack list` pushed its page marker on records **visited**, ahead of `may_view`: a 33-seed-pack server answered a non-staff viewer differently from an empty one, and the fixed visit budget let a viewer subtract rendered rows to count the hidden | The **work** stays bounded on records visited (D-LBK-5); the **marker** is bounded on records the viewer may see. ✗ **`commands.rs`'s Disclosure block's "at every switch … none of them branches on how much the viewer already knows" corrected in place** | `vf_b2_pack_list_is_not_a_pack_existence_oracle` |
| **B3** (MED) | `@tacsim/pack show` rendered every entry unpaged — 515 messages per non-staff command at `MAX_PACK_ENTRIES` | Paged at `PACK_PAGE` with a trailing row naming the remainder and the cursor to continue; the cursor is an **argument** (`show <id>[@<version>] [<from-entry>]`), not a switch | `vf_b3_pack_show_pages_its_entries_and_names_the_remainder` |
| **B4** (MED, +P4) | A pin needed no **declared subject**, so a typo became a durable pin answering `Ok` for a definition no pack declares; and `tacsim:pin:` was uncapped, undeletable (no `ModuleWrite::Del` in the crate) and invisible from the game | The pinned pack must declare the subject (refused through the existing existence-hiding message, so no new oracle); `MAX_PINS = 128`; `@tacsim/pin list` (paged) and `@tacsim/pin <subject>=` (the crate's one `Del`; no index floor is derived from a pin key). The ledger rows stay uncapped and unretired **on purpose** — deviation (e) | `vf_b4_a_pin_needs_a_declared_subject_and_the_family_is_capped_and_drainable` |
| **P5** (LOW) | `MAX_SEED_OBJECTS` 2 048 against `MAX_PACK_ENTRIES` 512 was unreachable — three dead checks — and the shipped test asserted loosely enough not to notice | **`MAX_SEED_OBJECTS = 256`**, below the entry cap, so the cap the plan and the latch both name is the one that fires (§7's 2 048 is superseded for cut 1). `e7c1_seed_max_seed_objects_refuses_before_the_first_object` rewritten to assert the exact variant | that test, plus `vf_p5_the_entry_cap_and_the_seed_object_cap_are_separate_refusals` |
| **P6** (LOW) | A duplicate entry id collapsed last-write-wins, and the digest covered only the survivor — so a file's authored bytes and its loaded semantics could differ with every gate green | `RulesPackWire::entries` decodes through `deserialize_unique_entries`, refusing `PackError::DuplicateEntry` and bounding at `MAX_PACK_ENTRIES` **while** decoding | `vf_p6_a_duplicate_entry_id_refuses_and_the_two_variants_stay_distinct` |
| **P7** (LOW) | The fixture's one route could not be flown carrying its own declared `propellant: 1200` under the one shipped `DriveProfile` (`payload_capacity: 1_000`) — every test evaluated at `payload = 0`, where capacity cancels | `store::nominal_drive`'s capacity is **4 000**, and its doc-comment states the ruling cut 6 will read: **propellant is inside `payload`**. Fixed in the profile rather than in the pack, so **no digest moved and `tacsim_corridor.json` needed no re-signing** | `vf_p7_the_corridor_is_flyable_carrying_its_own_declared_propellant` |
| **P8** (LOW) | `requires` was a name-and-number edge with no digest — the one reference in a digest-referenced system that was not — and a mutual cycle loaded | `PackRequirement.digest: Option<DefinitionDigest>`, compared in `check_requires` (`RequireDigestMismatch` naming both); `verify_sources` runs an explicit-stack DFS over the graph and refuses naming the cycle. **The field enters the hashed value only when `Some`**, so *no already-published digest moved* — a deliberate improvement on the fold brief's expectation that every requirement-bearing digest would move | `vf_p8_a_requires_cycle_and_a_requires_digest_mismatch_both_refuse` |
| **P9** (LOW, was PLAUSIBLE — **confirmed** in `seed.rs`'s unit scope) | `apply` is `pub` and re-checked nothing `plan` checked, so a second call over one live plan created a second object set and overwrote the latch | `is_applied`, `applied_version`, the pack kind and the object cap are re-run **inside `apply`**, in the fallible pre-flight — nothing fallible moved past the first `World::create` | `vf_p9_apply_re_checks_the_latch_and_a_second_call_creates_nothing` (`src/seed.rs`) |
| **S5** (LOW, was PLAUSIBLE — **shape only**, unreachable) | `put_seed`'s serialize-failure arm dropped the latch while the created objects still committed — the one fail-open direction the design forbids | **Both** halves taken: the arm pages level 2 and latches a **minimal fallback document** so the key still bars a re-seed, *and* the arm's unreachability is pinned rather than assumed | `s5_a_full_seed_latch_always_serializes` (`src/store.rs`) |
| **S7** (LOW) | The module's one irreversible command did not `gate_on_durable_ack` | `ctx.gate_on_durable_ack()` in `commands.rs::seed_cmd`'s `"commit"` arm, before the confirmation. Not strict: objects and latch are lost together, so re-running is correct | `vf_s7_commit_gates_on_the_durable_ack_and_dry_does_not` |
| **B5** (LOW, +S6) | `MAX_CONFIGURED_PACKS` was checked **after** every configured file had been read, inverting the tree's own bounded-on-visited discipline at the one place it is a boot precondition | `config_ext::read_pack_sources` accumulates the candidate list first and applies `MAX_TACSIM_CONFIGURED_PACKS` to it, then reads; sorted-directory order (OPEN 1) survives, and `verify_sources`' combined check stays as the defence-in-depth repeat | `vf_b5_the_configured_pack_cap_is_applied_before_any_file_is_read` (`mush-server/src/config_ext.rs`) |
| **B6** (LOW) | A typo in `[tacsim]` parsed clean, yielding `enabled = false` with no paths — silently disarming invariant (iv) | `deny_unknown_fields` on `TacsimCfg` and `TacsimTimeCfg`, on `EconomyCadenceCfg`'s precedent. Deliberately stricter than `WodCfg`/`ShadowrunCfg`: `[tacsim]` is the only section whose keys carry a **boot precondition** | `vf_b6_an_unknown_tacsim_key_refuses_to_start` (`mush-config/src/config.rs`) |
| **B8** (LOW) | `RulesPack::new`'s `# Errors` stated the route/kind rule exactly backwards against its own `misplaced` match and against the shipped fixture, and a misplaced route reported the endpoint symptom | ✗ **the `# Errors` line corrected in place** to *"a location or a route in a rules pack, and a commodity or recipe in a seed pack"*, and `(RulesDefinition, Route)` added to `misplaced` so the mistake gets the message that names the rule | `vf_b8_a_route_in_a_rules_pack_is_the_kind_rule_not_the_endpoint_symptom` |

**B7 — refuted, no change.** `may_view`'s axis is **pack-kind only** (a rules pack is public, a campaign-seed pack is staff's), and that is right for cut 1. The two arguments for acting now both fail: `PackManifest::hashed_value` is **hand-built**, so adding a `visibility` field to the manifest would move **no** published digest, and deferring the axis therefore ratchets nothing shut; and the forward-exposure example is misassigned, since §3.1 puts deposits in **campaign-seed** packs, which `may_view` already makes staff-only, while §3.3's `Deposit`/`SurveyBands` are `mush-industry` **runtime** records with their own F-lens fog and no `PackEntry` at all. Recorded here rather than built: **a per-entry visibility axis, if it is ever wanted, lands in cut 3 at no digest cost.**

**Also sound, confirmed by the verify and unchanged:** the digest itself and both properties §3.1 stakes on it (the manifest's own `digest` is structurally unrepresentable in the hashed value; a declaration over other bytes is refused at load naming both); the seed's one-batch round trip; `resolve`'s **pin** branch; `RulesPack::new`'s structural kind rule in the direction it implements; `pack_show` rendering **no entry payload** (label plus shape only), so a future table-bearing entry kind discloses only its id, label and kind; `World::destroy` + `sweep` plus `next_id` monotonicity making a destroyed placement a **tombstone**, never a reused id.

**Tests after the fold:** 40 in `crates/ruleset-tacsim/tests/e7c1.rs` (23 as built, 17 folded), 2 in-crate unit tests (`seed.rs`, `store.rs`), 3 in `mush-server`'s `config_ext`, 1 in `mush-config`'s `config`. `PINNED_BASELINE` stays **158** — the new `pin list` and unpin paths are **arguments** to the existing `pin` switch, so `legacy-handlers.toml`'s `@tacsim` stanza's `note` is refreshed and no stanza is added.

### 3.2 Cut 2 — `mush-inventory`, quantity truth with a location

**Ov, stated.** The proposal §4 gives `WorkOrder` a `material_source: Option<AssetContainerRef>` and speaks of a "material vault". **Q13A/Q2D override this**: quantities are neither asset containers nor account balances. A neutral `mush-inventory` is *the single source of quantity truth*, bound to **custody endpoints**, and industry, markets, contracts and simulation consume it — **none keeps a private stock number**. The proposal's material-vault quantities live here; `material_source` becomes an `EndpointRef`.

**Records** (Q2D fixes the fields exactly: **not** `legal`, **not** `custody`).

```rust
struct EndpointRef { provider: OwnerId, object: ObjId }     // an E3 custody endpoint
struct Pool  { endpoint: EndpointRef, commodity: CanonicalId, quantity: u64, revision: u64 }
struct Lot   { id: LotId, commodity: CanonicalId, quantity: u64, grade: Grade, lineage: Lineage }
enum  Lineage { Origin { source: ExternalRef }, Split { parent: LotId },
                Merge { parents: Vec<LotId> },              // ≤ MAX_LOT_PARENTS
                Tranche { tranche: TrancheId } }
struct PoolTranche { id: TrancheId, endpoint: EndpointRef, commodity: CanonicalId,
                     absorbed: Vec<LotId>, quantity: u64 }  // immutable lineage root
struct InventoryReservation {                               // doc 09 C8, exact fields (09:997-1006)
    id: ReservationId, owner: OwnerId, resource: ResourceRef, holder: ScopedIdempotencyKey,
    amount: u64, revision: u64, expires: ClockStamp,
    state: ReservationState,                                // Held | Consumed(commit) | Released | Expired
}
// ReservationId MUST render to a canonical `IdempotencyKeyText` (≤ MAX_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_BYTES
// = 128, `action.rs:31`, `:296-320`), because that is the only shape
// `AuthorityGuard::Reservation { provider, id, revision }` can name (`action.rs:1060-1064`).
```

**Custody is the endpoint's, not the lot's** (Q2D): a lot has one location and one custodian because its endpoint does. **Stolen or disputed status follows the *title* record** (`Encumbrance::Disputed`, `record.rs:521-527`, main; E6b's `report_stolen`). **Embargo legality is a jurisdiction/policy test at transaction time**, not a field travelling with matter — the same cargo is legal one hop away. **Licensing is the acting principal's entitlement**, not the cargo's (Q1A/Q14A).

**Verbs.** `credit`/`debit` a pool · `split(lot, n)` · `merge(lots)` · `transfer(what, to_endpoint)` · `reserve`/`consume(commit)`/`release`/`expire`. Interconversion is **one journaled, attributed act each way** (Q2D/T5): `dissolve(lot)` creates or updates an immutable `PoolTranche` and credits the pool; `crystallize(pool, n)` mints a lot whose **parent is that tranche**. **Lineage is never discarded — only coarsened in the active projection.**

**Invariants.** (1) Conservation under checked arithmetic; a `debit` below available (balance minus live holds) refuses. (2) Locality: no verb moves quantity except *between endpoints*; there is no endpoint-free balance. (3) The custody rules are E3's, unchanged — exactly one claiming provider (`custody.rs:103-131`), validate-then-mutate (`:195-226`); a hold that moves with a ship needs no second mechanism. (4) Lineage is append-only and acyclic, bounded by `MAX_LOT_PARENTS`/`MAX_LINEAGE_DEPTH`. (5) The reservation state machine is doc 09 C8's exactly; availability subtracts every live hold; the consuming action verifies holder **and** revision and applies the post-image in the same batch. (6) `mush-inventory` registers an `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator under its **own** `OwnerId`, through `ModuleRegistry::authority_guard_validator` (`mush-core/src/module.rs:1792-1800`, main); the registry is keyed `(OwnerId, AuthorityGuardKind)` (`action.rs:1420`, `:1520`) and routed per key (`:1534-1561`), so cut 4 registering the *same kind* under `mush-industry`'s `OwnerId` is legal by construction, not by luck. **There is no `brace` pattern to copy** (G3): `brace` registers definition validators, never an authority-guard validator, and no production module registers one. (7) Every record above uses the `TryFrom` wire (§4).

**Commands.** `@inv/show <endpoint>` · `@inv/lots <endpoint> [commodity]` · `@inv/lot <id>` (lineage, paged) · `@inv/move <what>=<endpoint>` · `@inv/split <lot>=<n>` · `@inv/merge <lot>,<lot>…` · `@inv/dissolve <lot>` · `@inv/crystallize <endpoint>/<commodity>=<n>` · `@inv/reservations <endpoint>`.

**Tests (~28).** *Conservation:* every verb sums; a `debit` past available refuses and writes nothing; `u64` overflow on merge refuses; a zero-quantity split refuses. *Lineage:* split → merge → split reconstructs both parents; `dissolve` then `crystallize` leaves the tranche as the child's parent and the original lot id reachable; a merge over `MAX_LOT_PARENTS` refuses; a cycle is unconstructible; coarsening the projection deletes no stored parent; `MAX_LINEAGE_DEPTH` refuses. *Locality:* no verb produces an endpoint-free pool; moving a ship moves its hold's contents by construction; a transfer to a destroyed endpoint refuses; a transfer between endpoints claimed by two providers refuses. *Reservations:* two orders cannot hold one unit; `consume` twice with the same commit is a HIT; a stale revision refuses; `release` after `consume` refuses; expiry is idempotent and survives restart; availability is balance minus live holds at every read. *Restart:* every record re-enters through its constructor on load (E5a lens-A: deserialization must not walk past the caps); a corrupt lot document is skipped **loudly** and its endpoint marked, never silently zeroed. *Custody:* a lot in a vault inherits the vault's access; `may_take` refuses a non-custodian — **unimplemented in cut 2 as first built, delivered by the fold (C8, owner decision D4)**: `provider::may_take` now delegates to `mush_economy::may_take_from_vault` (E3's own vault **withdraw** standing) and `provider::endpoint_visible` to `mush_economy::may_view_vault` (E3's own **view** standing), and the three lot verbs take the authorised endpoint (C1). *Added by the review:* a **corrupt lot skipped loudly must not raise availability** — the endpoint is marked and every `debit`/`reserve` against it refuses until reconciled, because the fail-open shape here is "the missing lot was the one the hold was against" (E5a lens A); a `Consumed` reservation replayed with the **same commit token under a different holder** refuses rather than HITs; availability read *between* the two writes of a partially applied batch never exceeds the pre-image; a `ReservationId` that will not render as `IdempotencyKeyText` is refused at construction, not at guard time; `@inv/show` on an endpoint the viewer may not read is byte-identical to `@inv/show` on one that does not exist; a `transfer` whose source and destination are the same endpoint is a no-op HIT, not a double credit; a reservation whose `expires` stamp is on a **different clock generation** refuses comparison rather than expiring early (K4a `checked_cmp`).

**Verify lenses.** **C (conservation and lineage)** — can any sequence create, destroy or orphan quantity, or lose a parent? **L (locality)** — can a balance exist without a live endpoint, or move without a custody act? **R (reservation)** — can two holders spend one unit, or a `Consumed` hold be released?

**Hands to cut 3:** the endpoint-bound pool/lot API extraction writes into, and the C8-conformant reservation shape cut 4 reuses for capacity.

*Every `file:line` in the body of §3.2 above predates the `91208ab` reformat and is **historical**: the symbol beside each is the citation (08i §8's rule). `custody.rs:103-131`/`:195-226` are `resolve`/`container_move`; `record.rs:521-527` is `Encumbrance::Disputed`; `action.rs:31`/`:296-320`/`:1060-1064`/`:1420`/`:1520`/`:1534-1561` are `MAX_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_BYTES`, `IdempotencyKeyText`, the `AuthorityGuard::Reservation` arm and `AuthorityGuardValidatorRegistry`; `module.rs:1792-1800` is `Registrar::authority_guard_validator`; `09:997-1006` is doc 09 C8's field list, delivered as `ReservationRecord`. None is re-numbered.*

#### 3.2 As built (2026-09-05, on `8e2367e`)

**Crates and edges.** New crate **`mush-inventory`** (`crates/mush-inventory/`): `lib.rs`, `record.rs`, `pool.rs`, `store.rs`, `guard.rs`, `provider.rs`, `facts.rs`, `commands.rs`, `module.rs`, plus `tests/e7c2.rs`. Its whole dependency set is **`{mush-core, mush-economy, mush-proto}`** plus `serde`, `serde_json`, `smol_str`, `thiserror`, `tracing` (dev: `mush-testkit`, `mush-store`, `tokio`). **The tree gained exactly three crate edges**: `mush-inventory → mush-core`, `→ mush-economy`, `→ mush-proto`, and `mush-server → mush-inventory` at the composition root.
