There is **no industry, market, tacsim, sim, tactics or org edge**, `cargo tree -p mush-inventory` is the test, and a test asserts the manifest's declaration list directly. (`mush-proto` is a deviation from the brief's list and is not optional: `ActionProvider::project_receipt` *returns* a `mush_proto::ProjectedActionResult`, so a provider crate cannot avoid the wire vocabulary. `ruleset-wod` carries the same edge for the same reason.)

**B1 — the ingress adapter seam, and the gap it discharges.** 08j §5 (iii), verbatim: *"Settle the ingress shape before cut 2: `CommandHandler`/`Ctx` is synchronous and cannot reach `ActionCoordinator::gateway_ingress`, so the first `Authoritative` registration needs an adapter seam that does not exist."* 08j §7 lists it as a fix-during-E7 item, owner **E**, "before cut 2"; 08i §2 row 5 says the same in one line: *"There is no `Ctx`-level action submit."* **Both are discharged by B1** — the item is marked so in 08j §7, and this is its shape:

```rust
// crates/mush-core/src/engine.rs — the selector, not an offer
pub struct AuthoritativeSubmit { pub actor: ObjId, pub action: DefinitionRef,
    pub subject: PerceivedRef, pub targets: Vec<PerceivedRef>,
    pub parameters: serde_json::Value, pub key: IdempotencyKeyText }
pub enum Deferred { …, AuthoritativeSubmit(Box<AuthoritativeSubmit>), … }
impl Engine { pub const fn has_authoritative_submit(&self) -> bool;
              pub fn take_authoritative_submit(&mut self) -> Option<Box<AuthoritativeSubmit>>; }
// crates/mush-core/src/module.rs — the one new `Ctx` verb
impl Ctx<'_> { pub fn submit_authoritative(&mut self, action: DefinitionRef,
    subject: PerceivedRef, targets: Vec<PerceivedRef>, parameters: Value,
    key: IdempotencyKeyText); }
// crates/mush-core/src/offer.rs — selector → this actor's own opaque offer
impl ProjectedLegalActionSink { pub fn projected_for_action(&self, action: &DefinitionRef)
    -> Option<&ProjectedLegalAction>; }
// crates/mush-core/src/action.rs — a guard whose subject is not its family's key
impl AuthorityGuardContext<'_> { pub fn scan_action_state<K, V>(&self,
    handle: &StateFamilyHandle<K, V>, after: Option<&str>, limit: usize)
    -> Result<StateFamilyPage<'a, V>, ActionStateError>; }
// crates/mush-server/src/action_coordinator.rs — the third ingress
pub(crate) const COMMAND_REQUEST_DOMAIN: &str = "cmd.legal_action.v1";
pub(crate) const MAX_COMMAND_REQUEST_BYTES: usize = 64 * 1024;
impl ActionCoordinator { pub fn command_ingress(submission: &AuthoritativeSubmit,
    idempotency_epoch: u64) -> Result<IngressEnvelope, String>; }
// crates/mush-server/src/world_task.rs — ONE spelling of the five steps
fn resolve_and_stage_offer(engine: &mut Engine, permit: Box<ActionPermit>,
    submission: OfferSubmission) -> Result<(StagedAuthoritativeAction, ObjId), String>;
fn command_offer_submission(engine: &Engine, submit: &AuthoritativeSubmit)
    -> Result<OfferSubmission, String>;
struct CommandSubmitDriver { idempotency_epoch: u64 }
enum CommandSubmitDrive { Idle, DeferredByLegacyPredecessor, Refused{..},
    AlreadyOwned{..}, AlreadyFinished{..}, Staged{..} }
enum Wake { …, CommandSubmits, … }        // armed by `command_submit_armed`
```

**How a command classified `Authoritative` now reaches `prepare_and_stage_resolved`.** (1) The handler calls `Ctx::submit_authoritative` **once**, mutating nothing and confirming nothing; `Engine::drain_deferred` routes the one `Deferred::AuthoritativeSubmit` into a single-slot queue. (2) The world loop arms `Wake::CommandSubmits` only when a prepared action could legally be staged *right now* — no queued legacy persistence work, no unresolved ledger predecessor, no pending commit — and drives at most **one** per step, exactly as `DurableScheduleDriver::drive_one` does. (3) `ActionCoordinator::command_ingress` mints the canonical envelope on surface `IngressSurfaceVersion(CanonicalId::new("cmd.legal_action"), 1)`, principal `PrincipalId::Object { id: actor }`, scope `{ principal, owner: action.key().owner, namespace: action.key().id }`. (4) `ActionCoordinator::preflight` runs the mandatory store lookup, and `ActionPreflight::{Vacant → stage, Retired → raise the epoch and retry ≤ 3, Pending → tell the actor it is already committing, Finalized → project the durable receipt, Quarantined | Conflict → refuse}` is an exhaustive match. (5) On `Vacant`, `command_offer_submission` regenerates **this actor's own** projected offers and turns the handler's *action selector* into the opaque `ActionOfferId` that projection just minted — a handler can never construct one, never skip projection, and a subject it did not name is a refusal rather than a silent re-target — and `resolve_and_stage_offer` runs the five steps.

**What the seam cannot do.** A handler cannot fan out: a **second** `submit_authoritative` in one command voids **that actor's own** first submission, stages nothing at all and tells them so. *(Corrected by the fold, C4: the arm used to execute `self.authoritative_submit = None`, which voided whatever the **engine** held — including a different actor's legitimate submission accepted in an earlier step, whose owner was told nothing at all. It now voids only what this drain queued for this actor.)* A handler cannot buffer: one submission per **actor** is the whole queue. *(Corrected by the fold, B7, owner decision D3: it was one slot per **world**, so one player's queued submission answered every other player's `@inv` mutation with "An earlier action is still committing" — a cross-actor denial and a cross-actor tell. The rule cited for it, D-E7-39 / 08i answer 6, is about the **store**, and `ActionCoordinator::preflight` already enforces it downstream. The queue is now a `BTreeMap<ObjId, _>` capped at `AUTHORITATIVE_SUBMIT_QUEUE_MAX` 64, still drained one per step, and the refusal names nobody else.)* A queued submission is also no longer mute: the engine acknowledges **receipt** with one `BestEffort` "Queued." line, so a submission dropped by a crash or parked by a freeze is legible at the terminal (fold R11). A handler cannot construct an offer id, a permit, a commit token or a bundle. And there is **no second spelling of the five steps**: `resolve_and_stage_offer` is called by the `WorldMsg::AuthoritativeAction` arm *and* by the command driver, and `e7c2_b1_both_ingresses_call_one_five_step_function` scans the production half of `world_task.rs` and asserts `ActionCoordinator::prepare_and_stage_resolved(` appears **exactly once**.

**How the confirmation is proved never to outrun the durable ack** (§4's standing checklist item 1). The handler emits **no** success presentation — asserted directly (`out.events()` is empty after `@b1submit`, and `@inv`'s mutating switches queue nothing). On successful staging the *seam* queues one `OnCommit` line, which `resolve_classified_sends` collects and the delivery boundary withholds until this exact bundle's receipt resolves, suppressing it entirely on abort; `e7c2_b1_the_confirmation_never_outruns_the_durable_ack` pins the class. On a HIT the line is projected from the **durable receipt** through `ActionCoordinator::project_finalized_receipt`, so no provider and no RNG runs again. On a refusal nothing was staged, so the actor's line is `BestEffort` and claims no mutation.

**The deterministic request identity (OPEN 4, taken).** `ActionCoordinator::status_id` refuses a `Deterministic` key (`ActionStatusError::UnsupportedKey`) and `fresh_action_epoch()` is `rand::random()`, so the ingress key must stay `IdempotencyKey::Request` and a *random* UID would make a retyped identical command a second commit. The UID is the first 16 bytes of `CanonicalHash::for_canonical_value(COMMAND_REQUEST_DOMAIN, 1, {actor, action, subject, targets, parameters, key}, 64 KiB)`. Two identical `@inv/move`s inside one process epoch therefore produce **one** commit and the second is a preflight HIT (`e7c2_b1_two_identical_submissions_in_one_epoch_produce_one_commit`). Cross-restart replay is the *mechanic's* job, not the ingress's: `ReservationId::derive(holder, resource)` makes the second attempt a HIT at the provider, and cut 2's lot and tranche ids are derived from the request rather than allocated for the same reason. **Corrected by the fold (R1, owner decision D1):** that was true of `Split`/`Merge`/`Crystallize` (the request-derived `LotId` returns `AlreadyExists`) and of `Dissolve` (the lot is gone, so `NoLot`), and **false for `InventoryVerb::Move`**, which derives nothing, consumes nothing, and *rewrote* the one request-keyed artefact it had. Both halves of D1 are taken. (a) The request-keyed applied marker is now the epoch-independent replay guard: `write_marker` **refuses** an existing marker instead of `txn.replace`ing it, and every verb that mints or moves writes one. (b) The handler's `key` carries the IC second and an ordinal inside it, so a deliberately retyped intent is a new request as the player means it — a sequence, never a random — while driving the same `AuthoritativeSubmit` value twice is still one commit. **Deviation:** the brief named the domain tag `inventory.cmd.v1`; it is `cmd.legal_action.v1`, matching the surface id in the same paragraph, because this seam is shared by every future `Authoritative` handler and must not carry one crate's namespace — the action's own owner and id are inside the hashed value, so two crates' commands cannot collide.

**Signatures.**

```rust
// crates/mush-inventory/src/record.rs
pub const NS: &str = "inventory";            pub fn owner() -> OwnerId;
pub const MAX_LOT_PARENTS: usize = 16;       pub const MAX_LINEAGE_DEPTH: u32 = 32;
pub const MAX_POOLS_PER_ENDPOINT: usize = 64; pub const RESERVATION_TTL_IC_DAYS: i64 = 7;
pub const MAX_LOTS_PER_ENDPOINT: u32 = 512;   pub const MAX_TRANCHE_INHERITED: usize = 64;
pub const MAX_TRANCHE_GENERATIONS: u32 = 64;  pub const MAX_RETAINED_TERMINAL_RESERVATIONS: usize = 32;
pub const RESERVATION_TTL_SECS: i64 = 7 * 86_400;
pub const MAX_POOL_QUANTITY: i64 = 1_000_000_000_000;  pub const MAX_LOT_QUANTITY: u64 = 1_000_000_000;
pub const MAX_TRANCHE_ABSORBED: usize = 256; pub const MAX_GRADE: u8 = 100;
pub const INV_PAGE: usize = 32;              pub const MAX_APPLIED_MARKERS: usize = 4_096;
pub const MAX_MARKER_ID_BYTES: usize = 96;   pub const ENDPOINT_CLASS: &str = "inventory.endpoint";
pub struct EndpointRef(pub VaultId);         // key_text() -> IdempotencyKeyText, subject() -> SubjectKey
pub struct Grade(u8);                        // TryFrom wire, 0..=MAX_GRADE
pub struct LotId(pub u64);  pub struct TrancheId(pub u64);   // Display `lot#<16 hex>` / `tranche#…`
pub enum Lineage { Origin { source: ExternalRef }, Split { parent: LotId },
                   Merge { parents: Vec<LotId> }, Tranche { tranche: TrancheId } }
pub struct Lot { /* id, endpoint, commodity, quantity, grade, lineage, depth, revision */ }
pub struct PoolTranche { /* id, endpoint, commodity, absorbed, quantity, depth */ }
pub struct Pool { pub endpoint, pub commodity, pub quantity, pub revision,
                  pub available, pub holds }        // PROJECTION; never stored
pub struct AppliedMarker { /* kind, from, to, commodity, quantity, actor, at */ }
pub const FACT_TRANSFERRED: &str = "inventory.transferred";
pub const FACT_LOT_CREATED:  &str = "inventory.lot.created";
// crates/mush-inventory/src/pool.rs — the twelve owner-exported verbs
pub struct InventoryFamilies { pub pools, pub books, pub lots, pub tranches, pub applied }
pub fn pool_resource(endpoint, commodity) -> Result<ResourceRef, InventoryError>;
pub fn pool_key(endpoint, commodity) -> Result<ReservationBookId, InventoryError>;
pub fn tranche_key(endpoint, commodity) -> Result<TrancheId, InventoryError>;   // one root per slot
pub fn derive_lot_id(request: RequestUid, ordinal: u32) -> Result<LotId, InventoryError>;
pub fn derive_tranche_id(request, ordinal) -> Result<TrancheId, InventoryError>;
pub fn reservation_deadline(now: &ClockStamp) -> Result<ClockStamp, InventoryError>;
pub fn reservation_id_text(id) -> Result<IdempotencyKeyText, InventoryError>;
pub fn reservation_id_from_text(&IdempotencyKeyText) -> Option<ReservationId>;
impl InventoryFamilies {
  pub fn pool(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity)      -> Result<Option<Pool>, InventoryError>;
  pub fn available(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity) -> Result<u64, InventoryError>;
  pub fn credit / debit(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity, amount) -> Result<Moved, InventoryError>;
  pub fn transfer(&self, txn, from, to, commodity, amount)       -> Result<Moved, InventoryError>;
  pub fn reserve(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity, holder, amount, now) -> Result<ReservationId, …>;
  pub fn consume(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity, id, rev, now, commit) -> Result<Moved, …>;
  pub fn release / expire(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity, id, rev[, now]) -> Result<(), …>;
  pub fn reservation(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity, id) -> Result<Option<ReservationRecord>, …>;
  pub fn split(&self, txn, lot, amount, child)   -> Result<Lot, InventoryError>;
  pub fn merge(&self, txn, parents, child)       -> Result<Lot, InventoryError>;
  pub fn dissolve(&self, txn, lot)               -> Result<(TrancheId, Moved), InventoryError>;
  pub fn crystallize(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity, amount, grade, child)
                                                 -> Result<(Lot, Moved), InventoryError>;
  pub fn tranche_for(&self, txn, endpoint, commodity) -> Result<Option<(TrancheId, PoolTranche)>, …>; }
// crates/mush-inventory/src/{guard,facts,store,provider,module}.rs
pub struct ReservationGuardValidator;   // new(StateFamilyHandle<ReservationBookId, ReservationBook>)
pub struct InventoryFactProvider;       // provider() == "inventory"
pub struct InventoryStore { /* quarantined, quarantined_keys, applied, complete */ }
pub fn store::reconcile(ctx: &mut Ctx<'_>, families: &InventoryFamilies);
pub fn store::endpoint_of(subject: SubjectKey) -> Option<EndpointRef>;
pub enum InventoryVerb { Move, Split, Merge, Dissolve, Crystallize }
pub fn provider::action_ref(registry: &Registry, verb) -> Option<DefinitionRef>;
pub fn provider::live_endpoints(world: &World, actor: ObjId) -> Vec<VaultId>;
pub fn provider::endpoint_visible(world, actor, endpoint) -> bool;
pub struct InventoryHandles(pub InventoryFamilies);      // published in module state
pub struct InventoryModule;  pub const SWEEP_TAG; pub const SWEEP_PERIOD_SECS: u64 = 60;
// crates/mush-economy/src/custody.rs — the one seam cut 2 consumes instead of rebuilding
pub enum EndpointState { Missing, Inactive(VaultStatus), BackingUnavailable,
                         Contested { provider: String }, Live }
pub fn endpoint_state(w: &World, vault: VaultId) -> EndpointState;
```

**The pool is a pair, and the one-bundle argument (OPEN 2, taken).** `inventory.pool` holds the `BoundedScalar`, `inventory.book` holds K's `ReservationBook`, **both keyed by the same `ReservationBookId::derive(&resource)`**, where `resource = ResourceRef { owner: inventory, subject: SubjectKey::Scoped { owner, class: "inventory.endpoint", id: "v<vault>" }, id: <commodity> }`. Every verb loads the pair, mutates through `ReservationBook::checked_apply_resource` or `checked_consume`, and writes **both halves inside one `ActionTxn`** — which becomes one `AuthoritativeBundle`, so a reboot sees all of it or none of it (`e7c2_restart_the_pool_book_and_lot_post_images_are_one_bundle` counts ≥ 4 replay writes for a `dissolve` and then aborts the prepared transaction, showing the pre-image exactly intact). **Cut 2 writes no second arithmetic**: the gate re-checks bounds, checked arithmetic, the scalar revision and post-mutation availability in one call, and a scalar whose `resource_revision` disagrees with the book is **refused, not repaired** (`ReservationBookError::ResourceRevisionMismatch`, pinned). §3.2's `Pool { quantity, revision }` is built on demand by `pool::project` and is **never stored**.

**The endpoint (OPEN 1, taken).** `EndpointRef(VaultId)`: the vault *is* the endpoint. Its `backing` answers *where*, its `status`/`owner`/`scope` answer custody and compartment, and a `CustodyRef::Registered` backing is nameable. Locality then costs nothing: **a hold that moves with a ship needs no second mechanism, because the backing moved and the pool did not** — no inventory record names a location other than its endpoint, so there is nothing to rewrite (`e7c2_l_moving_the_backing_moves_the_hold_by_construction`). E3's rules stay E3's: `mush_economy::endpoint_state` is a new **read seam in the economy** that asks the vault's own lifecycle, the frozen provider table's `backing_valid`, and `resolve`'s exactly-one-claiming-provider rule (returning `Contested { provider }`); cut 2 adds **no second custody model and registers no custody provider**. The seam exists because the provider table is `pub(crate)` to `mush-economy` — asking through the economy is what keeps the rule from being copied.

**Where endpoint legality is enforced, and why there.** `prepare_authoritative` receives an `ActionTxn` and **no world**, so a vault's status cannot be read inside a provider. It is enforced where the tree already enforces legality: `project_offer` publishes the actor's **live, usable** endpoints as `ProjectedParameterKind::Choice` values (capped at `MAX_ENDPOINT_CHOICES` 64, well inside K's `MAX_PROJECTED_ACTION_CHOICES` 256), and `Engine::resolve_legal_action` **regenerates and re-validates the offer immediately before preparation**. An endpoint frozen, closed, destroyed or contested between the command and the commit therefore drops out of the regenerated choice list and the submission is refused. This is not a check that can go stale — it is the same mechanism a station grant uses. A viewer with no live endpoint gets **no offer at all**, which is also why an unviewable endpoint and an absent one are indistinguishable.

**Lots, tranches and the laundering axis (B3, D-E7-4, D-E7-5, T5).** `inventory.lot` is keyed by `LotId` with **no world object per lot** (OPEN 3, taken; §9 q4's "there is no object-per-unit interim" stays true), and the lot's `endpoint` is a field. Interconversion is **one journaled, attributed act each way**: `dissolve(lot)` creates or extends the endpoint's tranche and credits the pool, consuming the lot record; `crystallize(pool, n)` mints a lot whose `Lineage::Tranche { tranche }` **is** that root. The round trip is written out: pouring a stolen lot into a pool and re-minting it leaves `PoolTranche.absorbed` still naming the **original `LotId`**, and `checked_absorb` is append-only, so nothing already recorded is ever dropped (`e7c2_c_dissolve_then_crystallize_cannot_launder_provenance`). **Corrected by the independent verify fold (C2, C7, B2):** that held only *within one endpoint* and only *without a quantity bound*. A pool-to-pool `transfer` carried no lineage at all, so dissolve-at-A → move A→B → crystallize-at-B broke the chain in one ordinary hop; and `crystallize` never compared what it minted with what the root absorbed, so one dissolved unit permanently licensed every unit that later arrived in that pool. The perimeter is closed in cut 2 (owner decision D2): a transfer draws the source root down by what left and records it on the destination's through a bounded, deduplicated `inherited: Vec<TrancheId>`, and `crystallize` draws a `remaining: u64` down through `checked_draw`. The root is also a **sequence** rather than a singleton — `tranche_key(endpoint, commodity, generation)` with a `predecessor: Option<TrancheId>` — because a full `absorbed` set (256) or one lot at `MAX_LINEAGE_DEPTH` used to kill `dissolve` and `crystallize` at that slot for ever, for every holder. Lineage is append-only and acyclic by construction — every arm names strictly older records, a lot naming itself is unconstructible, `MAX_LOT_PARENTS` bounds a merge and the **stored `depth`** bounds the chain so no verb ever walks it. **Lineage is never discarded, only coarsened in the active projection**: `@inv/lot` renders at most `MAX_LINEAGE_PAGE` parents and the record still carries all of them, asserted.

**The validator, and why cut 4's registration is legal by construction (B4, G3).** `mush-inventory` registers the tree's first `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator, through `Registrar::authority_guard_validator`, on `crates/mush-sim/src/lib.rs`'s pattern (family handle → validator → registration) and `crates/mush-tactics/src/module.rs`'s. `is_current` re-reads the book and answers `false` on a missing record, a bumped revision or a terminal state, and refuses outright any guard whose `provider` is not this crate's own owner; `witness` stays the default `None` (the only two witness requests are `ControlLease` and `Contact`). The registry is keyed `(OwnerId, AuthorityGuardKind)` and `Registrar::authority_guard_validator` stamps the **calling module's** owner, so cut 4 registering the same kind under `mush-industry`'s owner lands on a different key: `e7c2_r_cut_fours_same_kind_registration_composes_without_collision` composes exactly that pair and shows neither validates the other's guards. **The lifecycle is K's entirely** — `Held → Consumed { commit } | Released | Expired` through the book's checked transitions — and cut 2 writes no state machine, no second `ReservationId` and no `InventoryReservation`.

**The document table, and the direction of every loss (B6).** Cut 2 owns **no conventional document at all**, so §4 rule 2's "skipped loudly" does not apply to it and the kernel's stricter rule does: `ActionStateStore::hydrate` is atomic, and a record failing path, envelope, schema, bounds, typed validation or canonical decode→serialize equality **refuses the boot**.

| family | class | corrupt → | direction of the loss |
|---|---|---|---|
| `inventory.pool` (`BoundedScalar`) | authoritative | **boot refuses** | a pool nobody can bound makes every later `debit` a fabrication |
| `inventory.book` (`ReservationBook`) | authoritative | **boot refuses** | losing the book loses every live hold, so availability would *rise* — the one fail-open direction the design forbids |
| `inventory.lot` (`Lot`) | authoritative | **boot refuses** | E5a lens A: the missing lot is exactly the one the hold was against |
| `inventory.tranche` (`PoolTranche`) | authoritative | **boot refuses** | the immutable lineage root; losing it would let a re-mint claim clean provenance |
| `inventory.applied` (`AppliedMarker`) | authoritative | **boot refuses** | a marker is what a settlement points at; unreadable must read as neither "happened" nor "did not" |

What `store::reconcile` adds is the **cross-family** check the kernel cannot make. **The fold (R2, R7) widened it from presence to three shapes**, because the presence diff caught only one of them: a bundle's two `Put`s can also both land with one carrying the pre-image, and that pair booted clean, was never quarantined and was never paged even though `require_resource_revision` refused every later read *and* write against it; and a pool whose vault the economy never hydrated was invisible to every viewer including staff, though inventory is composed after the economy precisely so its boot can ask. `reconcile` now carries the scalar's revision beside the pool key, compares it with the book's `resource_revision`, asks `mush_economy::endpoint_state` for each named endpoint, and records **why** each endpoint is quarantined with the reconciliation order filled in (`Quarantine::{HalfPair, RevisionMismatch, VaultMissing}` and `runbook()`). A pool without its book, or a book without its pool, means one half of a single bundle was lost beneath the kernel: that endpoint is **quarantined** and every `debit`, `transfer`, `reserve` and `crystallize` against it refuses (`InventoryError::HalfPool`) while availability **refuses rather than rising**. The marker is **not a document**: it is derived at boot from the typed decode outcomes themselves, so it cannot go stale and needs no key. A book without its pool names its endpoint (only the book carries the `ResourceRef`) and marks the endpoint; a pool without its book can only be marked **by key**, and both are pinned. Reconciliation is bounded on records visited (`INV_PAGE` per page, `MAX_BOOT_PAGES` pages) and a truncated walk marks the mirror **incomplete** rather than claiming completeness.

**The facts, and the one deviation in them (OPEN 5, taken).** `InventoryFactProvider` registers the `inventory.*` namespace in `EconomyModule::with_facts` beside `JobsFactProvider`; a duplicate **panics** at composition and a test asserts it. Both facts are answered from the **applied marker the committed bundle itself wrote** (key shape `inventory:applied:{request}`, §4 rule 3) — never from a status, which is §10's E4c E3 finding — so a bundle that did not commit leaves no marker and the fact answers `None`, which is unsatisfied. **Deviation, with the reason:** `FactProvider::resolve` receives `&World` only, while typed state lives on the `Engine`, so on this tree a fact provider *cannot read a typed family at all*. Cut 2 therefore mirrors the marker family into `InventoryStore` at boot, on the coarse sweep tick (60 s) and at the top of every `@inv` command. The mirror is **behind** the authoritative truth by at most one of those and **never ahead**, which is the direction that matters: `facts.rs`'s own contract is *unknown is unsatisfied, everywhere*, so a settlement waits rather than paying early. **Recorded as cut 3's ask:** widen `FactProvider`/`WorldFacts` to carry the typed-state store, at which point the mirror and the sweep both disappear.

**The market decision, and who lifts the `Representation::Lot` refusal.** A `Supply::Market` (or `Supply::Stock`) listing's `quantity` stays the **market's own advertised counter** and becomes **no `Pool` claim**. Three grounds, all in code: `Listing`'s own doc comment — it holds no money and *reserves nothing*, and `quantity` decrements only at contract **activation**, the single serialization point that makes oversell unreachable; a quote reserves nothing by rule (08i §4 rule 1; doc 10 T4 keeps holds money-only); and `Representation::Lot` is policy only in E6c and a listing declaring it is **refused at creation**, its doc comment naming this section as the record's owner. Cut 2 therefore neither lifts that refusal nor adds a `mush-market` edge, and changes no `mush-market` file. The physical hand-over of a sold quantity is an ordinary attributed `mush-inventory` act linked back by `ExternalRef` and gated by the `inventory.transferred` fact (D-E7-31, §10). **Lifting the `Lot` refusal with an inventory-backed `Supply` arm is cut 3's or cut 11's.**

**The five OPEN decisions, as taken.** (1) **`EndpointRef(VaultId)`** — the vault is the endpoint; §3.2's `{ provider: OwnerId, object: ObjId }` could name neither a `Registered` backing nor a provider that is a `&str`. (2) **The pool is the `(inventory.pool, inventory.book)` pair** under one `OwnerId`, written in one bundle, with `Pool` as its projection. (3) **Lots are a typed family with no world object per lot**, and the endpoint is a field. (4) **A deterministic `RequestUid`**, so the ingress key stays `IdempotencyKey::Request`, `status_id` works unchanged and a retyped identical command is a preflight HIT. (5) **Yes, the fact provider ships in cut 2**, answered from an applied marker.

**Every contradiction, and what was built instead.** (i) **`InventoryReservation` does not exist**: cut 2 writes K's `ReservationRecord`/`ReservationBook` and defines no reservation shape. (ii) `EndpointRef` is OPEN 1's. (iii) `Pool` is a projection of `BoundedScalar` + `ReservationBook`, not a third record. (iv) registration is `Registrar::authority_guard_validator`, not `ModuleRegistry::…`. (v) `ReservationId` **already** renders as `IdempotencyKeyText` (`CanonicalHash::to_hex` is 64 lowercase hex bytes, inside the 128-byte cap and inside the byte class): asserted **once**, in `e7c2_r_a_reservation_id_always_renders_as_idempotency_key_text`, and deliberately **not** re-checked at guard time. (vi) the `inventory.*` `FactProvider` ships. (vii) `Grade`, `LotId`, `TrancheId` are defined here; **`ExternalRef` is used, never redefined**. (viii) the market decision above. (ix) the drifted citations are struck in §4.0a; the symbol beside each is the citation.

**Deviations, with reasons.** (a) **`mush-proto` is a third crate edge** — `ActionProvider::project_receipt` returns one of its types. (b) **`Lot` carries `endpoint` and `depth`** beyond §3.2's five fields: `endpoint` because OPEN 3 makes it a field so moving a ship moves no lot record, `depth` because `MAX_LINEAGE_DEPTH` must be enforceable without the unbounded walk the bound exists to forbid. Neither is `legal` or `custody`, and a test pins the record's exact field set. (c) **Tranches are their own family** (`inventory.tranche`) rather than living beside lots, and **one tranche per `(endpoint, commodity)`**, keyed deterministically — that is what lets `crystallize` name a parent without a scan, and it is why `absorbed` is append-only (the root is shared, so dropping an entry would drop somebody else's provenance). (d) **Endpoint legality lives in `project_offer`, not in `prepare_authoritative`**, for the reason given above. (e) **The command's `move` argument is spelled `<endpoint>/<commodity>:<n>`** — §3.2's `<what>`, made explicit. (f) **The B1 domain tag is `cmd.legal_action.v1`**, not `inventory.cmd.v1`. (g) **A same-endpoint transfer is a no-op HIT**: it bumps no revision at all, because crediting and debiting one pool twice would let a replay observe a balance that never existed. *(Corrected by the fold, C6: it is true of the pool pair, but the bundle still committed an `inventory.transferred` marker naming a quantity that never moved, and `FactProvider::resolve` returns only `{satisfied, actor, at}` — so no consumer of D-E7-31's gate could tell. `Moved::moved` now reports what actually moved and the marker is written only when it is non-zero.)* (h) `dissolve` takes no caller-supplied tranche id (see (c)). (i) `AuthorityGuardContext` gained `scan_action_state` — a bounded, immutable read, symmetric with the `action_state` point lookup it sits beside, because a `Reservation` guard names the reservation while the book is keyed by its resource.

**Rule 1 and the allowlist.** `@inv` is in `crates/mush-contract-tests/legacy-handlers.toml` in the same commit, sorted between `@instrument` and `@link`, **`category = "authoritative"`**, with a note naming the whole prepared path it rides. It declares **no `family`**, because it has no conventional document to migrate: the `family` field is required only for `legacy-post-image`. **`PINNED_BASELINE` is untouched at 158** — a new handler is never `baseline`. K8.5: `commands::register` calls `reg.classify_commands(CommandCompatibility::Authoritative)` before its first `reg.command`, and `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` now asserts `@inv` by name — the **first `Authoritative` registration on the tree**.

**Wiring.** `mush_inventory::InventoryModule::new()` is composed in `server_modules_with` **immediately after `"economy"`** (its boot reconciliation asks the economy about vault status — the housing precedent) and **before `"market"`**. The boot-order pins now read `["comsys","canon","org","faction","scenes","sim","tactics","economy","inventory","mail","jobs","housing","weather","party","market","script"]`, and cut 1's own pin the same fifteen plus `<ruleset>, "tacsim"`.

**Tests.** 46 new: **39** in `crates/mush-inventory/tests/e7c2.rs` (conservation ×5, lineage ×5, locality ×5, reservations ×8, restart ×5, guard ×2, disclosure/wiring ×3, facts ×2, boot ×2, records ×2) and **7** in `crates/mush-server`'s `world_task` test module for B1, over the `crates/mush-contract-tests/src/b1_seam.rs` fixture (a purpose-built `Authoritative` command lane that exists so B1 was CI-green **before** a single inventory record).

**Cut-3 handoff.** Cut 3 gets **the endpoint-bound pool/lot API extraction writes into** — `InventoryFamilies`' twelve verbs, reachable through `InventoryHandles` in module state — and **the C8-conformant reservation shape cut 4 reuses for capacity**, which is K's `ReservationBook` under a per-owner `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator. **Nothing else.** Cut 4 registers its own validator under `mush-industry`'s `OwnerId`, never a second one under this crate's. Two items are handed over explicitly: **the cross-owner write** (an `ActionTxn` is owner-stamped, so a `mush-industry` provider cannot write `inventory`'s families directly — cut 3 owns that bridge), and **widening `FactProvider` to carry the typed-state store**, which retires cut 2's marker mirror and its sweep.

**Independent verify fold (2026-09-05, on `35da42b`).** Twenty-six confirmed findings across the three lenses, folded in one pass; `R9` and `B11` are refuted at the foot of the brief and change no behaviour (B11's cosmetic half is taken: `EndpointState` now renders through a `Display` impl in `mush-economy` rather than `{:?}`). Owner decisions **D1–D4** were taken as stated. Nine claims this section made are corrected **in place** above.

* **C1 + B3 (HIGH)** — `split`/`merge`/`dissolve` ignored the endpoint the offer authorised, so one live endpoint let any actor restructure or destroy any lot in the world by id. `InventoryFamilies::{split, merge, dissolve}` now take `endpoint: EndpointRef` and answer `InventoryError::NoLot` — the **missing-lot** refusal, byte for byte, so no oracle appears — on a mismatch; the three `prepare_authoritative` arms read `endpoint_field`; `commands::lot_endpoint_text` names the **lot's** endpoint when it is in the actor's live set and `NO_SUCH_LOT` otherwise. `vf_c1_a_lot_at_an_endpoint_the_actor_may_not_act_on_refuses`, `vf_c1_every_lot_arm_reads_the_authorised_endpoint`.
* **R1 + B6 (HIGH)** — see the corrected paragraph above. `vf_r1_a_replayed_move_across_an_epoch_change_moves_nothing_twice`, `vf_r1_two_deliberately_retyped_moves_move_twice`.
* **C2 (HIGH)** — a transfer severed lineage; it now carries it. `vf_c2_a_transfer_carries_the_source_tranche_into_the_destination_root`.
* **R2 (HIGH)** — the pair check was presence-only; it now compares revisions and pages the endpoint by name. `vf_r2_a_revision_mismatched_pair_is_quarantined_and_paged`.
* **B1 (HIGH)** — `@inv/show|lots|reservations` visited exactly **one** page of a global family and filtered afterwards, so a viewer's own stock was displaced by unrelated endpoints and reported as `(nothing pooled here)`. They now page to exhaustion under a budget counted in **pages** (`MAX_LIST_PAGES` 128), cap rendered rows at `MAX_LIST_ROWS`, and hand back an honest `after=<cursor>` continuation — an **argument**, not a switch. `vf_b1_a_viewers_own_stock_is_never_displaced_by_other_endpoints`, `vf_b1_a_commodity_filter_surfaces_every_matching_lot`.
* **B2 + C5 + R4 (HIGH)** — the tranche's `absorbed` cap and depth max were permanent, unrecoverable ceilings per `(endpoint, commodity)`; the root is now a generation sequence naming its predecessor. `vf_b2_dissolve_survives_a_full_tranche`, `vf_b2_a_max_depth_dissolve_does_not_disable_crystallize`.
* **C3 (MEDIUM)** — terminal reservation records were never pruned, so one holder could hold once per slot for the life of the world and the 257th hold refused for ever. `reserve` now retires terminal records through K's own `checked_prune_terminal` in the same bundle, down to `MAX_RETAINED_TERMINAL_RESERVATIONS`. `vf_c3_a_pool_stays_reservable_past_its_book_capacity`, `vf_c3_a_holder_may_reserve_again_after_its_hold_is_consumed`.
* **C4 + R6 (MEDIUM)** and **B7 (MEDIUM)** — see the corrected "What the seam cannot do" above. `vf_c4_a_fan_out_never_voids_a_submission_from_an_earlier_step`, `vf_c4_a_fan_out_tells_every_actor_it_dropped`, `vf_b7_one_actors_submission_does_not_block_another`.
* **C6 + R5 (MEDIUM)** — see deviation (g) above. `vf_c6_a_self_transfer_publishes_no_transferred_fact`.
* **C7 (MEDIUM)** — see the corrected laundering paragraph. `vf_c7_crystallize_cannot_mint_more_than_its_root_absorbed`.
* **C8 (MEDIUM)** — see the corrected custody row. `vf_c8_the_inventory_gate_is_e3s_vault_gate`.
* **R3 + B5 (MEDIUM)** — the fact mirror silently stopped reflecting new commits past `MAX_APPLIED_MARKERS`, permanently rather than late, because marker ids were request digests and the walk kept the lexicographically first 4 096. Ids are now `{at}{request}` (canonical order **is** time order), the mirror keeps the newest, its own walk has `MAX_MARKER_PAGES` 256, truncation is a `tracing::error!`, and `facts::resolve` answers **nothing at all** on an incomplete mirror. `vf_r3_a_marker_written_past_the_mirror_cap_still_answers_its_fact`, `vf_r3_a_truncated_mirror_answers_no_fact`. **Not done:** pruning the marker family itself on the sweep — a tick holds a `Ctx`, not an owner `ActionTxn`, so cut 2 cannot write from one; the family is still monotone and that is carried forward as cut 3's.
* **R7 (MEDIUM)** — see the corrected boot sentence; `endpoint_visible` also answers `true` for staff on a quarantined endpoint whose vault is absent, so what is stranded is inspectable. `vf_r7_a_pool_whose_vault_did_not_hydrate_is_quarantined_and_paged`.
* **R8 (MEDIUM)** — `Quarantined`/`Conflict`/retirement-exhaustion preflights were logged at `info` beside a typo. `CommandSubmitDrive::Refused` carries `integrity: bool` and the integrity ones log at `tracing::error!` with the actor and the spool token; the actor's line is unchanged. `vf_r8_a_quarantined_preflight_is_an_integrity_event`.
* **R11 (MEDIUM)** — see "What the seam cannot do". `vf_r11_a_queued_submission_acknowledges_receipt_without_claiming_a_mutation`. **Partial:** the freeze notice is emitted where the command lane itself freezes; a poison freeze that merely disarms `command_submit_armed` still leaves the queue quiet, and that residue is recorded rather than closed.
* **B4 (MEDIUM)** — every `@inv` switch re-walked three whole typed families on the world single-writer before doing anything (~12 k record visits per read command, growing with the tree's pool count rather than the caller's). `store::reconcile` is **off the command path**; boot and the 60 s sweep keep the mirror, which is exactly what 08i answer 15 grants. `vf_b4_a_read_switch_does_not_rescan_the_tree`.
* **B8 + C9 + R10a (MEDIUM)** — see §7. `vf_b8_an_endpoint_refuses_a_sixty_fifth_commodity`.
* **B9 (MEDIUM)** — the disclosure and legality proofs ran with **no economy composed**, where both gates short-circuit. All three are rewritten on a two-module rig with a real `@vault/create`d vault, and every other fold test that needs one uses it. `vf_b9_the_disclosure_and_legality_proofs_run_against_a_real_vault`.
* **B10 + R10b (MEDIUM)** — the `Reservation` guard validator found its book by linear scan of the whole owner's book family and answered "not current" — permanently — when its page budget ran out. A sixth typed family, `inventory.reservation_index` (`ReservationId → ReservationBookId`), is written in the same bundle as every hold and `is_current` is two point lookups. `vf_b10_a_live_hold_is_current_past_the_page_budget`.
* **C10 (LOW)** — `tranche_for` trusted a 64-bit derived key; it now verifies the loaded record's own `endpoint`, `commodity` and `generation`. `vf_c10_a_tranche_whose_fields_disagree_with_its_key_is_not_returned`.
* **C11 (LOW)** — `split` and `merge` minted lots and published no `inventory.lot.created`; both arms now write the marker. `vf_c11_every_verb_that_mints_a_lot_publishes_lot_created` (a source pin over the three minting arms plus the marker's own record rule: building the full `ActionInvocation` a provider arm needs is not reachable from this crate's rig).
* **C12 (LOW)** — `live_endpoints` truncated before sorting and said nothing; it collects one past the cap, sorts, then truncates, and `endpoints_truncated` makes the omission legible to the command. `vf_c12_a_truncated_endpoint_set_is_declared_not_silent`.
* **B12 (LOW)** — the fresh commit rendered `deterministic_commit_token`'s digest raw into player output while only the **replay** got the provider's sentence. `StagedAuthoritativeAction` carries the provider's own summary, projected at stage time through the same table a HIT uses; the token is gone from player output. `vf_b12_the_first_commit_and_the_hit_read_the_same`.
* **B13 (LOW)** — `@inv/crystallize` coerced a malformed grade to 0 on a durable, unmergeable property; it refuses. An omitted grade still defaults to 0. `vf_b13_a_malformed_grade_refuses`.
* **B14 (LOW)** — the marker-cap `break` exited only the inner `for`; it is a labelled break. The orphaned-pool-key half of the quarantine had no operator surface at all; a **staff** switch `@inv/quarantine` lists quarantined endpoints, orphaned keys and the reconciliation order (the allowlist stanza's `note` is refreshed; no stanza is added, `category` stays `authoritative`, `PINNED_BASELINE` stays 158). `vf_b14_a_key_quarantine_is_reportable`, `vf_b14_reconcile_stops_at_the_marker_cap`.

**New records and families the fold adds.** `PoolTranche` gains `generation`, `predecessor`, `inherited` and `remaining` (all behind the same strict `TryFrom` wire, all bounded); `inventory.census` (`PoolCensus`) and `inventory.lot_census` (`LotCensus`) count what §7 publishes; `inventory.reservation_index` keys a reservation to its book. Two census families rather than one because a single `ActionTxn` may mutate one state record only once and `dissolve` both creates a pool and consumes a lot. **The document table does not move**: every one is authoritative typed state and a corrupt record still refuses the boot. `mush-inventory`'s dependency set is unchanged at `{mush-core, mush-economy, mush-proto}`; the two new economy predicates are owner-exported `pub fn` seams beside `endpoint_state` (08i answer 16).

### 3.3 Cut 3 — `mush-industry`: deposits, claims, survey, extraction

**Records** (Q1A and Q14A, verbatim field lists). `Deposit` is `mush-industry`'s; **`mush-economy` already owns an unrelated `DepositRecord`** (`contract.rs:567`, escrow bookkeeping) and neither name may drift into the other crate.

```rust
struct Deposit { id: DepositId, region: RegionId, commodity: CanonicalId, grade: Grade,
                 accessibility: Accessibility, reserve: u64, curve: YieldCurve,
                 surveyed_bands: BTreeMap<PrincipalId, SurveyBands> }
struct SurveyBands { grade_class: BandId, reserve_class: BandId, at: ClockStamp, fact: ExternalRef }
struct ClaimRecord { id: ClaimId, deposit_or_area: ClaimSubject, issuer: PrincipalId,
                     holder: PrincipalId, exclusivity: Exclusivity, priority: u32, term: ClockStamp,
                     transferability: Transferability, contest_state: ContestState, revision: u64 }
enum ContestState { Uncontested, Contested { contract: ContractId }, Suspended { by: PrincipalId },
                    Seized { by: PrincipalId, at: ClockStamp } }
```

A **licence is not tenure**: it is an `InstrumentKind::Entitlement` account issued by a government principal (`record.rs:50-61`, main) with campaign-cadence expiry, proving **authorization** to survey, extract or export — checked **at transaction time** against the *acting principal* (Q1A/Q14A/Q2D).

**Yield is deterministic** (Q1A). `curve` maps cumulative extraction to per-cycle yield; depletion is arithmetic, not a roll. **Variation comes from discrete campaign events** — accessibility change, equipment condition, collapse, licence suspension — each an attributed journaled act moving effective yield, never a per-period roll. A **survey reveals bands, never the exact reserve**, and the revealed band is per principal and is itself a recorded fact.

**Extraction** is scheduled, journaled **lot creation** on a deterministic key — doc 09 C8 already lists production and upkeep among bounded-idempotency-key actions (`09:1019-1028`, main). One cycle produces one `Lot` with `Lineage::Origin` at the extractor's endpoint (cut 2) and debits `reserve` in the same batch.

**Tenure lifecycle** (Q14A). A claim is **transferable**, **pledgeable** as collateral (`Encumbrance::Collateral`, `record.rs:537-542`, main), **contestable** through an E4 contract with a `Before` deadline and a named adjudicator (T3's shape), **suspendable** and **seizable** by the issuing authority. Overlapping political authority needs no fiction that an entitlement balance is land title: two governments may each issue a claim over one area, and `ControlKind::{DeJure, DeFacto}` (`mush-faction/src/data.rs:414-430`, main) says which is worth anything on the ground.

**Owner abandonment lands here** (T3; doc 10 §15; 08f §1.1 keeps it "in E7 with the mechanical recipe written out"): a unilateral `AssetEffect` setting `title_owner` to none with custody unchanged, a **direct attributed asset action** that need not masquerade as a bilateral contract — next to the claim seizure that needs the same unilateral title effect. The salvage claim that turns possession into title stays an ordinary contract with a `Before` contest deadline and a named adjudicator.

**Commands.** `@deposit/list <region>` · `@deposit/show <id>` (bands only, per viewer) · `@survey <deposit>` · `@claim/file <subject>=<issuer>` · `@claim/show|transfer|pledge|release` · `@claim/contest <claim>` · `@claim/suspend|seize <claim>` (issuer) · `@extract/start <deposit>=<facility>` · `@asset/abandon <object>` · `@licence/show <principal>`.

**Tests (~26).** *Band leakage:* an unsurveyed principal sees no band; a surveyed one sees **only** their band and never `reserve`, `curve` or another principal's band; a render at every verbosity is asserted not to contain the exact reserve; staff is the only exact read; a transferred claim does not transfer the transferor's bands. *Depletion:* cumulative extraction matches the curve over 200 cycles; a depleted deposit yields zero and refuses a new cycle; concurrent extractors cannot over-draw (each cycle is one reserve-consume); an accessibility event changes the *next* cycle, never a completed one; overflow refuses. *Claims:* two claims over one deposit coexist with distinct issuers and priorities; an exclusive claim refuses a second from the same issuer; a contest opens exactly one contract and moves `contest_state`; a contest settling for the challenger transfers the claim once, idempotently; suspension blocks extraction while leaving the record; seizure is attributed; a pledged claim cannot be transferred while the encumbrance stands; a claim past `term` stops authorizing and does not vanish. *Licences:* extraction without the entitlement refuses; an expired licence refuses at transaction time though the claim stands; an org-held licence authorizes a member acting on the org's scope, not the member personally. *Abandonment:* title goes to none, custody is unchanged, the act is attributed, and a re-run is a HIT. *Restart:* an extraction cycle interrupted mid-batch produces either no lot or exactly one. *Added by the review:* **refusals are indistinguishable** — an extraction refused for depletion, for a missing licence and for a suspended claim answer one message to a principal who may not know the reserve, and only staff get the reason (08c §6.2's D1 shape); `@deposit/show` on an unknown id and on a known unsurveyed one are byte-identical; a **band boundary is not an oracle** — repeated surveys by one principal do not narrow the band, and two principals' bands are computed from the same recorded classes, never from `reserve` re-quantised per viewer; a `SurveyBands` row is written once per `(deposit, principal, at)` and a replay is a HIT; a claim contest whose adjudicator contract is itself `Disputed` **suspends the claim rather than resolving it**, and pages level 2 with the command filled in; `ContestState::Seized` on a **pledged** claim refuses and pages, because seizure would silently destroy a lender's collateral; every record re-enters through its constructor (§4), and a `surveyed_bands` map over `MAX_SURVEY_BANDS` is refused on load, not truncated.

**Verify lenses.** **F (fog)** — can a viewer learn a reserve, a curve or another principal's band through any render, error, or arithmetic difference? **D (depletion)** — can total extracted exceed the reserve under concurrency, restart or event ordering? **T (tenure)** — can a claim be transferred, extracted under, or contested by someone the authority chain does not permit?

**Hands to cut 4:** lots at a facility endpoint, and the licence check the work order re-runs.

#### 3.3 As built (2026-09-05, on `f05bf7f`)

**Crates and edges.** New crate **`mush-industry`** (`crates/mush-industry/`): `lib.rs`, `record.rs`, `families.rs`, `licence.rs`, `custody.rs`, `provider.rs`, `facts.rs`, `store.rs`, `commands.rs`, `module.rs`, plus `tests/e7c3.rs`. Its whole dependency set is **`{mush-core, mush-economy, mush-org, mush-inventory, mush-proto}`** plus `serde`, `serde_json`, `smol_str`, `thiserror`, `tracing` (dev: `mush-testkit`, `mush-store`, `serde_json`, `smol_str`, `tokio`). **The tree gained exactly five crate edges**, and each is load-bearing:

* **`mush-core`** — the kernel: `ActionTxn`, `StateFamilyHandle`, `ActionProvider`, `ClockStamp::checked_cmp`, `CanonicalHash`, `RequestUid`.
* **`mush-economy`** — E3/E4 custody and title: `Vault`, `CustodyRef::Registered`, `CustodyProvider`, `issue_title`, `AssetOp`, `Encumbrance`, `EconomySnapshot`, and `endpoint_state`.
* **`mush-org`** — **unavoidable and not optional.** Every economy seam cut 3 calls is typed in `mush_org::{PrincipalId, OrgScopeId, SystemPrincipalId}` and `mush-economy` re-exports none of them: `issue_title(ctx, object, owner: PrincipalId, scope: Option<OrgScopeId>, issuer: SystemPrincipalId, catalog)`, `EconomySnapshot::accounts_of(&PrincipalId)`, `Encumbrance::Disputed { claimant: PrincipalId }`, `Vault.owner`. `mush-market → {core, org, economy}` is the precedent, and hiding the edge behind an economy re-export would not remove it.
* **`mush-inventory`** — D-E7-3: a body's remaining reserve **is** cut 2's pool, so industry keeps no private stock number.
* **`mush-proto`** — `ActionProvider::project_receipt` *returns* a `mush_proto::ProjectedActionResult`, so a provider crate cannot avoid the wire vocabulary. Cut 2 and `ruleset-wod` carry it for the same reason.

There is **no sim, market, faction, tacsim, party, tactics, scenes, jobs or housing edge**, in production or in dev. `cargo tree -p mush-industry` is the test and `e7c3_wiring_the_crates_edge_set_is_exactly_what_it_declares` asserts the manifest's declaration list directly, forbidden names and required names both. Nothing TacSim-shaped is inside the crate: no ore family definition, no band table, no route, no corridor fact, no location name, no recipe. The one string that names a ruleset is the claim title's `CatalogRef` coordinate, which is composition data with a builder override (`IndustryModule::with_claim_catalog`).

**B1 — the crate, its edge set, and the cross-owner write.** Cut 2's handoff named the gap by name: *"the cross-owner write (an `ActionTxn` is owner-stamped, so a `mush-industry` provider cannot write `inventory`'s families directly — cut 3 owns that bridge)"*. It is closed by one narrow, declared kernel seam:

```rust
// crates/mush-core/src/action_state.rs
pub const MAX_STATE_FAMILY_DELEGATES: usize = 4;
impl StateFamilyRegistry {
    pub fn delegates(&self, family_owner: &OwnerId, grantee: &OwnerId) -> bool;
    pub fn delegations(&self) -> Vec<(OwnerId, OwnerId)>;
}
// crates/mush-core/src/module.rs
impl Registrar<'_> { pub fn delegate_state_families(&mut self, grantee: &str) -> Result<(), ModError>; }
// crates/mush-inventory/src/module.rs
pub type InventoryHandleCell = Arc<std::sync::OnceLock<InventoryFamilies>>;
impl InventoryModule {
    pub fn with_quantity_delegates<I, S>(self, delegates: I) -> Self;
    pub fn with_handle_cell(self, cell: InventoryHandleCell) -> Self;
}
```

**Five properties make it narrow.** (1) It is declared by the **owning** module, never by the grantee: the registrar stamps the caller as the family owner, so no module can help itself to another's state. (2) It is refused for self-delegation, capped at four grantees per owner, and refused at freeze if either owner is not composed. (3) It enters the **state-family fingerprint**, so two deployments differing only in a delegation are not the same deployment. (4) It is never transitive and never symmetric — `e7c3_wiring_the_quantity_delegation_is_declared_and_narrow` asserts the production composition holds **exactly one** pair, `(inventory, industry)`, and that the reverse is false. (5) It widens the **bundle**, never the gate: every quantity mutation still goes through `InventoryFamilies`' exported verbs, which are the only place `ReservationBook::checked_apply_resource` lives, and `PreparedTxn::authoritative_replay_writes` already keys each write by `record.key.owner`, so the persisted namespace stays the family owner's and no bundle format changed. The read fence widens with it, for one reason: a grantee that may debit a body's pool must be able to tell an operator what is left in it.

**This is not E7k's item (iii).** §5's cross-owner *output materialization* is title + custody + placement + inventory in one bundle — four owners, an economy post and a sim placement, and cut 4's work-order output. This is two owners' typed families under one composition-declared grant, which is what OPEN 1's own fallback described ("the namespace is per-write, so one bundle can carry two owners' families"), adapted from `ModuleJsonSet` to typed state because cut 2 owns no conventional document.

**The endpoint seam.** `mush-industry` registers a `CustodyProvider` for `CustodyRef::Registered { provider: "industry", id: <OreDepositId>.0 }`, wired into `EconomyModule::with_custody` beside `mush_sim::InteriorCustodyProvider`. An ore body **holds quantity, not objects**: `contents` is always `Some(vec![])`, `holds` always `Some(false)`, `move_into`/`move_out` refuse, and `claims` is `false` so E3's exactly-one-claiming-provider rule is never contested in either direction. `backing_valid` answers `Some(true)` for a well-formed industry backing **without reading any state** — deliberately, because `endpoint_state` runs during the economy's own boot and this crate composes *after* the economy: a provider that needed this crate's loaded state would answer `false` for every body on every boot, which is the `mush-sim` interior ordering hazard with the modules in the order the boot pin requires. A registered backing has nothing that can be *unavailable*; whether the body behind it exists is answered by `industry.deposit`, which is where a missing one quarantines.

**Signatures.**

```rust
// crates/mush-industry/src/record.rs

pub const MAX_ORE_DEPOSITS_PER_PLACE: usize = 8; pub const MAX_SURVEY_BANDS: usize = 8;
pub const MAX_CLAIMS_PER_SUBJECT: usize = 8;     pub const MAX_CURVE_POINTS: usize = 16;

pub const MAX_CYCLE_YIELD: u64 = 1_000_000;      pub const MAX_EXTRACTION_CYCLES: u64 = 1_000_000;

pub enum AssetLifecycle { Active, WrittenOff, Abandoned }        // third, NON-terminal

```

**The deposit is an inventory endpoint (OPEN 1, taken), and the `ForeignOwner` finding that forces it.** §3.3 drafted `reserve: u64` on the record and "debits `reserve` in the same batch" as a cut-2 lot credit. That batch was **unreachable across owners in either direction**: `ModuleActionState::get`/`scan_family` call `require_owner` and answer `ModuleActionStateReadError::ForeignOwner`, `ActionTxn::checked_key` answers `ActionStateError::OwnerMismatch`, and `ActionProviderRegistry`'s cascade opens exactly one transaction stamped `entry.owner` for the root provider *and* every same-commit trigger. So the reserve moved to where the quantity authority already is: one `Vault` per body with a `CustodyRef::Registered` backing, and the unextracted commodity is that endpoint's cut-2 `Pool`. D-E7-3's "industry keeps no private stock number" is now **literally true** — `e7c3_records_a_deposit_keeps_no_private_stock_number` pins the record's exact field set and there is no live `reserve` in it. What survives on the record is `initial_reserve`, the *authored* figure the pool was seeded with: it is what makes cumulative extraction derivable without a second counter, it is **staff-only**, and it is recorded as a deviation below.

**One cycle, one bundle (B6, the D lens).** The exact write set is: the deposit endpoint's `(inventory.pool, inventory.book)` pair debited by the cycle's yield, one `inventory.lot` minted at the operator's endpoint with `Lineage::Origin { source }`, that endpoint's `inventory.lot_census`, the advanced `industry.run`, and the `industry.applied` marker the fact is answered from — **six or more replay writes in one `ActionTxn`**, carrying two owners' namespaces. `e7c3_d_the_bundle_carries_two_owners_post_images` asserts the namespace set is exactly `{industry, inventory}`; `e7c3_d_a_cycle_is_one_bundle_so_a_reboot_sees_all_or_none` prepares the whole bundle, counts its writes, **aborts** it, and shows the pre-image exactly intact — no lot, no debited reserve, no advanced run.

**Cut 3 writes no arithmetic of its own.** The single quantity mutation is `InventoryFamilies::debit`, which rides `ReservationBook::checked_apply_resource` — the call that re-checks bounds, checked arithmetic, the scalar revision **and post-mutation availability** in one place, where availability is balance minus every live hold. Two concurrent cycles serialize on the store's one pending-intent slot, and the second reads the already-debited pool. **Two reservations against one remaining unit are refused by the kernel**, at `checked_hold`, and never by a check in this crate: `e7c3_d_two_reservations_against_one_remaining_unit_cannot_both_consume` places two holds against a one-unit pool and shows the second refused with availability at zero.

**Yield is arithmetic, not a roll.** `YieldCurve::at` is a bounded walk over a monotone table (strictly increasing thresholds starting at zero, yields that never rise as the body depletes) with checked integer arithmetic in a fixed order — **no clock, no world, no RNG** — scaled by accessibility and clamped to what is left. Two evaluations always agree, so a replay is a HIT. `e7c3_d_cumulative_extraction_matches_the_curve_over_two_hundred_cycles` runs the cycle to exhaustion, checks each cycle's quantity against an independently computed expectation, and shows total extraction equal to the authored reserve **exactly** and the pool at zero, with the next cycle refused. `e7c3_d_an_accessibility_event_moves_the_next_cycle_and_never_a_completed_one` moves accessibility between two cycles and shows the completed cycle's lot untouched.

**The claim's record/title split (OPEN 2, taken).** `ClaimRecord` stays this crate's — it is the only thing that can hold term, exclusivity, priority and contest state, which is exactly why 14A rejects 14B/14C — and cut 3 creates **one world object per claim** and titles it through `mush_economy::issue_title` under the registered system principal `industry.claims`, with `CatalogRef { ruleset, code }` defaulting to `("tacsim", "claim")`. The two refusals `issue_title` already makes are satisfied rather than worked around: `approver_live` (the handler creates the object first, in the same command) and `is_title_issuer` (`industry_title_issuer()` is pushed into `EconomyModule::with_title_issuers` unconditionally, and `e7c3_wiring_the_industry_title_issuer_is_registered_with_the_economy` pins that both names agree). Transfer is `AssetOp::TransferTitle`, pledge is `Encumbrance::Collateral`, dispute is `Encumbrance::Disputed`. **A pledged claim cannot be transferred by E4's own rule**: `apply_asset_op`'s `TransferTitle` arm refuses with `EconomyError::Encumbered` while *any* encumbrance stands, so the record arm never runs and cut 3 writes no second check. `Seized` on a pledged claim **refuses and pages level 2 with the command filled in**, because seizure would silently destroy a lender's collateral; a contest whose adjudicator contract is itself `Disputed` **suspends the claim rather than resolving it**, and pages. Both stop only the subject. **The whole contest path is gated in `project_offer`** (E7c3 verify fold C1, the seam with `&Engine`, since `prepare_authoritative` gets no world): suspend, seize, contest and release are offered only to staff or an actor whose principal `acts_for` the named claim's `issuer()`, and to a non-staff actor the `claim` parameter is a `Choice` over exactly those administrable claims, so a foreign claim id is never offered and naming one is refused identically to a missing claim. `contest()` now takes the acting `ObjId` and stamps `Suspended`/`Seized { by }` as the **acting principal**, not the claim's issuer — the previous `by: live.issuer()` forged an authority's attribution regardless of who invoked.

**The licence predicate cut 4 re-runs (OPEN 3, taken).** There is **no `ExtractionRight` record**. `extraction_authorized` is one pure function over the state handed to it — the body, what is left in it, the caller's **bounded** page of live claims over it, the deployment's licence instrument and the clock — returning a typed refusal the renderer collapses. It conjoins (a) a live, unexpired, unsuspended, unseized claim whose holder authorizes the actor, where "authorizes" is E3's own `acts_for` so an **org-held** claim authorizes a member acting on the org's scope and not personally; (b) the *acting* principal's `Active` account in the extraction `InstrumentKind::Entitlement` instrument, derived exactly as `mush-market`'s `principal_account` does (re-derived rather than reached for, because cut 3 adds no market edge); and (c) the body not depleted and not unworkable. Every `term` comparison is `ClockStamp::checked_cmp`, so a stamp on another clock generation **refuses comparison rather than expiring early**. A licence is **not** tenure: a claim past its term stops authorizing and does not vanish, and an expired or suspended licence refuses at transaction time though the claim stands. Cut 4's work order calls this same function.

**Where legality lives, and why there.** `prepare_authoritative` receives an `ActionTxn` and **no world**, so a licence account, a vault's status and `acts_for` cannot be read inside a provider. Legality therefore lives where the tree already puts it (cut 2's deviation (d)): `ActionProvider::project_offer` receives `&Engine`, publishes the bodies this actor may work **right now** as `ProjectedParameterKind::Choice` values (capped at `MAX_DEPOSIT_CHOICES` 64, inside K's 256), and `Engine::resolve_legal_action` **regenerates and re-validates the offer immediately before preparation**. A licence that lapses, a claim that is suspended or a body that depletes between the command and the commit drops out of the regenerated list and the submission is refused — not a check that can go stale, but the mechanism a station grant uses. It is also the fog property: **a body the actor may not work never appears in the list at all**, and an actor with none gets no offer whatsoever. What the provider *can* see it re-checks anyway: the deposit, the run and the claim are this owner's typed families, and the cycle re-reads all three under **checked revisions** before it writes a thing (`e7c3_d_a_stale_deposit_or_run_revision_refuses_the_cycle`).

**The survey's no-RNG argument.** D-E7-6 and Q1A make yield deterministic and a survey a *revelation* of recorded classes, so `@survey` copies the body's **recorded** `grade_class`/`reserve_class` verbatim into a per-principal disclosure row. It is never `reserve` re-quantised per viewer, which is precisely what makes a repeated survey unable to narrow a band (`e7c3_f_a_survey_copies_the_recorded_classes_and_cannot_narrow_a_band` — a re-survey rewrites nothing and is a HIT) and two principals' bands the same two `BandId`s (`e7c3_f_two_principals_read_the_same_recorded_classes_never_reserve_requantised`). **There is no `rand::`, `rand_chacha`, `thread_rng`, `RngCore`, `SeedableRng` or `OsRng` anywhere in this crate**, so `crates/mush-contract-tests/tests/provider_rng_policy.rs` — which fails any file containing `impl ActionProvider for` that names one — is green **by construction**, not by a scan that happened to pass. If a later cut ever wants survey *quality* to vary, the only lawful source is `PrepareCtx::start_resolution(domain, inputs_hash)` on the prepared path, never ambient RNG, and that would move `@survey` to `Authoritative`. Cut 3 builds none of it.

**And cut 2's fold B1, not repeated.** `@deposit/list`'s visibility filter runs **inside** the page walk, not after it: the **page** budget counts records visited and the **row** budget counts rows the viewer actually gets, so a viewer with one surveyed body among a thousand is not told `(nothing here)` because the first page held somebody else's.

**And one conservation hole, closed the same way.** `mint_origin` is the one verb that *creates* matter, so `@extract/start`'s destination endpoint is a `Choice` over the actor's **own live endpoints** (cut 2's exact pattern) rather than free text: a free field would let an operator mint lots into somebody else's vault — a gift they cannot refuse and, at `MAX_LOTS_PER_ENDPOINT` 512, a denial they cannot undo. An actor with no live endpoint of their own gets no offer at all. **The run itself is one per deposit and its output tenure is closed too** (E7c3 verify fold C2 / D1(a) as taken): `put_run` refuses to replace a `Running` run whose operator differs (`IndustryError::RunNotYours`, rendered as the missing-run refusal), and `run_cycle` refuses a cycling actor who is not the run's own operator — without which a shared-claim co-tenant's `@extract/start` silently redirected the deposit's single run, and every later cycle's minted lot, to their own endpoint. A genuine second co-tenant contends for the one run rather than running concurrently; per-`(deposit, operator)` runs are cut 4's should concurrency become a first-class requirement.

**One more fog hole, closed while building it.** `@survey <body>` is the one verb that turns *"I have never heard of this body"* into *"here are its two bands"*, so without a locality gate it is an **existence oracle over the whole id space** for anybody willing to guess. **You survey what you are standing on**: a non-staff actor whose `location` is not the body's `place` gets the **same line an unknown id renders**, so being in the wrong place and naming nothing are indistinguishable. That is also why `@deposit/list` shows a non-staff viewer only the bodies they have already surveyed — listing an unsurveyed one without its bands would still say "it exists here".

**The abandonment change to `mush-economy`, with its three new symbols.** Additive, on the `consent_digest`-with-cut-9 precedent. `AssetLifecycle::Abandoned` is a **third, non-terminal** variant beside `Active`/`WrittenOff`; `AssetOp::Abandon { object }` takes the next free ordinal **10** and renumbers nothing (the ordinal is the second key of the plan-hash total order, and both digest pins stay green); `AssetEffectKind::Abandoned` sits beside the others. `title_owner` is **retained as the last owner** so provenance survives (T3), **custody is unchanged**, and an abandoned asset **authorizes nobody and takes no asset op but its title** — `apply_asset_op`'s live-asset gate admits `TransferTitle` (that is the salvage claim, and it stays an ordinary E4b2 contract with a `Before` deadline and a named adjudicator, which cut 3 does not write) and refuses everything else with the same `AssetNotActive` a written-off asset answers, so the two are indistinguishable to anyone who could not already see the record. Invariant 12 holds unchanged: abandoning an **encumbered** asset refuses, because a lender's collateral is not something an owner may walk away from. `@asset/abandon` is a **switch on the existing `@asset` spec**, so `CommandSpec::name` is unchanged, the allowlist needs no stanza and **`PINNED_BASELINE` stays 158**; it rides the `EconomyCarveOut` lane the whole `@asset` surface already declares. Authorization is stated explicitly rather than left to the catch-all: staff, the title owner, or `asset.title.transfer` on the asset's live compartment.

**The document table, and the direction of every loss (B7).** Cut 3 owns **one** conventional document and six authoritative typed families. The kernel's rule is the stricter one and applies to the six: `ActionStateStore::hydrate` is atomic, and a record failing path, envelope, schema, bounds, typed validation or canonical decode→serialize equality **refuses the boot**.

| family / document | class | corrupt → | direction of the loss |
|---|---|---|---|
| `industry.deposit` (`OreDeposit`) | **authoritative** | boot refuses | the curve and `accessibility` decide a cycle's yield *and* whether a cycle is legal; a body nobody can decode would either stop producing silently or produce on a default |
| `industry.claim` (`ClaimRecord`) | **authoritative** | boot refuses | the fail-open shape here is *"the missing claim was the one that had been seized"* |
| `industry.claim_index` (`ClaimIndex`) | **authoritative** | boot refuses | losing the live set is losing the exclusivity check and the per-subject cap at once |
| `industry.run` (`ExtractionRun`) | **authoritative** | boot refuses | losing `next_cycle` reuses a deterministic key, so a replayed cycle reads as fresh |
| `industry.applied` (`AppliedMarker`) | **authoritative** | boot refuses | a marker is what a settlement points at; unreadable must read as neither "happened" nor "did not" |
| `industry.place_census` (`PlaceCensus`) | **authoritative** | boot refuses | a lost census is a cap that stops being enforced, silently |
| `industry:survey:{deposit}` (`SurveyDoc`) | **exempt**, legacy-post-image | **skipped loudly, body marked** | disclosure only: no legality check reads a band, so a dropped row costs a surveyor their knowledge and costs nobody their authority |

`store::reconcile` adds the cross-cutting checks the kernel cannot make, because it decodes each family independently and never across crates. Three shapes **quarantine the body**, and a quarantined body authorizes nobody and yields nothing: the economy hydrated **no vault** for the endpoint it names (`Quarantine::VaultMissing`); cut 2 quarantined that endpoint (`EndpointQuarantined`, asked through `InventoryStore` rather than re-derived); or the survey document failed its wire (`SurveyLost`). Each carries a `runbook()` naming the reconciliation order, and the load-bearing rule is that **a skipped record must not raise authorization or availability** — `e7c3_d_a_quarantined_body_refuses_and_its_pool_never_rises` shows the pool exactly as credited. The survey map is **refused past `MAX_SURVEY_BANDS`, never truncated**, at the constructor *and* at the wire, because a silently dropped row is a principal who was told something the record then denies. A **fully-absent** pool pair at a still-hydrated vault (both `inventory.pool` and `inventory.book` gone, which the kernel does not refuse at boot because *missing* is not *corrupt*) is **not** a fourth quarantine shape: it degrades to `Depleted` — fail-closed — by design, so cut 3 constructs no `NoPool` variant (E7c3 verify fold R2; a fully-absent pool at a hydrated vault is unreachable through this crate's own verbs, so it can only arise from external corruption). Reconciliation is bounded on records **visited** (`INDUSTRY_PAGE` per page, `MAX_LIST_PAGES`/`MAX_MARKER_PAGES` pages) and a truncated walk leaves the store **fail-closed**: it does not claim the mirror is complete and `facts::resolve` answers nothing at all — the walk trips its page budget **or** the RAM mirror prunes past `MAX_APPLIED_MARKERS`, and the restored `truncated` flag (E7c3 verify fold R1; cut 3 had dropped cut 2's) makes the store incomplete on either, for the newest fact as much as the oldest, never a non-monotone flip. The durable marker family is **unbounded** — it grows one marker per committed cycle, command-bounded until pruned — so past the cap every industry fact fails closed and pages at `tracing::error!`; **pruning the durable family is cut 4's**, with the `FactProvider`/`WorldFacts` widening that retires the mirror.

**The facts.** `IndustryFactProvider` registers the `industry.*` namespace in `EconomyModule::with_facts` beside `JobsFactProvider` and `InventoryFactProvider`; a duplicate **panics** at composition and `e7c3_facts_a_second_industry_namespace_panics_at_composition` asserts it. `industry.extraction.cycled` is answered from the **committed bundle's own applied marker** (key shape `{at}{request}`, so canonical key order is time order and the mirror keeps the newest), never from a status — §10's E4c E3 finding — so a bundle that did not commit leaves no marker and the fact answers `None`, which is unsatisfied. **`industry.workorder.completed` is cut 4's arm on this same provider**, named here and answering `None` today, so cut 4 adds no second namespace. The same `&World`-only deviation cut 2 recorded applies: `FactProvider::resolve` cannot read a typed family, so the marker family is mirrored into `IndustryStore` at boot and on the 60 s sweep, behind the truth by at most one sweep and **never ahead** — and, with the `truncated` flag restored (R1), a mirror that could not hold the whole family (walk budget tripped or pruned past `MAX_APPLIED_MARKERS`) answers nothing at all rather than answering some.

**Commands and the K8.5 lanes.** `reg.classify_commands(..)` is called **before the first `reg.command` in each of three registration groups**, and each takes the ceiling class of its own switches:

| group | switches | class |
|---|---|---|
| `@deposit`, `@claim`, `@extract` | `open\|access\|list\|show`, `file\|show\|transfer\|pledge\|release\|contest\|suspend\|seize`, `start\|cycle\|stop` | `Authoritative` |
| `@survey` | *(none)* | `LegacyPostImage` |
| `@licence` | `show` | `ReadOnly` |

`k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` asserts all five by name and owner. Every `Authoritative` switch rides cut 2's B1 ingress seam with the same request identity shape — `{verb}.{ic_second:x}.{ordinal:x}` from `IndustryStore::next_attempt`, a sequence and never a random — and the handler mutates nothing and emits no success line.

**Wiring.** `mush_industry::IndustryModule::new().with_inventory(cell)` is composed in `server_modules_with` **immediately after `"inventory"`** — whose handles and delegation it needs — and **before `"market"`**, which it does not touch. The boot-order pins now read `["comsys","canon","org","faction","scenes","sim","tactics","economy","inventory","industry","mail","jobs","housing","weather","party","market","script"]` in all four places, and cut 1's own pin the same seventeen plus `<ruleset>, "tacsim"`. `IndustryCustodyProvider` joins `EconomyModule::with_custody`; `IndustryFactProvider` joins `with_facts`; `industry_title_issuer()` joins `with_title_issuers`.

**Rule 1 and the allowlist.** Five stanzas land in `crates/mush-contract-tests/legacy-handlers.toml` **in the same commit**, sorted by name in byte order — `@claim` between `@chown` and `@clock`, `@deposit` between `@create` and `@desc`, `@extract` between `@emit` and `@faction`, `@licence` between `@inv` and `@link`, `@survey` between `@stats` and `@tacsim`. Categories are `authoritative` (`@claim` → `industry.claim`, `@deposit` → `industry.deposit`, `@extract` → `industry.run`), `legacy-post-image` (`@survey` → `industry.survey`) and `read-only` (`@licence`, **no family** — it writes nothing at all, so there is nothing to migrate). **`PINNED_BASELINE` is untouched at 158** — a new handler is never `baseline` — and `@asset/abandon` adds no stanza.

**The five OPEN decisions, as taken.** (1) **The body is an inventory endpoint**, its reserve is cut 2's pool, and the cross-owner write is a composition-declared delegation rather than a second quantity carrier. (2) **A claim is a record whose transferable half is a title**, with transfer, pledge and dispute all E4's, reused verbatim. (3) **Extraction rights are a predicate, not a record** — `extraction_authorized`, which cut 4 re-runs. (4) **`place: ObjId`**, never `RegionId`. (5) **Cut 3 ships the extraction cycle as an authoritative action and registers no schedule source**; cut 4 attaches the schedule, and cut 3 defines no second key shape beyond `extract/{deposit}/{cycle}`.

**Deviations, with reasons.** (a) **`mush-org` is a fifth crate edge** — every economy seam cut 3 calls is typed in its `PrincipalId`/`OrgScopeId`/`SystemPrincipalId`, and `mush-economy` re-exports none of them (`mush-market → {core, org, economy}` is the precedent). (b) **`OreDeposit` carries `initial_reserve` and `revision`** beyond §3.3's field list: `initial_reserve` because cumulative extraction is `initial − pool` and the alternative is a second counter, which is the one thing OPEN 1 exists to prevent; `revision` because the provider re-reads the record under a checked revision. Both are staff-only in every render. (c) **`industry.deposit` is authoritative, not exempt** — §4.1's own closing rule, plus the fact that a provider cannot read a conventional document at all. (d) **`@deposit` is `Authoritative`, not `ReadOnly`** — opening a body credits a pool, which only an authoritative bundle can do; the two read switches never submit, exactly as cut 2 argued for `@inv`. (e) **`SurveyDoc` stores an ordered `Vec<SurveyRow>` rather than a `BTreeMap<PrincipalId, SurveyBands>`** — `mush_org::PrincipalId` has no `Ord` and serde map keys must be strings; the canonical order is the economy's own `principal_token`, which is the same total order a consent audience is canonicalized by. (f) **`ClaimIndex` replaces a claim census** — a counter cannot answer "every live claim over this subject" and an `ActionTxn` has no scan, so the index *is* the census (cut 2's fold-B10 mechanism). (g) **The cycle is one `debit`, not a `reserve` + `consume` pair** — cut 2 writes the pool pair once per transaction by construction, so both inside one bundle is a `DuplicateMutation`, and a hold placed and consumed in one bundle is not a hold: it carries quantity across no time at all. The over-draw gate is unchanged, because `checked_apply_resource` re-checks post-mutation availability. (h) **`InventoryFamilies::mint_origin` is added to cut 2** — it had no verb that mints a lot from *outside* the inventory (`crystallize` needs a tranche, `credit` makes pool), and conservation is the caller's bundle: the unit debited from the body is the unit minted at the operator. (i) **`InventoryModule::with_handle_cell`** — module state is installed after every module registers, so the handles cannot be read from `&World` while industry is registering. (j) **A claim's title and its record are two acts in one command**, in that order: a crash between them leaves a titled object with **no claim record**, which authorizes nobody — the fail-closed direction, chosen deliberately.

**Cut-4 handoff.** Cut 4 gets **lots at a facility endpoint** (cut 2's `Lot` at the run's `facility_endpoint`), **`extraction_authorized` verbatim** — the same function, same signature, re-run by the work order — the **deposit endpoint's pool API** through `IndustryFamilies::{deposit, remaining}` and cut 2's exported verbs, and the **`OwnerId`** under which it — not cut 3 — registers `mush-industry`'s `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator for **capacity**. Cut 3 registers no such validator at all.
