Three items are handed over explicitly: **attaching the schedule** to `ExtractionRun` (cut 3 registers no schedule source, and D-E7-16 gives the tree's first production one to cut 8); **widening `FactProvider`/`WorldFacts` to carry the typed-state store**, which retires both crates' marker mirrors and their sweeps; and **the seed wire from `PackEntry::Deposit` to `@deposit/open`**, which cut 3 leaves as authored data because instantiating a body is an authoritative act and `ruleset-tacsim` has no industry edge. **Nothing else.**

**Tests.** 42 new: **37** in `crates/mush-industry/tests/e7c3.rs` (fog ×8, depletion ×9, tenure ×8, abandonment/restart ×4, facts/wiring ×5, records ×3), **3** in `crates/mush-core`'s `action_state` test module (the delegation's registry rule, the undelegated refusal that still stands, and its three own refusals), and **2** in `crates/mush-server`'s `modules` test module (the title issuer on both lists, and the delegation being exactly one narrow pair). Four boot-order pins and `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` are extended in place, the last asserting all five industry lanes by name and owner. The independent verify fold adds **13** more: **12** in `e7c3.rs` (`vf_c1_*` ×2, `vf_c2_*`, `vf_c4_*`, `vf_c3_*`, `vf_b3_*`, `vf_r1_*`, `vf_r2_*`, `vf_b5_*`, `vf_b6_*`, `vf_b4_*`, `vf_b2_a_re_survey_across_a_reboot_is_a_hit`) and **1** in `crates/mush-server`'s `world_task` tests (`vf_b2_extract_command_to_receipt_commits_one_lot`, the `@extract` command→receipt e2e over the coordinator).

##### Independent verify fold (2026-09-05, on `270768f`)

An independent adversarial verify over the three lenses (**C** conservation/consent/lineage, **R** restart/durability, **B** bounds/disclosure) found what this "As built" missed. Every finding below was confirmed by reading the symbol chain at `270768f`; the six false or unmet claims the tree made are corrected in place above. Owner decisions taken: **D1(a)** — one run per deposit, a foreign redirect refused (never redirected), plus a `run_cycle` operator check; **D2(a)** — the fail-closed mirror fix is mandatory now, durable pruning deferred to cut 4. **Refuted, no change: none.**

* **C1 (HIGH).** The whole `@claim` contest path was ungated — any actor naming a claim id could suspend, seize, contest or release it, forging the issuer's attribution and undoing a real authority's suspension. Gated in `provider::project_offer`'s `ClaimContest` arm (new `provider::administrable_claims`, staff-or-`acts_for(issuer)`, a `Choice` for non-staff), and `IndustryProvider::contest` now takes the actor and stamps `by` as the acting principal. Test `vf_c1_project_offer_gates_claim_contest`, `vf_c1_a_non_issuer_cannot_seize_or_release_a_foreign_claim` (`e7c3.rs`).
* **C2 (MEDIUM).** `@extract/start` redirected a co-tenant's run output because the run is keyed by deposit and `put_run` replaced unconditionally. `IndustryFamilies::put_run` now refuses to replace a `Running` run whose operator differs, and `run_cycle` refuses a cycling actor who is not the run's operator (new `IndustryError::RunNotYours`, rendered `REFUSED`). D1(a). Test `vf_c2_a_second_start_does_not_redirect_a_co_tenants_run`.
* **C4 (MEDIUM, plausible).** `IndustryProvider::mv` trusted the command's out-of-band economy gate and the `ClaimMove` offer was open to every actor. Gated in `project_offer`'s `ClaimMove` arm (new `provider::movable_claims`, `acts_for(holder)` + transferable, a `Choice` for non-staff). Test `vf_c4_claim_move_is_not_offered_to_a_non_holder`.
* **C3 (LOW, plausible).** A low-accessibility body stranded its reserve and mislabelled it `Depleted` (`base × accessibility < MAX_ACCESSIBILITY` floored to 0). `OreDeposit::next_yield` now floors a workable, non-empty body whose curve still yields to ≥1. Test `vf_c3_a_low_accessibility_body_does_not_strand_its_reserve`.
* **B3 (MEDIUM).** `@claim/show` disclosed a fog-gated deposit id and both principals to any actor holding the claim id. Gated in `cmd_claim`'s show branch to staff / holder / issuer (`acts_for`) / co-located, `NO_SUCH_CLAIM` otherwise; the subject renders without the raw deposit id for a non-staff viewer and the `{:?}` Debug renders are replaced. Test `vf_b3_claim_show_is_not_a_deposit_oracle`.
* **R1 (MEDIUM).** The applied-marker mirror dropped committed markers by `pop_first` while `is_complete()` still returned true (a silent, non-monotone flip past `MAX_APPLIED_MARKERS`). Cut 2's `truncated` flag is restored in `store::reconcile`, so a pruned mirror is fail-closed for the whole family. **Deviation from the brief's D2(a):** the cap was **kept at 4096** (not raised to `MAX_MARKER_PAGES × INDUSTRY_PAGE`) — cut 2's sibling is *also* 4096/8192 flag-driven (the "cut 2 set them equal" premise did not hold at `270768f`), so restoring the flag is the whole correctness fix and keeping 4096 makes the `MAX_APPLIED_MARKERS + 1` regression test exercise `pop_first`. Durable pruning deferred to cut 4. Test `vf_r1_a_pruned_mirror_answers_no_fact_for_the_newest_too`; `facts.rs` and the §3.3 sentences corrected.
* **R2 (LOW).** `Quarantine::NoPool` was constructed nowhere, and the store docstring / §3.3 named a third quarantine shape the tree never built. The dead variant is **removed** and the docs corrected: a fully-absent pool pair at a hydrated vault degrades to `Depleted` (fail-closed) by design. Test `vf_r2_a_fully_absent_pool_degrades_to_depleted_by_design`.
* **B5 (LOW).** `ClaimIndex`'s "the live claims" doc-comment was false — the index is add-only and never pruned, so `MAX_CLAIMS_PER_SUBJECT` is a **lifetime** cap. Doc-comments corrected (`record.rs`, `families.rs`); exclusivity still checks the live authorizing set separately. Test `vf_b5_max_claims_bounds_the_lifetime_set`.
* **B6 (LOW).** The 9th co-located surveyor was locked out and saw a distinct "at most 8 principals" message (a full-roster leak). `cmd_survey` collapses the cap refusal to `NO_SUCH_DEPOSIT`; the lifetime cap stands. Test `vf_b6_a_ninth_surveyor_is_not_locked_out_or_leaked`.
* **B7 (LOW).** The `@extract` allowlist note misdescribed the cycle as a hold-then-consume. Corrected in `legacy-handlers.toml` to the as-built single `debit` (deviation (g)). No code change.
* **B4 (test gap).** The `@survey` locality gate was sound but untested. Added `vf_b4_survey_off_site_is_indistinguishable_from_unknown` (`e7c3.rs`): off-site renders byte-identically to an unknown id, on-site yields the two bands, staff survey from anywhere.
* **B2 (test gap).** The `@extract` command→receipt path and re-survey-across-reboot were untested. Added `vf_b2_extract_command_to_receipt_commits_one_lot` (`mush-server` `world_task` tests, over the coordinator: one lot at the operator's endpoint, `industry.run` advanced, a replay is a marker HIT) and `vf_b2_a_re_survey_across_a_reboot_is_a_hit` (`e7c3.rs`).

*Every `file:line` in the body of §3.3 above predates the `91208ab` reformat and is **historical**: the symbol beside each is the citation (08i §8's rule). `record.rs:50-61` is `InstrumentKind`; `:521-527` is `Encumbrance::Disputed`; `:537-542` is `Encumbrance::Collateral`; `contract.rs:567` is the escrow `DepositRecord`; `mush-faction/src/data.rs:414-430` is `ControlKind`; `09:1019-1028` is doc 09 C8's bounded-key action list. None is re-numbered.*

### 3.4 Cut 4 — `mush-industry`: facilities, recipes, work orders

This cut **is** the production-slice proposal, folded onto the binding decisions. Its §4 shapes, §5 clock semantics, §7 scenario and §8 E7a–E7d gates are adopted; four things change.

**Three overrides and one specification.** (1) **Ov — materials come from `mush-inventory`, not an asset container or "material vault"** (Q2D/Q13A) — `material_source: EndpointRef`. (2) **Ov — the value reservation is an E4 `Hold`, not an industry reservation**: T4 and doc 10 §15 keep holds money-only and forbid a resource field, while the proposal lists money, materials and capacity as undifferentiated "reservations". (3) *Not an override, a specification* — **the capacity reservation is Q3A's C8 shape-conformant record in `mush-industry`, holder = the work order's deterministic key**, with a named migration **trigger that opens a review, never an automatic migration** (§5); the proposal already keeps the economy journal authoritative (§2.3), so this adds a shape rather than reversing one. (4) **Ov — the campaign, not the ruleset, registers a restamp policy** (Q8D); the proposal's §3 boundary gives the ruleset "clock choice", which Q8D reserves to the kernel (generations) and the campaign layer (rate policy), leaving the ruleset a pack value.

**Records** (proposal §4, amended).

```rust
struct RecipeRef  { owner: OwnerId, id: CanonicalId, version: u32, digest: DefinitionDigest }
struct FacilityRecord { object: ObjId, owner: PrincipalId, capabilities: BTreeSet<CanonicalId>,
                        capacity_revision: u64, policy_revision: u64,
                        active_slots: u32, maximum_slots: u32 }
struct WorkOrder { id: WorkOrderId, revision: u64, requester: PrincipalId, beneficiary: PrincipalId,
                   facility: ObjId, recipe: RecipeRef, quantity: u32,
                   budget_account: AccountId,             // money → an E4 Hold (T4)
                   material_source: Option<EndpointRef>,  // ← was AssetContainerRef (Q2D)
                   destination: DeliveryTarget, holds: Vec<HoldId>,
                   reservations: Vec<ReservationId>, schedule: Option<ProductionSchedule>,
                   state: WorkOrderState, visibility: EconomicVisibility }
struct ProductionSchedule { started: ClockStamp, due: ClockStamp, clock_policy: CanonicalId,
                            progress_units: u64, total_units: u64 }
enum WorkOrderState { Draft, AwaitingApproval { request: ApprovalRequestId }, Ready, Running,
                      Interrupted { cause: InterruptionCause }, AwaitingDelivery,
                      Completed { commit: CommitToken }, Cancelled, Failed }
```

`active_slots` is an **advisory counter, not authority** — the proposal's own checklist says so. Availability is derived from live capacity reservations at every read, exactly as `held_amount` is a re-derived projection in E4 (08c D-E4-1).

**What an accepted order holds** (proposal §4, re-typed): money/requisition value as an E4 `Hold`; each conserved material input as a cut-2 inventory reservation; one facility slot or measured capacity as an industry reservation; any unique source asset being repaired or refit as an E4 custody lock/`Encumbrance`; an optional bounded destination capacity claim as an inventory reservation on the destination endpoint. **Every one uses the work order's deterministic holder key.** Completion consumes all required holds in the same transition that makes output available; cancellation and rejection release idempotently. A failed delivery may keep a completed output in `AwaitingDelivery` but **may not re-run production or consume the inputs twice**.

**Capacity's guard registration** mirrors cut 2's, under `mush-industry`'s own `OwnerId` — see cut 2 invariant (6); the two registrations differ only in owner, which the `(OwnerId, AuthorityGuardKind)` key makes safe.

**"Consume atomically" before K3's mixed write sets** (Q3A): one plan, one batch, one journal-authoritative work-order document naming every reservation it needs, a single companion-carrying terminal batch, **no fallible step after the first install** — E4b2's plan-first rule, the strongest atomicity the tree offers (08c §5.1).

**Clock semantics** (proposal §5, adopted; Q8D's ownership). `started` and `due` are captured on one exact generation; scheduler comparisons are legal only while the provider returns that identity (`ClockStamp::checked_cmp`, K `clock.rs:119`). On a generation mismatch the production provider performs **one explicit campaign-selected action**: restamp preserving remaining IC duration; restamp preserving the previously computed real due instant; or **pause as `Interrupted { TimelineChanged }`**. **The first slice uses the third, safest policy unless the campaign module registers one of the first two** (D-E7-8). A restamp is a durable attributed transition; **merely observing a new generation never edits the order in place**.

**Commands.** `@facility/list|show <id>` · `@wo/draft <recipe>=<facility>` · `@wo/set <id>/<field>=<value>` · `@wo/quote <id>` (deterministic price, duration, requirements, opaque offer) · `@wo/submit <id>` · `@wo/cancel <id>` · `@wo/show <id>` · `@wo/list [facility|mine]` · `@wo/deliver <id>` · `@wo/restamp <id>=<policy>` (campaign authority).

**The first vertical scenario** (proposal §7, re-based on cut 1's corridor fixture rather than a free-floating motor pool). A division member with requisition permission drafts one `alloy_smelt@1` at the Phobos yard, selecting the division wallet, an inventory endpoint holding `metals`, and a hangar destination; the server returns deterministic price/duration/requirements and an opaque legal offer; submission revalidates membership, role scope, account and endpoint authority, recipe digest, facility revision, destination capacity and offer binding; below-threshold requests reserve and start, above-threshold ones enter the existing org approval and reserve only after it; a duplicate submit joins the same order and a competing order cannot spend the held funds or material or overbook the second slot; restart during `Running` hydrates the same holds, schedule generation, progress and timer key; losing facility access interrupts rather than completing remotely; a timeline generation change pauses or restamps per registered policy; completion consumes holds once, constructs the output, assigns title and maintenance liability, places it at the destination and emits one causal completion event; **title alone grants no station and no control** (proposal §6).

**Tests (~30).** *Authorization:* a non-member, a member without the scope, a suspended scope, a frozen account and a closed endpoint each refuse at submit and write nothing; approval above the threshold reserves only after approval; approval replay is a HIT. *Reservation:* two orders contend for the last slot and exactly one starts; a duplicate submit joins the same order; a competing order cannot spend held funds or material; cancellation releases every hold exactly once and a second cancel is a HIT; expiry releases idempotently. *Atomicity:* every injected cut point in the terminal transition either retains the pre-state or converges to exactly one output; the allocator cannot reuse the output object id across restart (K3.1b); a probe re-entering asserts no `?` remains after the first irreversible mutation. *Clock:* a duplicate timer fire is a HIT; an out-of-order fire converges; a large jump is bounded by catch-up; **a generation change with no registered policy pauses as `Interrupted { TimelineChanged }` and never silently moves `due`**; a registered restamp preserves remaining IC duration as one attributed transition; observing a new generation without a transition edits nothing; comparing stamps across generations refuses. *Drift:* a recipe digest change makes a draft stale; a `capacity_revision` bump makes a `Ready` order revalidate; a `policy_revision` bump re-runs the licence check (cut 3). *Delivery:* a full destination leaves `AwaitingDelivery`, and a retry delivers without re-running production or re-consuming inputs. *Leakage:* a projected work order carries no hidden org, division, account or endpoint facts; the offer id is opaque; a malformed or oversized record fails closed. *Restart:* hydration at every state. *Added by the review:* **a terminal order may not strand a live hold** — `Failed`/`Cancelled` with a reservation still `Held` is unreachable, and the injected version of it releases and pages level 2 rather than logging (08c §5.2 M2/M3, the exact E4b2 finding); **an `Err` returned after the first irreversible mutation does not drop the batch** (08c §6.2 V1's `Outbox::pin_deferred`), probed by returning `Err` from the step after title assignment; **starvation** — one facility's queue of 64 blocked orders does not prevent order #65 at another facility from starting, and the scheduler's scan is fair (08c §4.8 S2, §5.2 B4); a **duplicate timer fire arriving after cancellation** is a HIT that does not resurrect the order; `active_slots` is asserted *never read as authority* by a probe that corrupts it and re-runs every availability path unchanged; `@wo/show` on an order the viewer may not see and on a nonexistent id are byte-identical, and `@wo/quote`'s price is a pure function of recorded inputs so a quote is never an inventory oracle; a work order naming a `RecipeRef` whose pack version was **retired** fails closed rather than re-pinning; every record re-enters through its constructor (§4), and a stored `reservations` list over `MAX_RESERVATIONS_PER_ORDER` refuses on load.

**Projection parity.** The line render and any DTO come from **one internal view** (proposal §7 item 10); DTO bytes are bounded; no TacSim panel ships (§1.1).

**Verify lenses.** **A (atomicity and contention)** — can any interleaving, retry or crash overspend, overbook, or produce two outputs? **G (generation)** — can a rate change silently move a deadline, teleport progress, or compare incomparable stamps? **P (projection)** — does any render, error or offer expose a fact the viewer may not know?

**Hands to cut 5:** a built unit with title, maintenance and custody assigned and **no station** — the exact seam that makes cut 5's focus model the only thing granting control.

#### 3.4 As built (2026-09-05, on `821145e`)

**Crates and edges — unchanged, and that is the test.** Cut 4 adds files to `crates/mush-industry/` (`facility.rs`, `workorder.rs`; extends `record.rs`, `families.rs`, `provider.rs`, `commands.rs`, `store.rs`, `facts.rs`, `module.rs`, `lib.rs`), extends `crates/ruleset-tacsim/src/pack.rs` (a `PackEntry::Facility` and two `RecipeDef` fields) and its corridor/core fixtures, and adds one file to the composition root (`crates/mush-server/src/industry_recipes.rs`). **`mush-industry`'s dependency set is byte-identical to cut 3's** — `{mush-core, mush-economy, mush-org, mush-inventory, mush-proto}` — `cargo tree -p mush-industry` is the test, and `e7c4_wiring` asserts no new edge. `ruleset-tacsim` gains **no** `mush-industry` edge: the recipe reaches industry by composition (the `RecipeAuthority` impl lives at the root, which already depends on both), not by an edge, and no callback into a ruleset happens at prepare time (the authority is consulted at offer/draft time, where a world is available).

**B1 — facilities, the capacity book, the second `Reservation` validator (OPEN 1).** `FacilityRecord { object, owner, capabilities, maximum_slots, capacity_revision, policy_revision }` is the **exempt catalog/policy half** (legacy-post-image, document `industry:facility:<obj>`, hydrated into `IndustryStore` at boot, skipped loudly on a wire failure) — it carries **no `active_slots`**, and `e7c4_b1_the_facility_record_carries_no_active_slots_counter` pins its exact field set. Availability is **derived at every read** by `IndustryFamilies::available_slots` through `ReservationBook::checked_available`; there is no counter to corrupt. The capacity book is `industry.facility_slots` (a `BoundedScalar` `0..=maximum_slots`, `current == maximum` — slots are **held and released, never consumed**) + `industry.facility_book` (K's `ReservationBook`) + `industry.capacity_index` (`ReservationId → ReservationBookId`), keyed by `ReservationBookId::derive(facility_resource(object))` where the resource subject is `SubjectKey::Scoped { owner: <industry>, class: "industry.facility", id: "f<obj>" }`, id `"slots"`. `CapacityGuardValidator` registers the tree's **second** `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator under `mush-industry`'s own `OwnerId`; the registry is keyed `(OwnerId, kind)`, so it lands on a different key from cut 2's and neither validates the other's guards (`e7c4_b1_both_reservation_validators_compose_without_collision`). **No second cap** for any of `MAX_RESERVATION_BOOK_RECORDS`/`_BYTES`/`MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES`/`_BUNDLE_BYTES`. The pack gains `PackEntry::Facility(FacilityDef { label, at, capabilities, slots })` (campaign-seed only, `at` a same-pack `Location`), one facility `phobos_forge` at `phobos_yard`, and **the corridor digest is re-derived** (below).

**B2 — recipes, the authority seam, the pinned lines (OPEN 2).** `RecipeRef { pack: CanonicalId, version: u32, id: CanonicalId, digest: DefinitionDigest }` carries **the pack's** published digest (§9 q3: no second digest, no second registry). `RecipeDef` is extended with `requires: BTreeSet<CanonicalId>` and `work_units: u64`, both bounded on load in `PackEntry::check` beside `MAX_RECIPE_LINES`/`MAX_RECIPE_UNITS`, both `#[serde(default)]` (so cut 1's authored recipes need not restate them) but always serialized — so **the core pack digest moves and is re-derived**. The `RecipeAuthority` trait (`digest_of`, `resolve`) is defined in `mush-industry` and implemented by `mush_server::industry_recipes::TacsimRecipeAuthority`, handed in at the root through `IndustryModule::with_recipes(Arc<dyn RecipeAuthority>)` — exactly the `EconomyModule::{with_custody, with_facts, with_title_issuers}` shape. A **retired** pack version fails closed as one `Option`: `published_digest` returns `None`, so `digest_of` and `resolve` do. A `WorkOrder` pins the resolved `PinnedRecipe` (inputs/outputs/`requires`/`work_units`) and the `RecipeRef.digest` at draft; **submit revalidates** the pinned digest against `digest_of` and the facility revisions against the live document before it reserves, refusing a drifted draft.

**B3 — `WorkOrder`, `ProductionSchedule`, the state machine (authoritative — contradiction (iii)).** `WorkOrder` and `ProductionSchedule` are **authoritative typed state** (family `industry.workorder`, prepared path), because a `Running` order's transition gates a competitor's capacity and its `reservations`/`holds` lists index authoritative records — the amendment cut 3 made for `OreDeposit`, made here for the work order. `WorkOrderState::{Draft, AwaitingApproval, Ready, Running, Interrupted { cause }, AwaitingDelivery, Completed { commit }, Cancelled, Failed}`; every persisted type re-runs its constructor caps through a `TryFrom` wire (a stored `reservations` list over `MAX_RESERVATIONS_PER_ORDER` **refuses on load, never truncates**). `ProductionSchedule` holds `{ schedule: ScheduleId, started, clock_policy, total_units, progress_units }` and **reads `due` from the `DurableSchedule`** — no second `due` (contradiction (v)). The deterministic holder key `wo/{order}` is asserted inside `MAX_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_BYTES`, and `wo_holder(order)` derives one stable `ScopedIdempotencyKey` per order so the terminal transition re-derives exactly the reservations submit placed.

**B4/B7 — the acquire path and the terminal transition.** Submit's provider reserves, in the fixed order, each material input (cut 2's `reserve`, under the delegation) → one capacity slot (B1's `reserve_slot`) → transitions `Draft → Running` and opens the schedule, **one bundle**, every reservation carrying `wo_holder(order)`. Deliver's provider **consumes** every material reservation (cut 2's `consume` over `checked_apply_resource` — cut 4 writes no arithmetic), **releases** the capacity slot (it returns to the facility), **materializes** the output via cut 2's `mint_origin`, writes the `industry.workorder.completed` marker the fact is answered from, and transitions to `Completed { commit }` — **no fallible step after the first install**; the marker (which refuses an existing one) and the request-derived lot id are the replay guards, so a retry mints nothing twice. Cancel releases every reservation exactly once and a second cancel is a HIT (the order is already terminal). `e7c4_a_a_work_order_run_conserves_its_inputs` proves 4 metals in → 1 alloy lot out with the source pool down exactly 4; `e7c4_a_cancel_releases_the_material_reservation` proves release restores availability. **Deviation, deferred and recorded:** the E4 money `Hold` (Place at submit, Capture at deliver, Release at cancel) is **not wired in cut 4** — the `WorkOrder` carries `budget_account` and `holds` shaped for it, but a hold is an economy-ledger saga a provider (no world) cannot place inside the authoritative bundle, so it is left as the command-side E4b1 `post` integration the cut-5 handoff names, on cut 3's "economy posts from the command" precedent. `budget_account`/`holds` are authoritative-migration-shaped and unused today.

**B5 — the production schedule source (D-E7-16 corrected).** `Registrar::schedule_source(CanonicalId "production", <WoAdvance action>, industry.production_schedule, ProductionScheduleAdapter)` is registered on **`crates/mush-sim/src/handoff.rs`'s exact shape** — the tree's first production one already existed there, so D-E7-16's "cut 8 gets the first" is stale and corrected here. A `DurableSchedule` on `ClockRef::WorldIc { generation }` is created in the same bundle as `Running`, with a `ProductionJob` sidecar keyed by `ScheduleId` (the `HandoffJob` pattern), a `Completed`-or-fresh occupancy check before reuse, and `ProductionScheduleAdapter::plan` re-deriving the candidate (`plan_due(..) == Advance(planned) where planned == advance`) and **refusing a stale candidate** rather than acting. The adapter sets the invocation's actor to the requester (`job.actor()`) while the kernel mints the `PrincipalId::Scoped { class: "scheduler" }` principal and `IdempotencyKey::Deterministic(advance.deterministic_key())` — cut 4 rebuilds none of it. **Corrected by the independent verify (R1):** the original build never tore the repeating schedule down when the order went terminal, so the next cadence tick discovered a candidate `wo_advance` refused with a non-retryable `ProviderRejected` and the driver froze the whole world into `apply_integrity_freeze` (a reboot re-froze on the same historical orphan). The terminal transition now tears both the schedule and its job down in the same bundle (`IndustryFamilies::tear_down_production`, called from the `wo_advance`→`AwaitingDelivery`, `wo_deliver` and `wo_cancel` bundles), and `ProductionScheduleAdapter::plan` additionally refuses a terminal/missing order's surviving schedule as **`Stale`** (a `DiscardedStale` the driver drops, never a freeze) — the belt-and-suspenders for any historical orphan.

**B6 — progress and the generation lens.** `WorkOrder::with_progress` advances `progress_units += units × occurrences`, checked and **bounded by `total_units`** (`e7c4_g_production_progress_is_bounded_by_total_units`), driven only by a schedule occurrence, never by observation. `InterruptionCause::{TimelineChanged, CapacityRevised, PolicyRevised, RecipeDrifted, FacilityLost}` is the pause vocabulary; the D-E7-8 default is `Interrupted { TimelineChanged }`. **Corrected by the independent verify (R2/D4):** the original claim that a generation change "with no registered policy pauses as `Interrupted { TimelineChanged }`" was *unreachable* — a `WorldIc` generation bump made the shared `ScheduleSourceRegistry::scan` fail closed with a hard `StaleGeneration` before any provider ran, freezing the world, and `@wo/restamp` always refused, so there was no recovery. Two changes, per the taken decision (fix the scan directly, the K line being closed): the scan now **skips a stale-generation schedule per-entry** (`ClockReadError::StaleGeneration → continue`, `crates/mush-core/src/schedule_source.rs`) — leaving it paused, the design's intent, while every other source still surfaces — and **`@wo/restamp` reconciles**, rebuilding the paused `DurableSchedule` at the current `WorldIc` generation and resuming an interrupted order (the default policy a frozen world needs). No kernel prepare-seam; the automatic order-interrupt reconciliation is not built (`store::reconcile` holds no authoritative txn), so recovery is the scan-skip pause plus `@wo/restamp`.

**The clock decision.** Production runs on `ClockRef::WorldIc { generation }`, stamped from `Ctx::world_ic_now()`. `ClockRef::Utc` has no mutable generation, so on `Utc` every restamp, pause and `checked_cmp` refusal in §3.4 would be dead code; `WorldIc` is the only arm D-E7-8 is about. Cut 4 validates the `[tacsim.time]` production catch-up policy key it consumes and leaves the rest parsed-and-inert. (The independent verify (R2/D4) then made the `WorldIc` generation change actually survivable: the shared scan skips a stale-generation schedule per-entry rather than freezing, and `@wo/restamp` reconciles it — see B6 above.)

**The quote's binding.** `@wo/quote` is `ReadOnly` and renders `quote_price` (total pinned input units × quantity) and `quote_duration` (`work_units × quantity`) — **pure functions of the recorded pinned inputs, reserving nothing** (`e7c4_p_a_quote_is_a_pure_function_of_recorded_inputs`), so a quote is never an inventory, capacity or reserve oracle and two worlds differing only in another principal's holdings quote identically.

**The document table, and the direction of each loss.** `industry.workorder` (`WorkOrder`) and `industry.production_schedule`/`_job`/`.wo_applied`/`.marker_ring`/`.wo_marker_ring`/the capacity families are **authoritative** — a corrupt record refuses the boot (losing a work order would strand its reservations; losing the capacity book would let availability rise — the one fail-open direction forbidden). `industry:facility:<obj>` (`FacilityRecord`) is **exempt**, skipped loudly, the loss being availability (a facility with no document offers no slots — fail-closed). **Corrected by the independent verify (R4):** the "availability rise forbidden" invariant and the B8 boot rule ("a corrupt work order is skipped loudly, its facility marked, and capacity is not released") were **not built** — a *wholly-absent* capacity pair (`load_capacity_pair`'s `(None, None)` arm) read as `maximum_slots`, the exact fail-open direction, invisibly (the kernel does not refuse a boot on *missing* records, only *corrupt* ones); and a *corrupt* authoritative work order refuses the **whole** boot rather than being skipped. Both are now honest: `store::reconcile` gains a bounded work-order arm that **quarantines a facility whose live (`Running`/`Interrupted`/`AwaitingDelivery`/`Ready`) order references a capacity book that is wholly absent** (`FacilityQuarantine::CapacityLost`), so its availability reads 0 and every draft/submit refuses until reconciled — the reservations stand, capacity is not released; and a corrupt authoritative work order **refuses the boot by design** (the kernel's stricter rule; the "skip loudly" of B8 is unreachable for an authoritative record and is corrected to a facility quarantine of the both-absent shape).

**D2 discharged — durable marker pruning.** The extraction `industry.applied` family is now bounded per deposit by `industry.marker_ring` (a `MAX_RETAINED_MARKERS_PER_DEPOSIT` = 8 FIFO written in each cycle's bundle; an evicted marker is deleted through `ActionTxn::remove`). This **cannot break replay idempotency**: a replayed old cycle refuses at the run's monotone `next_cycle`/revision check long before its marker would be read, so the marker is a fast guard the ring may retire while the run's monotonicity remains the durable one (`e7c4_d2_the_durable_marker_family_stays_bounded_and_conserves`, `e7c4_d2_the_marker_ring_evicts_the_oldest_past_the_cap`). **Corrected by the independent verify (R3/D3):** "D2 discharged" read as though the marker-family unboundedness were retired, but cut 4's **own** completion family `industry.wo_applied` shipped with **no ring** and was never pruned — so past `MAX_APPLIED_MARKERS` (4096) lifetime completions the RAM mirror truncated, `is_complete()` flipped false, and **every** industry fact (extraction *and* work-order) went dark for ever, a durable DoS a reboot re-derived. It now carries the same bounded retention: `industry.wo_marker_ring`, keyed **per facility** (a work order completes exactly once), FIFO-evicts through `IndustryFamilies::record_wo_completion` in the terminal bundle, so the completion family stays bounded and the fact mirror never truncates (`vf_r3_the_completion_family_stays_bounded`). The request-derived lot id and the refuse-an-existing-marker guard remain the durable replay guards, so the ring retires only a marker the delivery path will never read again.

**The facts.** `industry.workorder.completed` is a **second arm on cut 3's `IndustryFactProvider`**, answered from the committed terminal bundle's own `industry.wo_applied` marker mirrored into `IndustryStore` beside the extraction mirror (DECIDED (f)); a bundle that did not commit leaves no marker and it answers `None`. No second namespace is registered.

**Wiring, the allowlist, and the pins.** `IndustryFactProvider` is unchanged in `with_facts` (the arm is inside it); `industry_title_issuer` is unchanged; `IndustryModule::with_recipes(TacsimRecipeAuthority)` is added at the root; **no boot-order edit** (industry is already composed after inventory). `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` gains `("@wo", Authoritative)` and `("@facility", LegacyPostImage)` by name and owner. `legacy-handlers.toml` gains `@facility` (between `@extract` and `@faction`, `legacy-post-image`, family `industry.facility`) and `@wo` (between `@weather` and `@wod`, `authoritative`, family `industry.workorder`) **in the same commit**, byte-sorted, and **`PINNED_BASELINE` stays 158**.

**The two pack digests, moved and re-derived.** Extending `RecipeDef` moved the **core** digest to `25dd2279206de1ffa32e45714b0e57f89fda2ba543eebec703821bf749855cad`; adding the `phobos_forge` facility moved the **corridor** digest to `1885f24044f15bdb63b858ed861665465e2b4527233696d237ec84389f758792`. Both were re-derived, never hand-edited: the fixture-loading path recomputes and compares (`load_pack`'s `DigestMismatch`), so a stale declaration fails the boot loudly and the move is itself a test; `config_ext.rs`'s bad-digest needle was updated to the new core digest.

**The five OPEN decisions, as taken.** (1) `active_slots` is not a stored field; availability is derived. (2) `RecipeRef` addresses the pack and carries the pack's digest. (3) payment is an E4 `Hold` only (shaped, deferred as above). (4) `ClockRef::WorldIc`. (5) `@wo/quote` is `ReadOnly` and renders the projected offer, reserving nothing.

**Contradictions, amended in place.** (i) `active_slots` removed (OPEN 1). (iii) §4.1's cut-4 row is amended: `WorkOrder`/`ProductionSchedule` are **authoritative**, only the facility definition half and the recipe pack record stay exempt. (v) `ProductionSchedule` reads `due` from the `DurableSchedule`, no second copy. (vi) `material_source` `None` refuses at submit when the recipe has inputs. (vii) §7's caps: `MAX_WORK_ORDER_INPUTS` is **10** (superseding §7's 16), so `10 material + 1 capacity + 1 destination = 12 = MAX_RESERVATIONS_PER_ORDER` is reachable. (viii) the facility is at `phobos_yard`. (iv) the schedule source copies `handoff.rs`.

**Deviations, with reasons.** (a) The money `Hold` is deferred (above). (b) `@facility` is `LegacyPostImage`, not `ReadOnly`: it gains an `open` switch that writes the facility document (instantiation is a mutating act, exactly cut 3's `@deposit` deviation), seeded as authored data on cut 3's `@deposit/open` precedent since there is no edge-free auto-seed. (c) `WorkOrder` pins `facility_slots`/`capacity_revision`/`policy_revision` beyond §3.4's field list, because `prepare_authoritative` has no world and cannot read the exempt facility document — the draft command pins them and submit revalidates. (d) `RecipeAuthority::{digest_of, resolve}` take `&World` (the brief's world-free signature is impossible: `TacsimStore` is per-world module state); the impl lives at the root, keeping both crates' edge sets unchanged. (e) The full scheduler-driven command→receipt e2e and the E4-hold hazard tests are left to the independent verify that follows this build.

**Cut-5 handoff.** A completed work order materializes its output as a cut-2 lot with lineage at the destination and **no station**; a unit-representation recipe's title, maintenance and custody assignment (through `issue_title`, which needs a world the provider lacks) is the command-side seam cut 5 builds on — the seam that makes cut 5's focus model the only thing granting control.

**Independent verify fold (2026-09-06, on `6b5edf8`).** Twelve confirmed findings across the three lenses (C conservation/consent, R restart/durability, B bounds/disclosure); two cross-lens duplicates merged; none refuted; the four false-or-unmet as-built claims corrected in place above. Owner decisions taken as recorded (D1/D2/D3(a)/D4(a), R1/R4 mandatory); **no kernel prepare-seam was raised** (the K line is closed — the one kernel change, the schedule scan, was made directly on the E side per the taken decision). Per item (path + symbol + test):
- **C1 (HIGH) — free-mint floor + digest binding.** `PinnedRecipe::new` (`workorder.rs`) now refuses a recipe that mints output with **no** input (`IndustryRecordError::RecipeMintsFromNothing`), so a forged zero-input/inflated-output pinned is unconstructible and un-decodable off the wire; and `ProductionScheduleAdapter::plan` (`provider.rs`), which is world-available where `prepare` is not, refuses a **drifted/unauthored recipe digest** as `Stale` (never advances → never mints) via the `RecipeAuthority` threaded into the adapter. The command already binds via the authority. *Deviations:* the offer's recipe-id `Choice` and an authority-issued `fixed_parameters` carrier were **not** added (the `RecipeAuthority` trait has no list method and `prepare` has no world); an authorized insider inflating a *real* recipe's output lines remains fork-(a)/(b)'s, deferred. Tests `vf_c1_a_zero_input_recipe_is_unconstructible` (`e7c4.rs`) + coverage via the R1 driver test's plan path.
- **C2 (HIGH, covers B1) — facility authorization + record-sourced capacity + per-requester cap.** `@wo/draft`/`@wo/submit` (`commands.rs`) gate on `is_staff || acts_for(FacilityRecord.owner())`, refusing a non-owner byte-identical `NO_SUCH_FACILITY`; the capacity maximum stays record-sourced (`facility_rec.maximum_slots()`, and a later reservation cannot inflate an existing book); a new `MAX_WORK_ORDERS_PER_REQUESTER` (16) cap via `active_work_orders`. *Deviation:* the direct-offer facility gate stays at the command (`project_offer` is projected before the facility is picked; the per-facility offer `Choice` + `fixed_parameters` carrier is deferred with C1's). Tests `vf_c2_a_non_owner_cannot_hold_a_facilitys_slots`, `vf_c2_capacity_maximum_comes_from_the_record`.
- **R1 (HIGH, covers C3) — terminal schedule teardown.** `IndustryFamilies::tear_down_production` removes the schedule+job in the `wo_advance`→AwaitingDelivery, `wo_deliver` and `wo_cancel` bundles; `ProductionScheduleAdapter::plan` refuses a terminal/missing order's orphan as `Stale`. Tests `vf_r1_a_terminal_order_plans_no_more_production` (`e7c4.rs`), `vf_r1_a_terminal_schedule_does_not_freeze_the_world` (`mush-server` `world_task`, deviation (e)'s scheduler-driven e2e).
- **R2/D4 (MED) — the generation-change freeze.** Kernel scan (`schedule_source.rs`) skips a `StaleGeneration` schedule per-entry; `@wo/restamp` (`provider.rs`) reconciles it to the current generation. Test `vf_r2_a_generation_bump_does_not_freeze_the_scan`.
- **R3/D3 (MED) — `industry.wo_applied` bounded.** New `industry.wo_marker_ring` (per facility) + `record_wo_completion`. Test `vf_r3_the_completion_family_stays_bounded`.
- **R4 (MED) — both-absent capacity fails closed.** `store::reconcile` work-order arm + `FacilityQuarantine::CapacityLost`; `facility_available` reads 0 for a quarantined facility. Test `vf_r4_a_both_absent_capacity_pair_fails_closed`.
- **B2 (LOW) — `@wo/list` cursor** (`commands.rs`, `after` + `(more: @wo/list =<cursor>)`). Test `vf_b2_wo_list_pages_to_exhaustion`.
- **B3 (LOW) — `@facility/list` continuation** (`commands.rs`, byte-ordered `after` + `(more:)`). Test `vf_b3_facility_list_signals_truncation`.
- **B4 (LOW) — `wo_viewable` staff arm** (`commands.rs`, `|| is_staff`, doc-comment corrected). Test `vf_b4_staff_may_inspect_any_work_order`.
- **B5 (LOW) — `@wo/set` fall-through** (`commands.rs`, explicit refusal, no stanza change). Test `vf_b5_wo_set_is_not_silently_list`.
- **E4-hold hazard tests** remain **N/A** while the money `Hold` is unwired (deviation (a)); they land with the E4b1 command-side `post`.
- **Rule 1 / K8.5:** no new switch, stanza or `PINNED_BASELINE` change; `@wo` stays Authoritative (family `industry.workorder`), `@facility` LegacyPostImage (family `industry.facility`), baseline **158**. The new families (`industry.wo_marker_ring`) are typed state under the existing owner, not commands.

### 3.5 Cut 5 — `mush-sim`: the active-station focus

**The correction** (doc 10 §17 item 1). Station assignments are host-keyed (`HostAssignments` over `StationAssignment`, `crates/mush-sim/src/data.rs:455-462`, `:446-453`, main) and the derived index is actor → *set* of units (`:502-503`). K5a stopped the channel-union hazard by projecting one grant per occupied station (§2.2). What is still missing is an **actor-side active focus** so only one station supplies live human control. Preserve the existing records as **billets**; add the focus above them.

```rust
// WORLD-OWNED EPHEMERAL STATE (08i answer 10): keyed by the immutable account id, held on
// `World`, never persisted, never a K6 lease keyed on the account, never an `AccountRec`
// field. Boot starts with an empty map; a reconnecting player re-claims.
struct ActiveFocus { account: AccountId, actor: ObjId, unit: ObjId, station: StationId,
                     acquired_at: ClockStamp, epoch: u64, reach: ReachProof }
enum ReachProof { Console { room: ObjId },                            // C4A: a physical console…
                  RemoteLink { terminal: ObjId, path: EnvelopeId } }  // …or a valid remote link
// Draft 0.2's `FocusRelease` enum is gone as a record. Its variants survive only as the
// `reason: CanonicalId` on K6's `ControlHandoffDraft` — `focus.clean_switch`,
// `focus.traversal`, `focus.safe_boundary`, `focus.revoked`, `focus.linkdead` — carried
// into `ControlLeaseState::checked_request_handoff` by cut 5's ingress.
```

**Station authority is K6's, not cut 5's** (08i answer 10; 08i §3 row 2; §2.2). The durable thing is the **`(subject, channel)` control lease** in `ControlLeaseState`, requested, cancelled and applied through `crates/mush-sim/src/handoff.rs` (a private module; `HandoffProvider`/`HandoffScheduleAdapter` are not re-exported; apply gated by `require_scheduler`). Focus is the world's *ephemeral* answer to "which of this account's billets is foreground"; **changing focus must revoke or bump the authority witnesses** — the released station's `ChannelGrant` is republished (`refresh_actor_links`) and the unit's control generation bumped (`bump_control_gen`), so `channel_grant_is_current` fails every intent captured under the old focus — and any *lease* transfer that follows is a `control_handoff_request` with a `boundary` and a `reason`, never cut 5's own code. **Cut 5 drops Draft 0.2's release/handoff path entirely** and becomes the campaign ingress (holder, reason, boundary) over K6's kernel: one authoritative implementation of the migrated action, as 09 §7 requires (08i §4).

**Invariants.** (1) **At most one `ActiveFocus` per actor, and one foreground focus per account** (C21A/T12/Q20A) — acquiring another **atomically relinquishes the first**, and the atomicity is the world's: competing claims from two connections of one account are serialized by the world's single writer, so no interleaving produces two (answer 10; there is no store-side claim/release pair and no persisted claim to race on). (2) **Multiple connections may attach to one focus**; the session layer already fans out to many (`mush-session/src/lib.rs:5`, main), so "one focus" constrains *what is controlled*, never how many clients watch (Q20A). (3) A billet without focus is **eligibility only**: it still projects its `ChannelGrant` (so a delayed intent can be revalidated and refused), but the command path requires the grant's station to be the focused one. (4) Station changes **release the former lease through `control_handoff_request`, require physical traversal when consoles occupy different places, and complete at a safe pulse boundary** — the `boundary` on the `ControlHandoffDraft`, applied by `checked_apply_handoff` under the scheduler principal (C5A; doc 10 §18.1); NPC or automation fallback maintains the old station through the handoff. (5) **Ownership alone never satisfies reach or control.** (6) A **clean switch fires the released station's declared fallback immediately**; an **unclean disconnect** uses the existing linkdead episode rather than a second model (the contract at `mush-session/src/lib.rs:57-72`; the field `manager.rs:46` and its 300 s default `manager.rs:61-70`, used at `:455`, `:490`, `:524`; wired from `mush-config/src/config.rs:437`, `:455`), and doc 10 R5 makes the two rulesets share one fallback rule. The fallback is an **immediate deterministic controller** under K7.0's rule (08i answer 3): it submits directly on the action-idempotency scope without a `PendingDecision` because no asynchronous provider races it; if it ever competes with an existing LLM decision it must use that decision's record and idempotency key; `DecisionControllerId.owner` is open to `mush-sim`. LLM-driven fallback stays K7-gated (§4 rule 5). **[Independent verify fold, C1/C2: cut 5's first-shipped realization did NOT meet this end-to-end — the immediate fire self-staled on release and the switch fired nothing; both are now closed, and the unclean-loss half is a system/scheduler-principal relief authorized by the declared `sim.station_fallback` record with no departed-grant witness (`vf_c1_b_unclean_loss_system_principal_seats`). Wiring that system relief to a production linkdead trigger, and the fuller fork-(a) system-principal ingress, are a tracked follow-up.]** (7) Revocation or any control-surface change **bumps the unit's control epoch** (`bump_control_gen`, `crates/mush-sim/src/data.rs`), so stale intents fail `World::channel_grant_is_current` — which **exists on the converged main** (G4 closed, §2.5); cut 5 writes no comparison, and Draft 0.2's "cut 5 writes the comparison" is struck. (8) **Accepting an invitation does not seize focus** — a grant is dormant until activated, so a scheduled muster is accepted hours ahead without stranding the player (Q20A; cut 10 depends on this).

**The account seam, pulled forward here** (Q11A). `mush-core` defines a synchronous provider shaped like `FactProvider`/`CustodyProvider` (`mush-economy/src/facts.rs:62-81`, main), implemented by the server/store layer: `same_account(a, b) -> Option<bool>` and `all_accounts_distinct(characters)`, with synchronous updates when characters are created, linked or removed and when accounts are merged or renamed. **`None` = unknown and every calling policy fails closed.** Q11A's third verb — "atomic claim and release of the account's foreground focus" — is **not** on this provider after 08i answer 10: the provider answers *which account* a character belongs to; the world holds the focus map itself (above), keyed by that id. Core, not `mush-org`, because roster lock, side assignment, parties and seat grants should not each gain an org dependency, and an account has no org (Q11A). **Cut 5 ships the seam and the focus half; cut 10 adds the distinctness consumers** (§9 q5). Its **two prerequisites** land here: **(i)** an immutable opaque account id on `AccountRec`, stable across a rename (`mush-store/src/types.rs:718-741`, main, has none — the lowercased name *is* the key, so a rename is an identity change today), and **(ii)** a character → account-id map hydrated at boot so the oracle answers synchronously inside a world command. **Neither puts the id in a journaled or canon record** (R3/R7). **There is no boot-hydrated focus map** — boot clears focus, and a reconnecting player re-claims through `+focus` (answer 10).

**Commands.** `+focus <unit>/<station>` · `+focus/release` · `+focus` · `+stations` (my billets, with reach and focusability) · `@station/fallback <unit>/<station>=<who>` · `@account/audit <a>=<b>` (staff — answers "same account?" **without printing an identity**, R7).

**Tests (~26).** *One focus:* two `+focus` in one tick leave exactly one; a second connection attaches to the same focus; two connections issuing conflicting orders both resolve against one focus; an oracle answering `None` refuses the acquire. *Stale epoch:* an intent queued under grant epoch `e` fails after a revocation, a channel narrowing, an unplacement and a station removal; a grant on a *different* station of the same unit does not satisfy the check; priority is never taken from a different grant than the one supplying the channel (K5a). *Reach:* a console in another room refuses without traversal; a remote link refuses when the delivery path is down (cut 7 tightens this; cut 5 accepts a stub predicate); ownership without a billet refuses. *Handoff:* a switch mid-pulse completes at the boundary, not before — the `ControlHandoffDraft.boundary` is honoured by `checked_apply_handoff` and a probe asserts cut 5 owns no code path that changes a lease holder except through `control_handoff_request`; the released station's fallback is live before the boundary passes; a linkdead actor keeps the seat for `linkdead_grace` then falls back; a clean switch does not wait for the grace; the fallback's direct submission carries no `PendingDecision` and a fallback racing an injected LLM decision reuses that decision's key (answer 3). *Billets:* an actor with three billets projects three grants and controls one; a non-focused billet's command refuses **naming the focused station**. *Restart:* focus is cleared, not resurrected — after a restart no actor holds a focus until they re-acquire, the declared fallback drives the seat, and a probe asserts `+focus` performs **no store write at all** (the map is world memory; answer 10). *Witnesses:* every focus change bumps the unit's control generation and republishes the released grant, so an intent captured under the old focus fails `channel_grant_is_current`; a probe asserts no E7 crate registers a lease-like state family or a second `ControlLease` validator (08i §4). *Account:* a rename preserves the immutable id; the boot map answers inside a world command; the id appears in no journal (a byte scan asserts it). *Added by the review:* **no predicate fails open** — `ReachProof`, focusability and the oracle are asserted to refuse on `None`/unknown, and any derived `Ord` or `max`-shaped combinator over them is banned by a probe (E5a lens A found exactly this: `Access`'s derived `Ord` ranked `Refused` last, so the obvious strict combinator failed open); the account map missing a character answers `None` and **refuses the acquire**, it does not fall back to "distinct"; a focus claim whose witness bump fails to commit leaves **no** focus rather than a focus whose old grant is still current; two focus acquires racing across two connections of one account leave one focus and one loser, and the loser's error names no unit it may not see; a linkdead actor whose grace expires **while its unit is in a promoted domain** hands off at the pulse boundary, not on the timer; `+stations` on an actor with a billet on a unit they may not perceive lists the billet and nothing about the unit's location; `@account/audit` answers same/different and **never** an id, and a probe scans its output bytes.

**Verify lenses.** **F (one focus)** — can any interleaving, reconnect, crash or proxy path produce two live control surfaces for one account? **E (epoch)** — can a stale intent execute after any control-surface change? **H (handoff)** — can a seat be empty, doubly-held, or held past a revocation across a boundary?

**Hands to cut 6:** the focus and fallback semantics a carrier's crew keeps across a journey leg and across promotion into a live domain.

#### 3.5 As built — B1 (2026-09-05, `4a17852`); B2–B4, B7 and the focus half of B8 (2026-09-06, on `32840b4`); B5, B6 and the handoff half of B8 (2026-09-06)

**Three build passes.** The first landed **B1 (the account seam)** and the pre-code §4.0c confirmation log. The second landed the **F (one focus)** and **E (epoch)** lenses in full — **B2** (the world-owned ephemeral focus), **B3** (billets and `+stations`), **B4** (the bump-then-republish witnesses), **B7** (the `@identity/audit` distinctness verb), and the focus half of **B8** (the `+focus` / `+stations` / `@identity` commands, their per-group `classify_commands`, and three of the four allowlist stanzas). The third landed the **H (handoff)** lens — **B5** (the K6 `@station/handoff` ingress), **B6** (`@station/fallback` and the immediate deterministic controller), and the `@station` verb (the fourth allowlist stanza) — on the OWNER DECISION resolving OPEN 2 as **candidate (iii)**, recorded under "B5–B6 as built" below. B1's grounding is fixed by symbol in §4.0c B.

**B1 — the immutable account id, the oracle trait, the boot-hydrated map, the composition seam.**
- **`mush_core::AccountId`** (`crates/mush-core/src/account.rs`): opaque `Copy`/`Ord`/`Serialize`/`Deserialize` newtype over `CanonicalHash`, `#[serde(transparent)]` on the `DecisionId` pattern, **`Display` and `Debug` redacted** (`"<account>"` / `"AccountId(<redacted>)"`), **no `From<&str>`**. `AccountId::mint(name, created)` hashes `{ "name": lowercased, "created" }` under domain `account_id.v1` — deterministic and case-insensitive, so the boot back-fill is idempotent and a rename (there is none) would not move it.
- **`mush_core::AccountIdentity`** (same file): `account_of` (required) plus `same_account` / `all_accounts_distinct` (defaulted in terms of `account_of`), **every one `Option`, `None` = refuse**; the defaults cannot be made to fail open. No RNG, no `max`/`Ord` combinator over verdicts.
- **`AccountRec.id: Option<AccountId>`** (`crates/mush-store/src/types.rs:1113`, `#[serde(default)]`): a pre-id document decodes with `id == None`; the hand-written redacting `Debug` gained the field (it prints `Some(AccountId(<redacted>))`, never the hex). The live create path mints it (`crates/mush-gateway/src/accounts.rs`, `AccountId::mint(account, created).ok()`); the M1 import leaves `None` (the boot map mints on the fly). **Two store-conformance functions** (`crates/mush-store/src/suite.rs` — `account_id_round_trips_and_is_immutable`, `account_id_backfill_is_idempotent`) run against **memory** (`tests/memory_suite.rs`) and **Redis** (`tests/redis_suite.rs`'s `redis_gated!`, which the `redis-faults` job already covers by `-p mush-store --test redis_suite -- --ignored`, and which the `ci_gated_test_inventory` census leaves unchanged because a macro-generated suite is invisible to its source scan).
- **The oracle holder** (`crates/mush-sim/src/focus.rs`, `AccountOracle`) is carried in world state; **`SimulationModule::with_accounts(Arc<dyn AccountIdentity>)`** (`crates/mush-sim/src/lib.rs`) is the DECIDED builder seam (the `with_facts`/`with_recipes` shape), and `register` installs the oracle (`unwired` when absent — fail closed). **The boot map** (`crates/mush-server/src/accounts_identity.rs`, `hydrate`) is built once from `store.list_accounts()` before composition, bounded by `MAX_HYDRATED_ACCOUNTS`, minting each account's id from `(name, created)` when unstored; **a character claimed by two accounts leaves both unmapped and pages** (a structured error naming the accounts), never first-wins.

**Deviations (B1), recorded.** (a) **Installation is a post-composition world-state upgrade, not a `server_modules_with` parameter.** `build_engine` (`crates/mush-server/src/lib.rs`) builds the oracle from the store and, after `Engine::from_composition`, replaces the sim's installed (unwired) `AccountOracle` via `world.module_state_mut::<mush_sim::AccountOracle>()`. This achieves the DECIDED result (oracle built from the store at boot, installed into `mush-sim`, fail-closed) with **no change to `server_modules_with`'s ~11 call sites** (including the K8.5 composition and the allowlist tripwire, and the concurrently-edited `mush-server`); `with_accounts` is provided as the seam a caller may use. (b) **The persisted back-fill writes nothing yet.** There is no `set_account_id` store method; rather than widen the `ObjectStore` trait (+ memory + redis + conformance) the map **mints missing ids on the fly** — functionally identical because the mint is deterministic — and new accounts already persist a minted id. Adding `set_account_id` and the write loop is the one B1 sub-item deferred. (c) **The `mushd` all-in-one binary's oracle is not upgraded** (only the split `mush-server` `build_engine` path is); it composes with an unwired oracle (fail-closed) until B2 consumes it. (d) The `ServiceReq::CreatePlayer` additive account-id extension (OPEN 5 / prerequisite ii, the synchronous mid-session map update) is **not** made — the boot map is immutable, so a character created mid-session is unmapped until the next boot and `+focus` (B2) would refuse it (fail-closed), which is the correct default; the extension is an optimisation deferred with B2.

**Gates run for B1:** `cargo test -p mush-core -p mush-store -p mush-sim -p mush-server --lib` green (mush-core account ×3, the mush-store debug/id redaction and the two memory conformance tests, the mush-sim `focus` fail-closed probe, and the two `accounts_identity` hydration probes); the cross-crate `AccountRec` literal additions (`id: None` in tests, minted at the live create) compile.

**B2 — the world-owned ephemeral focus** (`crates/mush-sim/src/focus.rs`). `FocusState { by_account: BTreeMap<AccountId, ActiveFocus>, by_actor: BTreeMap<ObjId, AccountId> }` installed by `reg.state(FocusState::default())` in `SimulationModule::register` — an **in-RAM slot: never persisted, never in an `Outbox`, never hydrated at boot** (a probe, `e7c5_f_restart_clears_focus_and_focus_writes_no_focus_doc`, asserts a focus change writes only `cgen:<unit>` and a fresh boot's `FocusState` is empty). `ActiveFocus { account, actor, unit, station: StationId, acquired_at: ClockStamp, epoch: u64, reach: ReachProof }` — `epoch` is the acquired unit's control generation **captured at claim**, kept for the message and **never compared** (contradiction (viii)). `ReachProof::{ Console { room: ObjId }, RemoteLink { terminal: ObjId, path: ObjId } }` — `path` is an `ObjId` **stub** (cut 7 replaces it with a real `EnvelopeId`); its `is_reachable(world, actor)` refuses on the unknown (a console must be co-located; a remote link's terminal and path must both be live). The single-writer atomicity is `FocusState::claim(ActiveFocus) -> Vec<ActiveFocus>`: **relinquish-then-install in one `&mut` borrow**, returning every displaced focus for witness refresh; `relinquish(account)` is its idempotent inverse. **No `Ord`/`PartialOrd`** on `ReachProof` or `ActiveFocus`, and a **compile-time probe** (`e7c5_b2_reach_and_focus_are_not_ordered`, a `const { assert!(!IsOrd::<T>::VALUE) }`) fails the build if anyone derives one (E5a lens A).

**B3 — billets on the existing records, and `+stations`** (`crates/mush-sim/src/commands.rs`). A billet **is** `HostAssignments.seats[station].actor == actor` plus `HostStations.stations[station]` — **no second topology, no new record**. `focused_station(ctx, actor) -> Option<(ObjId, StationId)>` is the one predicate the command path consults; `holds_billet` is the eligibility check. `+stations` lists the actor's own billets marking the focused one, bounded on records **visited** by `MAX_BILLETS_LISTED` (64) — a bound on cut 5's enumeration, **not** a load cap on `seats` (§7 gap, contradiction (ix)) — and prints **no unit location** (a probe asserts the room names never appear).

**B4 — the witnesses (the E lens)** (`crates/mush-sim/src/commands.rs`). Every claim and release preflights the bump with `prepare_focus_bump` (`SpatialState::next_control_generation`, **fails closed before any mutation** — a claim whose bump would fail leaves no focus) and then `apply_focus_bump_and_refresh` = `apply_control_generation` → `data::persist_control_gen` → `links::refresh_actor_links` — **bump THEN republish**, the order `on_obj_destroyed` already uses (contradiction (v)), so the republished grant carries the new epoch and every intent captured under the old focus fails `World::channel_grant_is_current`. Focus **never filters `desired_links`** (invariant 3): a released billet still projects its grant, now under the new epoch, so a delayed intent is revalidated and **refused**. Cut 5 adds **no tenth `ChannelGrant` consumer and no second projection**; the **08g §10 C12 census is unchanged at nine** — `desired_links` stays the only production writer.

**B7 — the distinctness verb** (`crates/mush-sim/src/commands.rs`, `cmd_identity`). `@identity/audit <a>=<b>` (OPEN 1: a new primary verb, since `@account` is `mush-economy`'s money command) is **`ReadOnly`**, staff-gated (`require_admin`), reads the B1 `AccountOracle::same_account`, answers **same/different**, refuses on the unknown (`None` — never a default), and **never prints an identity** (R7; a probe scans the output for the hex and finds none). `same_account`/`all_accounts_distinct` were built and fail-closed in B1; cut 5 registers **no** distinctness *consumer* (roster lock and side assignment are cut 10's, §9 q5).

**B8 (focus half) — commands, per-group classification, and three allowlist stanzas.** In `commands::register` (after the existing `@spatial`/`@route` group): `reg.classify_commands(ReadOnly)` then `+stations` and `@identity`; `reg.classify_commands(LegacyPostImage)` then `+focus` (its `/release` and no-arg forms) — and `LegacyPostImage` is left set, restoring the module default. Each verb's recorded class is pinned by `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility`. **Three stanzas** into `crates/mush-contract-tests/legacy-handlers.toml` in byte order — `+focus` between `+fence`/`+frenzy` (`legacy-post-image`, family `sim.control_gen`), `+stations` between `+stat`/`+territory` (`read-only`), `@identity` between `@housing`/`@ignore` (`read-only`) — with **`PINNED_BASELINE` untouched at 158**. The fourth stanza (`@station`, between `@sshkey`/`@stats`) lands with B5.

**B5–B6 as built — OPEN 2 resolved as candidate (iii).** The tension the second pass surfaced (cut 2's `command_offer_submission` requires a projected offer, but `ActionProviderRegistry::project_legal_actions` drops any offer whose action declares a non-empty `required_channels` unless the submitter already holds the lease — so a first seat-taking request on a **vacant** lease could ride no command path) is resolved by the owner as **candidate (iii)**: the migrated request/cancel actions declare **`required_channels = []`** and carry the requester's control-generation as a **fixed parameter**, not a required-channel witness.

- **The ingress (`crates/mush-sim/src/station.rs`, `cmd_station`).** `@station/handoff`/`/cancel` are a thin selector on cut 2's `Ctx::submit_authoritative` seam onto the existing K6 actions in `crates/mush-sim/src/handoff.rs` — the lease state machine is driven **only** through `ControlLeaseState::{checked_request_handoff, checked_cancel_handoff, checked_apply_handoff}` (never re-implemented). `HandoffProvider::project_station_offer` (also `handoff.rs`) projects request/cancel with `required_channels = []`, **never apply** (apply stays `require_scheduler`, kernel-driven). `/show` is `ReadOnly`.
- **The authority split lives in the offer's own guard, by lease state.** A **vacant** lease is offered only to the actor's **focused** station on the unit (focus proves the billet, and fixes the one `(station, channel)`), and it establishes the lease for the focused holder — **cut 5 is the first production `ControlLeaseState` creator** (contradiction (vi) discharged: the first request names a holder and creates the state). A lease **held by the actor** offers a relief to the station's declared fallback controller (the held-relief case that must also project); a lease **held by another** offers nothing — a non-holder cannot relieve. An unbilleted, non-staff actor holds no grant on the unit, so it cannot even form the subject query — it can never create a lease.
- **The epoch binding is enforced at APPLY, not at projection.** The requester's control-generation on the named station rides the request as a fixed parameter, is carried on the durable `HandoffJob` as a `GrantWitness { actor, station, epoch }`, and is re-checked by `HandoffScheduleAdapter::plan` against the kernel's existing `World::channel_grant_is_current`. A focus change between request and apply bumps the unit's control generation (B4's `apply_control_generation`), so the pinned witness is stale and the in-flight apply is refused (`e7c5_h_focus_change_between_request_and_apply_invalidates_it`) — exactly OPEN 2's "a focus change invalidates a request in flight", now at apply. On apply, the lease's own epoch advances (`checked_apply_handoff`), so the old holder's captured control-lease authority no longer matches and the new holder's does (`e7c5_h_apply_bumps_the_lease_epoch_...`). No kernel projection-loop change; no tenth `ChannelGrant` consumer (C12 stays nine).
- **B6 — the fallback (`station.rs`).** `@station/fallback <unit>/<station>=<who>` (staff) writes one bounded whole-document **`sim.station_fallback`** record (`station_fallback:<unit>:<station>`, a legacy-post-image exempt write) and mirrors it into the world-owned `StationFallbacks` RAM slot the controller reads. On a clean release OR switch the released station's declared fallback fires **immediately** — `fire_station_fallback` submits a `control_handoff_request` through `submit_authoritative` under a **deterministic key** (`station.fallback.<unit>.<station>.<holder>`) with **no `PendingDecision`**, exactly the `mush-npc` `UseAbility` K7.0 immediate-controller shape (`e7c5_h_fallback_controller_submits_with_no_pending_decision`). **[Independent verify fold, C1/C2:** as first shipped the fire pinned the pilot's PRE-bump grant epoch and the offer gated on the transient `FocusState` the same release tore down, so the deferred submit was refused at resolution and the seat was left empty — and `+focus <other>` fired nothing at all. Now the fire runs AFTER the epoch bump (pinning the new epoch) and `HandoffProvider::project_station_offer` re-sources the relief from the departing actor's surviving billet grant, so the fallback actually seats; `focus_acquire`'s switch path fires it too. Driven to completion in `vf_c1_a_clean_release_fallback_actually_seats` (sim `e7c5.rs` and driver-level `mush-server`) and `vf_c2_a_switch_fires_the_released_stations_fallback`.**]**

**Deviations (B5/B6), recorded.** (h) **The staff command path names any station via a free-parameter offer is deferred.** `project_station_offer` gates non-staff on focus (which fixes the one authorized station securely, since the offer is client-discoverable via `legal_actions`); staff have no billet/focus to fix a station and the OWNER DECISION rejected a separate staff-only create path, so staff-arbitrary-station naming through the command is deferred. The create/relief **mechanism** accepts any next holder at the provider level and is exercised at the engine level (`e7c5_h_current_holder_relieves_to_a_billeted_next_holder`); staff who hold a billet use the focus path. (i) **"On apply, B4's witness order" is the lease epoch bump, not a `cgen` bump.** The scheduled apply is kernel-driven and has no `mush-sim` seam to run `bump_control_gen` → `refresh_actor_links` inside its commit; the correctness goal ("the old holder's stale intent fails") is met by `checked_apply_handoff`'s own epoch increment, which the `ControlLease` guard revalidates. A new-holder `cgen` republish on apply would need a lease-commit subscription and is a follow-up. (j) **The LLM-competition clause is a PASSING pin, not `#[ignore]`.** §4.0c C's "`#[ignore]` K7 pin" is realized as `e7c5_h_fallback_key_is_deterministic_not_an_llm_decision_k7`, a passing test asserting the controller's deterministic self-derived key — because the landed `ci_gated_test_inventory` census requires every `#[ignore]` test to be owned by a CI job that runs it green, which a genuinely unrunnable K7 pin cannot be. (k) **`StationFallbacks` boot hydration — LANDED (Independent verify fold, D2/R2).** As first shipped this was deferred, and the consequence was understated: `load_boot` had no `station_fallback:` branch (the key does not match `stations:`), so the persisted docs fell to the "unknown doc ignored" warn every boot and the RAM mirror stayed empty — after any reboot the declared fallback was silently inert (no clean-switch fire, no held-relief offer) until staff re-ran `@station/fallback` for every station, with no operator signal, across the routine reboot boundary. The fold adds a `station_fallback:` branch to `load_boot` (sibling of `cgen:`, decode `StationFallback` with `deny_unknown_fields`, drop-on-unreadable) that populates the slot after the `SpatialState` install, bounded by `MAX_STATION_FALLBACKS` (`vf_d2_station_fallbacks_hydrate_at_boot`).

**Deviations (B2–B8), recorded.** (e) **`+focus`'s allowlist stanza names a family (`sim.control_gen`), not none.** The brief asked the focus verbs to "name no family and say why", but the tripwire (`legacy_handler_allowlist.rs`) **requires** a non-empty `family` for every `legacy-post-image` entry; `+focus` is legacy-post-image because it persists the pre-existing `cgen:<unit>`. The stanza names `sim.control_gen` and its note says why there is **no new** migration target (the cgen doc predates cut 5; focus itself is world memory with no document, key or family). (f) **`ReachProof::RemoteLink.path` is an `ObjId` stub, not an `EnvelopeId`** — envelopes are cut 7's (`mush-propagation`), and cut 5 adds no crate edge; the stub predicate refuses on the unknown and cut 7 tightens it. (g) **`@station` is now registered and B5/B6 are built** — see "B5–B6 as built" above; the fourth allowlist stanza (`@station`, between `@sshkey`/`@stats`, `legacy-post-image` family `sim.station_fallback`, its note carrying the authoritative `/handoff` `/cancel` ingress and read-only `/show`) landed with it, `PINNED_BASELINE` unchanged at 158.

**Gates run (focus half, 2026-09-06, on `32840b4`):** `cargo test -p mush-sim -p mush-core -p mush-store -p mush-server -p mush-contract-tests -p mush-inventory -p ruleset-tacsim -j 6` = **1088 passed, 0 failed, 59 ignored** (54 suites); `cargo clippy -p mush-sim --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean; the allowlist tripwire and `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` green with the three new stanzas and `PINNED_BASELINE` 158.

**Gates run (handoff half, 2026-09-06):** `cargo test -p mush-sim -p mush-core -p mush-server -p mush-contract-tests -j 6` green; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -j 6 -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast -j 6` = **3781 passed, 0 failed, 61 ignored** (237 suites; the known `mush-gateway --test restart_survival` `-j6` spawn flake passes on its own re-run). K8 CI: `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` (with the new `@station` = `Authoritative` assertion), `schedule_source`, `provider_rng_policy`, `truth_serialization_policy`, `legacy_handler_allowlist` — all green; `ci_gated_test_inventory` green (the K7 pin is a passing test, deviation (j)). The H-lens independent verify fold follows.

**Independent verify fold (2026-09-06, on `61fa157`+).** A second, independent adversarial verify (lenses C/R/B) found what §3.5's first "As built" missed; the confirmed items are folded in here. **OWNER DECISION D1 taken: default (b) + the loss-case system-principal slice.**
- **C1 (HIGH; covers R-lens R1) — the declared fallback never seated.** The fire pinned the pilot's PRE-bump grant epoch and the offer gated on the transient `FocusState` the same release relinquished, so the deferred submit was refused (`Invalid`, stale) at resolution AND its offer no longer projected — invariant (6) broken end-to-end, and the two shipped H tests asserted only that a submit was *queued*. Fixed per D1(b): `focus_release` fires `fire_station_fallback` AFTER `apply_focus_bump_and_refresh` (pinning the NEW epoch), and `HandoffProvider::project_station_offer` grows a departed-holder branch (`departed_fallback_station`) that re-sources the relief from the surviving billet grant + persisted lease + declared fallback rather than `FocusState` — vacant→create-holder-fallback, held-by-self→relief, held-by-another→untouched. Unclean loss: a system/scheduler-principal `control_handoff_request` with NO station witness, authorized by the declared record (the existing B5 request ingress already accepts it; no departed grant trusted). Tests DRIVE resolution to completion and assert the seat FILLS: `vf_c1_a_clean_release_fallback_actually_seats` (sim `e7c5.rs` + driver-level `mush-server` `world_task`) and `vf_c1_b_unclean_loss_system_principal_seats`.
- **C2 (MEDIUM) — a `+focus <other>` switch fired no fallback.** `focus_acquire` never called `fire_station_fallback` for the atomically-released prior station. Fixed: it now fires for the switching actor's displaced focus, after the prior unit's bump, via the same fixed mechanism (`vf_c2_a_switch_fires_the_released_stations_fallback`).
- **B1 (MEDIUM) — `@station/handoff`/`/cancel` leaked station existence.** The existence check ran BEFORE the focus gate, so a perceiving non-holder got two distinguishable refusals (existing-not-driven vs "No such station."). Fixed: `station_handoff` gates on `holds_billet` first, so a non-holder gets byte-identical `NO_SUCH_STATION` whether or not the station exists; only a billet-holder sees the "+focus it first" hint. Module comments corrected; `vf_b1_station_existence_is_hidden_from_a_nonholder`.
- **D2/R2 (MEDIUM; folds C7/R2/B7-devk + B2) — see deviation (k) above.** Boot now hydrates the `StationFallbacks` mirror; `StationFallbacks::declare` is census-bounded by `MAX_STATION_FALLBACKS` and `@station/fallback` refuses past the cap (`vf_b2_station_fallbacks_are_bounded`, `vf_d2_station_fallbacks_hydrate_at_boot`).
- **B3 (LOW) — `+stations` scanned every seat.** The per-unit scan walked the whole (uncapped) `seats` map to skip other actors' seats, so `MAX_BILLETS_LISTED` bounded output but not scan cost. Fixed with a `seats_by_actor` derived index: `cmd_stations` iterates only the actor's own seats (`vf_b3_stations_scan_is_bounded`).
- **C3/D3 (LOW) — reach re-check.** D3 taken as (b): reach is an **acquire-time gate only** in cut 5 (the `path` is a cut-7 `ObjId` stub); continuous re-check belongs with cut 7's `RemoteLink`/`EnvelopeId`. Documented and pinned (`vf_c3_reach_is_acquire_time_only_in_cut5`), tracked to cut 7.
- **Deviations (h)/(i)/(j) — confirmed safe to defer** (unchanged). **R3 (idempotency-key epoch-scoping) — refuted, no change** (the epoch is folded into the request digest via the subject revision, not the discriminator string).

### 3.6 Cut 6 — `mush-sim`: carrier journeys, promotion and demotion

**The correction** (doc 10 §17 item 2, §16 item 9, C16A). A `Journey { traveler, legs, cursor, started_at, status, paused_at, grace_until, destination, by }` (`crates/mush-sim/src/route.rs:67-90`, main) **refuses any traveler that is placed, quarantined, a domain host, an interior space, a mount host or a station host** (`commands.rs:1529-1564`; the shared predicate `is_sim_bound`, `data.rs:678-690`; boot eligibility `boot.rs:728-763`). Replace that with a **carrier-root invariant**: the root is **not directly placed in a live domain while traveling**, but its station infrastructure, anchored interiors, occupants, mounts, cargo and carried craft **remain coherent and move with it**. The carrier root owns **one** journey; nothing aboard gets a duplicate record (C16A; doc 10 §18.4).

```rust
struct CarrierJourney { journey: JourneyId, root: ObjId, route: RouteDef, legs: Vec<Leg>,
                        departed: ClockStamp, due: ClockStamp, propellant: ReservationId,
                        catch_up: CatchUpPolicy, state: JourneyState }
enum JourneyState { Cruising { leg: u8 }, Promoted { domain: DomainId, resume: ResumePlan },
                    Arrived, Diverted { to: CanonicalId }, Captured { by: PrincipalId }, Failed }
struct DomainActivation { domain: DomainId, trigger: PromotionTrigger, at: ClockStamp,
                          clock: ClockRef /* DomainTick */, units: u32 }
enum PromotionTrigger { PlayerPresent, CredibleContact, Interception, OperationActivated }
struct DomainSummary { domain: DomainId, from: ClockStamp, to: ClockStamp, contacts: u32,
                       damage: Vec<UnitDamageLine>, facts: Vec<ExternalRef> }
```

**Journey semantics.** T1: cost and duration are deterministic functions of the `RouteDef` (cut 1), drive and payload, declared departure, propellant plan and the body-pair **alignment band on a campaign cadence** — no wall-clock randomness changes a contract after publication. Survey **S6A**: a scene cannot hold or delay a journey. At arrival, in order: (1) the journey commits at its scheduled campaign time; (2) the carrier changes operational location; (3) active onboard scenes receive a **typed arrival event**; (4) their local time advances to **at least** the arrival timestamp; (5) the scene continues as a new chapter, keeping participants and transcript. This needs the **scene IC time floor** — `scene_ic_time` is `ic_started_at + rounds_elapsed × round_seconds` (`crates/mush-scenes/src/data.rs:175-178`, main). It *is* guarded against `round == 0` (`saturating_sub(1)`, `:176`), but there is **no monotonic floor on the result**, which is the thing S6A needs — see §10. **E6b owns the floor; cut 6 owns the raise** (owner line, 2026-09-03), under the K-frozen scene-clock rule (08i answer 9): `SceneClockProvider` is read-only and exposes no generation bump, **generation 1 lasts the scene record's lifetime, and an IC-floor raise never changes it**; the explicit start → advance → pause/restart → advance monotonicity test is **E6b's** (08f §4), and cut 6's raise adds only the arrival-event case to it.

**Placement is cut 6's typed family** (08i answer 8; §2 row 23). `crates/mush-sim/src/data.rs` `SpatialState.placements` is legacy `reg.state` and no `revision_validator` exists in `mush-sim` or `mush-tactics`. Cut 6 **owns the typed `PlacementRecord` migration on K's `revision_validator` contract** (`Registrar::revision_validator`, the same contract the `toyspace` fixture exercises in `crates/mush-contract-tests/src/tactical_gate.rs`): one authoritative placement family under `mush-sim`'s `OwnerId`, whole-document post-images with checked revisions, the carrier-root invariant enforced *in* the validator so a stored cycle or a cruising root placed in a live domain is unconstructible rather than caught at boot. **Stations and assignments are not placement**: `HostStations`, `StationAssignment` and `HostAssignments` remain separate control-topology records (cut 5's billets) and are migrated by nobody in E7. **`DomainRecord` authority goes with cut 8** (the `DomainTick`/production-domain work), so cut 6's `@domain/promote` writes a `DomainActivation` request that cut 8's authority applies.
