K reviews the shapes at SP2 and implements no competing migration.

**Promotion and demotion** (Q15A). *The governing rule: the system never runs tactically as a whole.* A domain promotes to `DomainClock::Pulse` on **four deterministic triggers** — a player character present in a contested context, a **credible contact** between opposed parties, an **interception** along a journey leg, or the **activation of a declared operation** (cut 9). It demotes **only after a quiet boundary** (no contacts, no pending orders, no unresolved damage for `QUIET_BOUNDARY`) and once a **durable summary** is written. **Hard limit `MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS`** with a stated policy at the limit (§9 q1). **Orders, contacts, damage and position are preserved across promotion and demotion in both directions — demotion changes cadence, never state.** `DomainClock::Passive` is the only variant today (`mush-sim/src/data.rs:210-216`, main), and `DomainRecord.driver` is an exact digest-bearing `DefinitionRef` (`:218-241`, `:227-232`), so `Pulse` arrives as a second variant plus a driver definition, not a rewrite. **`@domain/promote` never mutates the clock registry** (08i answer 4, G-A): the `ClockProviderRegistry` is composition-frozen, so promotion registers nothing — it hands the runtime `domain_id` to cut 8's owner-scoped `DomainClockProvider`, which answers by domain id and generation. A promoted domain whose provider answers no generation refuses to tick and pages (§3.11's tactical-domain row).

**The manual-cadence hook** (doc 10 §15). `advance_cadence_manual` exists (`mush-economy/src/clock.rs:297-305`, main) with **no caller at `3967e12`** beyond its re-export and one E4a test; the in-flight E5b build adds a staff verb, and **neither is a sim caller**. This cut is the first: a journey boundary advances the T1 alignment-band cadence; cut 8 adds the sortie boundary for doc 10 §9's per-sortie maintenance.

**Commands.** `@journey/plan <carrier>=<route>` · `@journey/depart <carrier>` · `@journey/show` · `@journey/divert <carrier>=<route>` · `@domain/list` · `@domain/promote <where>` · `@domain/summary <domain>`.

**Tests (~28).** *Topology:* a carrier with three anchored interiors, six occupants, two mounted craft and a loaded hold departs and arrives with every relationship intact; an occupant who walks between compartments mid-leg is in the right place at arrival; ~~a craft launched mid-leg is refused while cruising and permitted once promoted~~ — **struck and re-issued against cut 8** (the independent verify fold, D4(ii)/deviation (i): the permitted-once-promoted half has no producer until cut 8's tactical domain writes `JourneyState::Promoted`, and cut 6's seal treats `Promoted` exactly as `Cruising`; the refused-while-cruising half IS built and now covers all four cells, `vf_c1e_the_promoted_cell_is_pinned`); destroying an anchored interior mid-leg orphans no occupant; the root is never directly placed in a live domain while `Cruising`. *Arrival:* a scene open aboard receives the typed arrival event and its IC time floor rises to the arrival stamp; **a scene cannot delay arrival** (Q6A) — a 40-round scene and a due arrival both resolve, arrival first; a journey arriving during a promotion completes at the safe boundary; the alignment cadence advances exactly once per boundary. *Promotion:* each of the four triggers promotes exactly once; a fifth trigger on an active domain does not re-promote; at `MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS` the next trigger queues and re-evaluates rather than dropping; demotion refuses while a contact, a pending order or unresolved damage stands; demotion writes the summary before changing cadence; damage, orders, contacts and positions survive promote → demote → promote byte-identically. *Catch-up:* a journey's policy **preserves remaining IC duration** across a rate generation change (S6A: journey clocks never pause — **deliberately not production's pause default**, D-E7-9); a long downtime does not teleport a carrier past an interception. *Restart:* a carrier mid-leg, mid-promotion and mid-demotion each hydrate; a promoted domain with no live `DomainTick` generation refuses to tick and pages. *Added by the review:* **a topology cycle is unconstructible** — a carrier cannot be loaded aboard a craft it carries, at any depth, and boot refuses a stored cycle loudly; **arithmetic overflow pages instead of stalling** — a `due` or leg sum that overflows opens level 2 with the recovered plan, because E5a's S2 was precisely one overflow turning into permanent silence; a demotion whose summary write fails leaves the domain **promoted** and pages, never demoted-without-summary; the alignment cadence advance is idempotent per boundary (a re-run of the boundary is a HIT, so a restart cannot double-advance a T1 band and re-price an open hauling contract); `@domain/summary` on a domain the viewer never observed is byte-identical to one that never existed; every carrier and domain record re-enters through its constructor (§4). *Added by Draft 0.3 (placement and the registry):* a `PlacementRecord` post-image with a stale revision is refused by the `revision_validator`, not overwritten; a stored placement cycle and a cruising root placed in a live domain are both refused **by the validator** before boot ever sees them; `@domain/promote` leaves the `ClockProviderRegistry` composition byte-identical (a probe snapshots it before and after); a station assignment edit never touches a placement document and vice versa; the scene IC floor raised by an arrival leaves `clock_generation` at 1 (answer 9).

**Verify lenses.** **T (topology)** — can any leg, promotion or crash orphan an occupant, a hold, a mount or a station? **S (state across cadence)** — can promotion or demotion lose, duplicate or reorder tactical state? **B (boundaries and limits)** — can a scene delay an arrival, or the active-domain limit be exceeded or silently swallow a trigger?

**Hands to cut 7:** a live domain with a stable `ClockRef::DomainTick` identity for propagation to run on, and journey legs for coarse scheduled delivery to run between.

#### 3.6 As built (2026-09-06, on `db148ba`) — RESOLUTION A: one Coarse journey tick over an exempt `sim.journey` record

**The mechanism, and why it is this one.** The pass before this grounded two designs and built neither's driver. The first (a whole-lane `SpatialState.placements` → typed-family migration) is whole-lane-or-nothing and is **deferred to E7k** (08h Q5 stages it as an explicit owner decision). The second — a `reg.schedule_source` firing a `WorldEvent` that a synchronous `mush-sim` observer reacts to — **cannot be built on this tree at all**: a `schedule_source`-fired action runs inside `prepare_authoritative` with no `&World`, no `Ctx` and no `Outbox`, and writes only typed `state_family` records through an `ActionTxn`; `WorldEvent` is emitted only from the `Ctx` dispatch layer; placement is a legacy `reg.state` slot `prepare_authoritative` cannot reach; and there is **no world-side seam on a scheduled authoritative commit** (cut 5's own deviation (i)). The owner's **RESOLUTION A** (2026-09-06) is what is built: the housing "pull, not a subscription" pattern, which needs none of those seams.

**B1+B2+B3 are ONE Coarse `reg.tick`.** `journey::tick` / `journey::advance_due` (`crates/mush-sim/src/journey.rs`), registered in `SimulationModule::register` as `reg.tick(journey::TICK_TAG /* "sim.journey" */, TickCadence::Coarse(Duration::from_secs(journey::TICK_SECS /* 1 */)), Arc::new(journey::tick))` — the housing assessment tick's shape (`mush-housing/src/tick.rs`) and the economy sweep's budget. Each fire reads `ctx.w.world_ic_now()` and, for every live journey whose WorldIc position has passed its next boundary, **in one atomic tick batch** (the `cjourney:` document write and the synchronous world mutation land in the same `Outbox`): advances the leg cursor; on the last boundary moves the carrier-root subtree through the ONE checked `transition::move_carrier_subtree`; and then calls B4's `SceneRecord::raise_ic_floor_on_arrival` for each onboard scene. Bounded per fire on `MAX_JOURNEYS_VISITED_PER_FIRE` 64 / `MAX_JOURNEYS_WORKED_PER_FIRE` 16 from a rotation cursor that advances past the last **visited** root (D-LBK-2), and per move on `MAX_CARRIER_SUBTREE` 256 records **visited** (D-LBK-5). *(Corrected by the fold, C8: as shipped the cap compared the FINISHED result of an unbounded, quadratic `SpatialState::subtree`, and `Transition::Place` had no cap at all. `SpatialState::subtree_bounded` now stops one record past the cap with a `BTreeSet` seen-index, and `projections_for` applies the one constant to `Place`, `CarrierMove` and the root hold alike.)*

**The record.** `journey::CarrierJourney { journey: JourneyId, root: ObjId, route: RouteDef, destination: SpatialParent, departed: ClockStamp /* WorldIc */, advanced_at: ClockStamp, cursor: u32, state: JourneyState, anomaly: Option<String> }` — the **exempt `sim.journey`** workflow envelope (§4.1), a bounded whole-document legacy post-image at the deterministic key `cjourney:<root>` (`journey::carrier_journey_key`; deliberately **not** `journey:`, which is the legacy `@route` runtime's, contradiction (i)) plus the allocator at `journey_meta`. `CarrierJourney::new` is the one constructor and `TryFrom<CarrierJourneyWire>` re-runs it — `MAX_LEGS` 32 *(corrected by the fold, B7: the leg cap is **`RouteDef`'s**, enforced twice inside `RouteDefWire::try_from`, whose fields are private and whose only constructor already refuses past it; the journey record's own arm was unreachable and is now a `debug_assert!`, so the record does not carry an independent bound)*, `cursor <= legs.len()`, both stamps on one `ClockRef::WorldIc` generation, a placed destination, and `MAX_JOURNEY_ANOMALY_BYTES` 240 — so a stored set over any cap is **refused on load, never truncated** (§4 rule 1) — and, since the fold (R3), never **deleted** either: `install_carrier_journeys` used to open `for key in dead { push_op(Del { key }) }`, and because an under-way carrier is deliberately not placed that document is the ONLY record of where the ship and its whole crewed subtree are, so any wire refusal was permanent loss. A refused document is now HELD (`JourneyBook::held`, the `boot.rs` held-placement posture and `domain::extract_domain_workflow`'s drop-but-never-delete), its root is quarantined, and staff are paged with a runnable command; `e7c6_s7` asserted only the drop and is amended. `JourneyState::{Cruising, Promoted { domain }, Diverted { to }, Arrived, Captured { by }, Failed }` — **no `resume`, no `Pulse`** (contradiction (iii)); `Cruising` carries no leg, because the cursor is the record's own field and a second copy would be a second answer. `JourneyState::is_under_way()` is the ONE predicate the seal, the move/carry gate, the crew-link derivation and the tick all share.

**The ONE checked carrier move.** `transition::move_carrier_subtree(ctx, root, parent: Option<SpatialParent>) -> Result<Vec<ObjId>, SpatialError>` over the new `Transition::CarrierMove { root, parent }` (`crates/mush-sim/src/transition.rs`). `parent: None` puts the root under way (its own placement becomes `Unplaced` with a non-quarantined `UnplacedInfo`; its subtree stays placed and rides with it); `Some(..)` brings it to rest. Every carrier-root placement change on the tree goes through it — `@journey/depart`, the tick's arrival, and that same tick re-run at boot. The **carrier-root invariant** is stated once, inside it: a root may never come to rest inside its own subtree at any depth, checked over the descendant relation (`st.subtree(root).contains(&parent_obj)`) **and** over the ancestor relation (`graph::ancestors`), which are duals, so a malformed record set cannot slip between them; only the root's own record changes, so the subtree is atomic by construction and the whole set's projections are recomputed against the post-state and pre-validated together (a refusal mutates nothing); and the walk is bounded, `SpatialError::CarrierTooLarge`. `Transition::Place`'s parent resolution was extracted to the shared `resolve_parent`, so `Place` and `CarrierMove` can never diverge on what a legal parent is.

**The seal, and the two gates that make the one path the only path.** `transition::carrier_seal` refuses a placement that crosses an under-way carrier's boundary in **either** direction (`SpatialError::CarrierSealed`), so a craft cannot launch mid-leg and nobody boards mid-passage, while movement *within* one carrier's subtree stays free. *(Corrected by the fold, C1: as shipped this was true of the `Transition::Place` arm ONLY — the seal had exactly one call site — so three other crossings were unchecked: `@journey/depart` launched a craft **out** of a cruising mothership through the `CarrierMove` arm, a journey **arrival** boarded a whole crewed carrier **into** one through the same arm, and `@spatial/unplace` — with it the economy's `InteriorCustodyProvider::move_out`, which deliberately skips this module's own `CarryAttempt` hook — walked any occupant straight out mid-passage. The seal is now applied to all three arms, and because the custody path bypasses the gate the check lives in the TRANSITION, not the hook.)* — which is how an occupant who walks between compartments mid-leg is in the right place at arrival. `lib.rs`'s `gate_reason` gains a `journey::journeying_reason` arm, because a cruising root is deliberately **not** placed and `placed_or_quarantined` therefore says nothing about it: without that arm the move/carry hooks would let anyone pick a ship up mid-passage. `@journey/plan|depart|divert` refuse a self-carrying destination before departure (`commands::refuse_self_carrying_destination`), so a carrier never flies a whole passage only to be refused at the end.

**B3's `desired_links` fix (contradiction (v), the T lens).** `links.rs`'s gate was a bare `!st.is_placed(unit)`. A carrier root under way is not directly placed, so that test silently dropped **every crew grant** the moment a ship left — the pilot lost the helm mid-passage and got it back on arrival, with no refusal and no line of output anywhere. It is now `!controls_are_live(st, w, unit)`, where `controls_are_live == is_placed(unit) || JourneyBook::under_way(unit)`. `journey::refresh_carrier_links` is the **refresh** half of cut 5's bump-then-refresh order, run after the durable truth changes: `move_carrier_subtree` happens before the journey record exists, so at that instant every grant is correctly dropped, and this call gives the crew the helm back one line later. It adds no tenth `ChannelGrant` consumer.

**Idempotency, and why there is no crash gap (B3).** The leg cursor is the idempotency key. `advance_due` skips a journey whose cursor is already current, and `apply_arrival` checks the **placement**, not the state, before moving: a journey whose durable record says `Arrived` while its carrier is not at the destination is **repaired** by the same code path a live arrival takes. `journey::install_carrier_journeys` (called from `on_world_loaded`) ends by calling `advance_due`, so **the boot reconcile IS the tick** — there is no second pass to disagree with it. *(Corrected by the fold: as shipped this was true of the PLACEMENT half only. R6 — `apply_arrival`'s `already` short-circuit returned before the arrival event, the room narrative and `raise_onboard_scene_floors`, so a replayed arrival lost the onboard IC-floor raise permanently and nothing else ever raised it; the post-move steps now sit outside the short-circuit and only the audience-facing halves are conditional. C3 — the heal never expired: `Arrived && !at destination` was a standing repair trigger forever, so any later legitimate placement of the carrier was dragged back, whole subtree included, every second. It is now scoped by `CarrierJourney::applied_revision` (D3(a)).)* Proven in-memory, no Redis: `e7c6_s1_…` (arrive, fold the kv, reboot, assert the placement revision and the whole subtree shape are unchanged — applied exactly once — then fire again and assert nothing moves), `e7c6_s3_…` (rewind ONLY the placement document, keeping the arrived journey: boot heals it, and healing is itself idempotent), `e7c6_s2_…` (mid-leg hydrate and finish), `e7c6_s4_…` (a duplicate fire is a HIT).

**Journeys never pause** (D-E7-9). A world rate-generation change makes the stored `departed` stamp incomparable, which would strand a journey forever. `journey::reanchor` re-labels it onto the new generation, preserving the IC duration still to run **exactly** (`departed = ClockStamp { clock: now.clock, at: journey.departed.at }`), logs it, and keeps going. *(Corrected by the fold, R2: the shipped arithmetic — `departed.at = now.at - Σ base_s[0..cursor]` — restarted the IN-FLIGHT leg, so one rate change put a journey 10 IC seconds from arrival back to 200 and a bump cadence shorter than a leg deferred arrival without limit. The world IC axis is continuous across a generation (`WorldTimeline::next_rate_epoch` sets `ic_anchor = current.ic_at(real_anchor)`), so only the generation LABEL needs changing; the two `fail()` overflow arms reachable only from that sum are deleted. `e7c6_s5` asserted `remaining == 200` — it pinned the bug as intent — and is amended.)* A `due`/leg-sum overflow instead **pages**: `journey::fail` stops **only that journey**, sets `Failed`, and records a bounded `anomaly` **with the recommended command already filled in** (08c §4.6) which `@journey/show` prints; `e7c6_s6_…` pins both. *(Corrected by the fold, R7: as shipped the command the page named — `@journey/divert` — was refused for exactly that journey (`journey_divert` filtered on `is_under_way`, and `Failed` is not), and the obvious second try `@journey/depart` was refused too because the stopped root is unplaced. Only the parenthesised `@spatial` fallback worked, and it worked only because C4's gate hole let it. `/divert` now accepts any journey that is not `Arrived`, and `vf_c4c_the_anomalys_command_actually_recovers` RUNS the exact line the anomaly prints.)*

**B4 (landed in `db148ba`), now called.** At arrival, in order (S6A): commit at the scheduled campaign time → move the subtree → emit the arrival (the pre-existing `WorldEvent::Moved`; cut 6 adds **no** `WorldEvent` variant) → for each interior room in the carrier root's `subtree`, `ScenesState::active_scene_at(room)` and raise its `ic_floor` to the arrival stamp through `SceneRecord::raise_ic_floor_on_arrival` (monotonic, bounded by `MAX_IC_FLOOR_LEAD_SECS`, refused after the seal, `clock_generation` untouched at 1), mirrored in the same batch through the new public `mush_scenes::persist_scene_record`. A refusal is **paged with its numbers and never holds the journey** (Q6A/S6A). `e7c6_b1_…`/`b2_…`/`b3_…`/`b4_…` pin the raise, generation 1, a forty-round scene that does not delay the arrival, a backwards raise, and a sealed scene.

**B5, promotion and demotion.** `crates/mush-sim/src/domain.rs`. `@domain/promote <id|host>[=<trigger>]` writes a `DomainActivation { domain, trigger, at: ClockStamp, units, queued }` **request** at `activation:<id>` (exempt `sim.domain`; **no `clock: ClockRef` field** — contradiction (iii) names cut 8's `DomainTick`), which cut 8's authority applies. The four deterministic triggers promote **exactly once**; a fifth on an active domain does not re-promote and does not rewrite the standing request; at `MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS` 8 the trigger **queues** and `domain::drain_queue` re-evaluates it whenever a slot frees (§9 q1) — never dropped. `@domain/demote` writes `DomainSummary { domain, from, to, contacts, units }` at `summary:<id>` **before** deleting the activation (the op order is pinned by test), and a summary that will not construct leaves the domain **promoted** and pages. Demotion refuses while the one pending thing cut 6 owns still stands: a carrier under way whose journey ends in that domain. `@domain/summary` is byte-identical for an unobserved and a nonexistent domain, and so is `@journey/show` (rule 4). `@domain/promote` **never mutates the `ClockProviderRegistry`** — a probe snapshots `registry.clock_providers().fingerprint()` before and after (`e7c6_b5_promote_writes_a_request_and_leaves_the_clock_registry_byte_identical`). Cut 6 adds no `Pulse`, no `DomainTickDriver`, no `DomainClockProvider`.

**B6, wiring.** `@journey` is a **new primary verb**, one `CommandSpec` and therefore ONE registration taking the **ceiling** class of its four switches: `/show` is a read and `/plan` writes nothing, but `/depart` and `/divert` write the exempt `sim.journey` record, so it publishes `CommandCompatibility::LegacyPostImage` under an explicit per-group `reg.classify_commands` inside `commands::register`, with `LegacyPostImage` restored after (K8.5). `@domain/promote|demote|summary` are **new switches on the pre-existing baseline `@domain`** — no new stanza. **One** allowlist stanza was added to `legacy-handlers.toml` in byte order, `@journey` between `@inv` and `@licence`, category `legacy-post-image`, family `sim.journey`; **`PINNED_BASELINE` is untouched at 158** and no baseline entry changed. `e7c6_p4_…` pins each verb's recorded class. The module-name pin in `server_modules_register_without_collision` was re-grounded and needed **no change**: cut 6 adds no module.

**The edge set.** Exactly **one** edge is added — `mush-sim → mush-scenes` (OPEN 3), acyclic because `mush-scenes` depends on `{core, canon, proto}` and never on `mush-sim`. `cargo tree -p mush-sim --depth 1` is cut 5's set plus that one edge: `mush-core`, `mush-economy`, `mush-proto`, `mush-scenes`, `serde`, `serde_json`, `smol_str`, `thiserror`, `tracing`. Note which of those are pre-existing: **`mush-sim → mush-economy` is E3c's** (`custody.rs`'s `CustodyProvider`), unconditional and already in cut 5's set — which is exactly what lets the fold's C7 fix read `EconomySnapshot::is_vault_backing` directly instead of installing a composition oracle. `mush-scenes` gains one public seam, `persist_scene_record`, and re-exports `ArrivalFloorRefusal`.

**The five OPEN decisions, as finally taken.** (1) The driver is **not** a `schedule_source` and **not** the Utc `route::sweep` but the one Coarse `journey_tick` on `ClockRef::WorldIc`, with the leg cursor as the idempotency key in place of a `journey/{carrier}/{leg}` schedule key — the mechanism the owner decided when the `schedule_source` seam was found not to exist. (2) The whole-lane placement migration is **deferred to E7k** (08h Q5); placement stays the synchronous `SpatialState` lane, gathered behind the one checked `move_carrier_subtree`. (3) Taken as drafted: the new `mush-sim → mush-scenes` edge and `SceneRecord::raise_ic_floor_on_arrival`. (4) Taken with the authoritative half deferred: `CarrierJourney` is the exempt `sim.journey` envelope and legality derives from **placement** (the seal reads the placement graph, never `JourneyState`, to decide what may move). (5) Propellant and the alignment band are **not wired** — see the deviations.

**§4.1's cut-6 rows, amended in place.** `CarrierJourney`, `DomainActivation`, `DomainSummary` → **exempt** workflow, legacy-post-image, families `sim.journey` / `sim.domain`: **confirmed, and this is what was built.** `PlacementRecord` → the row's "authoritative typed family on K's `revision_validator` contract" is **deferred to E7k** and is not cut 6's; placement remains the legacy `reg.state` lane. The closing rule's tell is respected: the exempt journey and domain records take no part in authority or prepared mechanics, and cut 6 registers no `revision_validator`, no `state_family`, no `schedule_source` and no second `FactProvider` namespace.

**§7's gap, closed.** §7 lists `MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS` 8, `QUIET_BOUNDARY` 10 and `MAX_LEGS` 32 but no per-carrier subtree cap. `journey::MAX_CARRIER_SUBTREE` = **256** is that constant, enforced where the depart/arrival walk visits (`SpatialError::CarrierTooLarge`) and pinned by `e7c6_t9_…`. The fold adds the census §7 also never declared: `journey::MAX_CARRIER_JOURNEYS` = **512**, refused at `@journey/depart` (B8).

**Deviations, with reasons.** (a) **Propellant is not reserved.** OPEN 5 makes `CarrierJourney.propellant` a cut-2 inventory `ReservationId`, but reserving it would need a `mush-sim → mush-inventory` edge, and the cut's binding rule (d) allows **no** crate edge except `mush-sim → mush-scenes`. The journey keeps no private quantity (the `RouteDef` carries the declared plan and nothing else); the reservation lands with the cut that may take the edge. (b) **The alignment-band advance is inert and unwired.** `advance_cadence_manual` operates on a `CadenceClock` inside the economy's own store, so calling it would mean `mush-sim` writing an economy document. Cut 6's `@journey/plan|depart` authors `alignment_band: None` (there is no syntax to declare one), so the boundary advance is structurally inert exactly as OPEN 5 requires — a `None` band advances nothing and refuses nothing — and the wire-up moves to the cut that owns the cadence write. (c) **`@journey/plan` is a dry run, not a durable step.** RESOLUTION A's `JourneyState` has no `Planned` variant, and inventing one would put a second answer beside `is_under_way`; `/plan` therefore takes exactly `/depart`'s arguments, runs exactly its validation, prints the legs with their IC boundaries and writes nothing (`e7c6_p2_…`). (d) **Leg boundaries come from `LegDef::base_s` directly**, which is exactly what `route_duration` yields at nominal thrust with no payload; cut 6 has no pack lookup for a `DriveProfile` and inventing one would be a second source of truth for a duration T1 froze. (e) **`DomainSummary` carries counts, not lines**: §3.6 drafts `damage: Vec<UnitDamageLine>` and `facts: Vec<ExternalRef>`, and neither type exists before cuts 7–9 declare what damage and external facts are. (f) **`JourneyState::Captured { by: ObjId }`** rather than a `PrincipalId`: cut 6 has no capture mechanic and no principal vocabulary; the arm exists so a record a later cut writes re-enters rather than being refused. *(Corrected by the fold, C4/D1: "re-enters rather than being refused" was true and beside the point — it re-entered straight into an UN-GATED terminal state. `Captured` and `Failed` are `is_under_way() == false`, and `journey::fail` left the root `Unplaced` and not quarantined, so all three arms of `gate_reason` answered `None` at once and a non-staff player standing in the origin room could `get` the dropship, crew and mounted craft included. Fixed by D1(a): a terminal journey quarantines its root and `journeying_reason` is widened to ANY journey record on an unplaced root — over record presence, never a second under-way predicate — so every future terminal arm is covered by construction. Two residual LOWs stay: `@journey/show` no longer renders the captor's raw object id (`render_state`), and changing `by: ObjId` to a `PrincipalId` is a wire-breaking change on a `deny_unknown_fields` wire, so R3 had to land first.)* (g) **The typed arrival event is the pre-existing `WorldEvent::Moved`** — RESOLUTION A forbids a `WorldEvent::JourneyArrived` variant. *(PROMOTED TO FIX by the fold, R9, still adding no variant: as shipped the event was dispatched **synchronously from the boot path** into `mush-script`/`mush-npc`/`mush-weather`, which are composed after `mush-sim` and had not hydrated; it carried `from: None`, so no `Left` ever paired with a scene's `Joined` and `on_leave` never ran; and a departure emitted nothing at all. `advance_due`/`apply_arrival` now take a `Source::{Tick, Boot}` and emit nothing from boot, `from` is read before the move, and `journey_depart` emits the departure half. A typed `CarrierArrived { root, at }` is cut 7's, when a `WorldEvent` variant is permitted again.)* (h) **`sim.journey.arrived|failed` FactProvider arms are not added**: cut 6 registers no provider at all (rule (f) forbids a second namespace, and the one `sim.*` provider is cut 5's), and the applied marker they would answer from is the journey record itself, readable now.

**Tests.** 32 in `crates/mush-sim/tests/e7c6.rs` (`e7c6_t1..t9` topology, `e7c6_s1..s7` state-across-cadence, `e7c6_b1..b4` boundaries, `e7c6_b5_*` promotion/demotion, `e7c6_p1..p7` projection and wiring) plus 7 in-crate units in `journey.rs`/`domain.rs` — **plus the fold's 34 `vf_*` regressions in the same file (66 total)**. The legacy `@route` runtime is untouched and the two never fight over one object (`e7c6_p6_…`).

**Independent verify fold (2026-09-06, on `ef63def`+).** An independent adversarial verify over the three lenses (**C** conservation/consent/control, **R** restart/durability, **B** bounds/disclosure) found what this "As built" missed: 30 items after merging cross-lens duplicates (7 HIGH, 12 MEDIUM, 11 LOW), plus 6 deviations confirmed safe to defer and **ten false or unmet claims, all corrected in place above**. **Nothing was refuted.** Owner decisions taken, all five recommended defaults: **D1(a)** a terminal journey quarantines its root and `journeying_reason` widens to any journey record on an unplaced root (phrased over record presence, never a second under-way predicate), with the `advance_due` debug-assert added; **D2(a)** a new `SpatialState.in_transit_room` beside `recovery_room` (same shape, default `LIMBO`), answered from `graph::project` for an under-way root; **D3(a)** a revision-scoped `applied_revision` on `CarrierJourney` (`#[serde(default)]`, landed after R3), **plus** `advance_due`'s filter-before-take, with the reap itself deferred; **D4(ii)** the promotion row struck and re-issued against cut 8 as deviation (i), with the four-cell pin landed; **D5(b)** `/depart` and `/divert` stay staff-only while `/show`, `/list` and `/plan` open to a billet-holder, everyone else getting the byte-identical rule-4 `"No journey."`. One line per item; every fix carries a `vf_*` regression in `crates/mush-sim/tests/e7c6.rs`.

- **C1 (HIGH; merges C1+C2+C6) — the seal was one-armed.** `carrier_seal` had exactly one call site. Now `carrier_seal_with` runs on `Place`, on `Unplace` (which is the economy's `move_out` path — it skips this module's own carry hook by design, so the check must live in the transition) and on **both directions** of `CarrierMove`, with the moving root's own journey `exempt` so the ONE carrier path stays one. Mirrored as command refusals in `journey_ineligible` and in `refuse_unreachable_destination` (renamed from `refuse_self_carrying_destination`), which `/plan`, `/depart` and `/divert` all run, so a carrier never flies a passage it will be refused at the end of. `vf_c1a_…`, `vf_c1b_…`, `vf_c1c_…`.
- **B1/D2 (HIGH) — a departed carrier never left the room it departed from.** `graph::project`'s `Unplaced` arm answered `None` ("leave it where it is"), so the ship, its exposed mounts and the " sets out." narrative stayed in the origin room for the whole passage and it teleported out at arrival, with `verify_projections` silent. `SpatialState.in_transit_room` (default `LIMBO`, the engine's own nowhere-room) is now answered for an under-way root, the exposed-`Attached` arm follows a host that projects even while unplaced, and `journey_depart` inserts the record into the book **before** the move so the plan can see it (rolled back on a refusal). `vf_b1_a_departed_carrier_leaves_the_room` asserts the PROJECTED location and the room contents, not just the record.
- **C4/D1 (HIGH; merges B2 and both lenses' deviation-(f) items) — a stopped journey un-gated the ship entirely.** `fail()` left the root `Unplaced` and not quarantined while `Failed`/`Captured` are `is_under_way() == false`, so all three arms of `gate_reason` answered `None` at once and a non-staff player could `get` a crewed dropship. `fail()` now holds the root (`transition::set_carrier_root_quarantine`), refreshes the links in the same edit (C5), and `step` carries the same repair as a backstop for a terminal record a later cut writes directly. `vf_c4a_…` drives a real `carry_attempt`; `vf_c4b_…` covers the `Captured` arm.
- **C3/D3 (HIGH; merges R1) — the arrival heal never expired.** `CarrierJourney::applied_revision` scopes the claim: `step` heals only while the root's live placement revision is at or below the one the arrival landed at, so a rolled-back placement document (the s3 gap) still heals while a legitimate later placement ends the claim. `vf_c3_…`; `e7c6_s3` stays green.
- **R2 (HIGH) — `reanchor` restarted the in-flight leg.** Corrected above; `vf_r2_…` plus the amended `e7c6_s5`.
- **R3 (HIGH) — a wire-refused `cjourney:` document was durably deleted at boot.** Corrected above; `vf_r3_an_unreadable_carrier_journey_is_held_not_deleted` and the amended `e7c6_s7`. This had to land **before** any `CarrierJourneyWire` growth, which is why `applied_revision` arrives after it, with `#[serde(default)]`.
- **R4 (HIGH) — one world-IC rate change wedged every promoted domain forever.** `DomainSummary::new` returned `ClockMismatch` whenever the window's ends named different generations, so after any rate change every standing activation was un-demotable, its page recommended a generation that never comes back, the wedge rehydrated across every reboot and wedged domains accumulated against `MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS`. The IC axis is continuous across a generation, so `domain_demote` (and B3's new `release_activation`) normalise the opening stamp onto the closing generation at the ONE construction site — a documented cross-generation window, with the elapsed IC figure exact. `vf_r4_…`.
- **C5 (MEDIUM) — `fail` never refreshed the links,** so a stopped journey's crew kept a live `ChannelGrant` on a root `controls_are_live` calls dead, until some unrelated edit silently dropped it. `vf_c5_…`. The C12 census stays **nine**: this was a missing call, not a new consumer.
- **C7 (MEDIUM) — a journey arrival into a vault-backed `sim.interior` was an unjournaled deposit** of the ship and everyone aboard (and the departure the mirror withdrawal), because `InteriorCustodyProvider::contents` derives stock purely from the placement graph. `arrival_parent` now refuses such a destination with a **plain read** — `ctx.w.module_state::<mush_economy::EconomyStore>()` then `snapshot().is_vault_backing(space)`, the pattern `mush-housing` already uses over the pre-existing E3c edge. The verify's proposed `Fn(ObjId) -> bool` composition oracle was **not** taken: it adds a parameter the edge already makes unnecessary. Docking into a vault wants a real deposit ACT, which is a write across that edge and therefore a later cut's. `vf_c7_…`.
- **C8 (MEDIUM; merges B5) — the cap bounded the RESULT, not the walk.** Corrected above; `vf_c8_place_is_capped_too` and the extended `e7c6_t9` (the refusal's `count` is at most `MAX_CARRIER_SUBTREE + 1`, which is the proof the walk stopped). Refuse-never-truncate is unchanged, and C1's fix removes the only in-game route by which a sealed subtree could grow past the cap mid-leg.
- **R5 (MEDIUM) — boot installed the activation book without re-evaluating the queue,** so a slot freed at boot starved the queued domains until the next `@domain/promote|demote`. `install_domain_workflow` now takes `&mut Ctx`, drops activations for domains that are no longer Active (loudly), drains the queue and persists exactly what came off it. `vf_r5_…`.
- **R6 (MEDIUM) — the crash-gap heal was one-directional.** Corrected above; `vf_r6_a_re_derived_arrival_still_raises_the_onboard_floors`, in the production module order (`ScenesModule` before `SimulationModule`). No second reconcile pass was added — the same call, correctly ordered.
- **R7 (MEDIUM; merges B4) — the page recommended a command the same code path refused.** Corrected above; `vf_c4c_…`.
- **R8 (MEDIUM) — terminal journeys ate the visit budget.** `advance_due` now filters before it takes: a terminal, coherent record costs no `visited` slot, so a live boundary is detected on the first fire rather than up to `ceil(N/64)` seconds late. The reap itself is deferred by a bounded grace (D3), and deviation (h) still has a record to read. `vf_r8_…`.
- **R9 (MEDIUM; merges B9 and both lenses' deviation-(g) items) — the arrival event was wrong three ways.** Corrected above; `vf_r9a_the_boot_arrival_emits_no_world_event` and `vf_r9b_the_arrival_event_names_where_it_came_from`, driven through a probe module composed **after** `mush-sim`.
- **R10 (MEDIUM) — every replayed arrival paged a false scene anomaly,** because the identical floor raise is refused `NotForward`. A `NotForward` whose floor is already at or past the stamp is now a HIT at `debug`; `AheadOfWorld` and `AlreadySealed` still page, which are the two arms that genuinely need a person. `vf_r10_…`.
- **B3 (MEDIUM; lands with R5) — a retired or paused domain kept its tactical slot forever,** and could not even be re-promoted to clear it. `domain_retire` and `domain_set_status(Paused)` now call `release_activation` (summary first, then the activation delete, then `flush_dequeued`), and boot drops an activation whose domain is gone or not Active. `vf_b3_…`.
- **B6 (MEDIUM) — GROUNDED, THEN DISCHARGED BY CUT 7.** `@journey/depart`'s `<route id>` was only a label: `build_route` fabricated a fresh `RouteDef` at the `manual` drive class with zero declared propellant and no alignment band, so `RouteDef::propellant()`, `alignment_band()` and `route_duration` had no cut-6 caller and deviations (a)/(d) read narrower than they are. The verify's fix ("resolve the id against cut 1's pack, which needs no edge") **cannot be built**: the authored pack store is `ruleset_tacsim::store`, and `ruleset-tacsim` depends on `mush-sim`, so the lookup would be a cycle. **E7 cut 7 built the composition-installed catalog instead**, in the `SimulationModule::with_accounts` / `IndustryModule::with_recipes` shape: `mush_sim::RouteCatalog` (`crates/mush-sim/src/route.rs`, a bounded read-only `resolve(&World, &CanonicalId) -> Option<RouteDef>` with `MAX_ROUTE_ID_BYTES` 64), carried into world state as `RouteCatalogState` by `SimulationModule::with_route_catalog`, implemented over `TacsimStore::route` at the composition root in `crates/mush-server/src/sim_routes.rs` and wired in `server_modules_with`. `build_route` offers the id to the catalog **only when the line carries no place tokens**, and refuses a route whose declared origin is not where the carrier stands; a hand-typed id that names no authored route still builds the manual fixture route, so every cut-6 corridor test is unchanged. `vf_b6_…` is renamed `vf_b6_an_authored_route_id_resolves_and_an_unknown_one_still_falls_back` and now pins **both arms**, asserting the authored route's `propellant()` / `alignment_band()` / `drive_class()` and the carrier's **projected** arrival at the authored destination — the observable outcome, not the record. `mush-sim`'s edge set is unchanged (the catalog is a trait, not an edge) and `cargo tree -p mush-sim --depth 1` is byte-identical.
- **B10 (MEDIUM) — `@journey` had no row in the production-composition compatibility pin.** Added as a ROW in `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility`'s sim-lane loop beside `@station` — not a new stanza; `PINNED_BASELINE` stays **158**.
- **C9/D5 (LOW) — there was no consent model at all.** `/depart` and `/divert` stay staff-only; `/show`, `/list` and `/plan` now answer for an actor who `holds_billet` on the carrier (cut 5's predicate, reused), with the byte-identical `"No journey."` for everyone else and `/list` filtered to the reader's own ships. **Recorded deviation:** crew-driven DEPART — a billeted commander sailing their own ship — is **cut 10's** (crew, proxy seats, musters, `force.assign`). `vf_c9_…`.
- **C10 (LOW, was PLAUSIBLE — walked through and fixed).** Nothing in cut 6 constructs a `Cruising` record whose root is placed, but nothing repaired one either: `step` heals only the `Arrived` direction, so a hand-edited or migrated document left a ship permanently sealed and permanently un-movable with no anomaly and no page. `install_carrier_journeys` now re-unplaces such a root through the ONE checked carrier move, and `fail()`s that journey if it cannot. `vf_c10_…`.
- **C19/D4 (LOW → decision) — an unrecorded deviation,** now **deviation (i)** below, with `vf_c1e_the_promoted_cell_is_pinned` asserting the outcome in all four cells.
- **B7 (LOW) — `CarrierJourney::new`'s `MAX_LEGS` arm was unreachable dead code.** `RouteDef`'s fields are private and `RouteDef::new` is its only constructor, so the cap is `RouteDef`'s and is enforced twice inside `RouteDefWire::try_from`. The arm is now a `debug_assert!` so it cannot be mistaken for a bound the journey record owns, and the three notes that advertised it are reworded.
- **B8 (LOW) — no cap on the journey census,** and every 1 s fire allocated a Vec of every root. `MAX_CARRIER_JOURNEYS` = 512 is refused at `@journey/depart`, and the fire now walks a wrapped `BTreeMap::range` taking at most `MAX_JOURNEYS_VISITED_PER_FIRE`. `vf_b8_…`.
- **R11 (LOW) — the rotation cursor was not persisted,** so every reboot restarted the rotation at the head of the book and re-starved its tail. It rides the `journey_meta` document that already exists (`rotation_cursor`, read back with a 0 default). `vf_r11_…`.
- **R12 (LOW) — `persist_journey` used `expect` inside the tick and boot handlers,** contradicting the module's own no-panic header. All three persistence helpers (`persist_journey`, `persist_activation`, `persist_summary`) now page; a summary that will not serialize leaves the domain PROMOTED rather than demoted-without-summary. `vf_r12_…`.
- **R13 (LOW) — catch-up collapsed every missed boundary into one advance.** `step` now advances exactly ONE boundary per slice, charging one `worked` slot each, so a downtime observes every boundary once and the existing budget bounds the catch-up rate — landed now rather than when cut 7/9 hangs an interception or an alignment-band advance on a boundary. `vf_r13_…`.

**The six deviations, confirmed safe to defer.** (a) **propellant is not reserved** — the record carries no `ReservationId` at all, so there is nothing to hydrate, leak or strand; two preconditions recorded: R3 had to land first (a new field on a `deny_unknown_fields` wire), and when it lands it hooks in `journey_depart` **between the destination refusal and `move_carrier_subtree`**, so a refused reservation refuses before the first placement mutation. Note B6: today there is not even a *declared* figure to reserve against. (b) **the alignment band is inert and unwired** — `alignment_band: None` at the one authoring site and `advance_cadence_manual` has zero call sites under `crates/mush-sim/src/`; pinned by `vf_db_the_alignment_band_is_inert`. When it is wired, C3, R6 and R2 must already be fixed (they are) and the effect must ride the same `Outbox` batch as the cursor write, idempotent per boundary. (c) **`/plan` is a dry run** — re-read in full and confirmed: no `ModuleOp`, no allocator bump (`vf_dc_plan_bumps_no_allocator`). One caveat recorded: a passing `/plan` is not a promise, because the conditions that become a stranding all arise after it. (d) **leg boundaries come straight from `LegDef::base_s`** — and that purity is what makes the leg cursor a key a reboot re-derives identically, so deferring the pack lookup *removes* a replay hazard; when a drive profile lands, its resolved seconds must be **frozen into the record at `/depart`**, never looked up at tick time (`vf_dd_…`). (e) **`DomainSummary` carries counts, not lines** — the durability property the plan asked for (summary before delete, one batch) holds exactly (`vf_de_…`); two caveats: the future fields need `#[serde(default)]`, and `units` is **fog-sensitive** — it is byte-identical for unobserved/nonexistent only because the whole `@domain` verb is staff-gated today, so the cut that opens a summary to players must gate it on participation. (f) folded into C4/D1 above. (g) **promoted to fix** under R9 above. (h) **no `sim.journey.arrived|failed` FactProvider arms** — the seam is named rather than built: cut 7 or 8 adds the arm to cut 5's existing `sim.*` provider (no second namespace), derived from the same `JourneyState` predicate rather than re-deriving "arrived" from placement, and cuts 8/9 must **poll** `JourneyBook` from a world-side tick or observer because a `prepare_authoritative` consumer can reach no legacy `reg.state` slot. Pinned from that angle by `vf_dh_an_arrived_journey_emits_no_further_sim_op`.

**(i) NEW deviation — launch-once-promoted is cut 8's.** `JourneyState::Promoted { .. }` is `is_under_way() == true`, so `carrier_seal` takes the identical branch for `Promoted` as for `Cruising`, and **nothing anywhere in cut 6 writes `Promoted`** — its first writer is cut 8's tactical domain authority. The plan's test row is struck above and re-issued against cut 8; `vf_c1e_the_promoted_cell_is_pinned` asserts the chosen outcome in all four cells (`Place` × `@journey/depart`, `Cruising` × `Promoted`), so the row can never again be neither built nor recorded.

**Fold gates.** `cargo fmt --all --check` clean; `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -j 6 -- -D warnings` clean; `cargo test -p mush-sim -p mush-scenes -p mush-core -p mush-server -p mush-contract-tests` green; `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast -j 6` **3890 passed / 0 failed / 62 ignored across 239 suites** (3856 → 3890, exactly the 34 added). All the K8 CI invocations green (`k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` with B10's new `@journey` row, `schedule_source`, `provider_rng_policy`, `truth_serialization_policy`, `legacy_handler_allowlist`, `ci_gated_test_inventory`, `gate5_fault_matrix`, `sheet_write_republish`). `cargo tree -p mush-sim --depth 1` is **byte-identical** — no fix added an edge — and cut 6 still registers no `revision_validator`, no `state_family`, no `schedule_source`, no second `FactProvider` namespace and no `WorldEvent` variant. `PINNED_BASELINE` **158**, the `ChannelGrant` census **nine**, `move_carrier_subtree` still the ONE checked carrier path with the carrier-root invariant stated exactly once inside it, and `JourneyState::is_under_way()` still the ONE predicate the seal, the move/carry gate, the links and the tick share.

**Hands to cut 7:** a live domain with a stable `ClockRef::DomainTick` identity for propagation to run on, and journey legs for coarse scheduled delivery to run between — **nothing else**. Plus two items the fold hands forward explicitly: the composition-installed **route catalog** B6 needs — **discharged by cut 7**, see B6 above and §3.7's "As built" — and the typed `CarrierArrived` event R9 defers, which cut 7 also did **not** add (it emits no `WorldEvent` at all).


### 3.7 Cut 7 — `mush-propagation`: one context, two resolvers

**Q4A/R4.** One crate owns **contacts and delivery envelopes over a shared physical propagation context, with separate resolvers**. 10a's 4C (both inside `mush-sim`) was cheaper; Q4A wins because propagation has consumers with no spatial dependency (doc 10 §18.2).

```rust
struct PropagationContext { domain: Option<DomainId>, geometry: Geometry, medium: Medium,
                            interference: Interference, at: ClockStamp }
// NO `ContactRecord`. The contact lifecycle record is K6's `ContactState`
// (`crates/mush-core/src/contact.rs`), owned and mutated only by `mush-tactics`' `ContactProvider`
// actions (`contact_observe / refresh / share / unshare / loss / reacquire`). Propagation produces
// bounded, deterministic OBSERVATION INTENTS and submits them through the trusted coordinator
// ingress (08i answer 1):
struct ObservationIntent { observer: ObserverRef,                   // Q16A: a platform or station,
                           truth: SubjectKey,                       //      never a side, never the world
                           op: ObservationOp, band: BandId, revealed: BTreeSet<AttrId>,
                           at: ClockStamp, key: IdempotencyKey /* Deterministic over (observer, truth, tick) */ }
enum ObservationOp { Observe, Refresh, Loss, Reacquire, Share { to: ObserverRef, via: EnvelopeId },
                     Unshare { from: ObserverRef } }                // one tactics action each
// Subjects: a real unit is `SubjectKey::Object { id }` and its truth stays private to tactics
// (an UNIDENTIFIED contact already works this way). A FALSE contact — artefact, decoy, spoof — is an
// authoritative PHANTOM subject owned by propagation: `SubjectKey::Scoped { owner: propagation,
// class: "phantom", id }`, so the family never carries a subject-less contact. A genuinely
// TRUTHLESS contact is `ContactState` v2, K's, and out of E7.
struct Phantom { id: IdempotencyKeyText, cause: FalseCause, created: ClockStamp, expires: ClockStamp }
// Provenance and confidence are a SIDE PROJECTION keyed by `ContactId` with no lifecycle authority
// (a K-approved `ContactState` v2 field set, or a propagation side family — decided at SP2):
struct ContactProvenance { contact: ContactId, band: BandId,
                           provenance: Provenance, generation: u64 }
enum Provenance { Local, Reported { source: ObserverRef, age: i64, via: EnvelopeId } }
struct Envelope { id: EnvelopeId, issuer: ObjId, recipient: RecipientRef, payload_ref: PayloadRef,
                  revision: u32, state: EnvelopeState, path: Vec<HopRef> }
enum EnvelopeState { Queued, Transmitted, Delivered, Acknowledged, Expired, Superseded }
```

**Why there is no `ContactRecord`** (08i answer 1, overriding 08i §6.1's "propagation registers its own family"). `mush-tactics` remains the **sole `ContactState` authority**; a second contact-lifecycle family would be two authoritative implementations of one migrated action, which 09 §7 forbids. Propagation owns the *ingress code* and the *phantom subjects*; the family, its validator (`ContactGuardValidator`) and its lifecycle rules are K's. The ingress is the trusted coordinator path — a `Registrar::schedule_source` adapter whose drafts enter `scheduled_action_ingress` under the `"scheduler"` principal class with deterministic keys, staged by `ActionCoordinator::prepare_and_stage_scheduled` — and every intent is **bounded** (`MAX_OBSERVATIONS_PER_PASS`, §7) and **deterministic** (same context, same set, same order). A cross-owner key is refused by `ActionTxn::checked_key`, which is what makes "propagation cannot forge tactics' state" a kernel property rather than a convention.

**One context, two resolvers — not one reachability step** (Q4A). They share geometry, medium, range, occlusion, interference and time-of-flight, and answer different questions. **Detection** resolves signature, emissions posture, medium, line of sight, sensor capability, noise floor and observer posture. **Delivery** resolves addressing, transmitter/receiver compatibility, routing, relays, encryption, jamming and store-and-forward. R4's two cases are *a jammed unit that still takes orders* and *a courier intercepted but never detected*. Contacts project as `PerceivedRef::Contact { provider, id, generation }` over the kernel's `ContactId` — produced by `mush-tactics`' `ContactState` family, which propagation *feeds* (above); Draft 0.2's "vocabulary with no producer until now" is struck. Unheld contacts already collapse to one unavailable result in `Engine::resolve_legal_action`, so the fog rule below starts from the kernel's behaviour, not against it.

**Contacts are local; sharing is a delivery** (Q16A). A transmitted report creates or updates the **recipient observer's own** `ContactState` entry — an `ObservationOp::Share` intent, i.e. tactics' `contact_share`, submitted only when the envelope carrying it is `Delivered` — with source, age, confidence and provenance on the side projection, so a stale relayed track is visibly stale and a false report is attributable. **There is no omniscient fused record anywhere.** **Unidentified** contacts exist from the start because the family already keeps truth private; **false** contacts exist from the start as propagation-owned phantoms (`SubjectKey::Scoped`), because retrofitting them would change every consumer's assumption that a contact has a subject. A **truthless** contact — one with no subject at all — is deferred to K's `ContactState` v2 and no E7 cut depends on it.

**Orders require delivery** (T15). Issuing revision *N* does not make it the recipient's active order. The recipient obeys the **newest valid revision it has actually received**; under ECM it continues its last order and fallback doctrine while the commander sees the replacement unacknowledged. On restored reachability, deliver the **newest valid superseding order**, not an obsolete backlog. **An acknowledgement is itself a delivery** that must reach the issuer (Q4A) — which is *why* the commander can see a replacement unacknowledged. Ordinary jamming blocks or delays; content corruption and deception require an explicit electronic-warfare result and are later.

**Cadence** (Q4A). Tactical propagation runs on the **domain tick** (cut 8). With no domain active, interplanetary mail, market reports and couriers run a **coarse scheduled delivery pass on a campaign cadence**.

**Commands.** `+contacts` · `+contact <id>` · `+order <recipient>=<order>` · `+orders` (issued, with envelope state) · `+orders/ack <id>` · `@prop/envelopes <subject>` · `@prop/pass` (staff).

**Tests (~28).** *Fog:* a projected contact never carries truth beyond `revealed_attributes`; a recursive leak check over every player- and AI-facing serialization finds no `ObjId` of an undetected unit (doc 09 Gate 3); one observer's record is invisible to another on the same side; a `False` contact renders identically to a real one; error messages do not disambiguate; **timing does not** (a detection miss costs the same work as a hit). *Provenance:* a relayed report carries source, age and `via`; relaying a relay increases age monotonically and bounds hops at `MAX_ENVELOPE_HOPS`; a report from a destroyed observer still renders with its age; a false report is attributable to its transmitter. *Delivery:* revision 3 delivered after 2 supersedes; revision 2 arriving after 3 is `Superseded` and never becomes active; a jammed recipient keeps its last delivered order and fallback doctrine; restored reachability delivers only the newest valid revision, not the backlog; expiry is idempotent. *Ack:* the issuer sees `Transmitted` until the ack itself arrives; an ack lost in transit leaves the order `Delivered` and the issuer uninformed — a probe asserts the commander's view says exactly that; a duplicate ack is a HIT. *Cadence:* with no domain active a courier resolves on the campaign pass; promoting a domain mid-flight moves that leg onto the domain tick without duplicating or dropping it. *Bounds:* `MAX_CONTACTS_PER_OBSERVER`, `CONTACT_TTL`, `ENVELOPE_TTL` and payload size refuse rather than grow. *Restart:* every envelope state hydrates; a `Transmitted` envelope in flight at restart is neither delivered twice nor lost. *Added by the review:* a relay **cycle** (A→B→A) terminates at `MAX_ENVELOPE_HOPS` and does not grow age without bound; the delivery pass is **panic-safe and its bound is paged, not logged** — E4c's V6 silenced every economic event in a process after four panics inside a bounded observer, and a propagation pass has the same shape; an envelope whose recipient object is destroyed mid-flight `Expire`s and is attributed, rather than being retried forever; a `False` contact and a real one cost the **same work and the same number of store reads**, so neither timing nor I/O count is an oracle; an ack for a **superseded** revision does not mark the newest one acknowledged; a payload over the size cap is refused at issue, not truncated at delivery; every `Envelope`, `Phantom` and `ContactProvenance` record re-enters through its constructor (§4) and a stored set over `MAX_CONTACTS_PER_OBSERVER` refuses on load. *Added by Draft 0.3 (one contact authority):* a probe asserts **no E7 crate registers a `ContactState` family or a `Contact` guard validator** — `mush-tactics` is the only owner; an observation intent with a key scoped to another owner is refused by `ActionTxn::checked_key`; a detection pass over one context produces the same ordered intent set twice and never more than `MAX_OBSERVATIONS_PER_PASS`; a phantom's `SubjectKey::Scoped` never resolves to a unit through any projection, and its expiry issues `Loss` to every holder; `contact_share` is submitted only after the carrying envelope is `Delivered` (a `Transmitted` envelope shares nothing).

**Verify lenses.** **K (knowledge leak)** — can any viewer, render, error or timing difference reveal an undetected unit or another observer's picture? **O (order lifecycle)** — can a stale order become active, a superseded one execute, or an ack be inferred without arriving? **B (bounds and restart)** — can contacts or envelopes grow without limit, or change state across a crash?

**Hands to cut 8:** the per-tick detection and delivery passes the pulse driver calls, and the `ContactState` entries (`PerceivedRef::Contact`) tactical intents target.

#### 3.7 As built (2026-09-06, on `48eda83`) — the ingress is TACTICS-owned, and there is still exactly one contact authority

**The one binding constraint held, and the kernel is what held it.** `mush-tactics` remains the sole `ContactState` authority. Cut 7 registers **no** second contact family, **no** second `AuthorityGuardKind::Contact` validator and **no** second lifecycle; `mush-propagation` does not even hold a `StateFamilyHandle` on the contact family, so `ActionTxn::checked_key` never has to refuse it. `e7c7_a1_only_mush_tactics_has_a_contact_guard_validator_not_propagation_sim_or_industry` asks the composed `AuthorityGuardValidatorRegistry` directly, with every owner that could hold a second authority enumerated in the name.

**Contradiction (i) resolved: §3.7's `mush-propagation` `schedule_source` registrant is UNBUILDABLE, and the ingress is tactics'.** Three fences in the tree, all re-read on this commit: `ScheduleSourceRegistryBuilder::register` (`crates/mush-core/src/schedule_source.rs`) refuses a registration whose schedule family owner or fired-action owner is not the registering module (`ScheduleSourceRegistryError::OwnerMismatch`); `Registrar::schedule_source` (`crates/mush-core/src/module.rs`) stamps the owner from the registering module; `scheduled_action_ingress` refuses a draft whose advance owner is not the fired action's owner (`ScheduleActionIngressError::OwnerMismatch`). The six `contact_*` actions are `mush-tactics`-owned, so a propagation-owned source **cannot fire them**. OPEN 1's recommended default is therefore what was built, in `crates/mush-tactics/src/sensor.rs`:

- **`mush_core::SensorPolicy`** (`crates/mush-core/src/sensor.rs`) — the trait, in **core**, so `mush-tactics` gains no `mush-propagation` edge. With it: `ObserverRef::{Platform, Station}` (Q16A — **no `Side` arm and no `World` arm exist to write an omniscient record with**), `ObservationKind` (six variants, one per migrated action, no seventh), `ObservationOp`, `ObservationIntent` (one constructor, a wire that re-runs it, and a key that must name its own kind), `bounded_pass`, `SensorPolicyHandle` (unwired arms nothing — fail closed) and the constants `MAX_OBSERVATIONS_PER_PASS` 256 / `MAX_CONTACTS_PER_OBSERVER` 128 / `CONTACT_TTL` 30 / `MAX_INTENT_AUDIENCE` 8.
- **`TacticsModule::with_sensor_policy(Arc<dyn SensorPolicy>)`** — the composition seam, wired at `server_modules_with` in the `with_recipes` / `with_accounts` shape.
- **The two halves of the ingress.** A durable schedule can be created only inside an authoritative bundle, and none of the six contact actions can bootstrap another's cursor, so the ingress is `handoff.rs`'s shape in two halves. **Arming** is `sensor_arm` — a tactics-owned authoritative action, offered to **staff only** (`module::sensor_arm_is_permitted`), which writes one **one-shot** `DurableSchedule` plus one `SensorJob` per intent. **It holds no `ContactState` handle at all**, which is a structural fact rather than a convention. **Firing** is six `reg.schedule_source` registrations — `sensor_observe|refresh|share|unshare|loss|reacquire` over six schedule families `sensor_*_schedules`, one source per family per owner, every id disjoint from `control_handoff`, `production` and `npc` (`e7c7_a4_…`, plus the unit pin in `sensor.rs`).
- **`SensorScheduleAdapter::plan`** re-derives the due observation from the composition-supplied policy and **prefers the policy's live parameters** when the policy still names the armed key; when it no longer does, the durably armed intent stands (arming was an authoritative decision) and the kernel's own revision check decides whether it may still apply. It also pre-checks the live `ContactState` and refuses `Stale` for a contact that is gone — `handoff.rs`'s pre-check exactly.
- **The leak-safe contact ledger** — `mush_core::{ContactLedgerRow, ContactLedgerState}`, published by `mush-tactics`' own `tactics.contact_ledger` Coarse tick from a bounded paged scan of the contact family. `Ctx::scan_action_state` is owner-fenced, so **only the family's owner can read it**; without this projection a policy in another crate could name no `ContactId` at all and could therefore only ever arm an `Observe`. A row carries the holder, the opaque id, the counters the holder already has a `PerceivedRef` for, and the **phantom id iff the subject is propagation's own `SubjectKey::Scoped`** — a real unit's `SubjectKey::Object` never produces a `Some`, so no undetected unit's id crosses. `MAX_CONTACT_LEDGER_ROWS` 1_024, paged not truncated. **False as shipped (O6):** `install` set `paging` and then `truncate`d, `is_paging()` had no reader anywhere in the tree, `for_observer` was uncalled because `commands.rs` re-implemented it across the *combined* holder set, and `ledger_tick`'s own overflow guard was overwritten two lines later — so a contact past row 1_024 was invisible in its own holder's `+contacts`, never refreshed and never lost. The independent verify fold makes the window **fair per holder** (no holder may occupy more than `MAX_CONTACTS_PER_OBSERVER` rows), makes the guard stop the scan, gives `is_paging()` a reader, and has `commands.rs` call `for_observer` per holder.

**`mush-propagation`, the crate.** `crates/mush-propagation/` with `context.rs`, `record.rs`, `pass.rs`, `policy.rs`, `commands.rs`, `lib.rs`.

- **`context.rs` — one context, two resolvers** (Q4A, D-E7-14). `PropagationContext { domain, geometry: Geometry { near, far, range, occluded }, medium: Medium::{Vacuum, Interior, Surface}, interference: Interference { noise_floor, jamming }, at }`, built read-only off `SpatialState` through `place_of` (the placement graph's projection first, the object's `Obj.location` as the fallback, `None` for a thing that is nowhere). `resolve_detection(&ctx, signature)` reads signature, occlusion and the noise floor and **never reads `jamming`**; `resolve_delivery(&ctx)` reads addressing, hops and jamming and **never reads occlusion**. That is R4's two cases made structural: `e7c7_p6_a_jammed_platform_is_still_detected_and_an_occluded_one_still_receives` drives both. Both outcomes carry the **same fields and the same lag** either way, so neither the shape nor the timing of an answer is an oracle (`e7c7_k5_…`). Every term saturates.
- **`record.rs` — the exempt records.** `Phantom { id, cause: FalseCause::{Artefact,Decoy,Spoof}, created, expires, holders }` at `phantom:<id>`; `Envelope { id, issuer, recipient, payload, revision, state, path, issued, advanced, ack_of, shares }` at `envelope:<id>`; `ContactProvenance { contact, band, provenance: Provenance::{Local, Reported}, generation, recorded }` at `provenance:<hex>`; `Sighting { observer, subject, phantom, first, last }` at `sighting:<digest>`; the allocator at `propagation_meta`. Every one has a private-field constructor and a wire that re-runs it (§4 rule 1) — **`Sighting` did not, until the independent verify fold (B10): it had public fields, a derived `Deserialize` and no constructor, so a `subject: null, phantom: null` document loaded and folded every such row for one observer onto one key. Its key was also the observer→subject truth mapping in plaintext (K13), and is now a domain-separated `propagation.sighting.v1` digest**, so a payload past `MAX_ENVELOPE_PAYLOAD_BYTES` 512, a path past `MAX_ENVELOPE_HOPS` 4, a non-canonical holder set or a negative reported age is **refused on load, never truncated** (`e7c7_b7_…` and the in-crate `every_record_re_enters_through_its_constructor`). A document that will not decode is **HELD, not deleted** — cut 6's R3 lesson, since the record is the only place the fact lives — and boot says so loudly. **Understated as shipped (B7):** the notice was a `tracing::warn!` naming `@prop/envelopes`, which reads `store.envelopes()` and therefore cannot show a held document at all, and `held` had no cap, so the four censuses bounded the books and nothing bounded the process. The independent verify fold caps it at `MAX_HELD_DOCUMENTS` 64, counts the overflow, pages the count through the store's own notice queue, and adds `@prop/held`, which names the key, the kind and the decode error. Census bounds: `MAX_ENVELOPES` 4_096, `MAX_PHANTOMS` 1_024, `MAX_PROVENANCE` 4_096, `MAX_SIGHTINGS` 4_096.
- **`Phantom::subject()` is the no-launder proof.** It returns `SubjectKey::Scoped { owner: "propagation", class: "phantom", id }` and **there is no code path from a phantom to an `ObjId`** — the type has no object field, the constructor takes none, and the serialized form contains no `"object"` tag. `e7c7_k1_a_phantom_subject_never_resolves_to_a_unit_through_any_projection` asserts the subject **and** the rendered contact line a holder actually sees.
- **`pass.rs` — the two functions cut 8 calls.** `delivery_pass(&mut store, &state, &world, &now) -> DeliveryReport` and `detection_pass(&mut store, &state, &world, &ledger, &now) -> Vec<ObservationIntent>`. Bounded on records **visited** (`MAX_ENVELOPES_PER_PASS` 64, `MAX_OBSERVERS_PER_PASS` 32, `MAX_SUBJECTS_PER_OBSERVER` 32; D-LBK-5), deterministic (`e7c7_a3_…` runs one context twice and compares the ordered key list, then runs it a third time and asserts a second pass arms nothing), and **paged rather than logged** when a bound binds — `@prop/pass` prints "the pass stopped at its bound … run @prop/pass again", which `e7c7_b8_…` pins (E4c V6). **Both halves of that claim were false as shipped, and the independent verify fold made them true** (K7, B3): detection truncated by `ObjId` order with no cursor and booked a sighting for every pair it *detected* rather than every pair it *armed*, so the paged remainder was permanently unreachable and the second run answered "nothing due"; delivery had no resume cursor, so a jammed head starved everything behind it and "run @prop/pass again to continue" did the same work again. Both passes now carry a **durable rotation cursor** and report where they stopped. No `?`, no `unwrap`, no `expect` and no panicking index after the first mutation.
- **`policy.rs`** — `PropagationSensorPolicy`, holding nothing, reading `PropagationStore` + `SpatialState` + `ContactLedgerState` from the world and answering the same ordered set twice.
- **`commands.rs`** — the one internal view (`render_contact`, `render_envelope`) every projection is built from, and the six verbs.

**The knowledge rule, and how it is proved.** A contact is one actor's knowledge. `viewable_holders` is the whole of the read gate: the actor itself plus every unit it holds a control station on (cut 5's `assignments_by_actor`, re-used, not re-derived) — **Q16A exactly, with no argument that widens it**. `e7c7_k3_one_observers_contact_is_invisible_to_another_and_the_two_refusals_are_identical` shows Ann's contact and Bob's empty list, then asserts that "held by another" and "does not exist" are the **same bytes**; `e7c7_k2_…` asserts a phantom-backed row and a real row render identically apart from the opaque id; `e7c7_k4_…` is the recursive `ObjId` leak scan over every player-facing render; `e7c7_k6_…` pins `@prop/envelopes` byte-identical for an unviewable and a nonexistent subject (§4 rule 4).

**Orders (contradiction (iv) resolved).** §3.7's "an `ObservationOp::Share` … submitted only when the envelope is `Delivered`" spans two owners, and OPEN 4's default is what was built: the **envelope** is propagation's and the **share** is tactics'. An `Envelope` carries `shares: Option<ContactId>` (written by `+order/report`); `detection_pass` emits a `Share` intent **only for a `Delivered`** report, and the tactics ingress commits it. `e7c7_o8_a_share_fires_only_after_delivered_and_a_queued_envelope_shares_nothing` drives both halves. The rest of T15: revision N is not the recipient's order until it arrives (`e7c7_o1`), an older revision arriving after a newer one is `Superseded` **on arrival and never active** (`e7c7_o2`), a jammed recipient keeps its last delivered order (`e7c7_o3`), restored reachability delivers the newest valid revision and supersedes the backlog (`e7c7_o4`), **an acknowledgement is itself a delivery** so a lost ack leaves the order `Delivered` and the issuer uninformed (`e7c7_o5`, which asserts the issuer's own rendered listing before and after), a duplicate ack is a HIT (`e7c7_o6`), a relay cycle terminates at `MAX_ENVELOPE_HOPS` (`e7c7_o7`), expiry is idempotent (`e7c7_o9`) and an over-size payload is refused **at issue** (`e7c7_o10`).

**Loss and compaction (hazard axis 3).** A phantom's expiry issues `Loss` to **every** holder, read from the ledger's own `phantom` column (`e7c7_b1_…` asserts one Loss per holder, by holder id). A report from a **destroyed observer** still renders with its age (`e7c7_b2_…`). An envelope whose recipient is destroyed mid-flight **`Expire`s and is attributed** and is not retried (`e7c7_b3_…`, which also asserts the second pass changes nothing). A contact past `CONTACT_TTL` is lost and one merely stale is refreshed — **both arms named in the test** (`e7c7_b4_…`).

**Restart.** Every envelope state hydrates exactly once and the allocator never re-uses an id (`e7c7_b5_…`); a queued/in-flight envelope at restart is **neither delivered twice nor lost**, and the declared interference hydrates with it (`e7c7_b6_…`).

**The commands and their lanes (B7).** `+contacts` · `+contact <id>` — **ReadOnly**; `+order <recipient>=<order>` / `+order/report <recipient>=<contact>` · `+orders` (`/ack <id>`) · `@prop/envelopes|pass|phantom|jam|held` — **`Authoritative`**, the ceiling class of its switches: `/pass` stages the tactics-owned `sensor_arm` through `Ctx::submit_authoritative`, which is an ingress adapter for the authoritative action coordinator and therefore `Authoritative`'s own definition. It published `LegacyPostImage` as shipped, against the ceiling rule the tree states for `@station` (B8); the independent verify fold moved the class, the three pins and the allowlist **category** together, leaving the stanza count, its position and `PINNED_BASELINE` 158 untouched. `classify_commands` is called **per registration group before the first `reg.command`** and `LegacyPostImage` is restored after (K8.5); `e7c7_p1_…` pins each verb's recorded class locally and `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` pins it in the production composition. `@prop`'s staff gate covers **`/pass`, `/phantom` and `/jam`**, every arm enumerated in `e7c7_p2_the_prop_staff_gate_covers_pass_phantom_and_jam_and_an_unknown_switch_refuses`, and an unknown switch **refuses** rather than falling through to the read.

**Allowlist and pins.** Five stanzas in `crates/mush-contract-tests/legacy-handlers.toml`, in byte order re-derived on this tree: `+contact` and `+contacts` between `+condition` and `+damage`; `+order` and `+orders` between `+obfuscate` and `+paradox`; `@prop` before `@property`. The two contact-viewing verbs name **no** future family and say why — the record is K6's `ContactState` and `mush-tactics` owns it. `PINNED_BASELINE` is **untouched at 158** and no baseline entry changed. The module-name pin in `server_modules_register_without_collision` (and the three other composition pins beside it) gains `"propagation"` immediately after `"tactics"`.

**The edge set.** `mush-propagation -> { mush-core, mush-sim }` and nothing else — in particular **no `mush-tactics` edge**: the policy seam is a core trait, and the one tactics action `@prop/pass` stages is resolved through the definition registry by `(owner, kind, id)` strings. `cargo tree -p mush-tactics --depth 1` is **byte-identical to cut 6's**. `mush-server` gains one dependency (`mush-propagation`); `mush-sim` gains none.

**The five OPEN decisions, as finally taken.** (1) **Taken as recommended, and the kernel forced it**: the sensor ingress is registered inside `mush-tactics::register` under tactics' owner, with propagation supplying a `SensorPolicy` through `TacticsModule::with_sensor_policy`; the trait is `mush-core`'s. (2) **Taken as recommended**: `propagation.provenance` is a side family keyed by `ContactId` with **no lifecycle authority** — it never gates legality, no provider reads it, and §4.1's closing rule is the tell if that ever changes. **Still flagged OPEN for K review.** (3) **Taken with the mechanism changed** — see deviation (a). (4) **Taken as recommended**: the `Delivered` envelope's share is re-derived read-only by the detection pass and handed to the tactics ingress; `envelope` advancement never writes `ContactState`. (5) **Taken as recommended**: `propagation.phantom` is the exempt family, minting a `SubjectKey::Scoped` that never resolves to a unit, with `Loss` issued to every holder on expiry. **Reachable only through an `install_ledger`-driven test as shipped (O7), and `drop_phantom` had no caller at all (B1/K12).** The independent verify fold gives the phantom→contact path a producer — `detection_pass` observes a live, un-sighted phantom into its holders' picture with the phantom's own scoped subject — and a bounded reaper drops an expired phantom once its Losses are applied. One `Loss` per **contact**, not per holder: the two ledger rows of a shared contact are one `ContactState`, and `checked_mark_lost` retires it for its whole audience in one transition (O9).

**§4.1's cut-7 row, confirmed in place.** `Envelope`, `Phantom`, `ContactProvenance` → **exempt**, workflow/side projection, legacy-post-image, families `propagation.envelope` / `.phantom` / `.provenance`; **observations are not records here at all** — they go through tactics' authoritative ingress. That is what was built, and the closing rule is respected: no legality check reads an envelope, a phantom or a provenance row, and cut 7 registers no `revision_validator` and no `state_family` of its own. The one addition the row did not name is `propagation.sighting`, the detection pass's own idempotency book — **exempt on the same terms**, named here so the tripwire's category is complete. The **tactics-side** families the ingress adds (`sensor_jobs` and the six `sensor_*_schedules`) are `mush-tactics`' typed families on the **authoritative** lane, which is where a durable cursor has to live.

**§7's constants, cited not redefined, plus what cut 7 declares.** Cited: `MAX_CONTACTS_PER_OBSERVER` 128, `CONTACT_TTL` 30, `MAX_ENVELOPE_HOPS` 4, `ENVELOPE_TTL` 30 IC days, `MAX_OBSERVATIONS_PER_PASS` 256, K's `MAX_CONTACT_AUDIENCE` 32 / `MAX_CONTACT_BYTES` 64 KiB, K's `MAX_PROJECTED_ACTION_TEXT_BYTES` 4 KiB (which **is** `MAX_SENSOR_ARM_BYTES`). Declared by cut 7 because §7 has no figure for them: `MAX_INTENT_AUDIENCE` 8 · `MAX_CONTACT_LEDGER_ROWS` 1_024 · `MAX_LEDGER_SCAN_PER_FIRE` 128 · `MAX_INTENTS_PER_ARM` 16 (what the 4 KiB offer parameter admits — measured: 16 real `Observe` intents encode to **3,525 of 4,096 bytes, 86 %** of the cap, so a larger intent shape is close to the edge, B9; a caller with more due work says how many remain and never truncates, which after the independent verify fold is true because `detection_pass` takes the caller's arm bound and books only what it emits) · `MAX_SENSOR_JOB_BYTES` 8 KiB · `MAX_ENVELOPE_PAYLOAD_BYTES` 512 · `MAX_ENVELOPES` 4_096 · `MAX_PHANTOMS` 1_024 · `MAX_PROVENANCE` 4_096 · `MAX_SIGHTINGS` 4_096 · `MAX_PHANTOM_ID_BYTES` 48 · `MAX_ENVELOPES_PER_PASS` 64 · `MAX_OBSERVERS_PER_PASS` 32 · `MAX_SUBJECTS_PER_OBSERVER` 32 · `MAX_CONTEXT_RECORDS` 64 (declared and **unapplied** until the independent verify fold; it now bounds `domain_of_place`'s walk and `Medium::of_place`'s scan) · `MAX_DETECTION_VISITS` 1_024 · `MAX_REAP_PER_PASS` 64 · `MAX_HELD_DOCUMENTS` 64 · `MAX_PAGED_NOTICES` 32 · `MAX_ORDERS_LISTED` 32 · `MAX_ROUTE_ID_BYTES` 64 · `DEFAULT_SIGNATURE` 12 · `REFRESH_AFTER_SECS` 10 · `JAMMING_BLOCKS_AT` 4 · `ADJACENT_RANGE` 2.

**Deviations, with reasons.** **(a) The delivery pass is a Coarse `reg.tick`, not a propagation-owned `schedule_source`.** OPEN 3's default names `reg.schedule_source("delivery", …)` firing an `envelope_advance` action. That is buildable — and it is incompatible with the other two things the plan says about the same records. A `schedule_source`-fired action runs inside `prepare_authoritative` and can write **only typed `state_family` records**; making the envelope one would make `+order` an **`Authoritative`** handler and would make §4.1's cut-7 row ("exempt — workflow, legacy-post-image") and §3.7's B7 classification ("LegacyPostImage for `+order`") both wrong, breaching §4.1's own closing rule that an exempt record takes no part in prepared mechanics. Cut 6 met the identical fork and the owner's **RESOLUTION A** settled it the same way. So the driver is `propagation.delivery`, a Coarse `reg.tick` on the world IC clock (`e7c7_p5_the_coarse_delivery_tick_advances_an_envelope_without_a_staff_verb` proves the tick, not the staff verb, is the driver), `@prop/pass` single-steps the same function, and the tag is disjoint from `sim.journey` and `route.sweep`. Nothing collides with cut 6, which registers no schedule source at all. **Two amendments from the independent verify fold.** The tick drives **delivery only**: `detection_pass` is reachable from `@prop/pass` (staff) and from `PropagationSensorPolicy::due` (which runs on a discarded scratch clone), so as shipped a server with no staff online detected nothing and created no contact — **cut 8's pulse owns the detection driver**, and building one here would pull it forward (B13, recorded not built, pinned by `vf_b13_…`). And the tick **dropped its whole report** (B3): it returned early on an empty one, never read `bound_hit`, and sent anomalies to `tracing::warn!`, so "paged, not logged" held only on the staff verb. The fold gives `PropagationStore` a bounded notice queue the tick pages into and `@prop/pass` and `@prop/held` drain. **(b) `sensor_arm` is a seventh tactics-owned action.** It is **not** a seventh contact action: it holds no contact handle and writes only cursors. It exists because the kernel offers no other way to create a `DurableSchedule`, and it is staff-offered only. **TRUE OF THE WRITE, FALSE OF THE EFFECT as shipped (O2, promoted).** The arming bundle genuinely holds no contact handle — and the action it later fires carried the arming actor's full authority over any contact in the game, because none of the six `contact_*` providers ever compared the invoking principal against `ContactState::audience()`, `SensorScheduleAdapter::plan` loaded the contact for its existence pre-check and never read the audience, and `prepare_authoritative` re-checked no principal at all. The gate held only *compositionally*.
