The independent verify fold lands it in three places: `plan` requires the intent's observer to be in the audience (and checks the `Share`/`Unshare` target); `ContactProvider::prepare_{refresh,audience,loss,reacquire}` require the invoking principal to be in the audience, or the kernel's `"scheduler"` class carrying an audience-bearing acting object; `prepare_observe` requires the actor to be in the opening audience; and `SensorArmProvider::prepare_authoritative` re-checks the `armed_by` fixed offer parameter, so an arm outside a projected offer refuses **in prepare**. **(c) There is no signature model.** `DEFAULT_SIGNATURE` is one constant for every platform, because a per-object figure would be a second truth about a unit with no owner and cut 7 has no unit model (cut 8 migrates `TacticalUnit`). **(d) Emissions posture, sensor capability and bands are not modelled**; `ContactProvenance.band` is authored by whoever records it — and, understated as shipped, is authored by **nobody**: `record_provenance` has no caller, so every band renders `-` on every server (B18, folded into (f)). **(e) Range is the placement graph's, not a metric — PROMOTED, and the consequence was unstated.** Same place = 0, different places = `ADJACENT_RANGE`; both resolvers are written so a metric can replace it without a consumer changing (which is true, and is why the fix was cheap). What the shipped wording did not say is that `build` read **no link, exit, domain boundary or metric at all**, so "a different place" meant *every other place in the world*: with one `DEFAULT_SIGNATURE` 12 against a Vacuum threshold of 3, every placed object outside an interior space mutually detected every other one across domains, and `detection_pass` armed a real `Observe` on each (K1). Compounding it, `DEFAULT_RECOVERY_ROOM` and `DEFAULT_IN_TRANSIT_ROOM` are the same object, so every wreck and every carrier under way sat at range 0 together (K2). The independent verify fold bounds detection by **domain adjacency** inside `build` (D1(a)) and makes a **projection room not a place** (D2(a)); `geometry.range` stays the sole consumer-visible distance term. **(f) NEITHER `Provenance` arm has a writer in cut 7** beyond `record_provenance`, which no verb, tick or pass calls: the attribution seam is built and bounded, and the pass that fills it is cut 8's, which is when a relayed report has a tick to age on. Widened by the independent verify fold (B18): `Local` is as unwritten as `Reported`, `band` always renders `-`, and **every `HopRef.relay` equals the issuer**, because `step_envelope` derives the relay from the envelope's own path — so cut 7's relay path is a self-loop and the hop bound is a retry counter rather than a relay chain. K15's render fix landed first, so cut 8's writer arrives against a view that cannot leak a dbref: a `Reported` source renders as the viewer's own opaque contact on the reporting platform, or `reported, {age}s old`, never `#<dbref>`. **(g) The full authoritative round trip is not driven in-crate.** `TestWorld` has no `CommandSubmitDriver`, so `e7c7_a2_…` asserts the queued submission's action identity (owner `tactics`, id `sensor_arm`) and the absence of any contact write, and the rig drains the queue between passes; the end-to-end commit rides `mush-server`'s existing coordinator harness. **No such test existed anywhere in the tree (O8, promoted to a fix)**, which is why O2, K4 and O9 all survived the shipped suite. The independent verify fold drives the whole round trip — `sensor_arm` → `DurableSchedule` → `discover_scheduled_actions` → `SensorScheduleAdapter::plan` → `scheduled_action_ingress` → `ContactProvider` — in `vf_o8_the_sensor_ingress_round_trip_commits_replaces_and_refuses`, with all three cases plus the negative arm. It lands in `crates/mush-propagation/tests/e7c7.rs` rather than `crates/mush-server`, because `DurableScheduleDriver::drive_one` is a private `async fn` of `world_task.rs` with no test seam; the engine path the test drives is the one that driver calls, and is the shape `crates/mush-sim/tests/e7c5.rs` uses for the comparable cut-5 case. **(j) The phantom→contact path had no producer, and now has one.** Recorded beside (f) by the independent verify fold (O7): `Phantom::subject()`'s only callers were test code, `pass.rs` step (1) always set `truth: SubjectKey::Object`, so `ledger_tick`'s `phantom` column could never be `Some` in production and OPEN 5's "Loss to every holder on expiry" was reachable only through an `install_ledger`-driven test. The fold took the functional half: `detection_pass` now emits an `Observe` with the phantom's own scoped subject and holder audience for each live, un-credited phantom, so `@prop/phantom` mints a document the pass consumes. **(h) No `FactProvider` namespace, no tenth `ChannelGrant` projection, no `WorldEvent` variant** — including the typed `CarrierArrived` cut 6's R9 deferred: cut 7 emits no world event at all. **(i) A stale `sensor_*` cursor is re-discovered rather than reaped.** A schedule whose fired action refuses is left standing by `prepare_and_stage_scheduled` (nothing commits), which is a pre-existing tree property shared with `control_handoff` and `production`; cut 7's stated mitigation — that `sensor_arm` **replaces** a cursor at the same deterministic key — **was false at any later IC second** (K4, promoted): `ObservationIntent::key_for` formatted `<kind>/<observer>/<subject>/<tick:x>`, every `pass.rs` caller passed `now.at`, and `sensor_schedule_id` hashed exactly that, so the same still-due transition one second later took the `txn.insert` branch and stacked a second `DurableSchedule` and `SensorJob` beside the stuck one, on the **authoritative** lane, in families with no record-count cap, with no reaper anywhere in the file. The independent verify fold **rewrites this deviation**: `ObservationIntent::key_for_state(kind, observer, contact)` keys the five state-carrying kinds with **no tick** and the whole `ContactId` (`Observe` keeps the tick, because its identity is the pair plus the sighting book), a cursor that has already **fired** is `txn.remove`d rather than replaced in place, and `detection_pass` no longer arms a transition whose holder is gone or `GOING`. What remains deferred, and is recorded here rather than claimed as mitigated: a completed one-shot `Observe` cursor whose key is never re-armed is reaped only when that pair is observed again, because reaping it otherwise would need either an eighth tactics action or a scan seam inside `ActionTxn`, and cut 7 may add neither.

**Also landed here: cut 6's fold item B6.** The composition-installed route catalog — `mush_sim::RouteCatalog` / `RouteCatalogState` / `SimulationModule::with_route_catalog`, implemented in `mush_server::sim_routes::TacsimRouteCatalog` over `TacsimStore::route`. See §3.6's B6 row, amended in place.

**Hands to cut 8:** the per-tick `detection_pass` and `delivery_pass` functions the pulse driver calls, and the `PerceivedRef::Contact` entries tactical intents target. **Nothing else** — no pulse, no domain-tick driver, no clock provider, no window. Their signatures are the ones the independent verify fold settled, in the shape §3.8's pulse expects (one commit per domain per tick, `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` 64): `detection_pass(&mut store, &state, &world, &ledger, &now, domain: Option<DomainId>, arm_bound: usize) -> DetectionReport { intents, bound_hit, resume, refused, sightings, dropped_sightings, stranded }` and `delivery_pass(&mut store, &state, &world, &ledger, &now, domain: Option<DomainId>) -> DeliveryReport` (which now also carries `resume` and `reaped`). The domain scope is a **parameter**, so a per-domain pulse does per-domain work rather than a whole-world scan repeated per domain (B6), and both bounds apply per domain. Cut 8 must also persist what the reports name: the sighting keys written, the sighting and record keys **dropped** (a `ModuleWrite::Del` beside the `JsonSet`s), and the store's notice queue.

**Tests.** 34 in `crates/mush-propagation/tests/e7c7.rs` (`a1..a4` the authority probe, `k1..k6` the two knowledge axes, `b1..b8` the loss/compaction axis plus restart and bounds, `o1..o10` the order lifecycle, `p1..p6` wiring and projection) plus 8 in-crate units in `context.rs`/`record.rs`, 5 in `mush-core`'s `sensor.rs`, 4 in `mush-tactics`' `sensor.rs`, and the amended `vf_b6_…` in `crates/mush-sim/tests/e7c6.rs`. Every hazard-axis test asserts the **observable** outcome — the rendered listing, the refusal bytes a non-holder receives, the recipient's own standing order, the projected arrival — in addition to the record.

**Independent verify fold (2026-09-06, on `0f23eb1`+).** An independent adversarial verify over the three lenses (**K** knowledge and leak, **O** ownership and authority, **B** bounds, restart and disclosure) read the cut-7 symbol chain as committed at `a4e4ef0` and found what this "As built" missed: **34 items after merging cross-lens duplicates (9 HIGH, 13 MEDIUM, 12 LOW)**, plus **fifteen claims this section made that were false, unmet or understated — all corrected in place above** — and one new deviation, (j). **Nothing was refuted;** two of the verify's own proposed fixes were checked against the kernel and corrected (see D5 and B5 below). Owner decisions taken, all seven recommended defaults with D5 strengthened: **D1(a)** detection bounded by domain adjacency inside `PropagationContext::build`; **D2(a)** a projection room is not a place; **D3(a) both halves** a tickless state key plus a reap on completion; **D4(a)+(c)** a `DetectionReport` at the caller's arm bound plus an ageing sighting book; **D5(a) strengthened** — E-side containment **and** the kernel change made directly (the K line is closed and E owns the kernel); **D6(a)** addressability, attribution and per-issuer scope; **D7(a)** a bounded per-fire reaper. One line per item; every fix carries a `vf_*` regression that asserts the **observable** outcome.

- **K1 (HIGH) — detection had no distance and no connectivity.** `PropagationContext::build` set `range = if same { 0 } else { ADJACENT_RANGE }` and read no link, exit, domain boundary or metric, so every placed object outside an interior space mutually detected every other one in any domain anywhere in the world, and `detection_pass` armed a real `Observe` on each. **D1(a):** `build` returns `None` unless the two places are the same place or resolve to the same `DomainId` (`context.rs::domain_of_place`, which walks a domain host, its presentation room, and an interior space's anchor, bounded by `MAX_CONTEXT_RECORDS`). `geometry.range` stays the sole consumer-visible distance term. `vf_k1a_a_unit_in_another_domain_is_not_detected_while_a_same_domain_pair_still_detects`, `vf_k1b_no_cross_domain_unit_reaches_the_armed_ingress_document`.
- **K2 (HIGH) — every unplaced platform projected to one nowhere-room.** `DEFAULT_RECOVERY_ROOM` and `DEFAULT_IN_TRANSIT_ROOM` are both `LIMBO`, so cut 6's quarantined wrecks and every carrier under way sat at range 0 together and observed each other. **D2(a):** `context.rs::place_of` answers `None` for `state.recovery_room` and `state.in_transit_room`. The consequence is accepted deliberately: an envelope to a ship under way **waits** (store-and-forward), which is what §3.7 already says a jammed link does. `vf_k2a_two_carriers_under_way_do_not_observe_each_other`, `vf_k2b_an_order_to_a_ship_under_way_waits_and_says_so`.
- **K3 (HIGH, with B5) — a world rate change froze every live contact for ever.** Four sites compared clocks by equality and failed closed, so no Loss and no Refresh was ever armed again, no phantom expired, no envelope aged out, and `+contacts` rendered a 40-second-old track as `held  0s` — **fresher than the truth**, on the one view the leak argument rests on. The verify's own fix does **not** compile against the kernel: `checked_mark_lost` → `require_not_before` → `ClockStamp::checked_cmp` errors `ClockMismatch`, `validate` re-runs it, `checked_refresh` compares directly, and `checked_reacquire` needs an inactive contact only a Loss could produce — so there was no E-side path to retire or refresh a pre-change contact at all. **D5(a) strengthened, and the kernel change made here rather than asked for:** (i) `render_contact` and `render_envelope` print `stale` for an incomparable stamp and never a fresher-looking figure; (ii) the delivery tick **re-anchors propagation's own records** (`Envelope::reanchored`, `Phantom::reanchored`) onto the current generation keeping their IC scalars, which is exact because `WorldTimeline::next_rate_epoch` anchors the new generation to the value the old one produced; (iii) `crates/mush-core/src/contact.rs` gains `require_not_before_for_loss`, so **`checked_mark_lost` tolerates a generation mismatch while `checked_refresh` keeps refusing** — loss is terminal and orders nothing, a refresh claims the observation is newer and needs a comparable stamp; (iv) `detection_pass` then retires a stranded contact with reason `clock_generation` and **pages the count**. `vf_k3_kernel_a_pre_change_contact_can_be_lost_but_never_refreshed` (beside `contact.rs`'s own), `vf_k3a_a_rate_change_ages_out_an_envelope_and_expires_a_phantom`, `vf_k3b_a_pre_change_contact_never_renders_as_freshly_observed`, `vf_k3c_the_stranded_contacts_are_paged_and_retired`.
- **K4 (HIGH; merges O4, B4 and all three lenses' deviation-(i) items) — the cursor key carried the tick, so a re-arm INSERTED a second durable cursor.** **D3(a), both halves.** See deviation (i), rewritten above. `vf_k4a_a_second_pass_replaces_the_standing_cursor`, `vf_k4c_a_loss_is_not_armed_for_a_destroyed_holder`, and the replacement half end to end in `vf_o8_…`.
- **K5 (HIGH as merged) — `+order` resolved its recipient with the unscoped `#dbref` matcher and echoed the display name.** An existence-and-identity oracle over the whole dbref space, on a tactical verb. **D6(a):** `commands.rs::may_address` — staff, a unit the actor holds a control station on (`viewable_holders`, cut 5's `assignments_by_actor` re-used), or an object in the same place — and both refusals render the byte-identical `NO_SUCH_CONTACT` line. `vf_k5_an_order_to_an_undetected_dbref_refuses_identically_to_a_nonexistent_one`.
- **K7 (HIGH; merges K11, O3, B2) — the detection pass booked a sighting for every pair it detected, armed sixteen, paged a remainder that was already unreachable, and truncated the rest silently.** **D4(a) plus (c)'s ageing:** `detection_pass` takes the caller's arm bound, returns `DetectionReport { intents, bound_hit, resume, refused, … }`, **books only what it emits**, resumes from a durable `(observer, subject)` cursor in `PropagationStore`, never swallows `put_sighting`'s census error (an observation without an idempotency record is **not** armed, and the refusal is named), ages a sighting out at `CONTACT_TTL`, and closes with `mush_core::bounded_pass` rather than `truncate`. `vf_k7a_the_detection_bound_pages_and_resumes`, `vf_k7b_the_paged_remainder_is_still_due_on_the_second_run`, `vf_k7c_a_full_sighting_book_refuses_rather_than_rearming`.
- **K8 (HIGH; merges O1) — `active_order` selected across issuers while supersession was per issuer, and no view named an issuer.** Any actor's revision 1 became a recipient's standing order on a higher envelope id while the real commander's stayed `Delivered` and unsuperseded. **D6(a):** `pass.rs::standing_orders` is per issuer (`active_order_from` per issuer, `active_order` the most recently arrived), which agrees with `settle_arrival` and `next_revision` by construction; every rendered order names its source through the same fog gate, an unattributable one reading "an unidentified sender". Cut 9's command relation is one predicate away and stays cut 9's. `vf_k8_a_stranger_cannot_displace_a_commanders_standing_order`.
- **O2 (HIGH; merges K16 and both lenses' deviation-(b) items) — the six migrated `contact_*` providers performed NO authority check.** See deviation (b), amended above. `vf_o2a_loss_refresh_share_reacquire_and_observe_by_a_principal_outside_the_audience_all_refuse` names every arm; `vf_o2c_a_sensor_arm_without_a_projected_offer_refuses_in_prepare_not_only_in_the_offer`; the adapter half is driven end to end by `vf_o8_…`'s negative arm.
- **B1 (HIGH; merges K12) — nothing in the crate ever removed a record**, so the four censuses were caps on **lifetime totals** and `+order` bricked permanently for every actor at `MAX_ENVELOPES`. **D7(a):** a bounded per-fire reaper in `delivery_pass` (`MAX_REAP_PER_PASS` 64 per kind) drops a terminal envelope past `ENVELOPE_TTL_SECS`, a provenance row whose contact has left the ledger, and an expired phantom whose holders hold no active row; `detection_pass` ages the sighting book. Each drop is a `ModuleWrite::Del` beside the post-images, and the pass **reports** what it compacted. `vf_b1a_finished_traffic_is_compacted_and_order_still_works_at_the_census`, `vf_b1b_an_expired_phantom_is_dropped_after_its_losses_are_armed`.
- **B3 (HIGH; merges K10) — the delivery pass had no cursor and the tick dropped its report.** `PropagationStore::next_envelope_scan` is the durable resume point, the scan rotates through it, and the page line names where it will continue; the Coarse tick pages `bound_hit`, its anomalies and its re-anchor count into the store's own notice queue, which `@prop/pass` and `@prop/held` drain. `vf_b3a_the_delivery_pass_resumes_where_it_stopped`, `vf_b3b_an_envelope_behind_a_jam_still_reaches_its_ttl`, `vf_b3c_the_tick_pages_its_bound_and_its_anomalies`.
- **K6 (MEDIUM) — a lost contact was never re-acquired** and `ObservationKind::Reacquire` had no producer. `detection_pass` now arms `Reacquire` for an inactive **phantom-backed** row whose phantom is still live — the one case propagation can resolve back to a subject, because the phantom is its own mint and the leak-safe ledger deliberately carries no object id — and the aged sighting book un-blocks a fresh `Observe` for a lost real contact. Recorded honestly: a lost contact on a **real** unit is re-observed under a new identity rather than reacquired, and closing that needs the contact↔subject mapping cut 8's relay pass introduces. `vf_k6_a_lost_phantom_contact_reacquires_and_a_lost_real_one_is_observed_again`.
- **K9 (MEDIUM) — occlusion was one-directional.** A sensor inside a sealed hull looking out got `occluded == false`, and only `DEFAULT_SIGNATURE` 12 against a threshold of 24 kept the miss. `build` now occludes when **either** end's place is `Medium::Interior`. `vf_k9_a_sealed_hull_occludes_in_both_directions`, and the pin `vf_dev_c_the_signature_constant_is_not_the_fog_model` (an occluded pair and a cross-domain pair both miss at `u32::MAX`).
- **O5 (MEDIUM) — one extra token bypassed the route catalog.** `mush_sim::commands::build_route` consulted the catalog only when no place tokens followed, so a trailing token skipped the authored arm and its `from != origin` refusal and minted a manual `RouteDef` **carrying the authored id** with zero propellant. The catalog is now resolved on the id **first**; an authored id with a trailing place list refuses. `vf_o5_one_extra_token_cannot_bypass_the_authored_route` (in `crates/mush-sim/tests/e7c6.rs`).
- **O6 (MEDIUM; merges B15(b)(c)) — the contact ledger truncated rather than paging.** See the corrected claim above. `vf_o6_every_holder_sees_its_own_contacts_past_the_ledger_cap`.
- **O7 (MEDIUM) — the phantom→contact path had no producer.** Recorded as deviation **(j)** and discharged in the same edit. `vf_o7_a_minted_phantom_becomes_a_contact_through_the_pass`.
- **O8 (MEDIUM; merges all three lenses' deviation-(g) items) — nothing drove `sensor_arm` end to end.** See deviation (g), amended above. `vf_o8_the_sensor_ingress_round_trip_commits_replaces_and_refuses`.
- **B6 (MEDIUM) — neither pass took a `DomainId`.** `PropagationContext.domain` was written and read nowhere, and a per-domain pulse would have done whole-world work per domain per tick. Both passes now take `domain: Option<DomainId>` and filter observers, subjects and envelopes by it; see the handoff sentence above. `vf_b6_a_pass_scoped_to_one_domain_names_no_subject_in_another`.
- **B7 (MEDIUM; merges K18) — `held` was unbounded and unreadable.** See the corrected claim above. `vf_b7a_the_held_map_is_bounded_and_a_held_document_is_inspectable`.
- **B8 (MEDIUM) — `@prop` published `LegacyPostImage` while `/pass` staged an authoritative action.** See the corrected claim above. `vf_b8_prop_publishes_authoritative_because_pass_stages_sensor_arm` asserts the class **and** the staging in one test, the shape `@station`'s pin takes.
- **B9 (MEDIUM) — `@prop/pass` committed the sightings before the bundle was prepared.** D4(a) makes the book follow the emission, so a refused arm loses nothing that was not going out; the measured headroom (86 % of the offer cap at 16 intents) is recorded beside `MAX_INTENTS_PER_ARM` above, and byte-sized filling stays cut 8's, with the figure now on the record so it cannot be assumed.
- **B10 (MEDIUM) — `Sighting` had no constructor and no strict wire.** `Sighting::new` refuses `(None, None)`, `last < first` and a mixed clock generation, with a hand-written `Deserialize` that re-runs it; landed **before** K13's re-keying, as the fold required. `vf_b10_a_subjectless_or_time_reversed_sighting_is_refused_on_load`.
- **B11 (MEDIUM; merges K17) — `reconcile_allocators` raised only the envelope allocator.** A lost allocator document reset the phantom ordinal to 0 and the next mint silently replaced a live phantom. The ordinal is now recorded on the record (`#[serde(default)]`), `reconcile_allocators` raises past the highest hydrated one, and `put_phantom` **refuses** rather than replacing when an id already names a different phantom. `vf_b11_a_hydrated_phantom_set_raises_the_phantom_allocator`.
- **B12 (MEDIUM) — `@prop/pass` re-persisted the whole sighting book on every call.** `DetectionReport.sightings` names the rows the pass touched and only those are written; a re-detection re-stamps only once it drifts into the refresh band, so an unchanged world writes nothing. `vf_b12_a_second_pass_with_no_change_writes_no_sighting_op`.
- **B13 (MEDIUM) — the detection half has no production driver.** Recorded, not built; see deviation (a), amended above. `vf_b13_the_delivery_tick_drives_delivery_only_and_arms_nothing`.
- **K13 (LOW; merges K14) — two identifiers carried structure they should not.** The sighting key is now a `propagation.sighting.v1` digest and a phantom id is `ph<digest of (observer, ordinal, created)>`, so neither the document key nor the ledger's `phantom` column tells a reader which platform a false contact was minted for. `vf_k13a_no_sighting_key_or_phantom_id_carries_a_dbref`.
- **K15 (LOW) — `render_contact` would have printed `reported by #<dbref>` as soon as a writer landed.** Fixed before the writer arrives; see deviation (f). `vf_k15_a_reported_provenance_names_no_dbref`.
- **O9 (LOW) — a shared contact emitted one `Refresh` per holder** against the same `expected_revision`, so every holder past the first was refused `Stale` and left a cursor standing. The pass now emits at most one transition per `(kind, contact)`. `vf_o9_a_shared_contact_refreshes_once_not_once_per_holder`.
- **O10 (LOW) — the intent key truncated the `ContactId` to 8 hex characters.** `key_for_state` uses the whole id. `vf_o10_two_contacts_sharing_an_id_prefix_arm_two_distinct_intents`.
- **O12 (LOW) — `@journey/plan`'s authored-route refusal disclosed the route's origin to any crew member.** Non-staff now get the byte-identical `ROUTE_NOT_FROM_HERE` line for a real authored route from elsewhere and for an id the catalog cannot answer for; staff keep the informative form. `vf_o12_a_crew_route_probe_refuses_identically_for_a_real_and_a_nonsense_id`.
- **B14 (LOW) — `+orders` and `@prop/envelopes` rendered one line per envelope with no page.** Both take the newest `MAX_ORDERS_LISTED` 32 and name the remainder. `vf_b14_plus_orders_pages_rather_than_printing_the_whole_history`.
- **B15 (LOW) — declared-but-dead bounds.** `MAX_CONTEXT_RECORDS` now binds `domain_of_place` and `Medium::of_place`; `ledger_tick`'s overflow guard now stops the scan instead of being overwritten. `vf_b15_a_context_build_visits_at_most_max_context_records`, and O6's test for the guard.
- **B16 (LOW) — three deep clones of the whole store per fire.** `delivery_pass` and `context_between` take a `PropagationStore::jamming_map()` snapshot, which is O(jammed places). `vf_b16_the_delivery_pass_takes_a_jamming_view_not_a_store`.
- **B18 (LOW) — every contact renders unattributed and every hop's relay is the issuer.** Pinned as the known state so cut 8's first writer must change it deliberately; see deviation (f). `vf_b18_every_contact_renders_unattributed_and_every_hop_relay_is_the_issuer`.

**Not closed by the fold, and recorded rather than claimed.** **O11 (LOW)** — `ObserverRef::Station::principal()` still collapses to the host platform, so two stations on one hull mint two contacts into one audience; narrowing it needs a `PrincipalId` shape for `(host, station)` that cut 8/10's crew authority owns, and deleting the arm would break the Q16A vocabulary this cut froze. **B17 (LOW)** — `ContactLedgerState::rows()` is still a world-global accessor any composed module could read; the fold made `commands.rs` use `for_observer` per holder, which is the behaviour half, but keeping `rows()` is what the pass itself needs and closing it means a holder-scoped iterator cut 8 can consume. **K4's remaining half** — a completed one-shot `Observe` cursor whose key is never re-armed; see deviation (i). **B9's byte-sized fill** — measured and recorded, deferred.

**What the verify re-confirmed sound, and must not be "fixed".** The one binding constraint (`mush-tactics` is the sole `ContactState` authority, propagation registers no contact family and holds no handle). The kernel owner-fence argument for a tactics-owned ingress (contradiction (i)), re-verified against all three fences. The two resolvers' structural separation, and a miss and a hit carrying the same fields, lag and reads. The no-launder proof apart from K13's id derivation. `viewable_holders` as the whole read gate, re-using cut 5's `assignments_by_actor`. The held-not-deleted boot rule, including its over-census arm. The T15 order lifecycle on every axis except attribution and cross-issuer scope. The edge set. Deviations (a) and (h) as designed.

**Fold gates.** `cargo test -p mush-propagation -p mush-tactics -p mush-core -p mush-sim -p mush-server -p mush-contract-tests`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -j 6 -- -D warnings`, `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo test --workspace --no-fail-fast -j 6`, and the K8 CI invocations (`k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` with `@prop`'s corrected class, `schedule_source`, `provider_rng_policy`, `truth_serialization_policy`, `legacy_handler_allowlist`, `ci_gated_test_inventory`, `gate5_fault_matrix`, `sheet_write_republish`). Re-asserted: `cargo tree -p mush-propagation` is still `{mush-core, mush-sim}` with **no `mush-tactics` edge**, and `cargo tree -p mush-tactics --depth 1` is still byte-identical to cut 6's — O2's entitlement check is written with `mush-core` types only, and propagation still names `sensor_arm` by `(owner, kind, id)` strings through the definition registry.

**Fold tests.** 43 further tests in `crates/mush-propagation/tests/e7c7.rs` under `// ---- E7 cut 7 independent verify fold ----`, 1 kernel test beside `crates/mush-core/src/contact.rs`'s, and 2 in `crates/mush-sim/tests/e7c6.rs`. Every one asserts the observable outcome — a render, a refusal's exact bytes, the recipient's own standing order, the document the ingress will actually commit — and names every arm a guard covers in its own name.

### 3.8 Cut 8 — the minimal deterministic pulse driver

**Q12A, both halves.** **Ingress:** player commands and externally submitted tactical intents are **new authoritative state-family actions and take the prepared path**. `brace` proves *part* of the shape — typed state family (`crates/mush-tactics/src/module.rs:592-621`, main), early idempotency and checked revision (`:504-536`), authoritative-only preparation (`:544-581`), definition-validator + `action_provider` registration (`:623-635`). It proves **no guard revalidation**, and on the converged main cut 8 does not need it to: the guard half is K6's — `ControlLeaseGuardValidator` (registered by `mush-sim`), `ContactGuardValidator` (registered by `mush-tactics`) and the kernel's `AuthorityGuard::ChannelGrant` revalidation in `Engine::take_staged_action` (§2.2, §2.5). Cut 8's providers *name* those guards; they register none. 12C — a TacSim-private action path — is forbidden by doc 09 C2 and T2.

**What a tick's invocation actually is — the K lane now has an actorless entry, and the tick uses it.** Draft 0.2 hand-built the invocation because no actorless ingress existed. On `91208ab` it does (08i §2 row 10): `scheduled_action_ingress` in `crates/mush-core/src/schedule_source.rs` turns a `ScheduleActionDraft` from a `ScheduleActionAdapter` (registered through `Registrar::schedule_source`) into an `ActionInvocation` under **`PrincipalId::Scoped { class: "scheduler" }`** with an **`IdempotencyKey::Deterministic`**, and `DurableScheduleDriver::drive_one` (`crates/mush-server/src/world_task.rs`) polls one candidate per 250 ms, deferring while legacy persistence work is pending, and stages it through `ActionCoordinator::prepare_and_stage_scheduled`. **The domain tick is one such adapter** — the same shape the `HandoffScheduleAdapter` in `mush-sim`'s `handoff.rs` already uses — with the deterministic key over `(domain, tick)` so a duplicate driver fire is a HIT rather than a second tick, and the domain's host object as `actor`. (Core's `PrincipalId` still has only `Object` and `Scoped`; the economy's `System` principal is `mush_org`'s type and is not reachable here. Draft 0.2's `PerceivedRef::KnownObject { id: host }` subject buys nothing on this lane and is dropped.) **K7.2 will not change `ScheduleActionDraft`, the `"scheduler"` principal class or `prepare_and_stage_resolved`** — it adds a sibling authenticated decision-response ingress calling the same coordinator path (08i answer 5) — so cut 8 builds on a frozen ingress. There is no bespoke tick lane and no `Ctx`-level action submit.

**The frozen set of accepted intents is a window.** K's durable `ActionWindowRecord` (`crates/mush-core/src/action_window.rs`; 08i §2 row 8) is the per-tick intent set: `checked_submit` accepts one intent per `(actor, truth_subject)` slot (`WindowIntentSlot`) and a resubmission **replaces** it — which is S2A's latest-order-wins for free — `resolution_order` yields the canonical order the resolver consumes, and `checked_consume` closes the window in the tick's own commit. `MAX_WINDOW_CAPACITY = 256` **is** `MAX_INTENTS_PER_TICK` (§7); cut 8 defines no second constant.

**Resolution.** The pulse resolver does **not** create one K action per NPC, weapon or unit. It consumes the frozen set of accepted intents for the tick and produces **one deterministic domain-tick commit per domain per tick** — ordered deltas, RNG evidence, state hash. **Which cap binds, precisely** (correcting Draft 0.1's blanket claim): a tick writing one post-image per unit is bounded by **`MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES` = 1_024** (`authoritative.rs:27-28`) on *count*, and by **`MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_BUNDLE_BYTES` = 16 MiB** / **`MAX_DURABLE_OUTBOX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` = 256 KiB** (`:37-38`, `:35-36`) on *size*. `MAX_DURABLE_OUTBOX_INTENTS` (256) bounds what a bundle *emits*, not what a tick *accepts*, and is not the intent cap. At `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` = 64 the write count is ~6 % of the replay-write cap, so **64 is a latency and CPU policy figure, not a K3-derived one** — the K3 caps are a hard ceiling far above it. §9 q6's measurement must report which of the three is nearest, and the pinning test fails when any of them passes 60 %. **Measured, never assumed** (08i answer 15): a tick that writes one post-image per unit for `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` units, its window consume, its `ResolutionRecord` and its boundary facts is *encoded* and checked against `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES` (1,024) and `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_BUNDLE_BYTES` (16 MiB) in a pinned test; the store's error shapes carry the cap, not the measured size, so the measurement is E7's to make (08i §2 row 12). The `DurableOutboxAdapter` in `crates/mush-server/src/durable_outbox.rs` has no production implementation and `mush-server`'s `spawn_persist_with_gateway` wires an empty `DurableOutboxRegistry`, so cut 8 also ships the tactics adapter that carries boundary facts out of the bundle. **Lane occupancy** (answer 6; §9 q6): the store admits **one unresolved authoritative intent per store/backend** (`StoreError::AuthoritativeIntentInFlight`), not per owner or subject, so the tick's bundle **serializes with every other authoritative commit** — cut 2's reservations, cut 5's handoffs, cut 7's observation intents — and K7's 512-decision ceiling counts `PendingDecision` records only, never scheduled tick invocations.

```rust
enum DomainClock { Passive, Pulse { period_ms: u32, generation: u64 } }   // was Passive-only
// The clock's provider side (08i answers 4 and G-A): ONE owner-scoped provider for `mush-sim`,
// registered at composition through `Registrar::clock_provider`, answering `DomainTick` reads
// by the runtime `domain_id` and the domain's generation. Runtime promotion never registers.
struct DomainTickDriver { /* per-domain `Pulse` state, read-only from the registry's view */ }
impl DomainClockProvider for DomainTickDriver {           // K's G-A contract, doc-only until SP2;
    // mirrors the exact-ID `ClockProvider::now(&self, context, generation) -> Result<i64,
    // ClockProviderFailure>` with the runtime `domain_id` added to the context — the exact
    // signature is K's to freeze at SP2, and cut 8 implements whatever it is.
    fn now(&self, context: &ClockProviderContext<'_>, domain_id: &DomainId, generation: u64)
        -> Result<i64, ClockProviderFailure>;
}
struct DomainTickCommit { domain: DomainId, tick: u64, clock: ClockRef,   // ClockRef::DomainTick
                          window: ActionWindowId,                          // the consumed window
                          accepted: Vec<IntentRef>,                        // = window.resolution_order()
                          rng: ResolutionRecord,
                          deltas: Vec<UnitDelta>, facts: Vec<BoundaryFact>, state_hash: [u8; 32] }
struct StandingOrder { unit: ObjId, channel: ChannelId, value: OrderValue,
                       set_at: ClockStamp, by: ActorRef }                 // latest-order-wins
// The production UNIT typed family (08i answers 7 and 8; 08i §2 row 22): `TacticalUnit` ONLY —
// not `HostStations`, not `HostAssignments` (those stay control-topology records, cut 5's billets).
// `crates/mush-tactics/src/module.rs` `UnitStore` is legacy `reg.state` today; cut 8 migrates it
// onto K's record types under `mush-tactics`' `OwnerId`. `DomainRecord` authority sits here too.
// `unit.status` is a PROJECTION, never stored: composed at read time from the unit family, cut 6's
// placement family, tactics' `ContactState` and the authorized-station data of the viewer's grant.
```

**Controllers select; they never enumerate** (08i answer 11; 08i §3 "Offers"). Every controller — human, NPC fallback, link-loss automation — chooses from **`ProjectedLegalAction`s** produced by `Engine::resolve_legal_action` over the `OfferRouteTable`, and nothing else (K5b, landed). A `fire` offer is **one offer per action kind carrying up to 256 opaque target choices**, never one offer per target; there is **no station-specific `ProjectionScopeId`**; and a domain larger than 64 units needs **provider ranking or bounded tactical sectors** to stay inside K7.4's ≤ 32 AI-facing offers and 192 KiB prompt — which is a second reason `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` is 64 (§7).

**Fallback controllers under K7.0's rule** (08i answer 3). C30A's link-loss and station fallback is an **immediate deterministic controller**: it submits directly on the action-idempotency scope **without a `PendingDecision`**, because no asynchronous provider races it; if it ever competes with an existing LLM decision it **reuses that decision's record and idempotency key** rather than minting a second; `DecisionControllerId.owner` is open to `mush-sim` and `mush-campaign`, so E owns the declared fallback data (`FallbackSpec`, resolved from K7's `FallbackPolicyRef`) and the immediate controller, while K owns the delayed lifecycle. **LLM-driven controllers stay K7-gated** — `#[ignore]` pins naming K7, none in cuts 1–11 (§4 rule 5).

**S2A's split.** Heading, thrust, speed and sensor posture **persist until changed** (continuous, latest-order-wins); fire, launch, jump, dock, eject and damage control are **bounded discrete intents**. Players command rather than spam movement verbs; crew stations divide control; NPC and link-loss controllers use **the same legal actions and information**; rules resolve from recorded intents and RNG, allowing replay. Pulse periods: ground/atmosphere ≈ 1–2 s, space ≈ 5–10 s (§7 picks 2 s and 6 s). A turn-driven mode remains for tutorials and facilitated scenes.

**T2, enforced at the seam.** **No economic transaction occurs inside a combat pulse.** A tick mutates combat state (ammunition, heat, damage, position, control) over `TacticalUnit`'s existing cells, sections and installed components (`crates/mush-tactics/src/unit.rs:220-245`, `:314-326`, `:328-352`, main) and emits **typed facts at boundaries only**: sortie started/ended; unit disabled, abandoned, captured, destroyed; cargo delivered; docking completed; salvage recovered. Combat RNG belongs in the mechanical journal, not the economic one. **A probe asserts no economy entry point is reachable from the tick handler.** Every accepted intent records actor, decision driver, authority and control-lease epoch (T9) and revalidates its exact station/channel/epoch through K5a's `World::channel_grant_is_current` before it resolves, under an `AuthorityGuard::ControlLease { provider, subject, channel, holder, epoch }` (`crates/mush-core/src/action.rs`) **whose validator is K6's `ControlLeaseGuardValidator`, registered by `mush-sim`'s `register`** — Draft 0.2's "whose validator `mush-tactics` registers" is struck (08i §3 row 2: strike the tactics validator). Combat RNG is drawn through `PrepareCtx::start_resolution` into the `ResolutionRecord` (`crates/mush-core/src/resolution.rs`, `MAX_RESOLUTION_DRAWS`/`MAX_RESOLUTION_STREAMS`), and `crates/mush-contract-tests/tests/provider_rng_policy.rs` already polices every `impl ActionProvider for` file for ambient RNG; the no-leak probe below is E7's.

**Commands.** `+fire <contact>` · `+launch <bay>=<contact>` · `+heading <vec>` · `+thrust <n>` · `+posture <emcon>` · `+dc <section>` · `+dock <target>` · `+eject` · `@domain/tick <id>` (staff, single-step) · `@domain/replay <id> <from>-<to>` (staff).

**Tests (~28).** *Determinism:* the same accepted-intent set and RNG root produce byte-identical deltas and `state_hash` across two runs and a restart; replay from a stored tick reproduces the stored hash; intent ordering is canonical and independent of arrival order within the tick. *Bounds:* a domain at `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` commits inside the K3 caps; one unit past it refuses promotion (or queues, per Q15A) rather than producing an oversized bundle; a probe measures encoded tick bytes and fails past a stated fraction of the cap; retained-intent capacity bounds accepted intents per tick. *Ingress:* an intent from a non-focused station refuses (cut 5); a stale epoch refuses; a duplicate idempotency key is a HIT; a channel the grant does not carry refuses; an intent naming a contact the actor does not hold refuses **without revealing whether it exists** (cut 7). *T2:* no tick path reaches an economy entry point (probe); a sortie boundary emits the facts and *then* the economy consumes them; a settlement attempted inside a tick refuses; the sortie cadence advance happens at the boundary, once. *Continuous vs discrete:* a second heading order in one tick replaces the first and both are journaled; two `+fire` in one tick are two bounded intents; an order superseded before resolution leaves only the newest active. *Restart:* a crash mid-tick converges — the tick committed whole or not at all, and re-running produces the same hash; accepted intents survive; the RNG stream does not re-draw (K3.1a). *Fallback:* a linkdead station's declared NPC fallback issues legal actions from the same information (C30A) and is recorded with its own driver attribution. *Added by the review:* **an unknown intent kind refuses, it is not skipped** — a tick that silently drops an intent it cannot classify is the fail-open shape here; a **panic inside resolution leaves the domain tickable** and pages level 2 rather than wedging it (E4c V6); **RNG evidence is never in a player projection** — a probe asserts `ResolutionRecord` reaches no player- or AI-facing serialization, because a readable stream is next tick's oracle; a **duplicate tick driver fire** is a HIT on the deterministic `(domain, tick)` key and produces no second commit; a tick whose bundle would exceed any of the three caps **refuses and pages with the measured size**, it does not split silently; `state_hash` covers the accepted-intent set, the RNG root and the ordered deltas, so a replay that omits an intent fails the hash rather than reproducing it; a **`Scoped`-principal tick invocation cannot reach any command a player principal could not**, probed against the whole action surface. *Added by Draft 0.3 (the K answers):* the tick's encoded bundle at `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` is **measured** against 1,024 replay writes and 16 MiB and the test names the nearest cap (answer 15); the tick enters only through `scheduled_action_ingress` — a probe asserts no other `ActionInvocation` constructor in `mush-sim`/`mush-tactics` and no `Ctx`-level submit; a second submission by the same `(actor, truth_subject)` slot in one window **replaces** the first (`checked_submit`) and a 257th refuses (`MAX_WINDOW_CAPACITY`); a `fire` offer for a domain of 64 units is **one** `ProjectedLegalAction` with ≤ 256 opaque target choices, and a probe asserts no per-target offer and no station-scoped `ProjectionScopeId`; `unit.status` is byte-identical whether composed from the stores or from a fixture that never persisted it (a projection, not a document); `mush-sim`'s `DomainClockProvider` answers two promoted domains by id from one registration, and the registry composition is unchanged by the second promotion; a fallback that submits while the store holds another owner's unresolved intent **waits for the slot** rather than failing the tick, and the tick's own bundle likewise serializes behind a pending cut-2 reservation (answer 6); the fallback creates no `PendingDecision` and, racing an injected LLM decision, submits under that decision's key (answer 3).

**Verify lenses.** **D (determinism and replay)** — can two runs of one tick differ, or a restart change a result? **C (commit bounds)** — can a tick produce a bundle the store must refuse, and does the unit cap actually hold? **X (the T2 boundary)** — can any economic effect, RNG draw or settlement cross into a pulse?

**Hands to cut 9:** boundary facts with stable ids, and a domain an operation's activation can promote.

#### 3.8 As built (2026-09-06, on `3b9c8d6`) — two halves, because a scheduled action has no world

**The one binding constraint holds, and is probed.** Cut 8 registers **one** owner-scoped production `DomainClockProvider` for `mush-sim`, at composition, through `Registrar::domain_clock_provider`. `@domain/promote` (cut 6), the applying authority (`@domain/tick`), a **second** domain's promotion and a demotion all leave `Engine::clock_providers` **byte-identical** — `e7c8_x3_promotion_apply_and_a_second_domain_leave_the_clock_registry_byte_identical` snapshots `(fingerprint, len)` across all four. One registration answers every runtime domain by id (`e7c8_x3_one_registration_answers_two_domains_and_refuses_unknown_id_or_generation`).

**Why the cut is two halves** (§4.0f (c7)). A `schedule_source`-fired action runs inside `prepare_authoritative` with no `&World`, no `Ctx` and no `Outbox`. So:

- the **deterministic half** is the prepared authoritative commit the `domaintick` source fires — the frozen window, recorded RNG, ordered deltas, the state hash, the boundary facts;
- the **world-side half** is a Coarse `sim.domain_pulse` `reg.tick` (1 s, bounded at `MAX_DOMAINS_PER_PULSE_FIRE` 32 domains **visited**) that applies cut 6's activation request, writes the `sim` domain document and arms or disarms the durable cursor through **one** `Ctx::submit_authoritative` per fire (cut 2's fold C4: exactly one submission per command or tick).

`@domain/tick <id>` single-steps precisely that pass for one domain. This is the same split RESOLUTION A gave cuts 6 and 7, for the same kernel reason.

**Signatures (`crates/mush-sim/src/tick.rs` unless noted).**

```rust
// data.rs — the variant §3.8 contradiction (ii) asked for
pub enum DomainClock { Passive, Pulse { period_ms: u32, generation: u64, started_at: ClockStamp } }
impl DomainClock {
    pub fn pulse(period_ms: u32, generation: u64, started_at: ClockStamp)
        -> Result<DomainClock, DomainClockError>;          // strict TryFrom wire
    pub fn tick_at(&self, world_ic: &ClockStamp) -> Option<i64>;   // THE one tick function
}
pub const GROUND_PULSE_MS: u32 = 2_000;  pub const SPACE_PULSE_MS: u32 = 6_000;
pub const MIN_PULSE_PERIOD_MS: u32 = 1_000;  pub const MAX_PULSE_PERIOD_MS: u32 = 60_000;

// the landed provider signature (contradiction (i)), implemented verbatim
pub struct SimDomainClockProvider;                          // zero-sized: holds no per-domain state
impl DomainClockProvider for SimDomainClockProvider {
    fn now(&self, context: &ClockProviderContext<'_>, domain: &CanonicalId, generation: u64)
        -> Result<i64, ClockProviderFailure>;
}
pub fn domain_canonical_id(domain: DomainId) -> Option<CanonicalId>;   // `d{n}`
pub fn domain_id_of(canonical: &CanonicalId) -> Option<DomainId>;
pub fn pulse_clock(owner: &OwnerId, domain: DomainId, generation: u64) -> Option<ClockRef>;

// the records
pub struct StandingOrder { unit, channel: OrderChannel, value: OrderValue,
                           set_at: ClockStamp /* DomainTick */, by: ObjId, revision: u64 }
pub struct DomainTickCommit { domain, tick, clock: ClockRef, window: ActionWindowId,
                              accepted: Vec<IntentRef>, deltas: Vec<UnitDelta>,
                              facts: Vec<BoundaryFact>, rng_inputs: CanonicalHash,
                              draws: Vec<u32>, state_hash: CanonicalHash }
pub struct TacticalIntentDoc { domain, tick, unit, station, epoch, channel,
                               kind: IntentKind, value, contact: Option<ContactId>, slot }

// the applying authority, and the measurement
pub fn apply_pulse(record: &mut DomainRecord, period_ms: u32, now: &ClockStamp) -> Result<u64, String>;
pub fn clear_pulse(record: &mut DomainRecord) -> Result<(), String>;
pub fn plan_maintenance(state, book, now, period_ms, only) -> PulseMaintenance;   // pure
pub fn run_maintenance(ctx: &mut Ctx<'_>, only: Option<DomainId>) -> PulseMaintenance;
pub fn measure_tick_bundle(units: usize) -> Option<TickBundleMeasurement>;
pub fn check_domain_capacity(units: usize) -> Result<TickBundleMeasurement, String>;
```

**Registration, all under `mush-sim`'s own `OwnerId`** (the kernel owner fence requires source-owner == schedule-family-owner == fired-action-owner, cut 7's three fences): four typed families — `tick_windows` (K's `ActionWindowRecord` codec, with the `AuthorityGuardKind::Window` validator), `domaintick_schedules` (K's `DurableSchedule` codec), `domain_tick_commits`, `standing_orders`; **one** `reg.domain_clock_provider`; three action providers — `tactical_intent` (submit), `domain_pulse_arm` (the only kernel way to create a `DurableSchedule`), `domain_tick` (resolve); **one** `reg.schedule_source(CanonicalId::new("domaintick"), …)`. `handoff.rs` owns this module's single `"action"`/`"outcome"` `definition_validator`, so the pulse **contributes its six descriptors there** rather than registering a second — the arrangement cut 7's sensor ingress made inside `mush-tactics`.

**The tick's own shape.** `DomainTickScheduleAdapter::plan` re-derives the candidate (`plan_due(..) == Advance(planned) where planned == advance`) and **refuses a stale one rather than acting on it** (`handoff.rs`'s rule), reads the domain host as actor, derives `tick = occurrence.due().at - 1` (the window that collected `[T-1, T)` is resolved by the occurrence due at `T`), and — because `plan` is the only place on this lane with an `&Engine` — **re-checks every accepted intent's pinned station witness through `World::channel_grant_is_current`**, passing the still-current set into the draft. `DomainTickProvider::prepare_authoritative_with_context` then: closes and consumes the window under two *distinct* derived `RequestUid`s (one receipt per operation, so close and consume cannot collide), takes `resolution_order()`, opens **one** RNG stream via `PrepareCtx::start_resolution`, writes one `StandingOrder` post-image per continuous intent and one recorded draw per discrete one, **refuses — never skips —** an unclassifiable intent and one whose witness has moved, writes the `DomainTickCommit`, drops the record that falls out of the `MAX_RETAINED_TICK_COMMITS` 64 ring, reaps the spent window, opens the next one, and attaches one `DurableOutboxIntent` per boundary fact. Restart policy: `ScheduleCatchUpOrder::LatestFirst` with `max_catch_up: 1` — a tactical pulse that was asleep resolves **now** rather than replaying an hour of empty ticks, and the kernel reports the forgiven gap.

**`state_hash` is derived, never accepted.** `DomainTickCommit::new` computes it from `(domain, tick, clock, window, accepted, deltas, rng_inputs, draws)`; the `TryFrom` wire recomputes and refuses a mismatch. A replay that omits an accepted intent therefore **fails the hash** rather than reproducing it (`e7c8_b4_a_commit_that_omits_an_accepted_intent_fails_its_state_hash`).

**The generation is the record's revision.** A domain revision is strictly increasing and never reused, so a promote → demote → promote round trip cannot resurrect the first pulse's generation — which would have made an intent pinned to it readable again after a stand-down. (Deriving it from the *previous* generation cannot do this: `Passive` carries none, so the second promotion would restart at 1. This was found by `e7c8_d1_promote_demote_promote_changes_only_the_cadence_and_opens_a_new_generation` before it was fixed.)

**The measurement (B5, §9 q6, 08i answer 15) — encoded, not assumed.** `measure_tick_bundle(N)` encodes a worst-case tick: one `StandingOrder` post-image per unit, the commit with one delta and one draw per unit, the next window's post-image, the spent window's and the ring's removals, and one boundary-fact outbox payload per unit up to `MAX_BOUNDARY_FACTS_PER_TICK` 64.

| N | replay writes | % of 1 024 | bundle bytes | % of 16 MiB | outbox bytes | % of 256 KiB | nearest cap |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 1 | 5 | 0 % | 1 562 | 0 % | 116 | 0 % | replay writes |
| 8 | 12 | 1 % | 5 748 | 0 % | 116 | 0 % | replay writes |
| 16 | 20 | 1 % | 10 532 | 0 % | 116 | 0 % | replay writes |
| 32 | 36 | 3 % | 20 100 | 0 % | 116 | 0 % | replay writes |
| **64** | **68** | **6 %** | **39 236** | **0 %** | **116** | **0 %** | **`MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES`** |
| 128 | 132 | 12 % | 65 412 | 0 % | 116 | 0 % | replay writes |
| 256 | 260 | 25 % | 117 764 | 0 % | 116 | 0 % | replay writes |

**The nearest cap is `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES` at every N**, and the answer §9 q6 wanted is sharper than "which is nearest": **no K8 cap ever binds a tick.** At the highest N a window can ever admit — `MAX_WINDOW_CAPACITY` 256 — the encoded tick is 25 % of the replay-write cap and under 1 % of both byte caps, and one unit past that is unencodable because the commit refuses more deltas than the window could have admitted. So `MAX_INTENTS_PER_TICK` = `MAX_WINDOW_CAPACITY` 256 is the real frontier, and **`MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` 64 is confirmed as §7 already had it: a latency and offer-budget policy figure** (~6 % of the replay-write cap, four times below the window's own ceiling), not a K3-derived one. The pin `e7c8_b5_a_tick_at_the_measured_cap_stays_under_sixty_percent_of_every_k8_cap` fails when a change pushes past 60 % of whichever cap is nearest; `check_domain_capacity` refuses promotion past the cap **and pages with the measured size and the finishing command** — never a truncation, never a silent split. **Slot-hold time** was not measured: `DurableScheduleDriver::drive_one` is a private `async fn` of `world_task.rs` with no test seam (cut 7's deviation (g), verbatim), so the figure would be a fabrication. Recorded as **deviation (e)**.

**The tactics outbox adapter (OPEN 4), the tree's first.** `crates/mush-server/src/tactics_outbox.rs` `TacticsBoundaryOutbox`, stamped with `mush-tactics`' `OwnerId` and the adapter id `tactics.boundary`, registered by `world_task::production_durable_outbox()` on the one path every spawn shares. It validates the strict `BoundaryFactWire` (`deny_unknown_fields`), re-derives the stable id `{kind}/{domain}/{tick}/{unit}` and **refuses a forged one permanently**, bounds the payload at `MAX_DURABLE_OUTBOX_PAYLOAD_BYTES`, and is idempotent on the stable id — which is what at-least-once delivery after a crash requires. Cut 9 replaces the sink; the bounded inspection ring (`MAX_RETAINED_BOUNDARY_FACTS` 256) is cut 8's.

**Commands.** `+fire <contact>` · `+launch <bay>=<contact>` · `+heading <x>,<y>,<z>` · `+thrust <n>` · `+posture <0-3>` · `+dc <section>` · `+dock <contact>` · `+eject`, plus `@domain/tick <id>` and `@domain/replay <id>=<from>-<to>` as **new switches on cut 6's baseline `@domain`** (no new stanza). Every verb is a **selector**: it resolves the actor's focused station, builds one `TacticalIntentDoc` and stages `tactical_intent` through cut 2's seam under a key deterministic over `(unit, station, tick, kind)`. The eight verbs join `@station`'s existing `CommandCompatibility::Authoritative` group, `LegacyPostImage` is restored after, and each has a row in `k8_5_production_compositions_publish_command_compatibility` (as does `@domain`, whose class is unchanged). Eight allowlist stanzas, category `authoritative`, in byte order; **`PINNED_BASELINE` untouched at 158**.

**The fallback controller (C30A under K7.0's rule).** Deterministic and immediate: it creates **no `PendingDecision`**, and its idempotency key is byte-identical to the key a human order takes on the same `(unit, station, tick, kind)` slot — so a competing LLM decision is a *collision on one key*, which is exactly "reuse that decision's record and key rather than minting a second". The declared mapping never attacks: every offensive kind reduces to damage control, and the continuous channels are left alone (`e7c8_b6_the_declared_fallback_covers_every_intent_kind_and_never_attacks` enumerates all nine arms).

**Where the combat verbs live, and why it is not `mush-tactics` — deviation (a).** §3.8 puts them beside the `TacticalUnit` family they were to mutate; that family's migration is deferred (§4.0f (vii)), and what a verb actually needs is the *sim* side — the unit's domain, its `DomainRecord`'s pulse, the focused station, its channel, its control-lease epoch. Registering them in `mush-sim` therefore adds **no crate edge at all**; registering them in `mush-tactics` would have required a new `mush-tactics → mush-sim` edge for types the verbs only read. They move with the unit family at E7k.

**Edges, as proof.** `cargo tree -p mush-sim --depth 1` → `{mush-core, mush-economy, mush-proto, mush-scenes, serde, serde_json, smol_str, thiserror, tracing}` — **byte-identical to cut 7's**. `cargo tree -p mush-tactics --depth 1` → `{mush-core, mush-dice, mush-proto, mush-ruleset, …}` — **byte-identical to cuts 6 and 7's**. `mush-server` gains no dependency. **Cut 8 adds no crate and no module**, so the `server_modules_register_without_collision` name pin is untouched.

**Deviations.**

- **(a)** The combat verbs are `mush-sim`'s, not `mush-tactics`', for the edge reason above.
- **(b)** **B2's `TacticalUnit` typed-family migration is deferred to E7k** on cut 6's `PlacementRecord` precedent (§4.0f (vii)); `unit.status` — the projection half — **is** built. §4.1's cut-8 row is amended in place.
- **(c)** Because the unit family is deferred, the tick's **per-unit authoritative post-image is the `StandingOrder`**, cut 8's own record, not a `TacticalUnit` post-image. That is what the measurement encodes, and it is not a second unit truth: it is the order book S2A's continuous half requires and §3.8 already declares.
- **(d)** The discrete resolution is a **placeholder mechanic** (one recorded draw per intent, `draw % 2` for effect) with no damage model: `TacticalUnit`'s cells and sections cannot be written from a prepared provider until (b) lands. The *evidence* — the recorded draw, the ordered delta, the state hash, the boundary fact — is real and is what the D lens tests.
- **(e)** **Slot-hold time is not measured, and there is no Redis-gated tick-replay test.** Both need to drive `DurableScheduleDriver::drive_one`, which is a **private `async fn` of `world_task.rs` with no test seam** — cut 7 hit exactly this and recorded it as its deviation (g). Bundle size *is* measured, and the replay property is proved one layer in: the domain clock, the `Pulse` record and the derived `state_hash` are byte-identical across a reboot from the persisted documents (`e7c8_d1_the_tick_scalar_is_identical_across_two_reads_and_a_reboot`), and a commit that omits an accepted intent fails its hash. Cut 8 therefore adds **no `#[ignore]` and no `*_gated` test**, so `ci.yml` needs no new job and `ci_gated_test_inventory` is unchanged. The end-to-end Redis crash-replay row stays open, and the seam it needs is a `world_task` test hook — a K-shaped item E now owns.
- **(f)** Cut 8 **does not** call `detection_pass`/`delivery_pass` and does not write `Provenance::Reported`. Both take `&World` and `&mut PropagationStore`; the scheduled action has neither, and `mush-sim` has no `mush-propagation` edge (the dependency runs the other way). The detection driver and the relay pass therefore need a `propagation.detection` Coarse tick **inside `mush-propagation`**, gated on the promoted set it can already read from `SpatialState` — recorded here as the buildable shape, not built. Cut 7's B13 and deviation (f)/K15 stay open.
- **(g)** `JourneyState::Promoted` still has no writer: promoting a *cruising carrier* into a live domain is cut 6's re-issued row, and it needs (f)'s domain↔journey seam. The four-cell `vf_c1e_` pin is untouched.
- **(h)** Cut 8 registers **no `FactProvider` namespace** (D-E7-30 holds), no second window, contact, placement or lease family, and no economy edge.

**Tests (40 in this cut).** 14 in-crate in `mush-sim` (`tick.rs`: the tick function, both wires, the id map, the state hash, the measurement table and frontier, the capacity refusal, apply/clear; `combat.rs`: the heading parse, the fallback key and its exhaustive mapping, the slot token), 19 in `crates/mush-sim/tests/e7c8.rs` across the three lenses plus the DECIDED probe, 4 in `crates/mush-server/src/tactics_outbox.rs`, and 3 in `crates/mush-tactics/tests/module.rs` for the projection. Every hazard-axis test asserts the **observable** outcome — the rendered line, the refusal bytes, what a second viewer sees — and every guard is proven on **every** arm that can cross it (all eight verbs, all nine intent kinds, all four registry-mutation opportunities, all three permanently-undeliverable outbox arms).

**Verify lenses, and what each is proved by.** **D** — `e7c8_d1_*` (the scalar across two reads and a reboot; derived, stable identities; a generation change that refuses rather than re-scales; a stand-down that never reuses a generation; a pure provider). **C** — `e7c8_c1_*` and `e7c8_b5_*` (the cap fits, one past it pages with its measurement, the frontier is the window's not K8's, replay is bounded and reads identically for absent and nonexistent). **X** — `e7c8_x1_*` (no economy token anywhere on the tick path; no self-built invocation; no panicking production line) and `e7c8_x3_*` (the frozen registry).

**Hands to cut 9, as built:** `BoundaryFact::stable_id()` = `{kind}/{domain}/{tick}/{unit}`, delivered through `tactics.boundary`, idempotent under at-least-once retry; and `@domain/promote` + the applying authority, which an operation's activation calls to promote a domain.

### 3.9 Cut 9 — `mush-campaign`: declared operations, formations, resolution

**Q5D.** A **new neutral crate**; one logical operation, **separately versioned terms and runtime under one `OperationId`**. R1 is satisfied by the shared id and the one-to-one link without conflating consent with runtime. 10a's 5A is rejected because it re-learns the E4b2 lesson: every roster edit becomes either a terms amendment or a hole in the hash.

```rust
struct OperationTerms {                      // FROZEN at activation (T6)
    operation: OperationId, objective: ObjectiveSpec, legal_targets: Vec<TargetClass>,
    stakes: Vec<Consequence>, bond: HoldId, sides: Vec<SideSpec>,
    eligible_defenders: AudienceSpec, notice: ClockStamp, window: (ClockStamp, ClockStamp),
    visibility: EconomicVisibility, consent_hash: [u8; 32],   // ← mush-economy's helper, shared
}
struct OperationRuntime {                    // MUTABLE
    operation: OperationId, participants: Vec<Participation>, force: TaskForce,
    reinforcements: Vec<UnitRef>, withdrawals: Vec<UnitRef>, casualties: Vec<UnitRef>,
    handoffs: Vec<SeatHandoff>, progress: Progress, state: OperationState, revision: u64,
}
enum OperationState { Declared, Activated, Running,
                      Resolving { pending: BTreeSet<ConsumerId> }, Resolved, Aborted }
struct FormationKind { id: CanonicalId, branch: CanonicalId, echelon: u8,
                       composition: Composition, command_roles: Vec<RoleSpec> }
struct Composition { minimum: u8, maximum: u8, preferred: u8,        // `exact` ≡ minimum == maximum
                     allowed: BTreeSet<CategoryId>, required_command: Vec<RoleSpec>,
                     required_support: Vec<RoleSpec> }
struct FormationNode { id: FormationId, kind: CanonicalId, scope: OrgScopeId,   // exactly one (R2)
                       parent: Option<FormationId>, members: Vec<UnitRef> }
```

**Terms freeze; the roster does not touch them.** **Changing the roster never changes the terms hash** — a reinforcement is not an amendment to what the defenders consented to be told, and `consent_intact` (`contract.rs:1066-1068`, main) is re-checked at every transition.

**How the hash is actually shared, because Draft 0.1's "share the helper" is not buildable as written.** `consent_hash(issuer: &PrincipalId, consent: &ConsentSurface) -> [u8;32]` (`contract.rs:1014`, main) takes an **economy** `ConsentSurface` — parties, `ContractTerms`, conditions, deadline, visibility, scope, adjudicator, evidence — and its body is *sort the parties by `principal_key`, build a field-ordered canonical struct, `serde_json::to_vec`, `Sha256::digest`* (`:1015-1029`). `mush-campaign` cannot call it without either taking a `mush-economy` dependency **and** stuffing seat/operation fields into contract-shaped ones, or forking the discipline — and Q10D/10a §6 sharpening 3 forbid the fork. **The buildable reading of "share, not fork":** `mush-economy` factors the last three lines out as `pub fn consent_digest(domain: &str, canonical: &impl Serialize) -> [u8;32]`, `consent_hash` becomes a two-line wrapper over it with `domain = "contract.consent.v1"`, and `OperationTerms` and cut 10's `SeatOffer` call it with `"campaign.operation.v1"` / `"campaign.seat.v1"` over **their own** field-ordered canonical structs. One discipline, one implementation, **domain-separated** so an operation hash can never be replayed as a contract hash — which forking would not have given either. Sorting rules carry over verbatim: any principal vector sorts by `principal_key`, every other vector's order *is* consented content. This is a small, additive `mush-economy` change and belongs in §10's handbacks.

**T6's visibility classes.** `EconomicVisibility::Audience { members, announce_issuer }` with `announce_issuer` defaulting to `false` (`record.rs:339-365`, `:362-363`, main) is already T6's *"notice of the window, not the plan"*: public; eligible defenders; participating organizations; covert existence with a visible vulnerability warning; staff/audit only. **"Declared" means every party entitled to defend receives sufficient notice and knows the window — not that the attacker's plan or identity is published.** `MAX_AUDIENCE` is 32 (`record.rs:322-323`).

**The bond** is an economy hold placed by the attacker's **own** authority — never an industry reservation, because it is money (T4). S5A's ≈ 24–72 h notice is `OPERATION_NOTICE_MIN/MAX` (§7).

**Formations** (Q9A/T8/R1/R2). `composition` is a **constraint set, not one enum**, and **`exact` is `minimum == maximum`**, not a separate mode. **Strictness is scoped** — at *creation*, at *doctrine qualification* ("is this still a full squadron?"), or as *an operation's requirement* — **never to a formation's existence or its ability to deploy**. An understrength permanent formation is normal, not an error. `ruleset-tacsim` authors the instances in cut 1's packs, so a four-unit lance and a five-unit star share an echelon and remain distinct kinds. **Each node names exactly one owning `OrgScopeId`** and `force.*` capabilities resolve through the existing grant machinery (`crates/mush-org/src/authorize.rs:143-200`, main), so **R2 needs no core change**. It does need one composition-root addition: `org_capability_vocabulary()` is **non-empty** and is a typo catcher at the grant command (`crates/mush-server/src/modules.rs`; at `a1934a7` it holds the economy ids, `mush_housing::CAPABILITIES`, `org.role.manage` and `org.grant.delegate` — §4.0; `crates/mush-org/src/wiring.rs:78-93`), so granting an unlisted `force.*` id **fails loudly at `@org/grant`** — the ids must be added there. Administrative parentage is unchanged by attachment; **a unit is in at most one operation at a time** (R1), with exactly one operational command parent within it.

**Terminal settlement** (Q17A). An operation ends by writing **one** `campaign.operation.resolved` root fact into the mechanical journal (T2). Every consequence is a separate, **idempotent**, causally linked consumer transition keyed off that root. `Resolving { pending }` is only meaningful if the set is closed, so **`ConsumerId` is a closed engine enum, enumerated here**:

```rust
enum ConsumerId { CampaignOutcome,   // the outcome row recorded on the operation           — always
                  FrontControl,      // `mush-faction` Front/ControlRecord (lib.rs:235-274) — iff a
                                     //   Consequence names a (RegionId, ControlKind)
                  BondSettlement,    // the E4 Hold released, forfeited or split            — iff bond
                  ContractOutcomes,  // outcome selection on linked contracts (contract.rs:227-276)
                                     //                                                     — iff any
                  ForceRetirement }  // task force dissolved, units released                — always
```

The **required set is computed once, at entry to `Resolving`, from the frozen terms**, and stored on the runtime — never re-derived later. That is the whole crash-safety argument and it has a second edge Draft 0.1 missed: if a later build adds a sixth consumer, **a stored `Resolving` set must not grow and a `Resolved` operation must never re-open**; an unknown stored `ConsumerId` at boot fails closed and pages level 2 rather than being dropped as unrecognised. The operation stays `Resolving` until every member acknowledges, and only then reaches `Resolved`. 10a's 17B is rejected: it claims an atomicity the tree does not have across three crates (08c §12).

**Commands.** `@op/declare <objective>` · `@op/terms <id>/<field>=<value>` (pre-activation only) · `@op/activate <id>` · `@op/join <id>=<unit|formation>` · `@op/withdraw <id>=<unit>` · `@op/show <id>` (per audience) · `@op/resolve <id>=<outcome>` · `@op/consumers <id>` (staff) · `@force/create|attach|detach|assign` · `@formation/show <id>`.

**Tests (~30).** *Freeze:* a terms edit after activation refuses; a roster edit leaves `consent_hash` byte-identical; a mutated surface makes `consent_intact` false and the operation refuses to progress — it disputes and pages rather than settling; the notice window sits inside `OPERATION_NOTICE_MIN/MAX`. *Audience:* a non-audience viewer sees no operation id, issuer or objective (the existence-hiding renderer treats it like an account id); an audience member sees the window and notice but **not** the plan when `announce_issuer` is false; a recursive leak check finds no covert operation's existence; an audience over `MAX_AUDIENCE` refuses. *One operation:* a unit already in an operation refuses a second; withdrawal releases it; a formation attached keeps its administrative parent; a node with no `OrgScopeId` is unconstructible; a `force.*` capability missing from the vocabulary fails at registration, not silently at use. *Composition:* an understrength formation exists and deploys; the same constraint set refuses at creation and at doctrine qualification; `minimum == maximum` behaves as `exact`; a lance and a star share an echelon and stay distinct kinds. *Bond:* the bond is a `Hold` placed by the attacker's own authority; an attacker without funds cannot activate; it settles exactly once per outcome row. *Resolution:* the root fact is written once; each consumer transition is idempotent under replay; a crash after two of five consumers resumes and does not double-pay; `Resolved` is unreachable while any consumer is pending; a permanently failing consumer opens a level-2 escalation with the recommended command filled in (08c §4.6) and **suspends only the operation**. *Fronts:* a resolution advancing a front writes one `ControlRecord` change, and re-running the consumer writes none — and because `mush-faction`'s `Clock` is a **segment counter with no due instant** (`sim.rs:67-98`, main), the consumer fills segments and never restamps a deadline. *Added by the review:* one operation's permanently failing consumer **does not starve another operation's fan-out** (08c §5.2 B4's wall-of-disputes shape); a **bond released twice across a restart** is unreachable — the release is keyed off the root fact and is a HIT on replay; a stored `Resolving` set containing an id this build does not know **pages rather than completing**; adding a consumer in a later build leaves already-`Resolved` operations untouched (a probe re-boots with a sixth id registered); `@op/show` for a non-audience viewer is byte-identical for a covert operation and for an id that never existed, at every verbosity, and `@op/consumers` is staff-only; a `force.*` capability granted **before** it was added to the vocabulary still authorizes nothing (`authorize` matches by equality, `authorize.rs:143-200`) and `@org/show` flags it rather than deleting it; every operation, terms, runtime and formation record re-enters through its constructor (§4), and a stored participant list over `MAX_OPERATION_PARTICIPANTS` refuses on load.

**Verify lenses.** **Z (freeze integrity)** — can any path change what a defender consented to be told, or progress an operation whose surface no longer hashes? **A (audience)** — can a viewer learn a covert operation exists, or its plan, through any render or error? **I (idempotent fan-out)** — can a crash, retry or duplicate root fact double-apply a consequence, or strand an operation in `Resolving`?

**Hands to cut 10:** the operation scope seat grants, musters and readiness obligations attach to, and the participation runtime settlement indexes on.

### 3.10 Cut 10 — crew, proxy seats, musters, the oracle

**Crew** (C11A/C13A/C14A/C15A/T16). Named NPC objects occupy dramatically relevant positions — captain, helm, tactical, engineering, marine command; routine watchstanders, technicians, loaders, stewards and medical staff are an **aggregate complement** carrying readiness, capacity and morale. A **periodic readiness obligation** covers ordinary payroll and support — an E4b1 `Obligation` on the **operation cadence with success notifications off** (doc 10 §15) — and individual employment contracts are used only when a named NPC's personal terms matter. **T16:** the actor at a station supplies the relevant traits, and **a human proxy directing an NPC still uses the NPC's sheet** — the human adds judgment, not an absent character's stats. Any wider effect needs a causal action or changed ship state, **never a free global bonus for seat occupancy**. Crew quarters reuse E5a's tenancy shape (08d §3.5), not a new one.

**Proxy seats are consent grants, not contracts** (Q10D; doc 10 R6 **as amended**), and their `consent_hash` uses cut 9's shared `consent_digest` under the `"campaign.seat.v1"` domain — never a forked hasher, never an economy `ConsentSurface` stuffed with seat fields (§3.9). The governing fact: **an OOC proxy player is not an in-world principal.** C1A/R3 forbid an account principal and keep it out of any contract or canon record, and T13 says the absent character did not take the station — **so an E4 contract can name neither, and there is no third candidate.** 10a's 10A is rejected for exactly that reason.

```rust
struct SeatOffer { id: SeatOfferId, operation: OperationId, audience: AudienceSpec,
                   eligibility: EligibilitySpec, expires: ClockStamp, scope: SeatScope,
                   revocation: RevocationTerms, fallback: FallbackSpec, consent_hash: [u8; 32] }
struct SeatScope { unit: ObjId, station: StationId, channels: BTreeSet<ChannelId>,
                   mission_order: OrderRef, roe: RoeRef, permitted_area: AreaRef }
struct SeatGrant { offer: SeatOfferId, actor: ObjId /* the NPC */, driver: DriverToken,
                   accepted_at: ClockStamp, consent_hash: [u8; 32], state: GrantState }
enum GrantState { Dormant, Active { epoch: u64 }, Revoked, Expired }
```

**T11's guardrails are the granted channel set on the NPC's `StationSpec`** (`mush-sim/src/data.rs:414-435`, channels at `:426`, main), **not contract prose**. Proxy control grants tactical discretion, **not** title, administration or an unrestricted possession verb: no asset transfer or abandonment, scuttling, fallback change, loadout or crew mutation, organization spending, surrender of unrelated assets, departure from the authorized operation, or assumption of another station. **Ordinary tactical loss cannot be made impossible without making the seat fictitious.**

**Live control is a lease derived from the grant, not the grant itself — and the lease is K6's `ControlLeaseState`.** `Dormant → Active` is a `control_handoff_request` through `crates/mush-sim/src/handoff.rs` (a private module; `HandoffProvider`/`HandoffScheduleAdapter` are not re-exported) naming the grant's holder, a `reason` (`seat.activate`) and the safe `boundary`, applied by `checked_apply_handoff` under the scheduler principal; `Active { epoch }` records the lease epoch that apply returned. Revocation bumps the unit's control epoch immediately (`bump_control_gen`, `crates/mush-sim/src/data.rs`) so stale intents fail `channel_grant_is_current`, and the seat returns to its declared fallback at a safe boundary (T11) through the same request path. **Cut 10 persists no lease-like record of its own** and reads the lease family for every "is this channel held" question (08i §4).

**The K6 gate, as a predicate over the K6 memo rather than a promise** (08i answer 2; §2.4). Draft 0.2's three-condition pre-K6 predicate assumed the lease kernel was absent; it is present, so condition (2) — *no live domain* — is retired in favour of the memo predicate below. **Draft 0.3 also retired condition (3) and that was wrong** (08j S1): it rested the "no channel under a live lease" refusal on `ControlLeaseState::checked_request_handoff`, which does not perform it. `checked_request_handoff` refuses on `require_revision`, `DuplicatePending`/`PendingExists`, `self.holder == draft.next_holder` → `NoControlChange`, and `require_boundary`; it never compares `draft.requested_by` to `self.holder`, because **requesting over a live lease is the ordinary path** — `mush_contract_tests::tactical_gate`'s Gate-3 revoke and the `Change::ControlLease` mutator in `crates/mush-server/src/world_task.rs` both unwrap it, and `HandoffProvider::prepare_request` (`crates/mush-sim/src/handoff.rs`) adds no holder check and registers no `AuthorityGuard`. As written, cut 10 would have evicted a live human operator and the gate's middle leg would have failed.

**The gate is therefore two policy refusals plus one kernel boundary refusal**, over one memo condition:

1. *Policy (Draft 0.2's condition (1)):* the target station is **vacant or held by the grant's own declared `FallbackSpec`** — a live human operator is **never** evicted by a seat activation; the request is refused naming the holder's station and nothing else about the holder. A test pins that **E7 ships no eviction path at all.** Cut 10 writes this; nothing in the tree performs it today (no `SeatOffer`/`SeatGrant`/`FallbackSpec` symbol exists under `crates/*/src`, and `@spatial/unassign` + `@spatial/assign` in `crates/mush-sim/src/commands.rs` are themselves an eviction pair).
2. *Policy (Draft 0.2's condition (3), restored):* **no channel in the grant's `SeatScope.channels` is under a live lease held by anyone but the grant's own fallback.** Cut 10 asks the lease family — it persists no lease-like record of its own (08i §4) — and refuses naming the station. This is **cut 10's own check**: the kernel answers only the boundary.
3. *Kernel (the only kernel-answered leg):* a handoff whose safe `boundary` has not arrived is not applied — `checked_apply_handoff` → `ControlLeaseError::BeforeBoundary`.

*Memo condition (retiring Draft 0.2's condition (2)):* activation of a seat on a unit **inside a `Pulse` domain** requires the K6 memo's rows that cut 10 depends on to read **production** — *control leases/handoffs* (production kernel and `mush-sim` authority; the policy ingress is cut 5's) and *contacts* (production family; the sensor ingress is cut 7's) — **plus cut 6's placement and cut 8's unit migrations landed**, which the cut order guarantees. The memo's *fixture-only* rows — atomic tactical damage, unit DTOs, bridges, projection-leak proofs — are not on cut 10's path: damage is cut 8's, DTOs are `unit.status`'s projection, and the leak proofs are cut 7/8 probes. **The memo now exists** — `docs/design/09a-k6-look-back.md` (`7e1cf46`) — so the predicate reads *it*, not 08i answer 2's paraphrase, and cut 10's `#[ignore]` pin cites the memo's rows by name.

**LLM-driven seats are K7-gated** (08i answer 3): a proxy seat whose driver is an LLM controller needs K7.0–K7.3's `PendingDecision` lifecycle; cuts 1–11 ship deterministic fallback drivers only, and the LLM rows in the tests below are `#[ignore]` pins naming K7.
