An **E4 contract links to the operation only when an actual fictional principal** — embodied character, named NPC, organization — **has economic terms**; an unpaid proxy seat needs none, and inventing one to hold the consent hash is 10a's 10A error.

**Attribution and audit** (Q19A/T9/R7). The mechanical journal records the **in-world actor** and an **opaque decision-driver token**, and nothing else. A **server-side, audit-only** record links that token to the account; ordinary projections, renders and exports cannot reverse it, and one staff verb can. OOC participation history and matchmaking standing **consume the audit record**, live outside the world, and **never become IC settlement**: T13's absent character earns **no** pay, advancement, reputation, knowledge or property, and standing is a **crew-call eligibility policy input only** (R6).

**Focus** (Q20A/T12). OOC proxy control **consumes the account's one foreground focus** (cut 5). The player's own character remains at their durable location and **cannot act concurrently**; entering proxy mode hands off that character's station, clearly identifies the represented NPC and exposes **only that NPC station's observer projection** (cut 7). Leaving restores AI control; it never moves or protects the player's character, who is present and vulnerable under E5's attention budget and non-response semantics (C22A/R5). **Accepting an offer does not seize focus** — the grant is `Dormant` until activated.

**Musters** (T14/Q21A). E7 uses **E6a's `mush-party`** — game-neutral, `{mush-core}` deps only, not a principal, never learning about money (08f §2). **There is never a TacSim-specific party record**; if E7 lands first it pulls that exact cut forward unchanged. An operation publishes start, expected duration, committed formations, character billets, proxy-eligible NPC stations, certifications and restrictions, command succession, secrecy and compensation. At muster a slot resolves to a player character, a human-directed NPC, an AI-controlled named NPC, automation, or vacancy. **Missing players reduce discretion and resilience but do not cancel a valid operation.** Late arrival and early departure use the same explicit handoff (C29A).

**The distinctness oracle's consumers** (Q11A/R3/C23A/C24A). Roster lock and side assignment call `all_accounts_distinct` and **store only the verdict**; the private account id never enters a journal, contract or canon record. An account may materially assist **only one side** of a defined operation, battle, claim contest or similar competitive episode.

**Participation facts** (R6/T13; doc 10 §15). Settlement splits index on **participation**, which E4b2's outcome-indexed terms already support once the sim emits `sim.station.served { actor, station, from, to }`, answered from an **applied marker, never a status** (08c §6.2; 08f §7 item 3). Kill, salvage and delivery credit are the same shape.

**Commands.** `+seat/offer <operation>/<unit>/<station>` · `+seat/list` · `+seat/accept <id>` · `+seat/decline <id>` · `+seat/activate <id>` · `+seat/leave` · `@seat/revoke <id>` · `+muster <operation>` · `+crewcall/list|apply` · `@crew/complement <unit>` · `@crew/readiness <unit>` · `@seat/audit <token>` (staff only).

**Tests (~30).** *Guardrails:* each of T11's nine forbidden acts refuses from a proxy seat and succeeds from the owner; a granted channel set narrower than the station's limits the proxy exactly; a proxy cannot assume a second station; an intent outside `permitted_area` refuses; **ordinary tactical loss is permitted** — a probe asserts the seat is not made safe. *Revocation:* revocation bumps the epoch and every queued human, NPC, pathfinder and LLM intent under the old grant fails stale; the seat returns to its declared fallback **at a safe boundary**, not mid-tick; a disconnect uses the same path; a revoked grant cannot be re-activated. *K6 gate:* activating a grant over a **live** human operator refuses naming the station, and a test pins that E7 ships no eviction path; activation is a `control_handoff_request` whose apply waits for the `boundary` (a probe asserts no `Active` state before `checked_apply_handoff` returns); a request over a channel leased to another holder is refused by **cut 10's own policy check** — `checked_request_handoff` never asks who holds the lease (08j S1), so a probe also asserts the kernel call alone does *not* refuse it. *Focus:* activating a seat releases the player's own station's control and fires its fallback; the player's character stays where it was, present and vulnerable, and cannot act; leaving restores AI control and does not move the character; accepting without activating seizes nothing. *Attribution:* the journal carries actor + driver token and **never** an account id (a recursive scan of journal bytes asserts it); the audit record resolves the token for staff and nobody else; an export cannot reverse it. *Absent-player settlement:* the absent character receives no pay, advancement, reputation, knowledge or property from a proxy sortie; canon attaches to the NPC, unit, organization and operation; matchmaking standing changes and **cannot** be spent on character power. *Distinctness:* two characters on one account fail `all_accounts_distinct` at roster lock and side assignment; an oracle answering `None` fails closed; only the verdict is stored. *Musters:* a slot resolving to vacancy does not cancel the operation; a late arrival takes a seat through the same handoff; a probe asserts no TacSim party type exists. *Readiness:* the obligation runs on the operation cadence, is silent on success, and its arrears follow E4b1's ladder. *Added by the review:* **the gate is tested as three independent refusals — two policy, one kernel** — a station held by another human actor (cut-10 policy), a channel leased to another holder (cut-10 policy; the kernel does not refuse it), and an apply before the boundary (`checked_apply_handoff` refuses) each refuse alone, and a seat on a unit in a `Pulse` domain activates **only** when the memo predicate reads production and cuts 6/8 are present (the `#[ignore]` pin names the memo); an LLM-driven seat is an `#[ignore]` pin naming K7; a `Dormant` grant on an operation that **aborts** expires rather than surviving to a later operation; a `DriverToken` is **per grant**, so the same player accepting two seats produces two tokens and neither correlates them in any projection; a grant whose **audit row is missing at boot** refuses activation and pages, it does not activate un-attributed; `+seat/list` shows nothing about an operation the viewer is not in the audience of, and a declined offer leaves no trace a third party can read; the aggregate complement's readiness arrears **stop the obligation's subject only** (08c §4.6), never the operation; every offer, grant and complement record re-enters through its constructor (§4), and `MAX_SEAT_OFFERS_PER_OPERATION` refuses on load rather than truncating.

**Verify lenses.** **G (guardrail escape)** — can a proxy reach any authority outside its channel set, area or operation? **R (revocation and boundary)** — can control persist past a revocation, be lost mid-tick, or be handed off before the boundary? **A (audit irreversibility)** — can the account behind a driver token be recovered by anything but the staff verb, or reach a journal, contract or canon record?

**Hands to cut 11:** a crewed, operationally committed force the proving loop can run, and the participation facts settlement indexes on.

### 3.11 Cut 11 — the recovery provider, the corridor seed pack, the rate policy

**The recovery provider** (Q7A/T7; doc 10 S3A). **No new engine machinery.** The provider is a **government or corporate organization principal** owning the loaner inventory, accounts, contracts and policy. A system faucet may **subsidize** it under the `[economy]` mint cap via `create_system_obligation` (`crates/mush-economy/src/api.rs:1105-1203`, main) — **a `System` principal can never itself be a contract party** (08f D-E6-4, which the tree forces). **Eligibility is a deterministic predicate over recorded facts examining accessible operational assets** — unencumbered, reachable, deployable — **rather than total title**, so a player whose only hull is an unreachable wreck still qualifies. **Staff are never the gate.** The grant is a contract placing `Encumbrance::Lease` on the loaner (`record.rs:528-536`, main), with upkeep an `Obligation` on the campaign cadence and restrictions as ordinary conditions. Q7A requires the plan to state five things, and it does:

| Question (Q7A) | Answer |
|---|---|
| Pool exhaustion | the queue holds; the provider stops granting and pages **level 1** — an empty pool is not a decision |
| Queue priority | first-eligible, then longest-waiting; ties by claim time |
| Per-player cooldown | `RECOVERY_COOLDOWN` after a lease ends, waived when the loss was inside the restrictions |
| Insurance | approved operations only; outside them the lease is uninsured and a loss becomes a debt |
| Replenishment | the provider's own production work orders (cut 4) and salvage recovery, funded by its budget — **never a mint into the pool** |
| Upgrade / loss | buy out at a declared price or re-lease one tier up; loss inside the restrictions is written off, outside it becomes a debt |

**The corridor seed pack** (Q6A/Q18A/doc 10 §11). The full Mars–Phobos–Ceres content as **data**: propulsion and route tables, the ten commodity families, component definitions, recipes, formation kinds, combat formulas, insurance and warfare policy in **rules-definition packs**; the corridor locations, the player corporation, the government faction and its rival, the refinery, the mining drones, the hauler, the patrol craft, the dropship, the infantry, the tank and the mech in a **campaign-seed pack**. **No hot mutation** (Q18A): a change publishes a new version that coexists, and live units, blueprints, recipes and open contracts stay **pinned** until an explicit migration or in-world refit — **also T1's guarantee that a published hauling contract's route assumptions cannot shift underneath it.** **The sale path is an ordinary E4b2 contract** between the seller and a funded NPC corporation principal — consideration in `InstrumentKind::{Currency, Commodity}`, a `Before` deadline, the physical hand-over an attributed cut-2 inventory act linked back by `ExternalRef` and gated by an `inventory.transferred` fact (D-E7-31). No listing, no quote, no vendor record, no order book: all four are E6c's (§1.1).

**The timeline's rate policy** (Q8D). **The kernel owns generations** (K4a, §2.1); **the campaign layer sets rate policy** — doc 10 S1A's ≈ one OOC hour per IC day is a **policy value, not a constant in code**. Every subsystem registers a **derived cadence adapter** over the one timeline. **Multiple named cadences are fine; multiple incompatible real→IC mappings are the danger** — WoD's and Shadowrun's clocks stay separate `CadenceId`s and never derive TacSim's rate, so a compression change cannot re-price a Shadowrun lifestyle (doc 10 §20). `catch_up_cap` stays **obligation-sweep policy** (`mush-economy/src/clock.rs:240-241`, `:264-295`, main); E7 gives **journeys, production, fronts and tactical domains each an explicit catch-up policy**, because they are not obligations:

| Subsystem | Catch-up policy on a new generation | Grounding |
|---|---|---|
| Journeys | **restamp preserving remaining IC duration** — journey clocks never pause | S6A |
| Production | **pause as `Interrupted { TimelineChanged }`** unless the campaign registers a restamp | proposal §5; D-E7-8 |
| Fronts | **no restamp — there is nothing to restamp.** `mush-faction`'s `Clock` is a segment counter (`segments`/`filled`, advanced by an `advance_on` canon kind) with no `due`, no `ClockStamp` and no deadline (`sim.rs:67-98`, main), so a rate change cannot move it. The generation is recorded on the consequence fact and nothing else happens. The *real* deadline S5A cares about is an **operation's notice and vulnerability window** (cut 9's `OperationTerms.window`), and that **restamps preserving the real instant**, because 24–72 h of notice to defenders is real-world notice | S5A; `mush-faction/src/sim.rs:67-98`. Draft 0.1's "a front clock is real-time pressure" was contradicted by the lines it cited |
| Tactical domains | **refuse to tick and page** — a live domain under an unknown generation is an integrity incident | Q12A; doc 09 §2.5 |

**Commands.** `@recovery/status` · `@recovery/apply` · `@recovery/grant <who>=<tier>` · `@recovery/return <lease>` · `@tacsim/time show` · `@tacsim/time rate=<n>` (campaign authority — opens a generation) · `@tacsim/time policy <subsystem>=<policy>`.

**Tests (~28).** *Eligibility:* an unreachable wreck does not disqualify; an unencumbered deployable hull does; a holder of a live loaner is refused a replacement, naming the lease; the predicate reads only recorded facts and never a staff flag. *Provider:* an exhausted pool queues and pages level 1; the provider's budget is exhaustible and it stops granting when dry (doc 10 S7A); replenishment comes only from production and salvage, and a probe asserts no path mints into the pool; a `System` payer is refused (D-E6-4). *Lease:* the loaner carries `Encumbrance::Lease` and cannot be sold or transferred; upkeep runs on the campaign cadence; a loss inside the restrictions writes off and outside creates a debt; a buyout clears the encumbrance once. *Packs:* publishing `v2` leaves every `v1`-pinned unit, blueprint, recipe and open contract unchanged; a live hauling contract's route assumptions do not shift (T1); a migration is explicit and attributed; a refit re-pins one subject; digest stability holds across restart. *Rate policy:* opening a generation is one attributed act; every subsystem's declared policy fires exactly once per generation; a journey does not pause; production pauses with no registered restamp; a tactical domain refuses to tick and pages; a Shadowrun `sr.month` cadence is unchanged by a TacSim rate change; `catch_up_cap` still governs the obligation sweep only. *Added by the review:* a **recovery lease outliving its provider org's dissolution** is unreachable — dissolution either returns or re-assigns every live lease and pages if it cannot, because an orphaned `Encumbrance::Lease` names a contract nobody can settle; the eligibility predicate is **pure over recorded facts** and a probe re-runs it twice across a restart for byte-identical verdicts; a queued applicant whose eligibility lapses while queued is re-evaluated at grant time, not at queue time; `@recovery/status` shows no other applicant's identity or position beyond the viewer's own; a **generation change mid-queue** does not reorder it (the queue is claim-ordered, not stamp-ordered); the corridor sale's contract refuses a `System` payer (D-E6-4) and a probe asserts the NPC corporation is an `Organization`. *The proving loop:* doc 10 §11's story runs end to end on the seed pack — extract, haul, intercept, manufacture, load, deploy, fight, settle, salvage, repair, pay — with the ground engagement and the local-space interception both resolving through cut 8 and settling through `campaign.operation.resolved`.

**Verify lenses.** **L (the loop)** — does doc 10 §11's story complete without a staff step, and does every arrow leave a recorded fact? **V (versioning)** — can a pack change reprice, re-time or re-resolve anything already created or contracted? **F (faucet and floor)** — can the recovery provider be drained into an uncapped faucet, or refuse a player the floor S3A guarantees?

---

## 4. Build discipline

As 08c §7 and 08a's standing rhythm, per cut: (1) **confirm the cut's slice of this plan against the tree it is built on** — one tree since `1d5aa3d`; every `file:line` in this document predates the `91208ab` reformat, so the confirmation is **by symbol** (08i's discipline), and cut 1's pass strikes any remaining line-number citation it touches; (2) build, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace`; (3) **a probe-confirming adversarial verify of that cut** — the lenses named in its section, throwaway probe tests — **before the next cut builds on it**; (4) fold every confirmed finding with a regression test named `vf_<lens><n>_…`; (5) commit with the verify outcome in the message; **push only on the owner's word**. Both digest pins (E2 `9c917eee…`, E3-shaped `b780e3a0…`) stay green in every cut, and every cut is **fmt-clean** — `cargo fmt --all --check` is a required CI step since `91208ab` (`rustfmt.toml`, `edition = "2024"`, `newline_style = "Unix"`; 08i §2 row 17), so 08a's "`cargo fmt` stays off" no longer applies.

**No code lands from this item.** The five OPEN decisions, the contradictions it names and the deviations cut 1 takes are recorded in §3.1's "As built".

**The production-slice proposal's §9 review checklist is adopted as E7's standing verify checklist**, quoted in full and applied to every cut:

> - No command or timer can confirm before its durable acknowledgement.
> - No `?` or other fallible operation remains after the first irreversible live mutation.
> - Every retryable entry has a bounded scoped idempotency key and semantic request hash.
> - Holds subtract from availability and facility capacity; embedded advisory counters are not authority.
> - Rate changes create generations; old facts retain old generations.
> - Channels and priorities are never unioned across station grants.
> - Title/custody/location/station/grant/lease/maintenance remain separate facts.
> - Completion cannot expose an object before all required owner projections agree.
> - All lists, catch-up loops, recipes, inputs, outputs, reservations, events, and DTO bytes are bounded.
> - Staff intervention is the last resort after deterministic reconciliation and scoped recovery have been exhausted; any page includes the exact order, failed invariant, and recommended runbook.

The last item is 08c §4.6's ladder in the proposal's words, and both are binding: **resolve programmatically wherever the best action is determinable; page a human only for a genuine decision; stop only the subject; never stop the world for a local problem.** The sixth item is satisfied on the converged main by `desired_links`/`refresh_actor_links` (one grant per seat, no union; §2.2) and is kept honest by cut 5's tests.

**Five standing rules the review added, because Draft 0.1 stated each in one cut and needed them in all eleven.**

1. **Every persisted record uses the `TryFrom` wire.** `#[serde(try_from = "…Wire")]` through the type's own constructor, so a stored document re-checks every cap and invariant on load. A derived `Deserialize` walks straight past them and makes §7 advisory — the exact E5a finding (08d §3.6 A2/A3); the in-tree precedents are `mush-core`'s `DefinitionRef`, `TacticalUnit` (`unit.rs:362`) and `ActionInvocation` (`action.rs:686`).
2. **Every cut states its boot rule.** For each record: which documents boot reads, what a malformed one does (**skipped loudly and its subject marked**, never silently zeroed or dropped), what a dangling reference does, and what is *quarantined* rather than refused. Boot never trusts a stored surface (08c §5.2 B6).
3. **Every retryable entry names its key shape in this document, not in the code review.** Cut 1 `tacsim:seed:{pack}@{version}`; cut 2 the reservation holder key; cut 3 `extract/{deposit}/{cycle}`; cut 4 the work order's `wo/{id}/{verb}` at a revision generation; cut 6 `journey/{carrier}/{leg}`; cut 8 `domain/{id}/tick/{n}`; cut 9 `op/{id}/{consumer}`; cut 10 `seat/{offer}/{verb}`. All are `IdempotencyKey::Deterministic` and all must render inside `MAX_IDEMPOTENCY_KEY_BYTES` (128).
4. **One internal view feeds every projection.** The line render and any DTO come from the same function; DTO bytes are bounded; a refusal renders through the hiding renderer, never raw (08c §5.2 A2, §6.2 D1–D8). Two refusals a viewer may not distinguish must be **byte-identical**, not merely similar.
5. **LLMs explain and select; they never invent** (proposal §3, doc 10 §7). No LLM path may originate a recipe, reservation, price, capability, destination, grant, contact or completion result; an LLM-sourced intent is an ordinary intent under an ordinary grant with its own driver attribution, and cut 10's revocation test covers it explicitly. **LLM-driven rows are K7-gated** (08i answer 3): cuts 1–11 ship deterministic fallback only; an immediate deterministic controller submits directly under K7.0's rule (§3.5, §3.8); every LLM-driver test is an `#[ignore]` pin naming K7, and no E crate adds a `WorldCommand::ModuleAsyncDone` arm (08i §4 rule 2).

The seeded corridor location (`crates/ruleset-tacsim/src/fixture/tacsim_corridor.json`'s `ceres_extraction`, `role: "extraction"`). `RegionId` and `ControlKind::{DeJure, DeFacto}` stay **cut 9's**, and §3.3's "two governments may each issue a claim over one area" needs only two `ClaimRecord`s with distinct issuers, which the default supports today |

The cut-2 anchors re-grounded** (`f05bf7f`), and what changed. `server_modules_register_without_collision` asserted the **16** names `["comsys","canon","org","faction","scenes","sim","tactics","economy","inventory","mail","jobs","housing","weather","party","market","script"]` — cut 2's `"inventory"` is in place, and cut 3 inserts `"industry"` **immediately after it** in all four pinned lists (the collision pin, cut 1's tacsim pin, and the two ruleset-composition pins). `PINNED_BASELINE` is **158** and `RULESETS` is still `["wod","shadowrun"]`; `@inv` is `category = "authoritative"` **with no `family`**, sorted between `@instrument` and `@link`, so `@licence` sorts after `@inv` and before `@link`. **D. G3 and the guard registry, re-confirmed.** `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` has exactly **one** production registration on the tree, cut 2's, under `mush-inventory`'s `OwnerId`. **Cut 3 registers none at all**: its extraction holds against cut 2's book under cut 2's validator, and cut 4's *capacity* book is the one that registers `mush-industry`'s — legal by construction, because the registry is keyed `(OwnerId, AuthorityGuardKind)`.

Because **cut 5 registers no decision source and ships no LLM path**, no LLM station decision can arise in cut 5; the "a fallback competing with an existing LLM decision reuses that decision's record and idempotency key" clause is therefore inherently K7 territory and ships as an `#[ignore]` pin naming K7 (§4 rule 5), while the **immediate-controller path (no `PendingDecision`) is built and tested**. **The load-bearing finding — B1's whole-lane placement migration requires the async authoritative-submit lane, and is landed as a grounded design, not code, in this pass.** OPEN 2 (owner-taken) migrates the **whole** `SpatialState.placements` lane onto a typed `sim.placement` `state_family` on K's `revision_validator` contract, so `@spatial/*` and the carrier-root invariant ride one validator. Confirmed by symbol on `ed4d96d`: a `state_family` is **written only through an `ActionTxn`** (`crates/mush-core/src/action_state.rs` `ActionTxn::{insert, replace, remove}`, keyed by `checked_key`), which exists **only inside a prepared authoritative action** (`prepare_authoritative`/`prepare_resolved_authoritative_with_context`, declaring its `revisions()` — the `tactical_gate.rs:481-518` pattern). `Ctx::action_state`/`scan_action_state` are **read-only** (`crates/mush-core/src/module.rs:4008`). But every placement mutation today is **synchronous**: `crates/mush-sim/src/transition.rs`'s `apply` does `st.placements.insert` + `persist_placement(out, …)` (an `Outbox` `JsonSet` on `placement:<obj>`), and `SpatialState.placements` (the in-RAM `BTreeMap`) is read **synchronously and everywhere** — `subtree`/`descendant_edges`/`is_placed`/`is_sim_bound`/`parent_of`/`children_of` (`data.rs`), `graph::{ancestors, project, rebuild}`, `links::desired_links` (`links.rs:27`), the `MoveAttempt`/`CarryAttempt` `PlacementGate` hooks, `boot::load_boot`/`install_journeys`, and the whole `@spatial/*` command surface (`commands.rs`). Re-pointing the durable write to the family means each placement mutation becomes a deferred `Ctx::submit_authoritative` → prepared action → `txn.replace` (cut 2's ingress seam), converting the synchronous placement lane to the async two-phase lane and changing the semantics every `@spatial`, move/carry-gate, `links`, `focus`, `station` and boot test depends on. That is a coherent-but-large migration (a new authoritative placement action provider with the carrier-root invariant read through `context.action_state` in `prepare_authoritative`, a boot hydration of the family, and the RAM projection fed from it) whose **half-done state does not compile or pass** — it is whole-lane or nothing, which is exactly why the owner ordered it "first and alone, CI-green." It cannot be landed CI-green with confidence inside this single pass without risking a tree that does not build, which §4's cardinal rule forbids. **Decision, honestly recorded:** B1's migration is fully **grounded** in §3.6 "As built" (the family descriptor, the validator's carrier-root check, the `@spatial/*` re-point, the boot hydration, each cited to the real API on `ed4d96d`); the **green landing** this pass makes is the one B4/OPEN-3 piece whose shape E6b and §3.6 already froze and which touches neither the placement lane nor the async seam — the arrival IC-floor-raise seam in `mush-scenes` (below). B2–B6 are grounded on top of B1's design. Nothing is left un-greenable: the tree at the end of this pass differs from `ed4d96d` only by an additive `mush-scenes` method + its tests and this document.

**The §3.6 contradictions, each closed on `ed4d96d`** (folded into §3.6 "As built"):

### 4.1 Rule 1 categories by cut

09 §7's rule 1 — "no new mutating legacy handlers" — is ratified in the **narrower** form the K side proposed (08i answer 12), which replaces 08i §4's proposal. **Allowed temporarily:** bounded whole-document *workflow/catalog* records with strict decoding (the `TryFrom` wire, rule 1 above), deterministic keys (rule 3), a **declared future family and migration slot**, and **no participation in authority or prepared mechanics**. **Not exempt, ever:** reservations, contacts, leases, windows, production state, mechanical offers, or anything that changes action legality. **The economy carve-out** (09 §7 item 5) applies where the Ledger/seal gates prevent migration — not automatically to every E6/E7 handler. A durable root plus an independently keyed consumer chain is acceptable transitional causality when the root retains the complete bounded pending-consumer set (cut 9's `Resolving { pending }`). **Mechanism:** a **pinned allowlist tripwire in `mush-contract-tests`** enumerates every mutating `Registrar::command` handler against a checked-in allowlist carrying a category per entry — `read-only | legacy-post-image | authoritative | economy-carve-out` — and its future family; **every E7 cut adds its handlers to that allowlist in the same commit**, named by future family, or the tripwire fails. K8.5 replaces the allowlist with command registration metadata of the same three-way shape.

| Cut | Records | Category | Future family / path |

| 2 | pools, reservations | **authoritative**, prepared path from the first commit — `ReservationBook` under `mush-inventory`'s `OwnerId`, `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator | K4b's `ReservationRecord`/`ReservationBook` (landed) |
| 2 | lots, tranches (lineage) | **authoritative** — the same owner's typed family; lineage is production state, not workflow | `inventory.lot` beside the book |

Draft 0.4 read "exempt, legacy-post-image" for all three records; §4.1's own closing rule overrides it. Cut 6 instead gathers every carrier-root placement change behind **one checked path**, `transition::move_carrier_subtree`, which is where the carrier-root invariant lives | `sim.placement` — **E7k**, after 08h Q5's review |
| 6 | `CarrierJourney`, `DomainActivation`, `DomainSummary` | **exempt** — workflow, legacy-post-image; **confirmed as built** (§3.6). Bounded whole documents at `cjourney:<root>` / `activation:<id>` / `summary:<id>` with strict `TryFrom` wires re-running every invariant (the leg cap 32 is `RouteDef`'s, re-run twice inside its own wire), deterministic keys, and no part in authority or prepared mechanics: legality derives from the **placement graph**, never from `JourneyState`, and cut 6 registers no `revision_validator`, no `state_family`, no `schedule_source` and no second `FactProvider` namespace | `sim.journey`, `sim.domain` (activation authority is cut 8's) |
| 7 | `Envelope`, `Phantom`, `ContactProvenance`, `Sighting` | **exempt** — workflow/side projection, legacy-post-image; **confirmed as built** (§3.7). Bounded whole documents at `envelope:<id>` / `phantom:<id>` / `provenance:<hex>` / `sighting:<observer>/<subject>` with strict wires re-running every constructor invariant, deterministic keys, and no part in authority or prepared mechanics: no legality check reads any of them, and cut 7 registers no `revision_validator` and no `state_family` of its own. **Observations are not records here at all** — they go through tactics' authoritative ingress. `Sighting` is the detection pass's own idempotency book, added in place by §3.7's "As built" so the tripwire's category set is complete | `propagation.envelope`, `propagation.phantom`, `propagation.provenance`, `propagation.sighting`; contacts = K6's `ContactState` (landed) |
| 7 | `SensorJob`, the six `sensor_*_schedules` cursors | **authoritative** — `mush-tactics`' typed families under `mush-tactics`' own `OwnerId`, written only inside an `ActionTxn` by the staff-offered `sensor_arm` action. A durable cursor cannot live anywhere else, and the arming provider holds **no** `ContactState` handle | `tactics.sensor_jobs`, `tactics.sensor_*_schedules` |
| 8 | `DomainTickCommit`, `StandingOrder`, the window, the pulse cursor | **authoritative** — `mush-sim`'s own typed families (`sim/domain_tick_commits`, `sim/standing_orders`, `sim/tick_windows` over K's `ActionWindowRecord`, `sim/domaintick_schedules` over K's `DurableSchedule`), written only inside an `ActionTxn` by `tactical_intent` / `domain_pulse_arm` / `domain_tick`, with `ResolutionRecord` carrying the recorded RNG. Tactical intents are mechanical offers; the eight combat verbs publish `Authoritative` | `sim.tick_commit`, `sim.standing_order`; window/schedule/resolution = K's (landed) |
| 8 | unit truth (`TacticalUnit`) | **deferred to E7k** — *amended in place by cut 8's §3.8 "As built" (contradiction (vii))*. Draft 0.4 made `tactics.unit` cut 8's migration; the conversion is **whole-lane-or-nothing** on exactly cut 6's `PlacementRecord` grounds — a `state_family` is writable only through an `ActionTxn` inside `prepare_authoritative`, while `UnitStore` is read **synchronously** by `@unit/show|create|damage|remove`, the destroy/detach gates, the recovery reporter, the panel feed, the `ObjDestroyed` backstop and every rendering ruleset, and written synchronously by three legacy commands. What cut 8 ships instead is the half with no such obstacle: **`unit.status` as a PROJECTION**, never stored, composed at read from the unit, `Obj.location` (placement's own projection, D1), cut 7's `ContactLedgerState` and K5a's grant | `tactics.unit` — **E7k**, beside `sim.placement` |
| 8 | `DomainRecord`'s `DomainClock::Pulse` | **legacy-post-image** — the cadence field of the pre-existing `sim` `domain:<id>` document, written by the world-side applying authority. It rides the same synchronous `SpatialState` lane `PlacementRecord` does and moves with it at E7k; it takes no part in prepared mechanics (the *provider* that answers a `ClockRef::DomainTick` read is composition-frozen and reads the record, never the reverse) | `sim.domain` — E7k with the placement lane |
| 9 | `OperationTerms`, `OperationRuntime`, `FormationKind`, `FormationNode` | **exempt** — workflow, legacy-post-image; the bond is an E4 `Hold` under the **economy carve-out** | `campaign.operation`, `campaign.formation` |
| 10 | `SeatOffer`, `SeatGrant`, complements, readiness | **exempt** — workflow, legacy-post-image; the readiness `Obligation` rides the economy carve-out; activation is K6's lease (authoritative) | `campaign.seat`, `campaign.crew` |
| 11 | recovery leases, the corridor sale, subsidies | **economy carve-out** — E4 contracts, `Encumbrance::Lease`, `Obligation`, `create_system_obligation`; migrate when K8.0 closes gates 1 and 3 | 08h Q5's order, after E7k's review |
| 11 | the recovery queue and applications, the corridor seed pack's version pins, the journey/production/front/domain catch-up policies | **exempt** — workflow/catalog, legacy-post-image; the lease, the sale and the subsidies are the carve-out row above | `recovery.queue`; `tacsim.pack` (cut 1's catalog); `tacsim.time` |
| K6 | leases, contacts, windows | **authoritative** — K's families; E7 registers no second one | — |

What this table does not license: a cut whose record is listed "exempt" may not later take part in a prepared guard, an offer or a legality check without moving to "authoritative" first; the tripwire's category is the tell.

## 5. Kernel migration — a later cut, "E7k"

The proposal's **E7k** milestone is retained as E7's twelfth, later cut. It does **not** ship with cuts 1–11 and **nothing in them blocks on it** (10a §4 dep 2). Also deferred to it, and named here so it is not forgotten: cut 9's `consent_digest` factoring (§3.9) is *not* part of E7k — it is a small additive `mush-economy` change that ships **with cut 9** (§10).

**The gate, with owners** (08i answer 13, G-B; §2.4). **All three §2.4 gates are now closed** (re-read on the merged main, 2026-09-05; 08j §4). Gate 2 was closed on `91208ab` for commands, ticks, boot callbacks, observers and contextual hooks (`Ctx::action_state`/`scan_action_state`), and its one residue — no typed read inside `move_attempt`, `carry_attempt`, rendering or perception hooks (G-B) — closed with K7's `HookReadContext` (`0e3f2e8`), which `crates/mush-housing/src/hooks.rs` `AccessHook` already takes. Gates 1 and 3 closed with **K8.0** (`3d0788d`): `ReplayWrite::LedgerChunkSet` with `ReplayTarget::Module` routing and both backends normalizing it, and canonically derived `ledger_seals` inside the bundle — with the **legacy-predecessor bar surviving on purpose** (`flush_prepared_action` still rejects an unresolved legacy ledger predecessor), so a migration commit moves a whole path or none of it. 08i row 26's E fallback draft is withdrawn. **E7k's remaining precondition is the K8 harness alone** — K8.1–K8.3, which 09 §K8 marks closed together with K8.0 and the CI jobs, so in practice the gate that remains is not a K item at all. **Then a migration review opens.** Q3A is explicit — *the trigger opens a review, never an automatic migration* — and 08c §12 says of the equivalent move that it "is then still an explicit **owner decision**, not a mechanical port" (`08c:1806-1808`, main), **because the journal would stop being sole authority for money-adjacent truth, which is the property every economy invariant is anchored to.**

**What would move — re-scoped** (08j S11). Draft 0.2 listed "the cut-2 inventory reservations and cut-4 capacity reservations **from `mush-inventory`/`mush-industry` documents** onto authoritative K3 state families". That contradicted this plan's own §4.1: cut 2's and cut 4's reservations are **authoritative from the first commit**, on K4b's `ReservationBook` (`crates/mush-core/src/resource.rs`), with per-owner `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validators — they are never conventional documents, so there is nothing there for E7k to move. K8.5's lane rule (09b §2) forecloses the alternative reading outright. What E7k actually moves is therefore **three things**: (i) **the Ledger append accompanying a settlement**, onto `PreparedLedgerAppend::authoritative_replay_write` inside the bundle; (ii) **work-order transitions** (cut 4's `LegacyPostImage` workflow documents, which name their future family at registration); and (iii) **cross-owner output materialization** — title + custody + placement + inventory in one bundle. *(Cut 3 did **not** take (iii): its extraction bundle carries two owners' **typed families** under one composition-declared `Registrar::delegate_state_families` grant, which is what OPEN 1's own fallback described. (iii) is four owners and includes an economy post and a sim placement — cut 4's work-order output — and it is still E7k's. See §3.3's "As built".)* Cut 9's resolution fan-out is a candidate too, and Draft 0.2's caveat — "only when the kernel provides a truly atomic mixed bundle" (Q17A) — **is now satisfied** by `ReplayWrite::LedgerChunkSet`, so the fan-out's obstacle is scope and review, not a missing primitive.

**Until then** the economy journal remains authority and E7's conventional documents are projections or workflow records. **Every record in cuts 2–4 and 9 is shaped for a mechanical migration** (proposal §2.3, adopted verbatim as a build rule): canonical whole-document post-images, one mutation per key per transition, explicit revisions, deterministic idempotency keys, and no hidden side effects after the final fallible preflight.

**What must prove equivalence before the conventional handler is removed** (proposal §8) — the **memory and Redis restart matrix** and the **acknowledgement-loss matrix**, both against both stores: restart after commit / before RAM install loads the committed post-state; restart after ack / before output recovers the receipt and keyframe; a lost ownership ack converges by replaying the same request UID; acknowledgement loss cannot duplicate state or re-run RNG; an action-created object survives allocator restart **without id reuse**; a partial materialization always has a complete exact pending intent; legacy pre-state dependencies cannot be lost beneath a later committed action (doc 09 K3 exit criteria, `09:2122-2133` *(K)*, under `#### K3.1. Durability-audit closure` at `09:2051`). Redis-gated tests are explicit ignored tests locally **and** an explicit Linux CI job, so skipped coverage cannot count as passing (K3.1e, `09:2071-2078` *(K)*).

---

## 6. Decisions

Each cites the 10a answer, doc 10 item, or `file:line` it rests on. **Ov** marks an override of the production-slice proposal.

- **D-E7-1** E7 is **one coherent narrow vertical slice** — one ground engagement and one local-space interception with authored units; construction balance, the weapon-family matrix, boarding, aerospace transitions and fleet tuning are later (**Q22A**; doc 10 §11). **Ov:** the proposal's outcome is "one generic production flow"; production is cuts 2–4 of eleven, not the whole of E7.
- **D-E7-2** **One C8-conformant reservation shape, two owners, and money is neither.** Doc 09 C8's exact record — `{ id, owner, resource, holder, amount, revision, expires, state }`, `09:997-1006` *(main)*, field-for-field as cut 2 writes it — delivered as `ReservationRecord`/`ReservationBook` in `crates/mush-core/src/resource.rs`; cut 2 writes that record, not its own — is used for **matter** in `mush-inventory` and **capacity** in `mush-industry`, each registering its own `AuthorityGuardKind::Reservation` validator under its own `OwnerId` (`module.rs:1792-1800`; registry keyed `(OwnerId, kind)`, `action.rs:1420`, `:1520`, routed `:1534-1561`, main). **This is not a judgement call between Q13A and Q3A — doc 10 T4 decides it in terms:** "factory and drydock capacity use industry-owned reservations; cargo, components and commodities use inventory/custody reservations". Draft 0.1 listed it as open (§9 q5); it never was. **Money stays an E4 `Hold` and never grows a resource field** (T4; doc 10 §15). **Ov:** the proposal lists money, material and capacity as undifferentiated "reservations" (§4).
- **D-E7-3** **`mush-inventory` is the single source of quantity truth**, location-bound to E3 custody endpoints; industry, markets, contracts and simulation consume it and **none keeps a private stock number** (**Q13A/Q2D**; `custody.rs:61-131`, main). **Ov:** the proposal's "material vault" quantities and `material_source: AssetContainerRef` become an `EndpointRef`.
- **D-E7-4** A lot carries `{ id, commodity, quantity, grade, lineage }` — **not `legal`, not `custody`** (**Q2D**). Custody is the endpoint's; stolen/disputed follows the **title** record (`record.rs:521-527`, main); embargo legality is a **jurisdiction test at transaction time**; licensing is the **acting principal's** entitlement.
- **D-E7-5** Interconversion is **one journaled attributed act each way**; **lineage is never discarded, only coarsened in the projection** (**Q2D**; T5).
- **D-E7-6** Yield is a **deterministic curve with depletion**; variation is discrete campaign events, not per-period rolls; a survey reveals **bands**, never the exact reserve (**Q1A**; doc 10 S4A).
- **D-E7-7** **Claims are records; licences are entitlements** — transferable, pledgeable, contestable, suspendable, seizable; a licence is an `InstrumentKind::Entitlement` account checked at transaction time (**Q14A/Q1A**; `record.rs:50-61`, main).
- **D-E7-8** The **default clock-generation policy is pause as `Interrupted { TimelineChanged }`, and the *campaign* registers a restamp** (proposal §5, adopted; **Q8D**). **Ov:** the proposal's §3 gives the ruleset "clock choice"; Q8D reserves generations to the kernel and rate policy to the campaign layer.
- **D-E7-9** **Every subsystem declares an explicit catch-up policy**, because journeys, production, fronts and tactical domains are not obligations; `catch_up_cap` stays obligation-sweep policy (**Q8D**; `clock.rs:240-241`, main). Journeys **never pause** (doc 10 Q6A) — deliberately the opposite of production's default.
- **D-E7-10** **One foreground focus per account, atomically acquired, with a declared fallback on release**; multiple connections attach to one focus (**Q20A**; C21A/T12). The focus is **world-owned ephemeral state keyed by the immutable account id, cleared at boot** — not a K6 lease keyed on the account, not an `AccountRec` field (08i answer 10; D-E7-34).
- **D-E7-11** **Billets are eligibility; only the focused station's `ChannelGrant` is live; channels and priority are never unioned across seats** (**C2A**; K5a's `ChannelGrant` in `crates/mush-core/src/world.rs` and `desired_links`/`refresh_actor_links` in `crates/mush-sim/src/links.rs`). **Delivered on the converged main** (08i §2 row 4); Draft 0.2's "the merge, not a rebase, supplies this" is discharged.
- **D-E7-12** The **carrier-root invariant replaces the traveler-unplaced rule** (doc 10 **§16 item 9**/C16A; `route.rs:67-90`, `commands.rs:1529-1564`, `data.rs:678-690`, main).
- **D-E7-13** Domains **promote on four deterministic triggers and demote only at a quiet boundary after a durable summary**, under a hard `MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS`; **state is preserved both ways** (**Q15A**).
- **D-E7-14** **One propagation layer, one physical context, two resolvers; an acknowledgement is itself a delivery** (**Q4A**; R4).
- **D-E7-15** **Contacts belong to a platform or station, never a side or the world**; sharing is a delivery carrying source, age, confidence and provenance; unidentified and false contacts exist from the start (**Q16A**). **The record is K6's `ContactState` and `mush-tactics` is its only lifecycle authority**; propagation supplies observation intents through the coordinator ingress and owns the false-contact phantoms (`SubjectKey::Scoped`); truthless contacts are K's v2 (08i answer 1; D-E7-36).
- **D-E7-16** **Tactical ingress takes the prepared K lane from the first pulse; resolution is one deterministic domain-tick commit per domain per tick, bounded by the K3 bundle limits** — specifically `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES` on count and the two byte caps on size (`authoritative.rs:27-28`, `:35-38`, main), with `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` a **policy** figure far below them (§3.8, §9 q6) rather than a derived one. The tick is invoked through K's actorless ingress — a `Registrar::schedule_source` adapter, `scheduled_action_ingress`, the `"scheduler"` principal class, a deterministic `(domain, tick)` key — over an `ActionWindowRecord` as the frozen intent set (**Q12A**; 08i §2 rows 8, 10; answer 5). Its clock is the one owner-scoped `DomainClockProvider` (answer 4), its bundle is measured (answer 15), and its bundle serializes with every other authoritative commit on the store's single pending-intent slot (answer 6). **Ov, narrowed:** the proposal's E7e binds *every* mutation to K5 legal offers; K5b **has landed** (`OfferRouteTable`, `Engine::resolve_legal_action`), cut 8's tactical actions and cut 4's quote bind to it, and the exempt workflow verbs of §4.1 do not until E7k.
- **D-E7-17** **No economic transaction occurs inside a combat pulse**; a tick emits typed boundary facts the economy consumes afterward; combat RNG stays in the mechanical journal (**T2**).
- **D-E7-18** An operation is **one `OperationId` with separately versioned frozen `OperationTerms` and mutable `OperationRuntime`; a roster edit never changes the terms hash** (**Q5D**; R1), using **`mush-economy`'s existing `consent_hash`**, shared not forked (`contract.rs:999-1030`, main; 10a §6 sharpening 3).
- **D-E7-19** **Formation composition is a constraint set with `exact ≡ minimum == maximum`, strictness scoped to creation, doctrine qualification, or an operation's requirement — never to existence or deployment** (**Q9A**; T8). Each node names one `OrgScopeId` (R2). R2 needs no **core** change; it does need the `force.*` ids added to `org_capability_vocabulary()` (`crates/mush-server/src/modules.rs`, which chains economy **and housing** capabilities plus the two org ids — §4.0), since that non-empty set is a typo catcher and granting an unlisted id fails at `@org/grant` (`wiring.rs:78-93`).
- **D-E7-20** An operation resolves through **one journaled root fact and idempotent consumer transitions, staying `Resolving` until every consumer acknowledges** (**Q17A**); a truly atomic mixed bundle waits for the kernel (08c §12).
- **D-E7-21** **A proxy seat is a consent grant, not an E4 contract**; live control is a lease derived from it, and the lease is **K6's `ControlLeaseState`**, requested and applied through `mush-sim`'s `handoff.rs` at a `boundary`; cut 10 ships grants, fallback, dual attribution and the activation ingress, persists no lease-like record, and gates activation inside a live domain on the **K6 memo's production rows plus cuts 6/8** rather than on a K6 kernel that is already in the tree (**Q10D**; doc 10 R6 as amended; 08i answer 2; §3.10).
- **D-E7-22** **The account id is opaque and audit-only**; the only exposure is a boolean distinctness oracle and one staff verb; it never enters a journaled, contract or canon record (**Q11A/Q19A**; R3/R7). Its two prerequisites — an immutable id on `AccountRec` and a boot-hydrated character→account map — ship in cut 5 (`mush-store/src/types.rs:718-741`, main, has neither).
- **D-E7-23** The **recovery provider is a funded organization**, eligibility tests *accessible* assets, and a `System` principal is never a party (**Q7A**; T7; 08f D-E6-4). Staff are never the gate.
- **D-E7-24** **Packs are immutable under their digest; versions coexist and consumers stay pinned** until an explicit migration or in-world refit (**Q6A/Q18A**; T1; `mush-sim/src/data.rs:227-232`, `mush-core/src/module.rs:622-634`, main).
- **D-E7-25** **The eleven cuts of 10a §4 are E7's structure**; the proposal's milestones **E7a–E7e** fold into cuts 2–4 and its **E7k** becomes §5. **Ov**, explicitly.
- **D-E7-26** **E7 builds no market and consumes none.** Listings, order books, quotes, **vendors** and the safe public projection all stay **E6c's** (08f §2, §5.2), and doc 10 §11's "one open market" is incomplete until E6c (10a §5). The physical loop is carried by contract, not by a vendor path — see D-E7-31.
- **D-E7-27** **E7 develops on the converged `main`** — the K merge Draft 0.2 made a precondition landed at `1d5aa3d`, the reformat at `91208ab` (headnote). **No E7 cut 1–11 blocks on a K item that is not in the tree**: Q3A's C8 interim is K-independent; K4a/K4b/K5a/K5b/K6's kernel and the scheduled ingress are landed and cited by symbol in §2; what remains K-side is K8.0 (E7k's gates 1 and 3), K7.0–K7.3 (cut 10's LLM rows), the doc-only G-A `DomainClockProvider` contract cut 8 implements, the G-B hook-read context no cut needs, and the K6 memo cut 10's predicate reads. Draft 0.2's G4 ("no epoch comparison exists in main") was true of `3967e12` and is closed by `World::channel_grant_is_current`. E works on `main` in small commits; K7 works on `codex/` branches with `git merge-tree` before each merge-back (08i answer 14).
- **D-E7-28** **Crew quarters reuse E5a's tenancy shape** rather than a second one; `Ended` stays reachable only through a `Resolution` (08d §3.5, D-E5-1).
- **D-E7-29** **A route is a pack-authored `RouteDef`** (cut 1), because none exists: a route is only `<room>[:secs]` tokens parsed in `route_create` today (`crates/mush-sim/src/commands.rs:1584-1648`, main), which T1's frozen route assumptions cannot rest on.
- **D-E7-30** **Five crates each register exactly one `FactProvider` namespace** — `mush-inventory` (`inventory.*`), `mush-industry` (`industry.*`), `mush-sim` (`sim.*`), `mush-tactics` (`tactics.*`), `mush-campaign` (`campaign.*`). None exists today, so every such `Fact` condition answers `None` (`facts.rs:35-40`, `:62-81`; `modules.rs:148`, main), and `EconomyModule::with_facts` **panics** on a duplicate namespace (`crates/mush-economy/src/module.rs:615`, assert `:617-625`) — one provider per crate, no exceptions. Draft 0.1 named three crates and cited the panic to the wrong file.
- **D-E7-31** **The corridor's sale is an ordinary E4b2 contract, not a market path.** `Supply::Stock`, quotes and vendors are **E6c's** (08f §2, §5.2), so Draft 0.1's and 10a §4 dep 1's "E6b's vendor-from-stock path" names something that does not exist. E7 sells through a two-party contract with a funded NPC `Organization` (never a `System` party — 08f D-E6-4), settling on an `inventory.transferred` fact rather than a new deliverable kind, so **no `mush-economy` deliverable change is needed** (T2; §1.1, §3.11).
- **D-E7-32** **`consent_hash` is shared by factoring, not by calling.** `mush-economy` exposes `consent_digest(domain, canonical)`; `consent_hash` becomes its wrapper; `OperationTerms` and `SeatOffer` call it under their own domain tags. One discipline, one implementation, domain-separated (**Q10D**; 10a §6 sharpening 3; `contract.rs:1014-1029`, main). Draft 0.1's "share the helper" was not buildable — the helper takes a `ConsentSurface` (§3.9).
- **D-E7-33** **Every persisted E7 record uses the `TryFrom` wire and states its boot rule** (§4 rules 1–2; 08d §3.6 A2/A3). A derived `Deserialize` makes every §7 cap advisory on load, which is the E5a finding this programme is not allowed to re-learn.

**Added by Draft 0.3 — the K answers, as decisions.**

- **D-E7-34** **Foreground focus is world-owned ephemeral state keyed by the immutable account id, cleared at boot; station authority is K6's durable `(subject, channel)` lease; a focus change revokes/bumps the grant witnesses and any lease transfer is a `control_handoff_request` with a `boundary`. Cut 5 ships no release or handoff code of its own** (08i answer 10; §3.5).
- **D-E7-35** **Cut 6 owns the typed `PlacementRecord` migration on K's `revision_validator` contract; stations and assignments (`HostStations`, `StationAssignment`, `HostAssignments`) are separate control-topology records, not placement; `DomainRecord` authority is cut 8's; `@domain/promote` never mutates the clock registry** (08i answers 4, 8; §3.6).
- **D-E7-36** **There is no `ContactRecord`.** `mush-tactics` is the sole `ContactState` authority; propagation supplies bounded deterministic observation intents through the trusted coordinator ingress, owns the false-contact phantoms as `SubjectKey::Scoped` subjects, keeps provenance/confidence as a side projection with no lifecycle authority, and defers truthless contacts to K's v2 (08i answer 1; §3.7).
- **D-E7-37** **Cut 8's driver implements K's owner-scoped `DomainClockProvider` (one per owner, answering by `domain_id` and generation); the production unit family is `TacticalUnit` only; `unit.status` is a projection over unit + placement + contact + authorized-station data; controllers select from `ProjectedLegalAction`s — one offer per action kind with ≤ 256 opaque target choices, larger domains need ranking or bounded sectors; the tick bundle is measured against 1,024 replay writes / 16 MiB, never assumed; the tick enters through `scheduled_action_ingress` over an `ActionWindowRecord`** (08i answers 4, 5, 7, 8, 11, 15; §3.8).
- **D-E7-38** **Immediate deterministic controllers submit directly under K7.0's rule (no `PendingDecision` where no asynchronous provider races); a fallback competing with an existing LLM decision reuses that decision's record and key; `DecisionControllerId.owner` is open to `mush-sim`/`mush-campaign`; LLM-driven rows stay K7-gated in cuts 5, 8 and 10** (08i answer 3).
- **D-E7-39** **Lane occupancy: one unresolved authoritative intent per store/backend; K7's 512 ceiling counts `PendingDecision` only; every E7 authoritative commit shares the one pending-intent slot** (08i answer 6; §9 q6).
- **D-E7-40** **Rule 1 is applied per cut through §4.1's categories and the pinned allowlist tripwire in `mush-contract-tests`; every E7 cut adds its handlers to it in the same commit with their future family named; the economy carve-out is claimed only where the Ledger/seal gates prevent migration** (08i answer 12).
- **D-E7-41** **E7k's gates 1 and 3 are K8.0's; gate 2 is closed except for the hook-read context (G-B), which no cut 1–11 needs; E7k opens after K8.0 and K8.1–K8.3** (08i answer 13; §5).
- **D-E7-42** **The K6 gate is a memo predicate:** cut 10's activation inside a live domain reads the K6 memo's production rows (leases, contacts) plus cuts 6/8's own migrations; fixture-only rows (damage, DTOs, bridges) are not on its path (08i answer 2; §3.10).

## 7. Constants

`MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS` 8 · `GROUND_PULSE_MS` 2_000 · `SPACE_PULSE_MS` 6_000 (inside S2A's 1–2 s / 5–10 s bands; **live from cut 8**, read from `[tacsim].ground_pulse_ms`/`space_pulse_ms` through `SimulationModule::with_pulse`, clamped into `MIN_PULSE_PERIOD_MS` 1_000 … `MAX_PULSE_PERIOD_MS` 60_000 rather than refused) · `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` 64 — **measured at cut 8 and confirmed** (§3.8 "As built"): a worst-case tick at N = 64 encodes **68 replay writes (6 % of `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES` 1 024), 39 236 bundle bytes (0 % of 16 MiB) and 116 outbox payload bytes (0 % of 256 KiB)**, so the **nearest cap is `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES`** and 64 is a **latency and offer-budget policy** figure, not a K3-derived one. The measurement's sharper finding: **no K8 cap binds at any N a window can admit** — at `MAX_WINDOW_CAPACITY` 256 the tick is 25 % of the replay-write cap and under 1 % of both byte caps, and 257 is unencodable — so `MAX_INTENTS_PER_TICK` 256 is the real frontier. The pin fails past 60 % of whichever cap is nearest; a domain over the cap **refuses promotion and pages with the measured size**. · `MAX_RETAINED_TICK_COMMITS` 64 (cut 8; the `@domain/replay` window and the commit family's own ring) · `MAX_BOUNDARY_FACTS_PER_TICK` 64 · `MAX_DOMAINS_PER_PULSE_FIRE` 32 · `MIN_ORDER_SCALAR` −1_000 / `MAX_ORDER_SCALAR` 1_000 · `MAX_SLOT_TOKEN_BYTES` 32 · `MAX_TICK_COMMIT_BYTES` 256 KiB · `MAX_STANDING_ORDER_BYTES` 4 KiB · `MAX_RETAINED_BOUNDARY_FACTS` 256 (the outbox's inspection ring) · `QUIET_BOUNDARY` 10 ticks · `MAX_INTENTS_PER_TICK` 256 (**= K's `MAX_WINDOW_CAPACITY`** in `crates/mush-core/src/action_window.rs` — cut 8 defines no second constant; bounded by the replay-write cap and the tick's encoded size, not by `MAX_DURABLE_OUTBOX_INTENTS` — Draft 0.1 derived it from the outbox cap, which bounds what a bundle emits, not what a tick accepts) · `MAX_OBSERVATIONS_PER_PASS` 256 (one window's worth; cut 7's per-pass intent bound) · K's `MAX_PROJECTED_ACTION_CHOICES` 256 (`crates/mush-core/src/offer.rs`; the per-offer opaque target-choice cap 08i answer 11 names — cited, not redefined; beside it `MAX_PROJECTED_LEGAL_ACTIONS` 128 and `MAX_PROJECTED_LEGAL_ACTION_SET_BYTES` 1 MiB bound a station's whole offer set) · `MAX_PACK_ENTRIES` 512 · `MAX_SEED_OBJECTS` **256** (Draft 0.4 said 2 048; cut 1's independent verify P5 found that unreachable behind the 512 entry cap, since every seeded object is an entry — see §3.1's fold block) · `MAX_LOT_PARENTS` 16 (E6's, 08f §9) · `MAX_LINEAGE_DEPTH` 32 · `MAX_POOLS_PER_ENDPOINT` 64 (**enforced** since the cut-2 fold, B8: it was declared, re-exported twice and counted nowhere — `InventoryFamilies::apply` now counts an endpoint's pools in a `PoolCensus` record written in the same bundle, on the create path only) · `MAX_LOTS_PER_ENDPOINT` 512 (new, same mechanism, `LotCensus`) · `MAX_TRANCHE_INHERITED` 64 · `MAX_TRANCHE_GENERATIONS` 64 · `MAX_RETAINED_TERMINAL_RESERVATIONS` 32 · `MAX_WORK_ORDER_INPUTS` **10** (superseded in place by cut 4's §3.4 "As built", contradiction (vii): §7's 16 against `MAX_RESERVATIONS_PER_ORDER` 12 made three checks dead, since 16 material + 1 capacity + 1 destination > 12; at 10, `10 + 1 + 1 = 12` is reachable with headroom, leaving `MAX_RECIPE_LINES` 16 as the pack cap) · `MAX_RESERVATIONS_PER_ORDER` 12 · `MAX_FACILITY_SLOTS` 8 · `MAX_FACILITY_CAPABILITIES` 32 · `MAX_RETAINED_MARKERS_PER_DEPOSIT` 8 (cut 4, D2's durable bound) · `RESERVATION_TTL` 7 IC days · `MAX_SURVEY_BANDS` 8 · `MAX_CLAIMS_PER_SUBJECT` 8 · `MAX_CONTACTS_PER_OBSERVER` 128 · `CONTACT_TTL` 30 ticks past `last_seen` · `MAX_ENVELOPE_HOPS` 4 · `ENVELOPE_TTL` 30 IC days · `MAX_FORMATION_DEPTH` 5 · `MAX_FORMATION_MEMBERS` 64 · `MAX_OPERATION_PARTICIPANTS` 128 · `OPERATION_NOTICE_MIN` 24 h / `OPERATION_NOTICE_MAX` 72 h (S5A) · `MAX_SEAT_OFFERS_PER_OPERATION` 32 · `SEAT_OFFER_TTL` 7 d · `MAX_CREW_NAMED` 16 · `RECOVERY_COOLDOWN` 14 d.

**Not constants — config.** `[tacsim]`: `enabled`, `pack_paths`, `seed_paths`, `corridor`, `active_domain_limit`, `ground_pulse_ms`, `space_pulse_ms`. `[tacsim.recovery]`: pool size, tiers, cap, thresholds, cooldown, insurance policy (Q7A). `[tacsim.time]`: the campaign's rate policy and the per-subsystem catch-up policies (Q8D) — **doc 10 S1A's ≈ one OOC hour per IC day is a policy value here, never a constant in code**. Parsed host-side like every other block: `mush-config` → `mush_server::config_ext` → `TacsimRuleset::with_config`, the `[shadowrun.economy]` pattern (08f §9).

**Which of those keys cut 1 actually ships** (OPEN 4, taken as written; the E6a `catalog_paths`/`quote_ttl_*` precedent). **Live in cut 1:** `enabled`, `pack_paths`, `seed_paths`, `corridor`. **Parsed and inert, documented as a later cut's:** `active_domain_limit` (cut 6), `ground_pulse_ms`, `space_pulse_ms` (cut 8), and **all of `[tacsim.time]`** (cuts 4/8/11). **`[tacsim.recovery]` is cut 11's and is not added at all.** And `[tacsim.time]` **never seeds the world timeline** (08i §3's configuration row): generation 1 is seeded from `[game].ic_ratio`/`ic_epoch` by `load_or_seed_world_timeline`'s `if docs.is_empty()` branch and persisted truth wins thereafter — `[tacsim.time]` is campaign rate *policy* read by cuts 4/8/11 and reaches that function never. §3.1's "As built" carries the pinned test.

## 8. The owner's answers (10a, 2026-09-03 — binding)

The owner accepted **every** recommended option — **Q1A Q2D Q3A Q4A Q5D Q6A Q7A Q8D Q9A Q10D Q11A Q12A Q13A Q14A Q15A Q16A Q17A Q18A Q19A Q20A Q21A Q22A** — plus 10a §4's eleven cuts, §5's handbacks, and the doc 10 R6 amendment (doc 10 is now Draft 0.4).

1. **Q1A** — deterministic yield curve per deposit with depletion; a survey reveals bands, never the reserve; production is scheduled, journaled lot creation on a deterministic key; variation is discrete campaign events; tenure is a `ClaimRecord`, a licence is an entitlement.
2. **Q2D** — a neutral inventory authority owns location-bound quantities **and** lots; the ruleset decides representation per commodity; every balance binds to an **E3 custody endpoint**; interconversion is one journaled act each way; a lot has no `legal` and no `custody` field.
3. **Q3A** — `mush-industry` ships a reservation record **shape-conformant to doc 09 C8** with a named migration **trigger that opens a review, never an automatic migration**; "consume atomically" before K3 means one plan, one batch, one journal-authoritative document, no fallible step after the first install.
4. **Q4A** — one `mush-propagation` crate owning contacts **and** delivery envelopes over a shared physical context with **separate detection and delivery resolvers**; tactical propagation on the domain tick, coarse scheduled delivery on a campaign cadence otherwise; **an ack is itself a delivery**.
5. **Q5D** — a new neutral `mush-campaign`; one logical operation with separately versioned frozen `OperationTerms` and mutable `OperationRuntime` under one `OperationId`; the bond is an economy hold; consequences write `ControlRecord` and `Front` through `mush-faction`.
6. **Q6A** — everything expressible as a table is a versioned TOML content pack in two kinds: immutable **rules-definition** packs referenced by digest, and **campaign-seed** packs instantiating mutable world state once.
7. **Q7A** — the recovery provider is a **funded organization** running an obligation + lease template; eligibility is a deterministic predicate over **accessible operational assets**; the plan states pool exhaustion, queue priority, cooldown, insurance, replenishment and upgrade terms.
8. **Q8D** — **one versioned world timeline owned by the kernel** with derived cadence adapters; a rate change opens a new generation and never reinterprets earlier facts; the campaign layer sets rate policy; every subsystem gets an explicit catch-up policy.
9. **Q9A** — a generic engine `FormationKind` with **composition as a constraint set** (`exact ≡ min == max`), ruleset-authored instances, and strictness scoped to creation / doctrine qualification / an operation's requirement.
10. **Q10D** — a `SeatOffer`/`SeatGrant` campaign record carrying **the contract's consent-hash discipline by sharing the helper**; an E4 contract only where a fictional principal has terms; the account reference is audit-only; live control is a lease derived from the grant.
11. **Q11A** — a synchronous account-identity provider in `mush-core` implemented by the server/store layer: `same_account`, `all_accounts_distinct`, atomic focus claim/release, synchronous updates; `None` fails closed; two prerequisites (an immutable id, a boot-hydrated reverse map) — amended by K answer 10 (§3.5, D-E7-34): focus is world-owned ephemeral state cleared at boot; the provider owns the immutable account id and the boot-hydrated character→account map only.
12. **Q12A** — tactical **ingress** enters through the prepared K lane from the first pulse; **resolution** is one deterministic domain-tick commit per domain per tick, bounded by the K3 bundle limits.
13. **Q13A** — a neutral `mush-inventory` bound to custody endpoints is **the single source of quantity truth**; industry, markets, contracts and simulation consume it.
14. **Q14A** — a unique `ClaimRecord` (transferable, pledgeable, contestable, suspendable, seizable) plus separate licence entitlements; overlapping political authority through `ControlKind::{DeJure, DeFacto}`.
15. **Q15A** — deterministic promotion on four stated triggers; demotion only at a quiet boundary after a durable summary; a hard limit on concurrently active domains with a stated policy at the limit; **state preserved both ways**.
16. **Q16A** — observations are **local**; a transmitted report creates the *recipient's* record with source, age, confidence and provenance; **unidentified and false contacts from the start**; no omniscient fused record.
17. **Q17A** — one journaled `operation.resolved` root fact, then **idempotent** consumer transitions; the operation stays `Resolving` until every consumer acknowledges.
18. **Q18A** — **no hot mutation** of a published definition digest; versions coexist; live units, blueprints, recipes and open contracts stay pinned until an explicit migration or in-world refit.
19. **Q19A** — an **audit-only** server record maps driver token → account; journals keep only the token; participation history and matchmaking standing consume the audit record and never become IC settlement.
20. **Q20A** — focus is **per account**, atomic to acquire, with a declared fallback on release; multiple connections may attach to one focus; a clean switch fires the fallback immediately, an unclean disconnect uses the existing linkdead episode; **accepting an invitation does not seize focus**.
21. **Q21A** — one neutral party/muster subsystem, and it is **E6a's `mush-party`**; there is never a TacSim-specific party record.
22. **Q22A** — **one coherent narrow vertical slice**: one ground engagement and one local-space interception with authored units; construction balance, the weapon-family matrix, boarding, aerospace transitions and fleet tuning are explicitly later.

## 9. Open questions, renumbered (defaults chosen)

Only what 10a and doc 10 leave open. Each default is what the build takes unless the owner says otherwise. **Three of Draft 0.1's thirteen are now closed** and are recorded under §11 instead: old q5 (T4 already decides it), old q11 (resolved by naming an owner) and old q13 (resolved to *merge, then develop on main*). **Items marked ★ are owner decisions in disguise** — they move scope, money or staff burden across a programme boundary, and a default is not the right instrument for them.

1. **The active-domain limit.** Q15A requires a hard limit and a stated policy but sets neither. Default **`MAX_ACTIVE_DOMAINS` 8**, and at the limit the trigger **queues** — held and re-evaluated each campaign tick, never silently dropped — because "refuse with notice" strands an interception and "degrade to scheduled resolution" is a second combat model. Config, so a host can raise it.
2. **Tick rates per domain class.** S2A gives bands, not values. Default **ground 2 s, space 6 s**, both config, both inside the bands.
3. **The pack digest algorithm.** Default **SHA-256**, matching `plan_hash` (08c §3.3) and the existing `DefinitionRef` digest discipline, with the domain/encoding version stated in the manifest rather than in the hashed bytes (doc 09 §2.6), and the manifest's own `digest` field excluded from the hashed bytes. **Closed by cut 1's §4.0 pass — the function already exists and is not re-derived:** `DefinitionDigest::for_canonical_value_with_limit(domain, &Value, max)` in `crates/mush-core/src/mechanics.rs` is SHA-256 over `canonical_json_bytes_with_limit` with the domain **length-prefixed** into the hash. Cut 1's `pack_digest` is a thin wrapper over it under the domain tag `tacsim.pack.v1`, and **cut 1 defines no second digest and no second canonical encoder** (§3.1).
4. **The interim for object-per-unit materials before cut 2.** Default: **there is none.** Cut 1's fixture ships locations, commodity *definitions*, a recipe and a route but **no quantities**, and nothing consumes matter until `mush-inventory` exists. An object-per-unit interim would be a second quantity authority — exactly what Q13A exists to prevent — and would need migrating out one cut later.
5. **Where the account-identity seam ships.** 10a §4 lists "the Q11 oracle" under both cut 5 and cut 10. Default: **cut 5 ships the seam plus the world-owned focus half** (it cannot enforce one focus without it); **cut 10 adds `same_account`/`all_accounts_distinct`** and their consumers.
6. **How `MAX_UNITS_PER_DOMAIN` is derived.** Default: **measure at cut 8** — encode a worst-case tick for N units, report which of `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_REPLAY_WRITES`, `MAX_AUTHORITATIVE_BUNDLE_BYTES` and `MAX_DURABLE_OUTBOX_PAYLOAD_BYTES` is nearest, and pin the constant where the encoded bundle stays under 60 % of it, with a test that fails when a change pushes past. 64 is the working figure and is a **latency** choice, not a K3-derived one (§3.8) — and, after 08i answer 11, an **offer-budget** choice too (K7.4's ≤ 32 AI-facing offers / 192 KiB prompt with ≤ 256 opaque target choices per offer). **Lane occupancy, recorded here as instructed (08i answer 6):** the store admits **one unresolved authoritative intent per store/backend** — not per owner, not per subject (`StoreError::AuthoritativeIntentInFlight`); K7's 512-decision ceiling counts **`PendingDecision` records only** and never scheduled tick invocations; every authoritative commit — cut 8's tick bundle, cut 2/4 reservations, cut 5/10 handoffs, cut 7 observation intents — **shares that one admission slot and serializes**, and `DurableScheduleDriver` offers one candidate per 250 ms poll and defers on legacy work. The measurement therefore reports **bundle size** (the three caps) *and* **slot hold time** per tick, because a 6 s space pulse holding the slot for 500 ms starves nothing but a 2 s ground pulse holding it for 1 s halves every other owner's throughput. A 128-record tick is compatible with the one-intent limit only as **one bundle** under the caps (answer 15).
7. ★ **If E6a has not shipped `mush-party` when cut 10 starts.** Default: **pull E6a's exact cut forward unchanged** (Q21A's own instruction), never a temporary record. **Flagged:** this moves a cut *out of* E6a and into E7's budget and schedule, and changes which programme owns `mush-party` at review time. That is a programme-scope call, not a build default. **CLOSED — moot — 2026-09-05 by cut 1's §4.0 pass:** E6a shipped it. `crates/mush-party/src/module.rs`' `PartyModule` is composed in `crates/mush-server/src/modules.rs`' `server_modules_with`, `mush-party → {core}` only. Nothing moves between programmes, cut 10 uses it unchanged, and §3's dependency note (4) is struck with this question — the eleven cuts are self-contained.
8. **Where the campaign's restamp-policy registry lives.** Default: **`mush-campaign`** (cut 9), with cut 4 shipping the pause default and the registration seam, so cuts 4–8 run correctly before the campaign layer exists.
9. **Whether `mush-industry` and `mush-inventory` are one crate.** Default: **two.** Q13A's neutrality argument is that market, contract deliverables and salvage need quantity truth without an industry dependency; one crate would give them one.
10. **The ten commodity families' exact ids.** Default: S4A's list transliterated — `metals`, `volatiles`, `carbon_feedstock`, `strategic_elements`, `structural_alloys`, `composites`, `electronics`, `propellant`, `industrial_parts`, `munitions` — authored in cut 1's rules pack and therefore changeable without code.
11. ★ **The scene IC time floor's owner** (was q11, now sharpened rather than defaulted). The *shape* is fixed here so the two candidates cannot diverge: a monotonic `ic_floor` on `SceneRecord` that `scene_ic_time` clamps to (`mush-scenes/src/data.rs:175-178`, main), raised by a journey arrival alongside a typed arrival event, `SceneTurn` as the carrier. The *owner* is a real choice: **E6b wants it** for §4.1's `ic_time` workaround (08f §7 item 4) and **cut 6 cannot be correct without it** (S6A). Default: **E6b ships it if E6b lands first; otherwise cut 6 does** — and either way cut 6 owns the arrival raise. **Flagged** because Draft 0.1 justified "whichever lands first" by citing 08f §11 q2, which had done the opposite: it *resolved* the shared `@econ/cadence` question by **naming E5b**. The precedent argues for naming an owner, not for leaving it to arrival order. **Closed 2026-09-03/04:** the owner named **E6b** (owner line, 2026-09-03), and 08i answer 9 fixes the K-side rule — `SceneClockProvider` is read-only, generation 1 lasts the scene record's lifetime, an IC-floor raise never changes it — with the start → advance → pause/restart → advance monotonicity test **E6b's** (08f §4). Cut 6 adds the arrival-raise case to that test and nothing else.
12. ★ **Whether the corridor waits for E6c's vendor path.** Default: **no** — cuts 3 and 11 sell through a plain E4b2 contract with a funded NPC corporation (D-E7-31), which is landed today and needs nothing from E6. **Flagged:** the alternative is to slip cuts 3 and 11 behind E6c so the corridor's first sale is a real listing, which is a schedule and content decision, not a technical one. Either way §11's "one open market" is incomplete until E6c.
13. ★ **Whether cut 10 is worth building before K6.** With the K6 predicate stated (§3.10), cut 10 ships offers, grants, fallback, attribution, musters and the distinctness oracle, but a proxy can take a seat **only** on a unit that is not in a live domain and whose channels are unleased — which is not the case T10 was written for. Default: **build it anyway**, because the consent record, the guardrails and the audit seam are the expensive half and K6 then adds only the boundary transfer. **Flagged** because the owner may prefer cut 10 to wait for K6 and cut 11 to run with NPC and automation crewing only. **Closed 2026-09-04 by the tree and 08i answer 2:** the K6 *kernel* — `ControlLeaseState`, `mush-sim`'s `handoff.rs`, the `ContactState` family — is landed, so the narrow pre-K6 predicate that motivated this question no longer exists; cut 10 activates through the kernel's own handoff at a boundary, and what it waits on is the **K6 memo's production rows plus cuts 6/8** (§3.10), which the cut order supplies. The owner's "build it before K6" stands as "build it before the memo, pinned to it".

## 10. What E7 hands back

**Fact kinds to register** on E4c's `FactProvider` (`crates/mush-economy/src/facts.rs:62-81`; the table at `crates/mush-server/src/modules.rs:148`, main), each owning a **stable id space**, the economy pointing *at* that journal and never duplicating it (T2). **None exists today** — `mush-jobs` is the only registered provider, so every one of these conditions currently answers `None` (unsatisfied), and `EconomyModule::with_facts` **panics** on a duplicate namespace (`mush-economy/src/module.rs:615`, assert `:617-625`), so it is strictly one provider per crate. **Every one is answered from an applied marker, never from a status** — E4c's E3 finding was `jobs.resolved` reading a status staff could set by hand (08c §6.2), and a sortie, journey, transfer or salvage fact must name the moment the domain journal recorded the effect:

`inventory.transferred { from, to, commodity, quantity }` · `inventory.lot.created` · `industry.workorder.completed` · `industry.extraction.cycled` · `sim.journey.arrived` · `sim.journey.failed` · `sim.station.served { actor, station, from, to }` (R6/T13's participation fact, doc 10 §15) · `sim.cargo.delivered` · `sim.docking.completed` · `tactics.sortie.started` · `tactics.sortie.ended` · `tactics.unit.disabled` · `tactics.unit.abandoned` · `tactics.unit.captured` · `tactics.unit.destroyed` · `tactics.salvage.recovered` · `campaign.operation.resolved` (Q17A).

The first four are new in Draft 0.2: without them the corridor's sale (D-E7-31) has nothing to settle on, and a work order a contract paid for has no fact to prove it ran.

**Three upstream asks.** (1) **The scene IC time floor** — `scene_ic_time` is `ic_started_at + rounds_elapsed × round_seconds` (`crates/mush-scenes/src/data.rs:175-178`, main); the `round == 0` guard exists but there is **no monotonic floor on the result**, so a journey arriving days later would leave later scene events stamped **before** arrival. S6A requires a floor `scene_ic_time` clamps to, raised by journey arrival alongside a typed arrival event; doc 09's `SceneTurn` clock is the natural carrier, and E6b already wants it (08f §7 item 4). §9 q11. (2) **The manual-cadence hook** — `advance_cadence_manual` (`crates/mush-economy/src/clock.rs:297-305`, main) has **no caller at `3967e12`** outside its re-export and one E4a test, and the staff verb the in-flight E5b build adds is not a sim caller either; cut 6 becomes the first at journey boundaries (T1's alignment bands) and cut 8 at sortie boundaries (doc 10 §9). (3) **`consent_digest`** — factoring `consent_hash`'s canonical-encode-and-SHA-256 tail into a domain-tagged helper so cut 9 and cut 10 share the discipline instead of forking it (D-E7-32; `contract.rs:1014-1029`, main). Additive, ships with cut 9, and `consent_hash`'s own output is unchanged for `domain = "contract.consent.v1"` — a pinned test asserts that.

**What E7 does not hand back, deliberately.** `scene.outcome` stays **E6b's** — composition-root data the server registers under adjudicator/facilitator authority; ~~the canon-outcome-kind table is wired **empty** today (`modules.rs:157`, main)~~ — **struck by cut 1's §4.0 pass:** `server_modules_with` wires `.with_canon_outcome_kinds(vec![SmolStr::new_static(mush_scenes::KIND_SCENE_OUTCOME)])`, so the table holds exactly E6b's one kind. Only the "empty" claim dies: **E7 still registers no scene-outcome kind of its own.** The **whole market** stays **E6c's** — listings, order books, quotes, vendors and the safe projection alike; E7 supplies **inventory locality** and, when E6c lands, will consume quotes. It consumes none today (D-E7-31). The party subsystem stays **E6a's** (Q21A). **Owner abandonment** (T3; doc 10 §15; 08f §1.1 keeps it "in E7 with the mechanical recipe written out") lands in **cut 3**, beside the claim seizure needing the same unilateral title effect.
